The most valuable part of Peyrol's cargo being strapped to his person, the first thing he did after being left alone in that attic room which was like the lantern of a lighthouse was to relieve himself of the burden and lay it on the foot of the bed. Then he sat down, and leaning his elbow far on the table he contemplated it with a feeling of complete relief. That plunder had never burdened his conscience. It had merely on occasion oppressed his body; and if it had at all affected his spirits it was not by its secrecy but by its mere weight, which was inconvenient, irritating, and towards the end of a day altogether insupportable. It made a free-limbed, deep-breathing sailor-man feel like a mere overloaded animal, thus extending whatever there was of compassion in Peyrol's nature towards the four-footed beasts that carry men's burdens on the earth. The necessities of a lawless life had taught Peyrol to be ruthless, but he had never been cruel.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔那点辎重里最值钱的部分，原是贴身捆在身上的；因此，一被单独留在这间形同灯塔灯室的阁楼里，他头一件事便是卸下这副重担，把它搁到床脚。然后他坐下身来，把胳膊远远地撑在桌上，带着一种彻底解脱的心情端详起它来。这件战利品从未压过他的良心。它只是在某些时候压迫过他的身体；倘若它也曾影响过他的心情，那也并非因为它来路秘密，而仅仅因为它本身的分量——这分量既碍事，又磨人，到一天下来简直叫人吃不消。它叫一个手脚灵便、肺活量深沉的水手，觉得自己不过是一头超载的牲口，由此把佩罗尔天性里那点怜悯之心，推广到了世上替人驮负担的四足走兽身上。无法无天的生涯逼着佩罗尔学会了狠，但他从来不曾残忍过。

===Original===
Sprawling in the chair, stripped to the waist, robust and grey-haired, his head with a Roman profile propped up on a mighty and tattooed forearm, he remained at ease, with his eyes fixed on his treasure with an air of meditation. Yet Peyrol was not meditating (as a superficial observer might have thought) on the best place of concealment. It was not that he had not had a great experience of that sort of property which had always melted so quickly through his fingers. What made him meditative was its character, not of a share of a hard-won booty in toil, in risk, in danger, in privation, but of a piece of luck personally his own. He knew what plunder was and how soon it went; but this lot had come to stay. He had it with him, away from the haunts of his lifetime, as if in another world altogether. It couldn't be drunk away, gambled away, squandered away in any sort of familiar circumstances, or even given away. In that room, raised a good many feet above his revolutionized native land where he was more of a stranger than anywhere else in the world, in this roomy garret full of light and as it were surrounded by the sea, in a great sense of peace and security, Peyrol didn't see why he should bother his head about it so very much. It came to him that he had never really cared for any plunder that fell into his hands. No, never for any. And to take particular care of this for which no one would seek vengeance or attempt recovery would have been absurd. Peyrol got up and opened his big sandalwood chest secured with an enormous padlock, part, too, of some old plunder gathered in a Chinese town in the Gulf of Tonkin, in company of certain Brothers of the Coast, who having boarded at night a Portuguese schooner and sent her crew adrift in a boat, had taken a cruise on their own account, years and years and years ago. He was young then, very young, and the chest fell to his share because nobody else would have anything to do with the cumbersome thing, and also for the reason that the metal of the curiously wrought thick hoops that strengthened it was not gold but mere brass. He, in his innocence, had been rather pleased with the article. He had carried it about with him into all sorts of places, and also he had left it behind him—once for a whole year in a dark and noisome cavern on a certain part of the Madagascar coast. He had left it with various native chiefs, with Arabs, with a gambling-hell keeper in Pondicherry, with his various friends in short, and even with his enemies. Once he had lost it altogether.

===Chinese===
他敞着上身、瘫坐在椅子里，体格壮实，头发花白；那颗长着罗马式侧影的脑袋，支在一截粗壮、刺满花纹的前臂上。他舒舒服服地坐着，两眼盯着自己的财宝，一副若有所思的神气。然而佩罗尔并非（一个浮光掠影的旁观者也许会这么以为）在盘算把它藏到哪里最妥当。倒不是他对这类东西缺乏经验——这类东西一向是从他指缝间化得飞快的。让他出神的，是这批东西的性质：它不像是一份靠辛劳、冒险、危难、饥寒换来的赃物，倒更像是一桩纯属他个人的好运。他清楚战利品是什么货色，也清楚它去得多快；可这一份，却是留着不走的。它跟着他，远离他这辈子出没的种种去处，仿佛来到了另一个世界。它喝不掉，赌不光，在他所熟悉的任何一种环境里挥霍不尽，甚至送人都送不出去。在这间屋里——这间屋子凌驾于他那已被革命翻了天的故土之上，而在那片故土上，他比世上任何地方都更像是个陌生人——在这间宽敞明亮、恍若四面环海的阁楼里，在一种巨大的安宁与踏实之中，佩罗尔看不出自己何苦为它操那么多心。他忽然想到，落到他手里的任何战利品，他其实从来都没真正上过心。是的，从来没有。而对这一份没有人会寻仇、也没有人会来追讨的东西格外小心地看护起来，那才叫荒唐。佩罗尔站起身，打开了他那只大檀木箱，箱子上扣着一把巨大的挂锁——这也是多年前的一宗旧赃，是他在北部湾（Gulf of Tonkin）畔一座中国市镇里，伙同几个海岸兄弟会（Brothers of the Coast）弄到手的：那几个人夜里登上一艘葡萄牙双桅纵帆船，把船员赶上一条小船放逐出海，然后自己驾着船出去逛了一圈——那是许多许多许多年前的事了。那时他还年轻，很年轻，这口箱子分到他名下，是因为别人谁也不愿搭理这件笨重家伙，也因为加固它的那些做工奇特的粗铁箍，其金属不是金子，不过是黄铜罢了。他那时少不更事，倒还挺中意这件物什。他带着它走南闯北，也把它丢下过——有一次，整整一年丢在马达加斯加海岸某处一个阴暗、恶臭的岩洞里。他把它托付给过形形色色的土著酋长、阿拉伯人、本地治里（Pondicherry）一个开赌窟的老板，简而言之，托付给他各色朋友，甚至托付给过他的敌人。有一次，他把它整个儿弄丢了。

===Original===
That was on the occasion when he had received a wound which laid him open and gushing like a slashed wineskin. A sudden quarrel broke out in a company of Brothers over some matter of policy complicated by personal jealousies, as to which he was as innocent as a babe unborn. He never knew who gave him the slash. Another Brother, a chum of his, an English boy, had rushed in and hauled him out of the fray, and then he had remembered nothing for days. Even now when he looked at the scar he could not understand why he had not died. That occurrence, with the wound and the painful convalescence, was the first thing that sobered his character somewhat. Many years afterwards, when in consequence of his altered views of mere lawlessness he was serving as quartermaster on board the Hirondelle, a comparatively respectable privateer, he caught sight of that chest again in Port Louis, of all places in the world, in a dark little den of a shop kept by a lone Hindu. The hour was late, the side street was empty, and so Peyrol went in there to claim his property, all fair, a dollar in one hand and a pistol in the other, and was entreated abjectly to take it away. He carried off the empty chest on his shoulder, and that same night the privateer went to sea; then only he found time to ascertain that he had made no mistake, because, soon after he had got it first, he had, in grim wantonness, scratched inside the lid, with the point of his knife, the rude outline of a skull and crossbones into which he had rubbed afterwards a little Chinese vermilion. And there it was, the whole design, as fresh as ever.

===Chinese===
那一次，他挨了一刀，伤口大开，血如泉涌，活像一只被割破的酒囊。当时一伙海岸兄弟会的人，为了某桩主张——又掺杂着个人的忌妒——猝然起了内讧，而这件事，他像未出娘胎的婴儿一样无辜。他始终不知道是谁砍的他那一刀。另一个兄弟，他的好哥们儿，一个英国小伙子，冲进来把他从乱局里拖了出去，此后他一连几天什么都不记得了。即便是现在，每当他望着那道疤，他都想不通自己当年怎么没死。那一回变故，连同那道伤和苦楚的养伤日子，是头一桩让他性子稍稍沉稳下来的事。许多年以后，由于他对一味无法无天的看法有了改变，转而在伊龙德尔号（*Hirondelle*）——一艘颇体面的私掠船（privateer）——上充当舵手长（quartermaster），他竟然又撞见了那口箱子，偏巧是在路易港（Port Louis），在全世界所有地方里，就在一家由一个孤身印度人开的小暗铺里。时候已晚，那条小巷空无一人，佩罗尔便走进去索回自己的东西，一切公道——一手攥着一块银元，一手握着一把手枪——结果被那人苦苦哀求着，求他把箱子赶紧搬走。他把空箱子扛在肩上弄走了，当晚那艘私掠船就出了海；直到那时，他才有工夫核实自己没弄错：因为当初刚把它弄到手不久，他曾带着一种阴冷的顽劣，用刀尖在箱盖内侧刻下一个骷髅交叉骨的粗糙轮廓，又往里头揉进一点中国朱砂。瞧，它就在那儿，整幅图案，鲜亮如初。

===Original===
In the garret full of light of the Escampobar farmhouse the grey-haired Peyrol opened the chest, took all the contents out of it, laying them neatly on the floor, and spread his treasure—pockets downwards—over the bottom, which it filled exactly. Busy on his knees he repacked the chest. A jumper or two, a fine cloth jacket, a remnant piece of Madapolam muslin, costly stuff for which he had no use in the world—a quantity of fine white shirts. Nobody would dare to rummage in his chest, he thought, with the assurance of a man who had been feared in his time. Then he rose, and looking round the room and stretching his powerful arms, he ceased to think of the treasure, of the future and even of tomorrow, in the sudden conviction that he could make himself very comfortable there.

===Chinese===
在埃斯坎波巴农庄那间满室光亮的阁楼里，头发花白的佩罗尔打开箱盖，把里头的东西统统取出来，整整齐齐地码在地板上，然后把自己的财宝——口袋朝下——铺在箱底，恰巧把箱底铺了个严严实实。他跪在地上忙碌着，把箱子重新装好。一两件厚水手衫、一件上好的呢料短外套、一截马达波兰细棉布（Madapolam muslin）的零头——这种值钱料子他在这世上全无用处——还有一批细白衬衫。谁也不敢翻他的箱子，他想，带着一个当年曾叫人望而生畏的汉子所特有的那份笃定。然后他站起身，环顾这间屋子，伸展两条结实的胳膊，便不再去想那笔财宝，不再去想将来，甚至不再去想明天——因为他忽然笃信，自己在这里定能过得十分舒坦。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
In a tiny bit of a looking-glass hung on the frame of the east window, Peyrol, handling the unwearable English blade, was shaving himself—for the day was Sunday. The years of political changes ending with the proclamation of Napoleon as Consul for life had not touched Peyrol except as to his strong thick head of hair, which was nearly all white now. After putting the razor away carefully, Peyrol introduced his stockinged feet into a pair of sabots of the very best quality and clattered downstairs. His brown cloth breeches were untied at the knee and the sleeves of his shirt rolled up to his shoulders. That sea rover turned rustic was now perfectly at home in that farm which, like a lighthouse, commanded the view of two roadsteads and of the open sea. He passed through the kitchen. It was exactly as he had seen it first—sunlight on the floor, red copper utensils shining on the walls, the table in the middle scrubbed snowy white; and it was only the old woman, Aunt Catherine, who seemed to have acquired a sharper profile. The very hen manoeuvring her neck pretentiously on the doorstep, might have been standing there for the last eight years. Peyrol shooed her away, and going into the yard washed himself lavishly at the pump. When he returned from the yard he looked so fresh and hale that old Catherine complimented him in a thin voice on his *bonne mine*. Manners were changing, and she addressed him no longer as *citoyen* but as Monsieur Peyrol. He answered readily that if her heart was free he was ready to lead her to the altar that very day. This was such an old joke that Catherine took no notice of it whatever, but followed him with her eyes as he crossed the kitchen into the *salle*, which was cool, with its tables and benches washed clean, and no living soul in it. Peyrol passed through to the front of the house, leaving the outer door open. At the clatter of his clogs a young man sitting outside on a bench turned his head and greeted him by a careless nod. His face was rather long, sunburnt and smooth, with a slightly curved nose and a very well-shaped chin. He wore a dark blue naval jacket open on a white shirt and a black neckerchief tied in a slipknot with long ends. White breeches and stockings and black shoes with steel buckles completed his costume. A brass-hilted sword in a black scabbard worn on a cross-belt was lying on the ground at his feet. Peyrol, silver-headed and ruddy, sat down on the bench at some little distance. The level piece of rocky ground in front of the house was not very extensive, falling away to the sea in a declivity framed between the rises of two barren hills. The old rover and the young seaman with their arms folded across their chests gazed into space, exchanging no words, like close intimates or like distant strangers. Neither did they stir when the master of the Escampobar Farm appeared out of the yard gate with a manure fork on his shoulder and started to cross the piece of level ground. His grimy hands, his rolled-up shirtsleeves, the fork over the shoulder, the whole of his working-day aspect had somehow an air of being a manifestation; but the patriot dragged his dirty clogs low-spiritedly in the fresh light of the young morning, in a way no real worker on the land would ever do at the end of a day of toil. Yet there were no signs of debility about his person. His oval face with rounded cheekbones remained unwrinkled except at the corners of his almond-shaped, shiny, visionary’s eyes, which had not changed since the day when old Peyrol’s gaze had met them for the first time. A few white hairs on his tousled head and in the thin beard alone had marked the passage of years, and you would have had to look for them closely. Amongst the unchangeable rocks at the extreme end of the peninsula, time seemed to have stood still and idle while the group of people poised at that southernmost point of France had gone about their ceaseless toil, winning bread and wine from a stony-hearted earth.

===Chinese===
在一小块挂在东窗窗框上的镜子里，佩罗尔正操着那把不堪佩挂在身的英国刀片刮脸——因为这天是礼拜日。那些以拿破仑获宣告就任终身执政而告终的政治动荡岁月，并未在佩罗尔身上留下什么痕迹，只不过他那头又浓又密的头发，如今已几乎全白了。佩罗尔小心收好剃刀，把穿着袜子的双脚伸进一双上等木屐（sabots）里，哒哒地下楼去。他那条棕色布裤在膝盖处敞着没系紧，衬衫袖子卷到了肩膀。这个由海上漫游者一变而为乡下人的家伙，如今在这座农庄里已安之若素——这座农庄像一座灯塔，居高临下地俯瞰着两处锚地和外面的大海。他穿过厨房。里面的一切一如他初见时那样——阳光洒在地上，墙上红铜炊具闪闪发亮，居中的桌子擦洗得雪白；只有那个老妇人，凯瑟琳姑母（Aunt Catherine），似乎轮廓变得更加分明了。连门口台阶上那只神气活现伸缩着脖子的母鸡，都仿佛过去八年一直站在那儿似的。佩罗尔嘘着把她赶开，走进院子，在水泵跟前痛痛快快洗了一通。他从院子回来时，红光满面、精神矍铄，老凯瑟琳便用细细的嗓音夸他气色好（bonne mine）。世风变了，她不再唤他“公民”，而是改口称他“佩罗尔先生”（Monsieur Peyrol）。他顺口应道，要是她的心还没有主，他当天就乐意领她去教堂成亲。这个玩笑老掉牙了，凯瑟琳压根儿不予理会，只用眼睛跟着他，看他穿过厨房走进正厅（salle）——那里面很凉快，桌椅擦得干干净净，却空无一人。佩罗尔径直穿过屋子走到前面，让外面的门敞着。听见他的木屐声，一个坐在外面长凳上的年轻人转过头来，漫不经心地点了点头算是招呼。他脸庞偏长，晒得黝黑光洁，鼻梁微弯，下巴轮廓颇为俊秀。他身穿一件深蓝色海军上装，敞着怀，露出白衬衫和一条黑领巾，领巾打的是活结，垂着长长的两端。白裤、白袜，配一双镶钢搭扣的黑鞋，这便是他全身上下的装束。一条交叉皮带上挂着的、装在黑色剑鞘里的黄铜柄佩剑，此刻正搁在他脚边的地上。佩罗尔银发红颜，在隔开些许的地方坐到了长凳上。屋前那片平整的碎石地并不算大，向海的方向倾斜下去，两边夹在两座光秃秃山丘的隆起之间。老漫游者和年轻水手双双抱臂胸前，凝视着虚空，一言不发，既像至交密友，又像素昧平生的路人。当埃斯坎波巴（Escampobar）农庄的庄主肩上扛着一把粪叉从院门里走出来时，两人也都没有动弹。他那双污黑的手、卷起的衬衫袖子、肩上的粪叉，他整个做工时的做派，多少带着一股亮相登场的味道；可这位爱国者却在清晨新鲜的亮光里，没精打采地拖着他那双脏木屐，那副拖沓相，是一个真正的庄稼人哪怕干了一整天重活之后也绝不会有的。然而他身上看不出丝毫衰弱的迹象。他那张颧骨圆润的鹅蛋脸不见一道皱纹，只有那一双杏仁形、亮晶晶的、梦想家般的眼睛的眼角处才有纹路——这双眼睛自老佩罗尔第一次与它对视的那天起，就不曾改变过。唯有他那蓬乱头发里和稀疏胡须中夹杂的几根白发，才标出了岁月的流逝，而且还得凑近了才找得见。在这半岛尽头亘古不变的岩石之间，时间仿佛停滞了、闲散了，而栖身在法兰西这最南端的一小群人，却照旧操持着他们永无休止的劳作，从一片铁石心肠的土地上挣出面包和葡萄酒来。

===Original===
The master of the farm, staring straight before him, passed before the two men towards the door of the *salle*, which Peyrol had left open. He leaned his fork against the wall before going in. The sound of a distant bell, the bell of the village where years ago the returned rover had watered his mule and had listened to the talk of the man with the dog, came up faint and abrupt in the great stillness of the upper space. The violent slamming of the *salle* door broke the silence between the two gazers on the sea.

===Chinese===
农庄的庄主直视着前方，从两人面前走过，朝佩罗尔留着没关的那扇正厅门走去。进门前，他把粪叉靠在了墙上。一阵遥远的钟声——那是村子的钟，多年前归来的漫游者正是在那个村子里饮过骡子、听过那个牵狗的人说话——在头顶那片广袤的寂静中传来，微弱而突兀。正厅的门砰地一声被狠狠摔上，打破了两个凝望大海者之间的沉默。

===Original===
“Does that fellow never rest?” asked the young man in a low indifferent voice which covered the delicate tinkling of the bell, and without moving his head.

===Chinese===
“那家伙难道从不歇着吗？”年轻人问道，声音低低的、漫不经心的，盖过了那隐隐约约的钟铃声，他的头也没有动一下。

===Original===
“Not on Sunday anyhow,” answered the rover in the same detached manner. “What can you expect? The church bell is like poison to him. That fellow, I verily believe, has been born a sansculotte. Every *decadi* he puts on his best clothes, sticks a red cap on his head and wanders between the buildings like a lost soul in the light of day. A Jacobin, if ever there was one.”

===Chinese===
“至少礼拜天是不歇的，”漫游者以同样淡然的口吻答道，“你还能指望什么呢？教堂的钟声对他来说就像毒药。这家伙呀，我千真万确地相信，他天生就是个无套裤汉（sansculotte）。每逢旬日（décadi），他就穿上自己最体面的衣裳，往头上扣一顶红帽子，在几座房舍之间游来荡去，活像个大白天里迷了路的孤魂。天字第一号的雅各宾派（Jacobin），错不了。”

===Original===
“Yes. There is hardly a hamlet in France where there isn’t a sansculotte or two. But some of them have managed to change their skins if nothing else.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。法兰西几乎没有哪个小村子里没有一两个无套裤汉。不过他们当中有些人，别的且不论，倒也设法换了张皮。”

===Original===
“This one won’t change his skin, and as to his inside he never had anything in him that could be moved. Aren’t there some people that remember him in Toulon? It isn’t such a long time ago. And yet. …” Peyrol turned slightly towards the young man. … “And yet to look at him. …”

===Chinese===
“这一个可不会换皮，至于他的内里嘛，他从来就没长过什么能被动得了的东西。土伦不是还有人记得他吗？那也并非多久以前的事啊。然而……”佩罗尔微微侧向年轻人那边……“然而看看他那副模样……”

===Original===
The officer nodded and for a moment his face wore a troubled expression which did not escape the notice of Peyrol, who went on speaking easily:

===Chinese===
军官点了点头，有那么一瞬间，他脸上掠过一丝不安的神情，这没有逃过佩罗尔的眼睛，于是他不紧不慢地接着说了下去：

===Original===
“Some time ago, when the priests began to come back to the parishes, he, that fellow”—Peyrol jerked his head in the direction of the *salle* door—“would you believe it?—started for the village with a sabre hanging to his side and his red cap on his head. He made for the church door. What he wanted to do there I don’t know. It surely could not have been to say the proper kind of prayers. Well, the people were very much elated about their reopened church, and as he went along some woman spied him out of a window and started the alarm. ‘Eh, there! look! The Jacobin, the sansculotte, the blood-drinker! Look at him.’ Out rushed some of them, and a man or two that were working in their home patches vaulted over the low walls. Pretty soon there was a crowd, mostly women, each with the first thing she could snatch up—stick, kitchen knife, anything. A few men with spades and cudgels joined them by the water-trough. He didn’t quite like that. What could he do? He turned and bolted up the hill like a hare. It takes some pluck to face a mob of angry women. He ran along the cart track without looking behind him, and they after him, yelling: ‘*A mort! A mort le buveur de sang!*’ He had been a horror and an abomination to the people for years, what with one story and another, and now they thought it was their chance. The priest over in the presbytery hears the noise, comes to the door. One look was enough for him. He is a fellow of about forty but a wiry, long-legged beggar, and agile—what? He just tucked up his skirts and dashed out, taking shortcuts over the walls and leaping from boulder to boulder like a blessed goat. I was up in my room when the noise reached me there. I went to the window and saw the chase in full cry after him. I was beginning to think the fool would fetch all those furies along with him up here and that they would carry the house by boarding and do for the lot of us, when the priest cut in just in the nick of time. He could have tripped Scevola as easy as anything, but he lets him pass and stands in front of his parishioners with his arms extended. That did it. He saved the patron all right. What he could say to quieten them I don’t know, but these were early days and they were very fond of their new priest. He could have turned them round his little finger. I had my head and shoulders out of the window—it was interesting enough. They would have massacred all the accursed lot, as they used to call us down there—and when I drew in, behold there was the *patronne* standing behind me looking on too. You have been here often enough to know how she roams about the grounds and about the house, without a sound. A leaf doesn’t pose itself lighter on the ground than her feet do. Well, I suppose she didn’t know that I was upstairs, and came into the room just in her way of always looking for something that isn’t there, and noticing me with my head stuck out, naturally came up to see what I was looking at. Her face wasn’t any paler than usual but she was clawing the dress over her chest with her ten fingers—like this. I was confounded. Before I could find my tongue she just turned round and went out with no more sound than a shadow.”

===Chinese===
“前些时候，神父们开始回到各堂区去，他——就是那个家伙”——佩罗尔朝正厅那扇门的方向摆了摆头——“你信不信？——腰里挎着一把马刀，头上扣着红帽子，就奔着村子去了。他直奔教堂门口。他去那儿想干什么，我可不知道。总不会是去念那种正经祷告的吧。唉，那些人正为他们重新开放的教堂兴奋着呢，他走着走着，有个女人从窗户里瞧见了他，便嚷起了警报。‘哎，那边！瞧啊！雅各宾派，无套裤汉，喝血的！瞧他呀。’里头冲出来好几个，还有一两个在自家菜地里干活的男人翻身越过了矮墙。不一会儿就聚起了一群人，大多是女人，各人手里抄着顺手抓到的头一样家什——棍子也好，菜刀也好，什么都行。几个扛着铁锹和木棒的男人在水槽边也加入了她们。他瞧着不大对劲。他能怎么办呢？他一转身，就像只野兔似的往山上逃。要面对一群怒气冲冲的女人，那可得有点胆量。他顺着车辙小径一路狂奔，头也不回，她们在后面紧追，嚷着：‘处死他！处死那喝血的家伙！（A mort! A mort le buveur de sang!）’这么多年来，因为这个那个的传闻，他在百姓眼里早就是个令人毛骨悚然的恶物了，如今他们觉得机会来了。那边神父宅里的神父听见了喧闹，走到门口。他只消看一眼就够了。他是个四十来岁的人，可精瘦、长腿，身手矫健——是不是？他撩起下摆就冲了出去，抄近道翻过一道道墙头，从一块巨石跳到另一块巨石，活像只成了精的山羊。那阵喧闹传到我那儿时，我正在楼上我的房间里。我走到窗前，看见众人正一路呐喊着紧追他不放。我正开始担心这蠢货会把那一群母老虎全都引到这儿来，她们会一拥而上攻进宅子，把咱们大伙儿一锅端了——就在这当口，那神父恰到好处地截了过去。他要绊倒塞沃拉（Scevola）本是易如反掌的事，可他放他过去，自己却张开双臂，挡在了他的教民面前。这一来就成了。他把庄主（patron）给救了下来，毫发无损。他拿什么话安抚了她们我不知道，可那是早年间的事了，他们都十分爱戴这位新来的神父。他本可以由着性子把他们搓圆捏扁。我把头和肩膀都探出了窗外——那场面着实精彩。她们本会把我们这一帮该死的人统统屠了的——她们在底下一直就是这么叫我们的——等我缩回身子，你猜怎么着，女主人（patronne）就站在我身后，也在瞧着呢。你到这儿来得够多了，总该如何道她是怎样在院子里、在屋子里走来走去的，一点声息也没有。一片树叶落在地上，也不比她的脚更轻。嗯，我估摸她是不知道我在楼上的，进那间屋子也只是照她一贯的脾气，总在找一样压根儿不在那儿的东西罢了；她瞧见我把头探在外头，自然就走过来，要看我在看什么。她的脸并不比平时更白，可她正用十根手指死死揪住胸前的衣裳——就这么揪着。我一下子懵了。还没等我找回舌头，她一转身就出去了，悄无声息，像个影子。”

===Original===
When Peyrol ceased, the ringing of the church bell went on faintly and then stopped as abruptly as it had begun.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔说完之后，教堂的钟声又隐隐约约响了一阵，接着便像它响起时那样突如其来地停了。

===Original===
“Talking about her shadow,” said the young officer indolently, “I know her shadow.”

===Chinese===
“说起她的影子，”年轻军官懒洋洋地说，“我认得她的影子。”

===Original===
Old Peyrol made a really pronounced movement. “What do you mean?” he asked. “Where?”

===Chinese===
老佩罗尔这回可是着实动了一下。“你什么意思？”他问，“在哪儿？”

===Original===
“I have got only one window in the room where they put me to sleep last night and I stood at it looking out. That’s what I am here for—to look out, am I not? I woke up suddenly, and being awake I went to the window and looked out.”

===Chinese===
“昨夜他们安顿我睡的那间屋子只有一扇窗，我就站在窗前朝外看。我到这儿来为的就是这个——朝外头张望，不是吗？我突然醒了，既然醒了，就走到窗前朝外看。”

===Original===
“One doesn’t see shadows in the air,” growled old Peyrol.

===Chinese===
“人不会看见飘在半空中的影子。”老佩罗尔瓮声瓮气地说。

===Original===
“No, but you see them on the ground, pretty black too when the moon is full. It fell across this open space here from the corner of the house.”

===Chinese===
“半空中的是看不见，可地上看得见，月圆的时候还黑得很呢。那影子从屋子的墙角这儿，投射过这片空地。”

===Original===
“The *patronne*,” exclaimed Peyrol in a low voice, “impossible!”

===Chinese===
“女主人，”佩罗尔压低了声音惊道，“不可能！”

===Original===
“Does the old woman that lives in the kitchen roam, do the village women roam as far as this?” asked the officer composedly. “You ought to know the habits of the people. It was a woman’s shadow. The moon being to the west, it glided slanting from that corner of the house and glided back again. I know her shadow when I see it.”

===Chinese===
“那个住在厨房里的老妇人会出来游荡吗，村子里的女人会游荡到这么远来吗？”军官从容不迫地问，“这些人的习性你该清楚。那是女人的影子。月亮在西边，那影子便从屋子那个墙角斜斜地滑过来，又滑了回去。她的影子我一眼就认得。”

===Original===
“Did you hear anything?” asked Peyrol after a moment of visible hesitation.

===Chinese===
“你听见什么动静没有？”佩罗尔明显犹豫了一阵之后问道。

===Original===
“The window being open, I heard somebody snoring. It couldn’t have been you, you are too high. Moreover, from the snoring,” he added grimly, “it must have been somebody with a good conscience. Not like you, old skimmer of the seas, because, you know, that’s what you are, for all your gunner’s warrant.” He glanced out of the corner of his eyes at old Peyrol. “What makes you look so worried?”

===Chinese===
“窗户开着，我听见有人打鼾。那不会是你，你睡得太高。再者，从那鼾声听来，”他冷冷地补了一句，“准是个良心过得去的人。可不像你，你这老在海上捞偏门的家伙，因为说到底，你就是这么个人，别看你揣着张炮长的委任状。”他拿眼角瞟了老佩罗尔一下。“什么事叫你愁成这样？”

===Original===
“She roams, that cannot be denied,” murmured Peyrol, with an uneasiness which he did not attempt to conceal.

===Chinese===
“她会游荡，这是否认不了的。”佩罗尔喃喃道，带着一种他也不打算掩饰的不安。

===Original===
“Evidently. I know a shadow when I see it, and when I saw it, it did not frighten me, not a quarter as much as the mere tale of it seems to have frightened you. However, that sansculotte friend of yours must be a hard sleeper. Those purveyors of the guillotine all have a first-class fireproof Republican conscience. I have seen them at work up north when I was a boy running barefoot in the gutters. …”

===Chinese===
“显而易见。影子我见了就认得，我瞧见它的时候，它也没吓着我，连这事儿光听你一说吓着你的四分之一都不到。不过，你那位无套裤汉朋友睡得也真够死的。那些给断头台供货的人，个个都揣着一副头等的、水火不侵的共和派良心。我小时候在北方光着脚在阴沟里跑的时候，就见过他们干活的情形……”

===Original===
“The fellow always sleeps in that room,” said Peyrol earnestly.

===Chinese===
“那家伙一向就睡在那间屋里。”佩罗尔认真地说。

===Original===
“But that’s neither here nor there,” went on the officer, “except that it may be convenient for roaming shadows to hear his conscience taking its ease.”

===Chinese===
“可这跟眼下的事不搭界，”军官接着说，“只不过对于那些出来游荡的影子来说，听见他的良心如此安枕，倒也许是件方便事。”

===Original===
Peyrol, excited, lowered his voice forcibly. “Lieutenant,” he said, “if I had not seen from the first what was in your heart I would have contrived to get rid of you a long time ago in some way or other.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔激动起来，硬是把声音压了下去。“中尉，”他说，“要不是我从一开始就看清了你心里装着什么，我早就变着法儿把你打发走了。”

===Original===
The lieutenant glanced sideways again and Peyrol let his raised fist fall heavily on his thigh. “I am old Peyrol and this place, as lonely as a ship at sea, is like a ship to me and all in it are like shipmates. Never mind the patron. What I want to know is whether you heard anything? Any sound at all? Murmur, footstep?” A bitterly mocking smile touched the lips of the young man.

===Chinese===
中尉又拿眼角瞟了一下，佩罗尔把举起的拳头重重地砸在自己大腿上。“我是老佩罗尔，这地方孤零零的就像海上的一条船，对我来说也就像一条船，这里头的所有人对我来说都像同船的弟兄。庄主的事先不去管它。我只想知道，你到底听见什么没有？哪怕一丁点动静？悄语声、脚步声？”年轻人嘴角浮起一丝苦涩的、嘲弄的笑。

===Original===
“Not a fairy footstep. Could you hear the fall of a leaf—and with that terrorist cur trumpeting right above my head? …” Without unfolding his arms he turned towards Peyrol, who was looking at him anxiously. … “You want to know, do you? Well, I will tell you what I heard and you can make the best of it. I heard the sound of a stumble. It wasn’t a fairy either that stubbed its toe. It was something in a heavy shoe. Then a stone went rolling down the ravine in front of us interminably, then a silence as of death. I didn’t see anything moving. The way the moon was then the ravine was in black shadow. And I didn’t try to see.”

===Chinese===
“连仙女的脚步声都没有。你还能听得见一片叶子落地吗——何况还有那条恐怖分子的恶狗就在我头顶上像吹号似的打着呼噜？……”他双臂仍抱在胸前，转过身朝向佩罗尔，佩罗尔正焦虑地望着他……“你想知道，是不是？好吧，我把我听见的告诉你，你爱怎么往好处想就怎么往好处想吧。我听见有人绊了一跤的声音。绊了脚趾头的也不是什么仙女。那是一只穿着笨重鞋子的大脚。接着有一块石头顺着我们前头那条沟壑骨碌碌地滚下去，滚个没完没了，再往后便是一片死一般的寂静。我什么也没看见在动弹。照那时候月亮的方位，那道沟壑正罩在一团漆黑的阴影里。我也没打算去看。”

===Original===
Peyrol, with his elbow on his knee, leaned his head in the palm of his hand. The officer repeated through his clenched teeth: “Make the best of it.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔把胳膊肘撑在膝盖上，头枕在掌心里。军官咬紧牙关又重复了一遍：“爱怎么往好处想就怎么往好处想吧。”

===Original===
Peyrol shook his head slightly. After having spoken, the young officer leaned back against the wall, but next moment the report of a piece of ordnance reached them as it were from below, travelling around the rising ground to the left in the form of a dull thud followed by a sighing sound that seemed to seek an issue amongst the stony ridges and rocks near by.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔微微摇了摇头。那年轻军官说完话后，便向后靠到墙上，可紧接着，一声炮响传到了他们耳中——那声音仿佛来自下方，绕过左边那片隆起的高地传来，先是一记沉闷的钝响，继之以一声叹息般的余音，那余音仿佛在附近的石脊与岩壁间寻找着一个出口。

===Original===
“That’s the English corvette which has been dodging in and out of Hyères Roads for the last week,” said the young officer, picking up his sword hastily. He stood up and buckled the belt on, while Peyrol rose more deliberately from the bench, and said:

===Chinese===
“那就是这一整个星期以来在耶尔锚地（Hyères Roads）进进出出兜圈子的那艘英国轻巡洋舰（corvette）。”年轻军官说着，赶忙抄起他的佩剑。他站起身系好皮带，佩罗尔则不慌不忙地从长凳上站起来，说道：

===Original===
“She can’t be where we saw her at anchor last night. That gun was near. She must have crossed over. There has been enough wind for that at various times during the night. But what could she be firing at down there in the Petite Passe? We had better go and see.”

===Chinese===
“她不可能还停在我们昨夜看见她下锚的那个位置。那一炮离得很近。她一定是横渡过来了。入夜以来不时也有足够的风力叫她做到这一点。可她在底下那片小海峡（Petite Passe）里朝什么开炮呢？咱们最好过去瞧瞧。”

===Original===
He strode off, followed by Peyrol. There was not a human being in sight about the farm and not a sound of life except for the lowing of a cow coming faintly from behind a wall. Peyrol kept close behind the quickly moving officer who followed the footpath marked faintly on the stony slope of the hill.

===Chinese===
他大步流星地走了，佩罗尔跟在后头。农庄四周一个人影也看不见，也没有一丝生灵的声响，只有一声牛哞隐隐约约从一堵墙后传来。佩罗尔紧紧跟着步履迅捷的军官，后者顺着那条在碎石山坡上隐约可辨的小径走去。

===Original===
“That gun was not shotted,” he observed suddenly in a deep steady voice.

===Chinese===
“那一炮没装实弹。”他突然开口说道，嗓音低沉而平稳。

===Original===
The officer glanced over his shoulder.

===Chinese===
军官回头瞥了他一眼。

===Original===
“You may be right. You haven’t been a gunner for nothing. Not shotted, eh? Then a signal gun. But who to? We have been observing that corvette now for days and we know she has no companion.”

===Chinese===
“也许你说得对。你这炮长到底不是白当的。没装实弹，嗯？那就是一炮信号。可信号打给谁呢？我们盯着那艘轻巡洋舰有些日子了，我们知道她身边并没有伴。”

===Original===
He moved on, Peyrol following him on the awkward path without losing his wind and arguing in a steady voice: “She has no companion but she may have seen a friend at daylight this morning.”

===Chinese===
他继续往前走，佩罗尔在那条难走的小径上跟着他，气也不喘，以平稳的嗓音争辩道：“她身边是没有伴，可她今晨天亮的时候未必没有瞧见一个朋友。”

===Original===
“Bah!” retorted the officer without checking his pace. “You talk now like a child or else you take me for one. How far could she have seen? What view could she have had at daylight if she was making her way to the Petite Passe where she is now? Why, the islands would have masked for her two-thirds of the sea and just in the direction too where the English inshore squadron is hovering below the horizon. Funny blockade that! You can’t see a single English sail for days and days together, and then when you least expect them they come down all in a crowd as if ready to eat us alive. No, no! There was no wind to bring her up a companion. But tell me, gunner, you who boast of knowing the bark of every English piece, what sort of gun was it?”

===Chinese===
“呸！”军官驳道，脚下丝毫没放慢，“你如今说话倒像个孩子，要不然就是把我当孩子哄了。她能看多远？她若是正朝她眼下所在的那个小海峡驶去，那她在天亮时又能有什么视野呢？嗬，那些岛屿岂不是替她遮掉了三分之二的海面，而且偏偏就遮在英国近岸分舰队在地平线下游弋的那个方向！这叫什么封锁！一连好多天你连一面英国帆影也瞧不见，然后在你最意想不到的时候，他们又成群结队地扑将下来，好像要把我们生吞活剥了似的。不，不！没有那股风能给她送来一个伴。不过你倒告诉我，炮长——你不是吹嘘自己辨得出每一门英国炮的响声吗——那是门什么炮？”

===Original===
Peyrol growled in answer.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔瓮声瓮气地答道。

===Original===
“Why, a twelve. The heaviest she carries. She is only a corvette.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，一门十二磅炮。她所载的最重的一门。她不过是一艘轻巡洋舰罢了。”

===Original===
“Well, then, it was fired as a recall for one of her boats somewhere out of sight along the shore. With a coast like this, all points and bights, there would be nothing very extraordinary in that, would there?”

===Chinese===
“那么说，这一炮打响是用来召回她某一条沿着海岸躲在看不见的某处的小艇了。像这样一条海岸，尽是岬角和湾汊，这事儿原本也没什么好稀奇的，是不是？”

===Original===
“No,” said Peyrol, stepping out steadily. “What is extraordinary is that she should have had a boat away at all.”

===Chinese===
“是没什么稀奇。”佩罗尔说，脚步稳稳地迈着，“稀奇的是她竟然会放了一条小艇出去。”

===Original===
“You are right there.” The officer stopped suddenly. “Yes, it is really remarkable that she should have sent a boat away. And there is no other way to explain that gun.”

===Chinese===
“这话你说得对。”军官猛地停住脚步，“是呀，她竟会放出一条小艇去，这确实非同寻常。除此之外，也再没别的法子能解释那一炮了。”

===Original===
Peyrol’s face expressed no emotion of any sort.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔的脸上什么神情也没有表露。

===Original===
“There is something there worth investigating,” continued the officer with animation.

===Chinese===
“那儿有点名堂，值得查它一查。”军官兴致勃勃地接着说。

===Original===
“If it is a matter of a boat,” Peyrol said without the slightest excitement, “there can be nothing very deep in it. What could there be? As likely as not they sent her inshore early in the morning with lines to try to catch some fish for the captain’s breakfast. Why do you open your eyes like this? Don’t you know the English? They have enough cheek for anything.”

===Chinese===
“要不过是条小艇的事，”佩罗尔说，不带一丝一毫的兴奋，“那里头就未必有什么深意。能有什么呢？十之八九是他们一清早放她靠岸，带着钓线想给舰长钓几条鱼当早饭。你这般瞪大了眼睛做什么？你还不了解英国人吗？他们脸皮厚着呢，什么事都干得出来。”

===Original===
After uttering those words with a deliberation made venerable by his white hair, Peyrol made the gesture of wiping his brow, which was barely moist.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔说完这番话，语气的从容被他那头白发衬得颇有几分威严，随即做了个擦拭额头的手势——额上其实几乎没怎么出汗。

===Original===
“Let us push on,” said the lieutenant abruptly.

===Chinese===
“咱们赶紧走吧。”中尉突然说道。

===Original===
“Why hurry like this?” argued Peyrol without moving. “Those heavy clogs of mine are not adapted for scrambling on loose stones.”

===Chinese===
“干吗这么急？”佩罗尔争辩道，身子却没动，“我这双笨重的木屐，可不适合在碎石上攀爬。”

===Original===
“Aren’t they?” burst out the officer. “Well then, if you are tired you can sit down and fan yourself with your hat. Goodbye.” And he strode away before Peyrol could utter a word.

===Chinese===
“不适合吗？”军官发作起来，“那好，你要是累了，尽可以坐下拿帽子给自己扇扇风。再见。”佩罗尔还没来得及说一个字，他就大步走开了。

===Original===
The path following the contour of the hill took a turn towards its sea-face and very soon the lieutenant passed out of sight with startling suddenness. Then his head reappeared for a moment, only his head, and that too vanished suddenly. Peyrol remained perplexed. After gazing in the direction in which the officer had disappeared, he looked down at the farm buildings, now below him but not at a very great distance. He could see distinctly the pigeons walking on the roof ridges. Somebody was drawing water from the well in the middle of the yard. The patron, no doubt; but that man, who at one time had the power to send so many luckless persons to their death, did not count for old Peyrol. He had even ceased to be an offence to his sight and a disturber of his feelings. By himself he was nothing. He had never been anything but a creature of the universal bloodlust of the time. The very doubts about him had died out by now in old Peyrol’s breast. The fellow was so insignificant that had Peyrol in a moment of particular attention discovered that he cast no shadow, he would not have been surprised. Below there he was reduced to the shape of a dwarf lugging a bucket away from the well. But where was she? Peyrol asked himself, shading his eyes with his hand. He knew that the *patronne* could not be very far away, because he had a sight of her during the morning; but that was before he had learned she had taken to roaming at night. His growing uneasiness came suddenly to an end when, turning his eyes away from the farm-buildings, where obviously she was not, he saw her appear, with nothing but the sky full of light at her back, coming down round the very turn of the path which had taken the lieutenant out of sight.

===Chinese===
沿着山势蜿蜒的小径转了个弯，拐向靠海的那一面，很快中尉便以一种令人吃惊的猝然之势消失在了视线之外。紧接着，他的脑袋又冒出来一瞬，就只一个脑袋，可那也一下子又没了。佩罗尔怔怔地站在那儿。他朝军官消失的方向望了一阵，又低头去看农庄的那些房舍——此刻正在他下方，但相距并不太远。他能清清楚楚地看见鸽子在屋脊上踱步。有人正在院子正中那口井里打水。准是庄主；不过这个人，从前可是有权把那么多不幸的人送上死路的，在老佩罗尔眼里却算不得什么。他甚至已经不再刺痛他的眼睛、搅扰他的心绪了。单就他本身而言，他什么也不是。他从来不过是那个时代那场普遍嗜血狂热里的一个造物罢了。就连从前对他存着的那些疑心，到如今在老佩罗尔胸中也已荡然无存了。这家伙微不足道到了这般地步，倘若佩罗尔在某个格外留神的当口发现他竟连影子都没有，他也不会觉得奇怪。在下面那儿，他已缩成了一个吃力地提着水桶离开井边的侏儒身影。可她在哪儿呢？佩罗尔抬手遮住眉眼，暗自问道。他知道女主人不会离得太远，因为早上他还瞧见过她一面；可那是在他听说她夜里也出来游荡之前的事了。他心头那股越来越重的不安忽然消散了——他把目光从农庄房舍那儿移开（她显然并不在那儿），便看见她出现了：背后只是一片满是亮光的天空，她正顺着那条刚才让中尉消失无踪的小径，从那个拐弯处走了下来。

===Original===
Peyrol moved briskly towards her. He wasn’t a man to lose time in idle wonder, and his sabots did not seem to weigh heavy on his feet. The *fermière*, whom the villagers down there spoke of as Arlette as though she had been a little girl, but in a strange tone of shocked awe, walked with her head drooping and her feet (as Peyrol used to say) touching the ground as lightly as falling leaves. The clatter of the clogs made her raise her black, clear eyes that had been smitten on the very verge of womanhood by such sights of bloodshed and terror as to leave in her a fear of looking steadily in any direction for long, lest she should see coming through the empty air some mutilated vision of the dead. Peyrol called it trying not to see something that was not there; and this evasive yet frank mobility was so much a part of her being that the steadiness with which she met his inquisitive glance surprised old Peyrol for a moment. He asked without beating about the bush:

===Chinese===
佩罗尔轻快地朝她走去。他不是那种会把工夫虚掷在无谓惊奇上的人，脚上的木屐似乎也不觉得有多沉。那个被底下村民唤作阿尔莱特（Arlette）的农妇（fermière）——他们叫她时仿佛她还是个小姑娘，口气里却透着一种古怪的、骇然敬畏的意味——垂着头走着，双脚（照佩罗尔的说法）像落叶一般轻点着地面。木屐的哒哒声使她抬起了那双黑色的、清亮的眼睛，那双眼睛在她方才步入豆蔻年华之际，便被那样一些杀戮与恐怖的景象所重创，以至于在她心里留下了一种畏惧，不敢朝着任何一个方向久久定睛去看，生怕会瞧见某个死者的残缺幻影从虚无的空气中走出来。佩罗尔把这叫做“竭力不去看见那并不存在的东西”；而这种既躲闪又坦然的眼波流转，原是她天性中如此不可割裂的一部分，以至于此刻她竟那样定定地迎住了他探询的目光，倒叫老佩罗尔也有那么一瞬感到意外。他开门见山地问道：

===Original===
“Did he speak to you?”

===Chinese===
“他跟你说话了没有？”

===Original===
She answered with something airy and provoking in her voice, which also struck Peyrol as a novelty: “He never stopped. He passed by as though he had not seen me”—and then they both looked away from each other.

===Chinese===
她回答的声气里带着一种轻飘飘的、撩人的味道，这也让佩罗尔觉得新鲜：“他根本没停下。他打我身边过去，就像没看见我似的”——说罢两人都不约而同地把目光移开了。

===Original===
“Now, what is it you took into your head to watch for at night?”

===Chinese===
“那么，你夜里出来到底是要守候什么呢？”

===Original===
She did not expect that question. She hung her head and took a pleat of her skirt between her fingers, embarrassed like a child.

===Chinese===
她没料到会有这一问。她垂下头，用手指捏着裙子上的一道褶子，像个孩子似的局促起来。

===Original===
“Why should I not,” she murmured in a low shy note, as if she had two voices within her.

===Chinese===
“我为什么不能呢。”她用一种低低的、怯生生的声调喃喃道，仿佛她体内藏着两个声音。

===Original===
“What did Catherine say?”

===Chinese===
“凯瑟琳怎么说？”

===Original===
“She was asleep, or perhaps only lying on her back with her eyes shut.”

===Chinese===
“她睡着了，要不就是只仰面躺着、闭着眼睛。”

===Original===
“Does she do that?” asked Peyrol with incredulity.

===Chinese===
“她会那样吗？”佩罗尔将信将疑地问。

===Original===
“Yes.” Arlette gave Peyrol a queer, meaningless smile with which her eyes had nothing to do. “Yes, she often does. I have noticed that before. She lies there trembling under her blankets till I come back.”

===Chinese===
“会。”阿尔莱特朝佩罗尔露出一个古怪的、毫无意义的微笑，那笑意和她眼睛里头全不相干。“是的，她常常那样。这我早就留意到了。她躺在毯子底下直打哆嗦，一直等到我回来。”

===Original===
“What drove you out last night?” Peyrol tried to catch her eyes, but they eluded him in the usual way. And now her face looked as though it couldn’t smile.

===Chinese===
“昨夜是什么把你催出去的？”佩罗尔想捉住她的目光，可它们照旧躲开了他。这一刻她的脸看上去就像再也不会笑了似的。

===Original===
“My heart,” she said. For a moment Peyrol lost his tongue and even all power of motion. The *fermière* having lowered her eyelids, all her life seemed to have gone into her coral lips, vivid and without a quiver in the perfection of their design, and Peyrol, giving up the conversation with an upward fling of his arm, hurried up the path without looking behind him. But once round the turn of the path, he approached the lookout at an easier gait. It was a piece of smooth ground below the summit of the hill. It had quite a pronounced slope, so that a short and robust pine growing true out of the soil yet leaned well over the edge of the sheer drop of some fifty feet or so. The first thing that Peyrol’s eyes took in was the water of the Petite Passe with the enormous shadow of the Porquerolles Island darkening more than half of its width at this still early hour. He could not see the whole of it, but on the part his glance embraced there was no ship of any kind. The lieutenant, leaning with his chest along the inclined pine, addressed him irritably.

===Chinese===
“是我的心。”她说。一时间佩罗尔说不出话来，甚至连动一动的力气都没有了。那农妇垂下了眼帘，她全部的生气似乎都汇聚到了那双珊瑚色的嘴唇上，鲜明欲滴，轮廓完美得不带一丝颤动，佩罗尔把胳膊向上一甩，就此撂下了这场交谈，头也不回地顺着小径快步走开了。可一拐过弯，他便放慢了步子，朝瞭望的地方走去。那是山顶下方一块平整的空地。那地方坡度相当明显，以至于一棵从土里笔直长出、矮壮结实的松树，仍然朝着约莫五十来英尺的陡崖边沿探出去老远。佩罗尔的眼睛首先收进眼底的，是小海峡的水面——在这尚早的时分，波克罗勒岛（Porquerolles Island）巨大的阴影遮暗了它一大半的宽度。他没法看见水道的全部，但在他目光所及的那一片上，没有任何一只船的影子。中尉胸脯贴着那棵倾斜的松树，没好气地冲他说起话来。

===Original===
“Squat! Do you think there are no glasses on board the Englishman?”

===Chinese===
“蹲下！你以为那艘英国船上就没有望远镜吗？”

===Original===
Peyrol obeyed without a word and for the space of a minute or so presented the bizarre sight of a rather bulky peasant with venerable white locks crawling on his hands and knees on a hillside for no visible reason. When he got to the foot of the pine he raised himself on his knees. The lieutenant, flattened against the inclined trunk and with a pocket glass glued to his eye, growled angrily:

===Chinese===
佩罗尔一言不发地照做了，有那么大约一分钟光景，他呈现出一种古怪的模样：一个颇为敦实、顶着一头可敬白发的庄稼人，不知为了什么缘故，在山坡上手脚并用地爬行。等爬到那棵松树跟前，他才直起膝盖跪了起来。中尉紧贴在那根斜斜的树干上，一只袖珍望远镜粘在眼上，气呼呼地嘟囔道：

===Original===
“You can see her now, can’t you?”

===Chinese===
“你现在能看见她了，是不是？”

===Original===
Peyrol in his kneeling position could see the ship now. She was less than a quarter of a mile from him up the coast, almost within hailing effort of his powerful voice. His unaided eyes could follow the movements of the men on board like dark dots about her decks. She had drifted so far within Cape Esterel that the low projecting mass of it seemed to be in actual contact with her stern. Her unexpected nearness made Peyrol draw a sharp breath through his teeth. The lieutenant murmured, still keeping the glass to his eye:

===Chinese===
佩罗尔跪在那里，这会儿能看见那条船了。船离他不到四分之一英里，顺着海岸在他上方，几乎只消他那洪亮的嗓门喊一嗓子就能传到。凭他肉眼的目力，便能跟上船上水手们的动作——他们在甲板上像一个个黑点似的移动。船已漂进埃斯泰雷尔角（Cape Esterel）这么远，以至于那片低低探出的岬地似乎当真挨着了她的船尾。她离得这么近，出乎意料，叫佩罗尔倒抽一口冷气，从牙缝里嘶嘶地吸了进去。中尉仍旧把望远镜贴在眼上，低声说道：

===Original===
“I can see the very epaulettes of the officers on the quarterdeck.”

===Chinese===
“我连后甲板上军官们的肩章都看得一清二楚。”

## V / 第五章

===Original===
As Peyrol and the lieutenant had surmised from the report of the gun, the English ship which the evening before was lying in Hyères Roads had got under way after dark. The light airs had taken her as far as the Petite Passe in the early part of the night, and then had abandoned her to the breathless moonlight in which, bereft of all motion, she looked more like a white monument of stone dwarfed by the darkling masses of land on either hand than a fabric famed for its swiftness in attack or in flight.

===Chinese===
正如佩罗尔和那位中尉从那一声炮响中所揣度的，前一夜还停泊在耶尔锚地的那艘英国船，天黑之后便已起锚开航。轻风在入夜之初将她送至小海峡（Petite Passe）一带，而后便弃她于那了无气息的月光之中；她在其中纹丝不动，倒更像一座被两侧渐暗的陆地巨影衬得矮小了的白色石碑，而不像一艘素以进击迅猛、遁逃如飞而著称的海上船体。

===Original===
Her captain was a man of about forty, with clean-shaven, full cheeks and mobile thin lips which he had a trick of compressing mysteriously before he spoke and sometimes also at the end of his speeches. He was alert in his movements and nocturnal in his habits.

===Chinese===
她的舰长是个四十来岁的人，两颊刮得精光、丰润饱满，一双薄唇灵巧善动，他有个习惯，开口之前总要把嘴唇神秘地抿紧，有时话说完了也要这样抿上一抿。他举止警觉，习性昼伏夜出。

===Original===
Directly he found that the calm had taken complete possession of the night and was going to last for hours Captain Vincent assumed his favourite attitude of leaning over the rail. It was then some time after midnight and in the pervading stillness the moon, riding on a speckless sky, seemed to pour her enchantment on an uninhabited planet. Captain Vincent did not mind the moon very much. Of course it made his ship visible from both shores of the Petite Passe. But after nearly a year of constant service in command of the extreme lookout ship of Admiral Nelson’s blockading fleet he knew the emplacement of almost every gun of the shore defences. Where the breeze had left him he was safe from the biggest gun of the few that were mounted on Porquerolles. On the Giens side of the pass he knew for certain there was not even a popgun mounted anywhere. His long familiarity with that part of the coast had imbued him with the belief that he knew the habits of its population thoroughly. The gleams of light in their houses went out very early, and Captain Vincent felt convinced that they were all in their beds, including the gunners of the batteries who belonged to the local militia. Their interest in the movements of H.M.’s twenty-two gun sloop *Amelia* had grown stale by custom. She never interfered with their private affairs, and allowed the small coasting craft to go to and fro unmolested. They would have wondered if she had been more than two days away. Captain Vincent used to say grimly that the Hyères Roadstead had become like a second home to him.

===Chinese===
文森特舰长（Vincent）一发觉这无风已将整夜彻底攥住、还要持续上好几个钟头，便摆出了他最惯常的姿势——伏靠在船舷的栏杆上。那时已过了午夜不久，四下里一片死寂，明月行在纤尘不染的天空上，仿佛将她的迷魂魔力倾泻在一颗无人居住的星球之上。文森特舰长倒不十分介意这月亮。当然，它使他的船从小海峡两岸都能被人瞧见。但在他奉纳尔逊勋爵（Lord Nelson）的封锁舰队之命、统率那艘最远端前哨舰长年当值将近一年之后，他已几乎摸清了岸防每一门炮的方位。在轻风弃他而去的这个所在，波克罗勒岛上所架那寥寥几门炮里最大的一门也够不着他。在海峡靠吉安半岛的这一侧，他十分确信，哪里连一门小炮也没有架过。他与这一带海岸长年的熟稔，使他深信自己已把沿岸百姓的起居习性摸了个透。他们屋里的灯火熄得极早，文森特舰长心想，他们必定都已上了床，就连那些属于地方民团的炮台炮手也不例外。他们对英王陛下那艘二十二炮单桅快速帆船阿米莉亚号（Amelia）的来来去去，早因司空见惯而兴致寡淡了。她从不干涉他们的私事，也听任那些沿岸小船来去自如、不受滋扰。她若是离开了两天以上，他们倒会纳闷起来。文森特舰长常冷冷地说，耶尔锚地于他，已像是第二个家了。

===Original===
For an hour or so Captain Vincent mused a bit on his real home, on matters of service and other unrelated things, then getting into motion in a very wide-awake manner, he superintended himself the dispatch of that boat the existence of which had been acutely surmised by Lieutenant Réal and was a matter of no doubt whatever to old Peyrol. As to her mission, it had nothing to do with catching fish for the captain’s breakfast. It was the captain’s own gig, a very fast pulling boat. She was already alongside with her crew in her when the officer, who was going in charge, was beckoned to by the captain. He had a cutlass at his side and a brace of pistols in his belt, and there was a businesslike air about him that showed he had been on such service before.

===Chinese===
大约有一个钟头，文森特舰长稍稍出神地想起了他真正的家，又想了些公务上的事和另一些不相干的念头，随后他便清醒利落地行动起来，亲自督促着把那只小艇打发出去——那只小艇的存在，已被雷亚尔中尉敏锐地猜中，而在老佩罗尔看来更是确凿无疑的事。至于小艇的使命，那与替舰长钓几条鱼当早饭毫不相干。那是舰长自用的小艇，一艘划起来极快的船。艇已靠在舷侧，艇员也都就位，这时奉命带队的军官被舰长招手叫了过去。他腰间挎着一柄短剑，腰带上别着两把手枪，周身透着一股干练之气，一看便知他干过这等差事。

===Original===
“This calm will last a good many hours,” said the captain. “In this tideless sea you are certain to find the ship very much where she is now, but closer in shore. The attraction of the land—you know.”

===Chinese===
“这无风还要持续好些个钟头，”舰长说道，“在这没有潮汐的海上，你回去时准能发现船还差不多在现在这个位置，只是更靠近岸罢了。陆地是有吸力的——这个你懂。”

===Original===
“Yes, sir. The land does attract.”

===Chinese===
“是，长官。陆地的确有吸力。”

===Original===
“Yes. Well, she may be allowed to put her side against any of these rocks. There would be no more danger than alongside a quay with a sea like this. Just look at the water in the pass, Mr. Bolt. Like the floor of a ballroom. Pull close along shore when you return. I’ll expect you back at dawn.”

===Chinese===
“嗯。那么，不妨让她贴上这些礁石中任何一块的边。这样的海面下，就跟靠在码头边一样毫无危险。你瞧瞧海峡里的水，博尔特先生（Bolt）。简直跟舞厅的地板一个样。回来时贴着岸边划。我等你天亮回来。”

===Original===
Captain Vincent paused suddenly. A doubt crossed his mind as to the wisdom of this nocturnal expedition. The hammerhead of the peninsula with its sea-face invisible from both sides of the coast was an ideal spot for a secret landing. Its lonely character appealed to his imagination, which in the first instance had been stimulated by a chance remark of Mr. Bolt himself.

===Chinese===
文森特舰长忽然顿住了。他心头掠过一丝犹疑，捉摸着这一趟夜行究竟是否明智。半岛那锤头状的尽头，朝海的一面从海岸两边都望不见，正是暗中登岸的绝妙之处。它那孤寂的气质触动了他的想象，而这想象最初原是因博尔特先生一句无心之言才撩拨起来的。

===Original===
The fact was that the week before, when the Amelia was cruising off the peninsula, Bolt, looking at the coast, mentioned that he knew that part of it well; he had actually been ashore there a good many years ago, while serving with Lord Howe’s fleet. He described the nature of the path, the aspect of a little village on the reverse slope, and had much to say about a certain farmhouse where he had been more than once, and had even stayed for twenty-four hours at a time on more than one occasion.

===Chinese===
事情的起因是，上一个星期阿米莉亚号在半岛外巡弋时，博尔特望着海岸，提起他对那一带熟得很：许多年前他曾在豪勋爵（Lord Howe）的舰队里服役，实实在在登上过那处岸。他描绘了那条小径的形貌、背海坡上一座小村子的样子，又对某家农庄说了许多——那农庄他去过不止一回，有几次甚至一住就是二十四小时。

===Original===
This had aroused Captain Vincent’s curiosity. He sent for Bolt and had a long conversation with him. He listened with great interest to Bolt’s story, how one day a man was seen from the deck of the ship in which Bolt was serving then, waving a white sheet or tablecloth amongst the rocks at the water’s edge. It might have been a trap; but, as the man seemed alone and the shore was within range of the ship’s guns, a boat was sent to take him off.

===Chinese===
这一番话引起了文森特舰长的好奇。他把博尔特叫来，与他长谈了一番。他饶有兴味地听博尔特讲那桩旧事：当年有一天，从博尔特所在那条船的甲板上，有人瞧见岸边礁石丛中有个男人挥舞着一条白床单——也许是块桌布。那也许是个陷阱；但那男人似乎是独自一人，而海岸又处在船炮射程之内，于是便放下一只小艇去把他接走。

===Original===
“And that, sir,” Bolt pursued impressively, “was, I verily believe, the very first communication that Lord Howe had from the royalists in Toulon.” Afterwards Bolt described to Captain Vincent the meetings of the Toulon royalists with the officers of the fleet. From the back of the farm he, Bolt himself, had often watched for hours the entrance of the Toulon harbour on the lookout for the boat bringing over the royalist emissaries. Then he would make an agreed signal to the advanced squadron and some English officers would land on their side and meet the Frenchmen at the farmhouse. It was as simple as that. The people of the farmhouse, husband and wife, were well-to-do, good class altogether, and staunch royalists. He had got to know them well.

===Chinese===
“而那一次，长官，”博尔特郑重其事地接着说，“我敢说，正是豪勋爵从土伦的保王党人那里接到的头一个消息。”随后博尔特又向文森特舰长描述了土伦保王党人与舰队军官们一次次会面的情形。从农庄背后，他博尔特本人，曾一连好几个钟头盯着土伦港的入口，守望那只把保王党密使送过来的小艇。然后他便向先遣分队发出约定好的信号，几名英国军官就会在他们这一侧登岸，与那些法国人在农庄里碰头。事情就这么简单。农庄里那对夫妻，家境殷实，门第体面，又是死心塌地的保王党人。他跟他们混得很熟。

===Original===
Captain Vincent wondered whether the same people were still living there. Bolt could see no reason why they shouldn’t be. It wasn’t more than ten years ago, and they were by no means an old couple. As far as he could make out, the farm was their own property. He, Bolt, knew only very few French words at that time. It was much later, after he had been made a prisoner and kept inland in France till the Peace of Amiens, that he had picked up a smattering of the lingo. His captivity had done away with his feeble chance of promotion, he could not help remarking. Bolt was a master’s mate still.

===Chinese===
文森特舰长纳闷，住在那里的会不会还是同一对人。博尔特看不出有什么理由不会。那不过是十年前的事，再说他们也绝非一对老人。就他所知，那农庄是他们自家的产业。他博尔特那时候只懂很少几个法国字。要等许多之后，他做了俘虏、被拘在法国腹地直到亚眠和约（Peace of Amiens）签订，他才零零星星拾得了一点那门话的皮毛。他忍不住感慨，那场囚禁断送了他本就渺茫的升迁之望。博尔特至今仍是个大副候补。

===Original===
Captain Vincent, in common with a good many officers of all ranks in Lord Nelson’s fleet, had his misgivings about the system of distant blockade from which the Admiral apparently would not depart. Yet one could not blame Lord Nelson. Everybody in the fleet understood that what was in his mind was the destruction of the enemy; and if the enemy was closely blockaded he would never come out to be destroyed. On the other hand it was clear that as things were conducted the French had too many chances left them to slip out unobserved and vanish from all human knowledge for months. Those possibilities were a constant worry to Captain Vincent, who had thrown himself with the ardour of passion into the special duty with which he was entrusted. Oh, for a pair of eyes fastened night and day on the entrance of the harbour of Toulon! Oh, for the power to look at the very state of French ships and into the very secrets of French minds!

===Chinese===
文森特舰长与纳尔逊勋爵舰队里许多各级军官一样，对这套远距封锁的方略心存疑虑，而勋爵似乎无意改弦更张。然而谁也不能怪罪纳尔逊勋爵。舰队上下谁都明白，他心里惦念的乃是歼灭敌军；可敌人若被紧紧封锁，他便永远不会出港来送死了。但另一方面，同样清楚的是，照眼下的法子行事，法国人仍大有可乘之机，可以神不知鬼不觉地溜出去，一连数月从世人的耳目中消失得无影无踪。这些可能性成了文森特舰长的一块心病，他已将自己满腔的热忱投入了所受托的这项特殊使命之中。啊，要能有一双眼睛，日日夜夜死死盯住土伦港的入口就好了！啊，要能看透法国军舰的真实现状、窥见法国人内心深处的秘密就好了！

===Original===
But he said nothing of this to Bolt. He only observed that the character of the French Government was changed and that the minds of the royalist people in the farmhouse might have changed too, since they had got back the exercise of their religion. Bolt’s answer was that he had had a lot to do with royalists, in his time, on board Lord Howe’s fleet, both before and after Toulon was evacuated. All sorts, men and women, barbers and noblemen, sailors and tradesmen; almost every kind of royalist one could think of; and his opinion was that a royalist never changed. As to the place itself, he only wished the captain had seen it. It was the sort of spot that nothing could change. He made bold to say that it would be just the same a hundred years hence.

===Chinese===
但这些他对博尔特一字未提。他只说，法国政府的脾性已然变了，那农庄里保王党人的心思，自打他们重新得以奉行自己的宗教，说不定也跟着变了。博尔特的回答是，当年他在豪勋爵舰上，与保王党人打过不少交道，土伦撤退前后都有。三教九流，男女老少，有理发匠也有贵族，有水手也有买卖人；差不多你能想到的每一种保王党人他都见过；而依他的见解，保王党人是永远不会变的。至于那个地方本身，他只恨舰长没亲眼去看看。那是种什么事都改变不了的所在。他胆敢放言，就是再过一百年，它也还是老样子。

===Original===
The earnestness of his officer caused Captain Vincent to look hard at him. He was a man of about his own age, but while Vincent was a comparatively young captain, Bolt was an old master’s mate. Each understood the other perfectly. Captain Vincent fidgeted for a while and then observed abstractedly that he was not a man to put a noose round a dog’s neck, let alone a good seaman’s.

===Chinese===
这位属下的恳切之态，引得文森特舰长盯着他狠狠看了两眼。这人与他年纪相仿，但文森特还算是个年轻的舰长，博尔特却已是个上了年纪的大副候补了。两人彼此心照不宣。文森特舰长局促了一阵，随后心不在焉地说，他可不是那种会给一条狗脖子上套绞索的人，更何况是一个好水手。

===Original===
This cryptic pronouncement caused no wonder to appear in Bolt’s attentive gaze. He only became a little thoughtful before he said in the same abstracted tone that an officer in uniform was not likely to be hanged for a spy. The service was risky, of course. It was necessary, for its success, that, assuming the same people were there, it should be undertaken by a man well known to the inhabitants. Then he added that he was certain of being recognized. And while he enlarged on the extremely good terms he had been on with the owners of the farm, especially the farmer’s wife, a comely motherly woman, who had been very kind to him, and had all her wits about her, Captain Vincent, looking at the master’s mate’s bushy whiskers, thought that these in themselves were enough to ensure recognition. This impression was so strong that he had asked point-blank: “You haven’t altered the growth of the hair on your face, Mr. Bolt, since then?”

===Chinese===
这句没头没脑的话，并未在博尔特那专注的目光里引起半点诧异。他只是略一沉吟，便以同样心不在焉的口吻说道：一个身着军装的军官，不大可能被当作间谍绞死。这差事自然是有风险的。要办得成，前提是——假如住在那里的还是当年那些人——这件事就得由一个那户人家熟识的人来办。接着他又说，他笃定自己会被认出来。他于是大谈特谈起自己当初与农庄主人，尤其是农庄主妇，交情何等深厚——那主妇是个模样周正、母性十足的女人，待他极好，心思又顶明白；而文森特舰长一面端详着这位大副候补那两部浓密的络腮胡子，一面心想，光凭这两部胡子，就足以保证他被认出来了。这印象如此强烈，以至于他曾直截了当地问过一句：“打那以后，你脸上这胡子的长法可没改过吧，博尔特先生？”

===Original===
There was just a touch of indignation in Bolt’s negative reply; for he was proud of his whiskers. He declared he was ready to take the most desperate chances for the service of his king and his country.

===Chinese===
博尔特那否定的回答里带着一丝愤愤之色，因为他颇以自己的络腮胡子为傲。他声称，为了国王和国家的差事，他随时准备去冒最凶险的险。

===Original===
Captain Vincent added: “For the sake of Lord Nelson, too.” One understood well what his Lordship wished to bring about by that blockade at sixty leagues off. He was talking to a sailor, and there was no need to say any more. Did Bolt think that he could persuade those people to conceal him in their house on that lonely shore end of the peninsula for some considerable time? Bolt thought it was the easiest thing in the world. He would simply go up there and renew the old acquaintance, but he did not mean to do that in a reckless manner. It would have to be done at night, when of course there would be no one about. He would land just where he used to before, wrapped up in a Mediterranean sailor’s cloak—he had one of his own—over his uniform, and simply go straight to the door, at which he would knock. Ten to one the farmer himself would come down to open it. He knew enough French by now, he hoped, to persuade those people to conceal him in some room having a view in the right direction; and there he would stick day after day on the watch, taking a little exercise in the middle of the night, ready to live on mere bread and water if necessary, so as not to arouse suspicion amongst the farmhands. And who knows if, with the farmer’s help, he could not get some news of what was going on actually within the port. Then from time to time he could go down in the dead of night, signal to the ship and make his report. Bolt expressed the hope that the *Amelia* would remain as much as possible in sight of the coast. It would cheer him up to see her about. Captain Vincent naturally assented. He pointed out to Bolt, however, that his post would become most important exactly when the ship had been chased away or driven by the weather off her station, as could very easily happen. “You would be then the eyes of Lord Nelson’s fleet, Mr. Bolt—think of that. The actual eyes of Lord Nelson’s fleet!”

===Chinese===
文森特舰长又添了一句：“也是为了纳尔逊勋爵。”彼此都心知肚明，勋爵在六十里格开外布下那道封锁，意欲成就何等大事。他是在跟一个水手说话，再多说便不必了。博尔特觉得自己能不能劝动那户人家，把他藏在半岛尽头那处荒僻海岸上的屋子里、相当长的一段时日里？博尔特以为这是世上最容易的事。他只管上去重续旧交便是，但他并不打算鲁莽行事。这事得在夜里办，那时自然四下无人。他要在从前惯常的地方登岸，用一件地中海水手的斗篷——他自己有一件——把军装裹在里面，径直走到门前敲门。十有八九是农庄主自己下来开门。他眼下的法语，他希望已足够劝动那户人家把他藏进某间朝向对路的屋子里；然后他便日复一日地守在那儿蹲点，只在半夜里稍稍活动活动筋骨，必要时哪怕只靠面包清水度日也心甘情愿，免得引起雇工们的疑心。再者，有农庄主帮衬，谁知道他能不能打探到港口里头正有些什么动静呢。然后他可以时不时地在深夜摸下山，向船只发信号、递报告。博尔特表示，他希望阿米莉亚号能尽量待在看得见海岸的地方。能瞧见她在近旁，对他是个莫大的慰藉。文森特舰长自然一口应允。不过他向博尔特指出，恰恰是在船只被敌人赶走、或被恶劣天气逼离岗位的时候——这种事很容易发生——他那处据点才最为紧要。“到那时候，你就是纳尔逊勋爵舰队的眼睛了，博尔特先生——想想这个。纳尔逊勋爵舰队实实在在的眼睛！”

===Original===
After dispatching his officer, Captain Vincent spent the night on deck. The break of day came at last, much paler than the moonlight which it replaced. And still no boat. And again Captain Vincent asked himself if he had not acted indiscreetly. Impenetrable, and looking as fresh as if he had just come up on deck, he argued the point with himself till the rising sun clearing the ridge on Porquerolles Island flashed its level rays upon his ship with her dew-darkened sails and dripping rigging. He roused himself then to tell his first lieutenant to get the boats out to tow the ship away from the shore. The report of the gun he ordered to be fired expressed simply his irritation. The *Amelia*, pointing towards the middle of the Passe, was moving at a snail’s pace behind her string of boats. Minutes passed. And then suddenly Captain Vincent perceived his boat pulling back in shore according to orders. When nearly abreast of the ship, she darted away, making for her side. Mr. Bolt clambered on board, alone, ordering the gig to go ahead and help with the towing. Captain Vincent, standing apart on the quarterdeck, received him with a grimly questioning look.

===Chinese===
打发了属下之后，文森特舰长在甲板上熬了一整夜。天光终于破晓，比它所取代的那片月光还要淡几分。可小艇仍不见踪影。文森特舰长又一次扪心自问，自己行事是否有什么不慎之处。他面无表情，精神抖擞得仿佛刚刚才上甲板，在心里反复掂量着这事，直到一轮红日越过波克罗勒岛的山脊，将它平直的光芒闪射到他的船上——船帆被露水洇得发暗，索具仍在滴水。他这才振作起来，吩咐大副放下小艇，把船拖离海岸。他下令打出的那一炮，所表达的不过是他心头的烦躁罢了。阿米莉亚号船头朝着海峡中央，在那一串小艇之后，以蜗牛般的速度移动着。一分钟一分钟过去。忽然间，文森特舰长瞧见自己的小艇正遵照命令贴着岸边往回划。将将驶到与船并齐之处，它便一甩头，朝船舷直扑过来。博尔特先生独自一人攀上船来，吩咐大艇先行一步、去帮着拖船。文森特舰长独自站在后甲板上，以一种冷峻探问的目光迎住他。

===Original===
Mr. Bolt’s first words were to the effect that he believed the confounded spot to be bewitched. Then he glanced at the group of officers on the other side of the quarterdeck. Captain Vincent led the way to his cabin. There he turned and looked at his officer, who, with an air of distraction, mumbled: “There are nightwalkers there.”

===Chinese===
博尔特先生头一句话，大意是说他认为那个该死的地方中了邪。接着他朝后甲板另一头那群军官瞟了一眼。文森特舰长领头往自己的舱房走去。进了舱，他转过身来望着他的属下，那人一脸神不守舍的模样，含含糊糊地嘟囔道：“那儿有夜行的人。”

===Original===
“Come, Bolt, what the devil have you seen? Did you get near the house at all?”

===Chinese===
“得了，博尔特，你到底撞见了什么鬼？你到底有没有走近那屋子？”

===Original===
“I got within twenty yards of the door, sir,” said Bolt. And encouraged by the captain’s much less ferocious—“Well?” began his tale. He did not pull up to the path which he knew, but to a little bit of beach on which he told his men to haul up the boat and wait for him. The beach was concealed by a thick growth of bushes on the landward side and by some rocks from the sea. Then he went to what he called the ravine, still avoiding the path, so that as a matter of fact he made his way up on his hands and knees mostly, very carefully and slowly amongst the loose stones, till by holding on to a bush he brought his eyes on a level with the piece of flat ground in front of the farmhouse.

===Chinese===
“我离那扇门不到二十码，长官，”博尔特说。在舰长那句缓和了许多的“嗯？”的鼓励下，他开始讲起自己的经历。他并没有把船划到他认得的那条小径跟前，而是划到一小片海滩边，他让手下把船拖上去、在那里等他。那片海滩朝陆的一面被一丛茂密的灌木遮住，朝海的一面又被几块礁石挡着。然后他走向他所说的那个“沟壑”，一路上仍避开那条小径，所以事实上他大半时候是手脚并用地爬上去的，在乱石之间爬得极小心、极缓慢，直到他攀住一丛灌木，把眼睛探到与农庄门前那片平地齐平的高度。

===Original===
The familiar aspect of the buildings, totally unchanged from the time when he had played his part in what appeared as a most successful operation at the beginning of the war, inspired Bolt with great confidence in the success of his present enterprise, vague as it was, but the great charm of which lay, no doubt, in mental associations with his younger years. Nothing seemed easier than to stride across the forty yards of open ground and rouse the farmer whom he remembered so well, the well-to-do man, a grave, sagacious royalist in his humble way; certainly, in Bolt’s view, no traitor to his country, and preserving so well his dignity in ambiguous circumstances. To Bolt’s simple vision neither that man nor his wife could have changed.

===Chinese===
那几栋屋舍的样貌，他熟稔无比，自打战争伊始他参与那桩看来极其顺利的行动以来，竟毫无改易——这使博尔特对眼下这桩虽则尚属模糊、但其最大魅力无疑在于与少年时光的种种心绪关联的营生，生出了必成的莫大信心。在他看来，没有什么比大步跨过那四十码开阔地、去叫醒他记忆中那位农庄主更容易的事了——那是个殷实人，一个在他那卑微处境中仍庄重而明智的保王党人；在博尔特眼里，他断不是卖国之徒，且在暧昧的境遇中将尊严保全得极好。在博尔特单纯的眼光里，那人和他的妻子都不可能变。

===Original===
In this view of Arlette’s parents Bolt was influenced by the consciousness of there having been no change in himself. He was the same Jack Bolt, and everything around him was the same as if he had left the spot only yesterday. Already he saw himself in the kitchen which he knew so well, seated by the light of a single candle before a glass of wine and talking his best French to that worthy farmer of sound principles. The whole thing was as well as done. He imagined himself a secret inmate of that building, closely confined indeed, but sustained by the possible great results of his watchfulness, in many ways more comfortable than on board the *Amelia* and with the glorious consciousness that he was, in Captain Vincent’s phrase, the actual physical eyes of the Fleet.

===Chinese===
博尔特之所以这样看待阿尔莱特的父母，是受了他自觉自身并未改变这一念头的影响。他还是从前那个杰克·博尔特，他身边的一切也都原样未动，仿佛他昨天才离开那个地方。他已仿佛看见自己置身于那间他熟之又熟的厨房里，借着一支蜡烛的光，坐在一杯葡萄酒前，操着他最拿手的法语同那位立身端正的好农庄主攀谈。整件事简直如同已经办成。他想象自己成了那栋屋子里的一名秘密住户，固然被严严地禁足，却有守望所可能带来的重大成果支撑着他，在许多方面比在阿米莉亚号上还舒服，还怀着那一股光荣的自觉——用文森特舰长的话说，他就是舰队实实在在的肉眼。

===Original===
He didn’t, of course, talk of his private feelings to Captain Vincent. All those thoughts and emotions were compressed in the space of not much more than a minute or two while, holding on with one hand to his bush and having got a good foothold for one of his feet, he indulged in that pleasant anticipatory sense of success. In the old days the farmer’s wife used to be a light sleeper. The farmhands which, he remembered, lived in the village or were distributed in stables and outhouses, did not give him any concern. He wouldn’t need to knock heavily. He pictured to himself the farmer’s wife sitting up in bed, listening, then rousing her husband, who, as likely as not, would take the gun standing against the dresser downstairs and come to the door.

===Chinese===
当然，他并没有把自己的这些私心向文森特舰长吐露半字。所有这些念头与情绪，都压缩在短短一两分钟之内——他一手攀着那丛灌木，一只脚稳稳踩住一处落脚的地方，就在那儿沉醉于那种即将功成的美妙预感之中。当年那农庄主妇睡觉很轻。至于那些雇工——他记得他们住在村子里，或分散在马厩和棚屋里——他一点也不担心。他用不着敲得太重。他想象着农庄主妇在床上坐起来倾听，接着推醒丈夫，那人十之八九会抄起楼下餐具柜旁靠着的那杆枪，走过来开门。

===Original===
And then everything would be all right.… But perhaps… yes! It was just as likely the farmer would simply open the window and hold a parley. That really was most likely. Naturally. In his place Bolt felt he would do that very thing. Yes, that was what a man in a lonely house, in the middle of the night, would do most naturally. And he imagined himself whispering mysteriously his answers up the wall to the obvious questions—“*Ami*”—“*Bolt*”—“*Ouvrez-moi*”—“*vive le roi*”—or things of that sort. And in sequence to those vivid images it occurred to Bolt that the best thing he could do would be to throw small stones against the window shutter, the sort of sound most likely to rouse a light sleeper. He wasn’t quite sure which window on the floor above the ground floor was that of those people’s bedroom, but there were anyhow only three of them. In a moment he would have sprung up from his foothold on to the level if, raising his eyes for another look at the front of the house, he had not perceived that one of the windows was already open. How he could have failed to notice that before, he couldn’t explain.

===Chinese===
然后一切便万事大吉。……可也许……对啦！同样有可能的是，农庄主干脆打开窗户，隔窗与他对话。这才真是最有可能的。自然不过。换了是博尔特自己在那个位置，他觉得他也会这么办。是的，一个人孤零零地住在这么一所屋子里，半夜三更的，最顺理成章的就是这么做。于是他想象自己神秘兮兮地贴着墙根，低声答复着那些显而易见的问话——“「朋友」（*ami*）”——“「博尔特」”——“「给我开门」（*ouvrez-moi*）”——“「国王万岁」（*vive le roi*）”——诸如此类的话。顺着这些鲜活的画面，博尔特忽然想到，自己最好做的事，莫过于朝那扇百叶窗扔几颗小石子——那种最能把觉轻的人惊醒的声响。他并不十分确定一楼之上那一排窗户里，哪一扇才是那对夫妻的卧室，可总共也不过三扇罢了。再过一会儿，他就要从落脚处一纵身跳上那片平地了——谁知就在这时，他抬起眼再朝屋子的正面望了一眼，竟发现其中一扇窗早已敞开着。他先前怎么会没留意到这个，他自己也解释不清。

===Original===
He confessed to Captain Vincent in the course of his narrative that “this open window, sir, checked me dead. In fact, sir, it shook my confidence, for you know, sir, that no native of these parts would dream of sleeping with his window open. It struck me that there was something wrong there; and I remained where I was.”

===Chinese===
他在叙述中对文森特舰长承认道：“这扇敞开的窗，长官，把我生生钉在了原地。说实在的，长官，它动摇了我的信心，因为您是知道的，长官，这一带土生土长的人，做梦也不会开着窗户睡觉。我当时就觉得那里头有点不对劲；于是我就待在原地没动。”

===Original===
That fascination of repose, of secretive friendliness, which houses present at night, was gone. By the power of an open window, a black square in the moonlighted wall, the farmhouse took on the aspect of a mantrap. Bolt assured Captain Vincent that the window would not have stopped him; he would have gone on all the same, though with an uncertain mind. But while he was thinking it out there glided without a sound before his irresolute eyes from somewhere a white vision—a woman. He could see her black hair flowing down her back. A woman whom anybody would have been excused for taking for a ghost. “I won’t say that she froze my blood, sir, but she made me cold all over for a moment. Lots of people have seen ghosts, at least they say so, and I have an open mind about that. She was a weird thing to look at in the moonlight. She did not act like a sleepwalker either. If she had not come out of a grave, then she had jumped out of bed. But when she stole back and hid herself round the corner of the house I knew she was not a ghost. She could not have seen me. There she stood in the black shadow watching for something—or waiting for somebody,” added Bolt in a grim tone. “She looked crazy,” he conceded charitably.

===Chinese===
房屋在夜里所呈现的那种安谧的、含蓄而友善的迷人气息，荡然无存。凭着一扇敞开的窗——月光下墙壁上一个黑黝黝的方块——那农舍登时显出捕人陷阱的模样。博尔特向文森特舰长保证，那扇窗本不至于拦住他；他还是会照样上前，只不过心里没了底。可就在他权衡之际，他那双游移不定的眼前，不知从哪儿无声无息地飘过一个白色的影子——一个女人。他看得见她那头黑发披散在背后。这样一个女人，任谁把她当作鬼魂都不为过。“我不敢说她把我的血都冻住了，长官，可她确实让我一时浑身发凉。许多人见过鬼，至少他们嘴上这么说，这事我存着个不偏不倚的见解。月光下看她，是个古怪的东西。她的举止也不像梦游的人。她要不是从坟里钻出来的，那就是从床上跳下来的。可当她悄悄退回去、躲到屋子的拐角后面，我便知道她不是鬼了。她不可能瞧见我。她站在那片漆黑的影子里，守望着什么——或者，在等什么人，”博尔特用一种阴沉的声调补了一句。“她瞧着像是疯疯癫癫的，”他厚道地体谅了一句。

===Original===
One thing was clear to him: there had been changes in that farmhouse since his time. Bolt resented them, as if that time had been only last week. The woman concealed round the corner remained in his full view, watchful, as if only waiting for him to show himself in the open, to run off screeching and rouse all the countryside. Bolt came quickly to the conclusion that he must withdraw from the slope. On lowering himself from his first position he had the misfortune to dislodge a stone. This circumstance precipitated his retreat. In a very few minutes he found himself by the shore. He paused to listen. Above him, up the ravine and all round amongst the rocks, everything was perfectly still. He walked along in the direction of his boat. There was nothing for it but to get away quietly and perhaps.…

===Chinese===
有一件事他是明白的：自打他那个年头以来，这农舍里头已经起了变化。博尔特对此十分恼火，仿佛那是上个礼拜才发生的事。那个躲在拐角处的女人，仍在他视野之内，一脸戒备，仿佛只等他一在空旷处露面，就要尖叫着跑开、惊动四乡八邻。博尔特很快拿定主意：他必须从这山坡上撤下去。从他原先的位置往下挪时，他不幸弄松了一块石头。这一下催促了他的撤退。没过几分钟他便到了岸边。他停下来听了听。在他头顶，沿着沟壑上去，以及礁石丛中四面八方，一切都寂然无声。他朝着自己小艇的方向走去。除了悄悄溜走，也许……再没别的法子了。

===Original===
“Yes, Mr. Bolt, I fear we shall have to give up our plan,” interrupted Captain Vincent at that point. Bolt’s assent came reluctantly, and then he braced himself to confess that this was not the worst. Before the astonished face of Captain Vincent he hastened to blurt it out. He was very sorry, he could in no way account for it, but—he had lost a man.

===Chinese===
“是了，博尔特先生，我怕我们只好放弃我们的计划了，”文森特舰长在这时插话道。博尔特勉勉强强地表示同意，随即鼓起勇气承认，这还不算最糟的。对着文森特舰长那张惊愕的脸，他赶忙一股脑儿倒了出来。他万分抱歉，他自己也莫名其妙，可是——他丢了一个弟兄。

===Original===
Captain Vincent seemed unable to believe his ears. “What do you say? Lost a man out of my boat’s crew!” He was profoundly shocked. Bolt was correspondingly distressed. He narrated that, shortly after he had left them, the seamen had heard, or imagined they had heard, some faint and peculiar noises somewhere within the cove. The coxswain sent one of the men, the oldest of the boat’s crew, along the shore to ascertain whether their boat hauled on the beach could be seen from the other side of the cove. The man—it was Symons—departed crawling on his hands and knees to make the circuit and, well—he had not returned. This was really the reason why the boat was so late in getting back to the ship. Of course Bolt did not like to give up the man. It was inconceivable that Symons should have deserted. He had left his cutlass behind and was completely unarmed, but had he been suddenly pounced upon he surely would have been able to let out a yell that could have been heard all over the cove. But till daybreak a profound stillness, in which it seemed a whisper could have been heard for miles, had reigned over the coast. It was as if Symons had been spirited away by some supernatural means, without a scuffle, without a cry. For it was inconceivable that he should have ventured inland and got captured there. It was equally inconceivable that there should have been on that particular night men ready to pounce upon Symons and knock him on the head so neatly as not to let him give a groan even.

===Chinese===
文森特舰长简直不敢相信自己的耳朵。“你说什么？从我艇员里丢了一个人！”他震惊至深。博尔特也跟着十分难受。他叙述道，他离开他们之后不久，那几个水手便听见——或者自以为听见——小海湾里某处传来几声微弱而古怪的响动。舵手长便打发其中一人，也是艇员里年纪最大的那个，沿岸边去探看一番，看他们拖在海滩上的那只艇从小海湾的另一侧是否望得见。那人——正是西蒙斯（Symons）——手脚并用地爬着去绕那一圈，结果——他再没有回来。这才是小艇迟迟未能回船的真正缘故。博尔特自然不愿就这么把人丢了。要说西蒙斯开了小差，那绝无可能。他的短剑留了下来，身上全无武器，可他若是被人猛地扑倒，总该能发出一声喊、让整个海湾都听见才是。然而直到天亮，一片深沉的死寂笼罩着整段海岸——那死寂里，仿佛一声耳语都能传出好几里格去。倒像是西蒙斯被什么超自然的法子凭空摄走了，没挣扎，没喊叫。因为要说他会贸然闯进内陆、在那里被擒，那是不可想象的。同样不可想象的是，偏偏在那一夜，会有人埋伏好了，专等扑倒西蒙斯，并且干净利落地一下把他打闷过去，连一声哼都不让他发出来。

===Original===
Captain Vincent said: “All this is very fantastical, Mr. Bolt,” and compressed his lips firmly for a moment before he continued: “But not much more than your woman. I suppose you did see something real.…

===Chinese===
文森特舰长说道：“这些未免太玄虚了，博尔特先生，”他紧紧抿了抿嘴，顿了一顿才接着说，“可也未见得比你那个女人更玄。我想，你确是看见了什么实在的东西……

===Original===
“I tell you, sir, she stood there in full moonlight for ten minutes within a stone’s throw of me,” protested Bolt with a sort of desperation. “She seemed to have jumped out of bed only to look at the house. If she had a petticoat over her night-shift, that was all. Her back was to me. When she moved away I could not make out her face properly. Then she went to stand in the shadow of the house.”

===Chinese===
“我跟您说，长官，她就那么站在朗朗月光底下，离我不过一投石之遥，足足站了有十分钟，”博尔特带着一种近乎绝望的口气抗辩道，“她好像是从床上跳起来，只为看一眼那屋子。她若是衬衣外面套了条裙子，也就只那么一点。她背对着我。她走开的时候，我也没能把她的脸看真切。后来她就站到屋子的影子里去了。”

===Original===
“On the watch,” suggested Captain Vincent.

===Chinese===
“在守望，”文森特舰长提点了一句。

===Original===
“Looked like it, sir,” confessed Bolt.

===Chinese===
“瞧着像是这么回事，长官，”博尔特承认道。

===Original===
“So there must have been somebody about,” concluded Captain Vincent with assurance.

===Chinese===
“这么说，附近一定有个人，”文森特舰长笃定地下了结论。

===Original===
Bolt murmured a reluctant, “Must have been.” He had expected to get into enormous trouble over this affair and was much relieved by the captain’s quiet attitude. “I hope, sir, you approve of my conduct in not attempting to look for Symons at once?”

===Chinese===
博尔特勉勉强强地咕哝了一声：“想必是有的。”他原以为这件事会给自己招来天大的麻烦，见舰长态度平静，大大松了一口气。“我希望，长官，您赞同我没有立刻动手去搜寻西蒙斯这一处置？”

===Original===
“I was afraid of spoiling our chances to carry out your plan, sir, by disclosing our presence on shore. And that could not have been avoided. Moreover, we were only five in all and not properly armed.”

===Chinese===
“我是怕，长官，一旦暴露了我们在岸上的形迹，就会坏了您那计划成败的指望。而那是免不了的。再者，我们统共才五个人，又没像样的家伙。”

===Original===
“Yes. You acted prudently by not advancing inland,” said the captain.

===Chinese===
“嗯。你没有贸然深入内陆，处置得很谨慎，”舰长说道。

===Original===
“The plan has gone down before your nightwalker, Mr. Bolt,” Captain Vincent declared dryly. “But we must try to find out what has become of our man if it can be done without risking too much.”

===Chinese===
“你的计划已经在那个夜行人面前栽了跟头了，博尔特先生，”文森特舰长冷冷地说道。“可我们总得想法子弄清楚咱们那个人究竟怎样了，只要这事能办得不至于冒太大的险。”

===Original===
“By landing a large party this very next night we could surround the house,” Bolt suggested. “If we find friends there, well and good. If enemies, then we could carry off some of them on board for exchange perhaps. I am almost sorry I did not go back and kidnap that wench—whoever she was,” he added recklessly. “Ah! if it had only been a man!”

===Chinese===
“明儿夜里，咱们派一大拨人登岸，把那屋子围起来，”博尔特提议道，“要是咱们在那儿碰上的是朋友，那再好不过。要是仇敌，那咱们就掳几个回船上去，也许还能拿来交换。我简直后悔没折回去把那个丫头掳来——管她是谁呢，”他不管不顾地补了一句，“唉！要是个男人就好了！”

===Original===
“No doubt there was a man not very far off,” said Captain Vincent equably. “That will do, Mr. Bolt. You had better go and get some rest now.”

===Chinese===
“毫无疑问，不远处就有一个男人，”文森特舰长不紧不慢地说道。“就到这里吧，博尔特先生。你最好现在去歇一会儿。”

===Original===
Bolt was glad to obey, for he was tired and hungry after his dismal failure. What vexed him most was its absurdity. Captain Vincent, though he too had passed a sleepless night, felt too restless to remain below. He followed his officer on deck.

===Chinese===
博尔特巴不得遵命，因为这场灰头土脸的失败之后，他又累又饿。最叫他懊恼的，是这事荒唐得离谱。文森特舰长虽同样熬了个通宵，却心绪烦乱，在舱下待不住，便跟着他的属下上了甲板。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
By that time the *Amelia* had been towed half a mile or so away from Cape Esterel. This change had brought her nearer to the two watchers on the hillside who would have been plainly visible to the people on her deck, but for the head of the pine which concealed their movements. Lieutenant Réal, bestriding the rugged trunk as high as he could get, had the whole of the English ship’s deck open to the range of his pocket-glass which he used between the branches. He said to Peyrol suddenly:

===Chinese===
到那时，阿米莉亚号已被拖离埃斯泰雷尔角约莫半英里。这一变动使她离山坡上的两个观察者更近了些，要不是那棵松树的树头遮住了他们的动静，船上甲板上的人本可以清清楚楚地看见他们俩。雷亚尔中尉尽可能高地跨骑在那根粗糙的树干上，用他那副袖珍望远镜隔着枝叶扫视，整条英国船的甲板尽收于望远镜的射程之内。他忽然对佩罗尔说道：

===Original===
“Her captain has just come on deck.”

===Chinese===
“她的舰长刚刚上了甲板。”

===Original===
Peyrol, sitting at the foot of the tree, made no answer for a long while. A warm drowsiness lay over the land and seemed to press down his eyelids. But inwardly the old rover was intensely awake. Under the mask of his immobility, with half-shut eyes and idly clasped hands, he heard the lieutenant, perched up there near the head of the tree, mutter counting something: “One, two, three,” and then a loud “*Parbleu!*” after which the lieutenant in his trunk-bestriding attitude began to jerk himself backwards. Peyrol got up out of his way, but could not restrain himself from asking: “What’s the matter now?”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔坐在树根处，好半天没有搭腔。一种暖洋洋的倦意笼罩着大地，似乎在往下压他的眼皮。可是在内心里，这老漫游者却清醒极了。在他那副纹丝不动的假面之下——半阖着眼，双手慵懒地交握着——他听见中尉高踞在靠近树头的地方，嘴里嘟嘟囔囔地数着什么：“一、二、三，”接着便是一声响亮的“*Parbleu!*”（天哪！），随后中尉便维持着跨骑树干的姿势，开始一耸一耸地向后挪动身子。佩罗尔让到一旁给他腾地方，却忍不住问道：“又怎么了？”

===Original===
“I will tell you what’s the matter,” said the other, excitedly. As soon as he got his footing he walked up to old Peyrol and when quite close to him folded his arms across his chest.

===Chinese===
“我来告诉你怎么了，”那一位激动地说。他一站稳脚跟，便径直走到老佩罗尔跟前，到了近身处，双臂交叉抱在胸前。

===Original===
“The first thing I did was to count the boats in the water. There was not a single one left on board. And now I just counted them again and found one more there. That ship had a boat out last night. How I missed seeing her pull out from under the land I don’t know. I was watching the decks, I suppose, and she seems to have gone straight up to the towrope. But I was right. That Englishman had a boat out.”

===Chinese===
“我做的头一件事，就是数水里的小艇。船上原先连一艘都没留下。可我方才又数了一遍，发现多出了一艘。那条船昨夜放出去一只小艇。我怎么竟没看见她从陆地这边划出去的，我自己也不明白。我大概是在盯着甲板看吧，而她似乎是径直划到了拖缆跟前。不过我没看错。那个英国人昨夜确实放出了一只小艇。”

===Original===
He seized Peyrol by both shoulders suddenly. “I believe you knew it all the time. You knew it, I tell you.” Peyrol, shaken violently by the shoulders, raised his eyes to look at the angry face within a few inches of his own. In his worn gaze there was no fear or shame, but a troubled perplexity and obvious concern. He remained passive, merely remonstrating softly:

===Chinese===
他猛地一把抓住佩罗尔的双肩。“我相信你一直都知道。我告诉你，你早就知道。”佩罗尔被猛烈地摇晃着肩膀，抬起眼来，望着那张离自己不过几英寸远的愤怒的脸。在他那副饱经风霜的目光里，没有畏惧，也没有羞愧，有的只是一种不安的困惑与显而易见的关切。他始终被动地任其摇晃，只是轻声劝解着：

===Original===
“*Doucement. Doucement.*”

===Chinese===
“*Doucement. Doucement.*”（轻一点，轻一点。）

===Original===
The lieutenant suddenly desisted with a final jerk which failed to stagger old Peyrol, who, directly he had been released, assumed an explanatory tone.

===Chinese===
中尉猛不丁地松了手，最后那一下猛拽也没能撼动老佩罗尔分毫；佩罗尔一经放开，便换上了一副解释的口吻。

===Original===
“For the ground is slippery here. If I had lost my footing I would not have been able to prevent myself from grabbing at you, and we would have gone down that cliff together; which would have told those Englishmen more than twenty boats could have found out in as many nights.”

===Chinese===
“因为这里的地面滑得很。我要是失了脚，就免不了要伸手去抓你，那咱们俩就得一块儿滚下那道悬崖去；这可比二十只小艇花上二十个夜晚所能探听到的，还要让那些英国人明白得多。”

===Original===
Secretly Lieutenant Réal was daunted by Peyrol’s mildness. It could not be shaken. Even physically he had an impression of the utter futility of his effort, as though he had tried to shake a rock. He threw himself on the ground, carelessly saying:

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉暗自被佩罗尔的温和慑住了。那是撼不动的。即便就气力而论，他也觉得自己那一番力气全属徒劳，仿佛想要摇动一块岩石。他一屁股坐到地上，漫不经心地说道：

===Original===
“As for instance?”

===Chinese===
“比方说？”

===Original===
Peyrol lowered himself with a deliberation appropriate to his grey hairs. “You don’t suppose that out of a hundred and twenty or so pairs of eyes on board that ship there wouldn’t be a dozen at least scanning the shore. Two men falling down a cliff would have been a startling sight. The English would have been interested enough to send a boat ashore to go through our pockets, and whether dead or only half dead we wouldn’t have been in a state to prevent them. It wouldn’t matter so much as to me, and I don’t know what papers you may have in your pockets, but there are your shoulder straps, your uniform coat.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔以一种与他那一头白发相称的从容缓缓坐了下来。“你总不至于以为，那条船上约莫一百二十双眼睛里，少说也有一打正在扫视着海岸吧。两个人从悬崖上滚下去，那可是个触目的景象。英国人势必大感兴趣，会派一只小艇上岸来搜我们的口袋，而无论我们是死了还是只死了半条命，都断无气力去拦阻他们。这对我倒也罢了，我不知你口袋里可有什么文书，可还有你的肩章，你的制服外套。”

===Original===
“I carry no papers in my pocket, and.﻿ ﻿…” A sudden thought seemed to strike the lieutenant, a thought so intense and far-fetched as to give his mental effort a momentary aspect of vacancy. He shook it off and went on in a changed tone: “The shoulder straps would not have been much of a revelation by themselves.”

===Chinese===
“我口袋里没带什么文书，而且……”一个念头似乎猛地攫住了中尉，那念头如此强烈又如此牵强，竟使他那番绞尽的脑汁在一瞬间显出一种茫然之态。他甩了甩头把它驱开，换了一种口气接着说道：“光凭肩章倒也说明不了多少。”

===Original===
“No. Not much. But enough to let her captain know that he had been watched. For what else could the dead body of a naval officer with a spyglass in his pocket mean? Hundreds of eyes may glance carelessly at that ship every day from all parts of the coast, though I fancy those landsmen hardly take the trouble to look at her now. But that’s a very different thing from being kept under observation. However, I don’t suppose all this matters much.”

===Chinese===
“不错。说明不了多少。可也足够让她的舰长知道，自己已被人盯上了。因为一个口袋里揣着望远镜的海军军官的死尸，还能意味着别的什么呢？每天从海岸四面八方，也许有成百双眼睛漫不经心地瞥过那条船，不过我估摸那些岸上人如今难得再费神去瞧她一眼了。可那跟被人盯梢完全是两码事。话虽如此，我想这些也关系不大。”

===Original===
The lieutenant was recovering from the spell of that sudden thought. “Papers in my pocket,” he muttered to himself. “That would be a perfect way.” His parted lips came together in a slightly sarcastic smile with which he met Peyrol’s puzzled, sidelong glance provoked by the inexplicable character of these words.

===Chinese===
中尉正从那个突如其来念头的魔力中缓过劲来。“文书……揣在口袋里，”他自言自语地嘟囔着。“那倒是个万全的法子。”他微微分开的双唇合拢了，漾起一丝略带讥诮的笑意，以此来迎对佩罗尔那一道困惑的、斜瞟过来的目光——这目光正是被这几句莫名其妙的话所引出来的。

===Original===
“I bet,” said the lieutenant, “that ever since I came here first you have been more or less worrying your old head about my motives and intentions.”

===Chinese===
“我敢打赌，”中尉说道，“打从我头一回到这儿来，你这颗老脑袋就多多少少一直在为我的动机和用意犯嘀咕吧。”

===Original===
Peyrol said simply: “You came here on service at first and afterwards you came again because even in the Toulon fleet an officer may get a few days’ leave. As to your intentions, I won’t say anything about them. Especially as regards myself. About ten minutes ago anybody looking on would have thought they were not friendly to me.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔直截了当地说：“你起初是因公来的，后来再来，那是因为即便在土伦舰队里，一个军官也能请上几天假。至于你的用意嘛，我什么都不想说。尤其是冲着我来的那一部分。约莫十分钟前，旁人要是看着，准会以为你对我没存什么好心。”
