“匀实。我出来锁上门之后，又贴着门听了一阵，好像听见他打起了呼噜。”

===Original===
Peyrol looked interested and also slightly anxious.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔面露兴味，也带着一丝焦灼。

===Original===
“I had to come up and show myself this morning as if nothing had happened,” he said. “The officer has been here for two days, and he might have taken it into his head to go down to the tartane. I have been on the stretch all the morning. A goat jumping up was enough to give me a turn. Fancy him running up here with his broken head all bandaged up, with you after him.”

===Chinese===
“今天早上我不得不上来露个面，装作什么事也没发生的样子，”他说，“那位军官已经来了两天了，保不齐他会一时兴起，要下到塔尔塔纳（tartane，地中海单桅三角帆船）上去看看。我整个上午都提着一颗心。一只山羊跳起来，都够叫我心惊一下的。你想想，要是他裹着满头绑带、顶着那颗打破的脑袋闯上这儿来，你还在后头撵着他——那成什么样子。”

===Original===
This seemed to be too much for Michel. He said almost indignantly:

“The man’s half killed.”

“It takes a lot to even half kill a Brother of the Coast. There are men and men. You, for instance,” Peyrol continued placidly, “you would have been altogether killed if it had been your head that got in the way. And there are animals, beasts twice your size, regular monsters, that may be killed with nothing more than just a tap on the nose. That’s well known. I was really afraid he would overcome you in some way or other.…”

“Come, *maître*! One isn’t a little child,” protested Michel against this accumulation of improbabilities. He did it, however, only in a whisper and with childlike shyness. Peyrol folded his arms on his breast:

“Go, finish your soup,” he commanded in a low voice, “and then go down to the tartane. You locked the cabin door properly, you said?”

“Yes, I have,” protested Michel, staggered by this display of anxiety. “He could sooner burst the deck above his head, as you know.”

“All the same, take a small spar and shore up that door against the heel of the mast. And then watch outside. Don’t you go in to him on any account. Stay on deck and keep a lookout for me. There is a tangle here that won’t be easily cleared and I must be very careful. I will try to slip away and get down as soon as I get rid of that officer.”

===Chinese===
这话米歇尔似乎受不了。他几乎是愤愤不平地说：

“那人已经半个身子进了鬼门关了。”

“要叫一个海岸兄弟会的人哪怕半死，也非得费好大的劲不可。人有种种，各有不同。比方说你吧，”佩罗尔平心静气地接着说，“要是方才挡在那儿的是你的脑袋，你早就整个儿死透了。还有些畜生，块头比你大一倍，活脱脱的怪物，可只要在鼻子上轻轻一拍就能要了命。这是尽人皆知的。我当真怕他会用什么法子把你制服了……”

“得了，*maître*（师傅）！我又不是三岁的小孩。”米歇尔对这一连串匪夷所思的设想提出了抗议。不过他只是压低了嗓音说的，带着孩子气的腼腆。佩罗尔双臂抱在胸前：

“去，把你的汤喝完，”他低声吩咐道，“然后就下到塔尔塔纳上去。你说舱门拴牢了？”

“拴牢了，拴牢了。”米歇尔被这副如临大敌的焦虑模样弄得有些发懵，连声抗议道，“他就是宁可把头顶上的甲板顶破了也出不来，这你是知道的。”

“不管怎样，拿根小帆桁（spar）去，把那道门抵住，顶在桅根上。然后在外头守着。无论如何不要进到他跟前去。待在甲板上，替我留意着点动静。这儿的乱麻一时半会儿解不开，我得格外小心。等我打发了那军官，就设法溜出来下去。”

===Original===
The conference in the sunshine being ended, Peyrol walked leisurely out of the yard gate, and protruding his head beyond the corner of the house, saw Lieutenant Réal sitting on the bench. This he had expected to see. But he had not expected to see him there alone. It was just like this: wherever Arlette happened to be, there were worrying possibilities. But she might have been helping her aunt in the kitchen with her sleeves rolled up on such white arms as Peyrol had never seen on any woman before. The way she had taken to dressing her hair in a plait with a broad black velvet ribbon and an Arlesian cap was very becoming. She was wearing now her mother’s clothes of which there were chestfuls, altered for her of course. The late mistress of the Escampobar Farm had been an Arlesienne. Well-to-do, too. Yes, even for women’s clothes the Escampobar natives could do without intercourse with the outer world. It was quite time that this confounded lieutenant went back to Toulon. This was the third day. His short leave must be up. Peyrol’s attitude towards naval officers had been always guarded and suspicious. His relations with them had been very mixed. They had been his enemies and his superiors. He had been chased by them. He had been trusted by them. The Revolution had made a clean cut across the consistency of his wild life—Brother of the Coast and gunner in the national Navy—and yet he was always the same man. It was like that, too, with them. Officers of the King, officers of the Republic, it was only changing the skin. All alike looked askance at a free rover. Even this one could not forget his epaulettes when talking to him. Scorn and mistrust of epaulettes were rooted deeply in old Peyrol. Yet he did not absolutely hate Lieutenant Réal. Only the fellow’s coming to the farm was generally a curse and his presence at that particular moment a confounded nuisance and to a certain extent even a danger. “I have no mind to be hauled to Toulon by the scruff of my neck,” Peyrol said to himself. There was no trusting those epaulette-wearers. Any one of them was capable of jumping on his best friend on account of some officer-like notion or other.

===Chinese===
这场阳光下的密议既毕，佩罗尔踱出院门，把头探出屋角，便瞧见雷亚尔中尉正坐在那条长凳上。这一幕他原是料到的。可他没有料到的是，中尉竟独自一人坐在那儿。事情向来如此：只要阿尔莱特（Arlette）在什么地方，便总有叫人揪心的种种可能。不过这会儿她也许正在厨房里帮姑母的忙，把袖子挽到——佩罗尔从未在任何女人身上见过那样白皙的胳膊。她近来喜欢把头发编成一条辫子，系一根宽宽的黑丝绒带子，再戴上一顶阿尔式女帽（Arlesian cap），那打扮很是相称。她如今穿的是她母亲的衣裳，那样的衣裳满满几箱子呢，当然是改小了给她穿的。埃斯坎波巴农庄已故的女主人原是个阿尔姑娘（Arlesienne），家底也殷实。是啊，即便是女人的衣裳，埃斯坎波巴这地方的人也尽可不与外界往来而自给自足。这个讨厌的中尉早该回土伦去了。这已经是第三天了。他那短短的假想必到期了。佩罗尔对海军军官向来抱着戒备和猜疑的态度。他与他们的瓜葛错综复杂。他们做过他的敌人，也曾是他的上级。他被他们追剿过，也被他们信任过。大革命在他那狂放的一生中划下一道干净的切口——海岸兄弟会的弟兄，与共和国海军的炮长——可他始终是同一个人。他们那边也是如此。国王的军官，共和国的军官，不过是换了一层皮罢了。他们个个都以斜眼睨视一个自由的漫游者。便是眼前这一位，与他说话时也忘不了自己的肩章。对肩章的鄙夷与不信任，在老佩罗尔心里扎根极深。然而他也并非当真恨雷亚尔中尉。只是这家伙到农庄来，通常总是一场祸事；而他此刻在此，更是个天大的麻烦，甚至到了某种程度，还是一重危险。“我可不想被人揪着后脖颈子拖到土伦去。”佩罗尔暗自思忖。那些佩肩章的家伙是信不得的。他们当中随便哪一个，都有可能因为脑袋里冒出什么军官派头的念头，便翻脸跳到自家最好的朋友头上去。

===Original===
Peyrol, stepping round the corner, sat down by the side of Lieutenant Réal with the feeling somehow of coming to grips with a slippery customer. The lieutenant, as he sat there, unaware of Peyrol’s survey of his person, gave no notion of slipperiness. On the contrary, he looked rather immovably established. Very much at home. Too much at home. Even after Peyrol sat down by his side he continued to look immovable—or at least difficult to get rid of. In the still noonday heat the faint shrilling of cicadas was the only sound of life heard for quite a long time. Delicate, evanescent, cheerful, careless sort of life, yet not without passion. A sudden gloom seemed to be cast over the joy of the cicadas by the lieutenant’s voice though the words were the most perfunctory possible.

“*Tiens! Vous voilà.*”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔绕过屋角，在雷亚尔中尉身旁坐了下来，心里莫名地觉得自己是在同一个油滑的角色角力。中尉坐在那儿，并不知晓佩罗尔方才把他从头到脚打量了一遍，看上去却没有半点油滑之态。恰恰相反，他像是安坐得纹丝不动。如在自己家中一般。太如在自己家中了。甚至在佩罗尔于他身旁坐下之后，他看上去依旧岿然不动——至少是难以打发走的。在这寂静的正午暑热里，蝉鸣那微弱的尖嘶，是相当长一段时间里所能听见的唯一声响。一种精致、短促、欢快而漫不经心的生之气息，却又并非没有激情。中尉开口了，虽然说的是最敷衍不过的话，他的嗓音却似乎骤然给蝉们的欢愉罩上了一层阴翳。

“*Tiens! Vous voilà.*”（哟！你在这儿。）

===Original===
In the stress of the situation Peyrol at once asked himself: Now why does he say that? Where did he expect me to be? The lieutenant need not have spoken at all. He had known him now for about two years off and on, and it had happened many times that they had sat side by side on that bench in a sort of “at arm’s length” equality without exchanging a single word. And why could he not have kept quiet now? That naval officer never spoke without an object, but what could one make of words like that? Peyrol achieved an insincere yawn and suggested mildly:

“A bit of siesta wouldn’t be amiss. What do you think, lieutenant?”

===Chinese===
在这剑拔弩张的情势下，佩罗尔当即在心里盘问：他干吗说这话？他又以为我本该在哪儿？中尉本可以一言不发的。他与这人断断续续相识已有两年光景，不止一次地，他们曾并肩坐在这条凳子上，处在一种“保持距离的”平等之中，彼此一言不发。那他这会儿又为什么不能安安静静的呢？那海军军官说话从来都是有的放矢，可这样的话能琢磨出什么名堂来？佩罗尔挤出一个虚伪的哈欠，慢悠悠地提议道：

“打个盹儿倒也不坏。你看呢，中尉？”

===Original===
And to himself he thought: “No fear, he won’t go to his room.” He would stay there and thereby keep him, Peyrol, from going down to the cove. He turned his eyes on that naval officer and if extreme and concentrated desire and mere force of will could have had any effect Lieutenant Réal would certainly have been removed suddenly from that bench. But he didn’t move. And Peyrol was astonished to see that man smile, but what astonished him still more was to hear him say:

“The trouble is that you have never been frank with me, Peyrol.”

===Chinese===
他心里却想：“休想，他才不会回自己房间去呢。”他会赖在那儿，从而把佩罗尔拴住，不让他下到小湾去。他把目光转向那个海军军官，倘若极度的、凝神聚力的渴望和纯粹的意志力能有什么效力的话，雷亚尔中尉早就该忽然从那条凳子上消失了。可他纹丝不动。而佩罗尔惊讶地看见那人居然笑了，更叫他惊讶的是听见他说：

“毛病就出在你从来没有对我坦白过，佩罗尔。”

===Original===
“Frank with you,” repeated the rover. “You want me to be frank with you? Well, I have wished you to the devil many times.”

“That’s better,” said Lieutenant Réal. “But why? I never tried to do you any harm.”

===Chinese===
“对你坦白，”漫游者重复道，“你要我对你坦白？好吧，我不止一次把你咒到魔鬼那儿去过了。”

“这就对了。”雷亚尔中尉说，“可是为什么？我从没想过要害你。”

===Original===
“Me harm,” cried Peyrol, “to me?”… But he faltered in his indignation as if frightened at it and ended in a very quiet tone: “You have been nosing in a lot of dirty papers to find something against a man who was not doing *you* any harm and was a seaman before you were born.”

“Quite a mistake. There was no nosing amongst papers. I came on them quite by accident. I won’t deny I was *intrigué* finding a man of your sort living in this place. But don’t be uneasy. Nobody would trouble his head about you. It’s a long time since you have been forgotten. Have no fear.”

===Chinese===
“害我？”佩罗尔嚷道，“害我？”……可他的愤慨说到一半便泄了气，仿佛被自己的怒火吓着了似的，末了用一种极平静的声调收尾道：“你净在一堆破烂文书里头翻翻拣拣，想给一个并没招惹*你*、而且在你出生之前就是个水手的人罗织点什么。”

“大错特错了。我可没有翻拣什么文书。我是纯粹偶然撞见的。我不否认，发现你这号人住在这种地方，我着实感到*intrigué*（心生好奇）。不过你尽管放心。没有人会为你费神。你早被遗忘了很久了。不必害怕。”

===Original===
“You! You talk to me of fear…? No,” cried the rover, “it’s enough to turn a fellow into a sansculotte if it weren’t for the sight of that specimen sneaking around here.”

===Chinese===
“你！你跟我说什么害怕……？不，”漫游者嚷道，“要不是亲眼瞧着那个货色在这儿鬼鬼祟祟地转悠，就凭这一句话，也够把人逼成一个无套裤汉（sansculotte）了。”

===Original===
The lieutenant turned his head sharply, and for a moment the naval officer and the free sea rover looked at each other gloomily. When Peyrol spoke again he had changed his mood.

“Why should I fear anybody? I owe nothing to anybody. I have given them up the prize ship in order and everything else, except my luck; and for that I account to nobody,” he added darkly.

===Chinese===
中尉猛地转过头去，一时间，那海军军官和自由的海上漫游者阴沉地对视着。待到佩罗尔再开口时，他已经换了心绪。

“我凭什么要怕谁？我不欠任何人。我已经把战利船连同其余一切，都如数交还了他们，除开我的运气——这个嘛，我不向任何人交代。”他阴郁地补了一句。

===Original===
“I don’t know what you are driving at,” the lieutenant said after a moment of thought. “All I know is that you seem to have given up your share of the prize money. There is no record of you ever claiming it.”

===Chinese===
“我不明白你这话什么意思。”中尉想了一想才说道，“我只知道，你像是放弃了你那份战利奖金。没有任何记录表明你曾申领过。”

===Original===
Peyrol did not like the sarcastic tone. “You have a nasty tongue,” he said, “with your damned trick of talking as if you were made of different clay.”

“No offence,” said the lieutenant, grave but a little puzzled. “Nobody will drag out that against you. It has been paid years ago to the Invalides’ fund. All this is buried and forgotten.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔不喜欢那讥诮的口吻。“你这张嘴可真够损的，”他说，“动不动就端出一副仿佛你是用别的料子捏出来的腔调，真叫见鬼。”

“无意冒犯。”中尉说，神色庄重，却也有几分不解，“没有人会把那件事翻出来作数。那笔钱多年前早已拨入荣军院（Invalides）基金了。这一切都已掩埋尘封、无人记起了。”

===Original===
Peyrol was grumbling and swearing to himself with such concentration that the lieutenant stopped and waited till he had finished.

“And there is no record of desertion or anything like that,” he continued then. “You stand there as *disparu*. I believe that after searching for you a little they came to the conclusion that you had come by your death somehow or other.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔自顾自地嘟嘟囔囔、骂骂咧咧，全神贯注得厉害，中尉于是住了口，等他骂完。

“也没有任何开小差之类的记录，”随后他接着说道，“你在名册上被记作*disparu*（失踪）。我想，他们稍许找了你一阵之后，便断定你多半已经不知怎么的死掉了。”

===Original===
“Did they? Well, perhaps old Peyrol is dead. At any rate he has buried himself here.” The rover suffered from great instability of feelings, for he passed in a flash from melancholy into fierceness. “And he was quiet enough till you came sniffing around this hole. More than once in my life I had occasion to wonder how soon the jackals would have a chance to dig up my carcass; but to have a naval officer come scratching round here was the last thing.…” Again a change came over him. “What can you want here?” he whispered, suddenly depressed.

===Chinese===
“是吗？好吧，也许老佩罗尔确实已经死了。不管怎么说，他已经把自己埋在这儿了。”漫游者的情绪极不稳定，转眼间便从一腔悒郁转为满心暴烈。“本来他够安分的了，直到你跑来在这窟窿周围东嗅西嗅。我这辈子不止一次琢磨过，那些豺狼什么时候才有机会来刨出我的尸首；可万万想不到，最后竟是来了一个海军军官，在这儿刨来刨去……”他又变了一副神情。“你到底想要什么？”他压低了声音，忽然颓丧下来。

===Original===
The lieutenant fell into the humour of that discourse. “I don’t want to disturb the dead,” he said, turning full to the rover, who, after his last words, had fixed his eyes on the ground. “I want to talk to the gunner Peyrol.”

Peyrol, without raising his eyes from the ground, growled: “He isn’t there. He is *disparu*. Go and look at the papers again. Vanished. Nobody here.”

===Chinese===
中尉顺着他这话的调子接了下去。“我无意惊扰亡者，”他说着，转过身正对着漫游者——后者说完最后那句话，便把眼睛盯在了地上。“我想跟炮长佩罗尔谈一谈。”

佩罗尔眼睛不离地面，闷声闷气地咕哝道：“他不在这儿。他*disparu*了。再去翻翻那些文书吧。人没了。这儿没这个人。”

===Original===
“That,” said Lieutenant Réal in a conversational tone, “that is a lie. He was talking to me this morning on the hillside as we were looking at the English ship. He knows all about her. He told me he spent nights making plans for her capture. He seemed to be a fellow with his heart in the right place. *Un homme de cœur.* You know him.”

===Chinese===
“那，”雷亚尔中尉用一种闲谈的口吻说道，“那是谎话。今天早上在山坡上，我们一块儿望着那条英国船的时候，他还在跟我说话呢。那条船的事他无所不知。他跟我说，他多少个夜晚都在盘算怎样把她拿捕。看样子倒是个心眼摆得正的人。*Un homme de cœur.*（一个有血性的汉子。）你认识他的。”

===Original===
Peyrol raised his big head slowly and looked at the lieutenant.

“Humph,” he grunted. A heavy, noncommittal grunt. His old heart was stirred, but the tangle was such that he had to be on his guard with any man who wore epaulettes. His profile preserved the immobility of a head struck on a medal while he listened to the lieutenant assuring him that this time he had come to Escampobar on purpose to speak with the gunner Peyrol. That he had not done so before was because it was a very confidential matter. At this point the lieutenant stopped and Peyrol made no sign. Inwardly he was asking himself what the lieutenant was driving at. But the lieutenant seemed to have shifted his ground. His tone, too, was slightly different. More practical.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔慢慢抬起他那硕大的脑袋，望着中尉。

“唔。”他咕哝了一声。一声沉闷的、不置可否的咕哝。他那颗衰老的心被触动了，可眼下的乱局如此纠缠，他对任何一个佩肩章的人都不得不存着戒心。他侧着脸，保持着一种勋章头像般的纹丝不动，一面听着中尉向他保证：这一回，他可是专为要同炮长佩罗尔谈一谈才到埃斯坎波巴来的。先前之所以没有这样做，是因为此事极其机密。说到这里，中尉打住了，佩罗尔毫无表示。他心里暗自盘问，中尉到底想干什么。可中尉像是换了个话头。他的语气也略有不同。更加务实了些。

===Original===
“You say you have made a study of that English ship’s movements. Well, for instance, suppose a breeze springs up, as it very likely will towards the evening, could you tell me where she will be tonight? I mean, what her captain is likely to do.”

“No, I couldn’t,” said Peyrol.

“But you said you have been observing him minutely for weeks. There aren’t so many alternatives, and taking the weather and everything into consideration, you can judge almost with certainty.”

“No,” said Peyrol again. “It so happens that I can’t.”

===Chinese===
“你说你一直在研究那条英国船的动向。那么，比方说，假设起了风——傍晚时分十之八九是要起的——你能不能告诉我，今夜她会在哪儿？我是说，她的舰长可能会怎么做。”

“不，我说不上来。”佩罗尔说。

“可你说过，你已经一连几个星期细细地观察他了。来来去去也就那么几种可能，再把天气和种种情形一并算进去，你几乎可以笃定地判断。”

“不，”佩罗尔又说了一遍，“偏巧我说不上来。”

===Original===
“Can’t you? Then you are worse than any of the old admirals that you think so little of. Why can’t you?”

“I will tell you why,” said Peyrol after a pause and with a face more like a carving than ever. “It’s because the fellow has never come so far this way before. Therefore I don’t know what he has got in his mind, and in consequence I can’t guess what he will do next. I may be able to tell you some other day but not today. Next time when you come… to see the old gunner.”

“No, it must be this time.”

“Do you mean you are going to stay here tonight?”

“Did you think I was here on leave? I tell you I am on service. Don’t you believe me?”

===Chinese===
“说不上来？那你可比你所瞧不上眼的那些老海军上将们还要不济了。你为什么说不上来？”

“我告诉你为什么。”佩罗尔顿了顿，一张脸比先前更像石刻了，“因为那家伙从前从来没有往这边走得这么远过。所以我不知道他心里打的什么主意，也就猜不出他下一步会干什么。改天我也许能告诉你，可今天不行。下回你来……来看老炮长的时候。”

“不成，非得这回不可。”

“你是说，你今晚要在这儿过夜？”

“你以为我是来这儿休假的？我告诉你，我是因公来的。你不信？”

===Original===
Peyrol let out a heavy sigh. “Yes, I believe you. And so they are thinking of catching her alive. And you are sent on service. Well, that doesn’t make it any easier for me to see you here.”

“You are a strange man, Peyrol,” said the lieutenant, “I believe you wish me dead.”

“No. Only out of this. But you are right. Peyrol is no friend either to your face or to your voice. They have done harm enough already.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔重重地叹了口气。“好吧，我信你。这么说，他们是在盘算着活捉她了。而你，是因公被派来的。唉，这并不能让我瞧见你在这儿更舒坦半分。”

“你这人真怪，佩罗尔，”中尉说，“我相信你是巴不得我死。”

“不。只是巴不得你离开这儿。不过你说得对。佩罗尔跟你的脸也好、跟你的嗓音也好，都不是朋友。它们已经闯的祸够多了。”

===Original===
They had never attained to such intimate terms before. There was no need for them to look at each other. The lieutenant thought: “Ah! he can’t keep his jealousy in.” There was no scorn or malice in that thought. It was much more like despair. He said mildly:

“You snarl like an old dog, Peyrol.”

“I have felt sometimes as if I could fly at your throat,” said Peyrol in a sort of calm whisper. “And it amuses you the more.”

“Amuses me. Do I look lighthearted?”

===Chinese===
他们之间还从未达到过这般亲昵的程度。彼此都用不着对看。中尉心想：“啊！他憋不住那股醋意了。”这念头里既无鄙夷，也无恶意，倒更像是绝望。他温和地说道：

“你活像一条老狗在龇牙，佩罗尔。”

“有时候我真觉得能扑上去掐住你的喉咙。”佩罗尔用一种平静的低语说道，“你便越发觉得有趣了。”

“觉得有趣。我瞧着像心里快活的样子吗？”

===Original===
Again Peyrol turned his head slowly for a long, steady stare. And again the naval officer and the rover gazed at each other with a searching and sombre frankness. This newborn intimacy could go no further.

“Listen to me, Peyrol.…”

“No,” said the other. “If you want to talk, talk to the gunner.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔又慢慢转过头去，久久地、稳稳地凝视着对方。那海军军官与漫游者再次以那种锐利而阴沉的坦率对视。这新生的亲密已无法再深一步了。

“听我说，佩罗尔……”

“不，”那一位说，“你要说，就同炮长去说。”

===Original===
Though he seemed to have adopted the notion of a double personality, the rover did not seem to be much easier in one character than in the other. Furrows of perplexity appeared on his brow, and as the lieutenant did not speak at once, Peyrol the gunner asked impatiently:

“So they are thinking of catching her alive.” It did not please him to hear the lieutenant say that it was not exactly this that the chiefs in Toulon had in their minds. Peyrol at once expressed the opinion that of all the naval chiefs that ever were, Citizen Renaud was the only one that was worth anything. Lieutenant Réal, disregarding the challenging tone, kept to the point.

“What they want to know is whether that English corvette interferes much with the coast traffic.”

===Chinese===
漫游者似乎已经接受了这种一分为二的人格设定，然而无论扮演哪一个角色，他看上去都不比另一个更自在些。他额上浮起困惑的皱纹，见中尉没有马上开口，炮长佩罗尔不耐烦地问道：

“这么说，他们是在盘算活捉她了。”听见中尉说，土伦那边的长官们心里盘算的并不完全是这一桩，佩罗尔老大不高兴。他当即发表见解：古往今来的海军长官里头，唯有公民雷诺还算一个人物。雷亚尔中尉不理会那挑衅的口气，扣住正题。

“他们想知道的是，那艘英国轻巡洋舰（corvette）对沿岸的航运是不是骚扰得很厉害。”

===Original===
“No, she doesn’t,” said Peyrol, “she leaves poor people alone unless, I suppose, some craft acts suspiciously. I have seen her give chase to one or two. But even those she did not detain. Michel—you know Michel?—has heard from the mainland people that she has captured several at various times. Of course, strictly speaking, nobody is safe.”

“Well, no. I wonder now what that Englishman would call ‘acting suspiciously.’”

===Chinese===
“不，不算厉害，”佩罗尔说，“她不怎么为难穷人，除非——我估摸——有哪条船行迹可疑。我亲眼见她追过一两条。可连那几条她也没扣下。米歇尔——你认识米歇尔吧？——从大陆那边的人嘴里听说，她各个时节前后倒也拿捕过几条。当然，较真起来，谁也算不上安全。”

“嗯，是不安全。我倒纳闷了，那个英国人所谓的‘行迹可疑’到底是怎么个标准。”

===Original===
“Ah, now you are asking something. Don’t you know what an Englishman is? One day easy and casual, next day ready to pounce on you like a tiger. Hard in the morning, careless in the afternoon, and only reliable in a fight, whether with or against you, but for the rest perfectly fantastic. You might think a little touched in the head, and there again it would not do to trust to that notion either.”

The lieutenant lending an attentive ear, Peyrol smoothed his brow and discoursed with gusto of Englishmen as if they had been a strange, very little-known tribe. “In a manner of speaking,” he concluded, “the oldest bird of them all can be caught with chaff, but not every day.” He shook his head, smiling to himself faintly, as if remembering a quaint passage or two.

===Chinese===
“哈，这你可问到点子上了。你难道不知道英国人是什么货色？今天还随随便便、和和气气，明天就恨不得像老虎一样扑到你身上。早上还凶神恶煞，下午就漫不经心了，唯独到了真刀真枪干一仗的时候才靠得住——无论跟你并肩还是与你为敌——其余的时候简直不可理喻。你也许会觉得他脑瓜子有点不正常，可话又说回来，你要是信了这一条，那也同样要吃亏。”

中尉侧耳倾听，佩罗尔舒展了眉头，津津有味地谈起英国人来，仿佛他们是一个稀奇古怪、鲜为人知的部落。“打个比方说吧，”他总结道，“他们当中最老到的鸟，也能用谷壳子诱住——可不能天天都奏效。”他摇了摇头，自顾自地淡淡一笑，像是想起了那么一两桩古怪的旧事。

===Original===
“You didn’t get all that knowledge of the English while you were a gunner,” observed the lieutenant dryly.

“There you go again,” said Peyrol. “And what’s that to you where I learned it all? Suppose I learned it all from a man who is dead now. Put it down to that.”

“I see. It amounts to this, that one can’t get at the back of their minds very easily.”

“No,” said Peyrol, then added grumpily, “and some Frenchmen are not much better. I wish I could get at the back of your mind.”

===Chinese===
“你这一身关于英国人的学问，总不是当炮长那会儿学来的吧。”中尉干巴巴地说了一句。

“你又来了，”佩罗尔说，“我打哪儿学来的，与你何干？就当我全是从一个死人那儿学来的好了。你就这么记着吧。”

“我明白了。说到底就是，人不容易摸透他们心里头真正的盘算。”

“是啊，”佩罗尔说，又没好气地补了一句，“有些法国人也强不到哪儿去。我倒恨不得能摸透你心里头的盘算。”

===Original===
“You would find a service matter there, gunner, that’s what you would find there, and a matter that seems nothing much at first sight, but when you look into it, is about as difficult to manage properly as anything you ever undertook in your life. It puzzled all the bigwigs. It must have, since I was called in. Of course I work on shore at the Admiralty, and I was in the way. They showed me the order from Paris and I could see at once the difficulty of it. I pointed it out and I was told.…

“To come here,” struck in Peyrol.

“No. To make arrangements to carry it out.”

“And you began by coming here. You are always coming here.”

“I began by looking for a man,” said the naval officer with emphasis.

===Chinese===
“你会在那儿找到一桩公务，炮长——你会找到的就是这个——一桩乍看没什么、细究起来却比你这辈子干过的任何一桩都难料理得当的差事。它把所有那些大人物都难住了。准是难住了，不然不会把我叫进去。当然了，我在海军部里做的是岸上差事，碍着他们的手眼。他们把巴黎来的命令拿给我看，我一眼就看出了其中的难处。我把它指出来，于是他们吩咐我……”

“到这儿来。”佩罗尔插嘴道。

“不。是去安排如何执行。”

“而你的头一步就是上这儿来。你老是上这儿来。”

“我的头一步，是去找一个人。”那海军军官加重了语气说。

===Original===
Peyrol looked at him searchingly. “Do you mean to say that in the whole fleet you couldn’t have found a man?”

“I never attempted to look for one there. My chief agreed with me that it isn’t a service for navy men.”

“Well, it must be something nasty for a naval man to admit that much. What is the order? I don’t suppose you came over here without being ready to show it to me.”

The lieutenant plunged his hand into the inside pocket of his naval jacket and then brought it out empty.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔审视地望着他。“你是说，堂堂整个舰队里，你竟找不出一个人来？”

“我压根儿没打算在那儿找。我的长官跟我想到一块儿去了：这不是海军人干的差使。”

“嚯，能让一个海军人承认到这个份上，那这差事一定不是什么好事。命令是什么？我猜你总不至于两手空空跑到这儿来，没打算拿给我看吧。”

中尉把手伸进海军上衣的内袋，抽出来时却空空如也。

===Original===
“Understand, Peyrol,” he said earnestly, “this is not a service of fighting. Good men are plentiful for that. The object is to play the enemy a trick.”

“Trick?” said Peyrol in a judicial tone, “that’s all right. I have seen in the Indian seas Monsieur Surcouf play tricks on the English… seen them with my own eyes, deceptions, disguises, and suchlike.… That’s quite sound in war.”

“Certainly. The order for this one comes from the First Consul himself, for it is no small matter. It’s to deceive the English Admiral.”

“What—that Nelson? Ah! but he is a cunning one.”

===Chinese===
“你明白，佩罗尔，”他郑重地说，“这不是一桩动刀枪的差事。干那个，好汉子多得是。目的是要给敌人设一个套。”

“设套？”佩罗尔用一种评判的口气说，“那没问题。我在印度洋上亲眼见过苏库夫先生（Monsieur Surcouf）怎样捉弄英国人……是我亲眼所见的，各种障眼法、伪装之类。……打仗时这完全说得过去。”

“自然。这一桩的命令是第一执政（First Consul）亲自下的，因为事关重大。是要骗过那位英国海军上将。”

“什么——纳尔逊？啊！那人可精着呢。”

===Original===
After expressing that opinion the old rover pulled out a red bandana handkerchief and after rubbing his face with it, repeated his opinion deliberately: “*Celui-là est un malin.*”

This time the lieutenant really brought out a paper from his pocket and saying, “I have copied the order for you to see,” handed it to the rover, who took it from him with a doubtful air.

===Chinese===
发表完这一番见解，老漫游者掏出一条红印花大手帕，擦了擦脸，然后一字一顿地重申他的看法：“*Celui-là est un malin.*（那一位可是个厉害角色。）”

这一回，中尉果真从口袋里掏出一张纸来，说道：“我把命令抄了一份给你过目。”说着递给漫游者。漫游者满脸狐疑地接了过去。

===Original===
Lieutenant Réal watched old Peyrol handling it at arm’s length, then with his arm bent trying to adjust the distance to his eyesight, and wondered whether he had copied it in a hand big enough to be read easily by the gunner Peyrol. The order ran like this: “You will make up a packet of dispatches and pretended private letters as if from officers, containing a clear statement besides hints calculated to convince the enemy that the destination of the fleet now fitting in Toulon is for Egypt and generally for the East. That packet you will send by sea in some small craft to Naples, taking care that the vessel should fall into the enemy’s hands.” The Préfet Maritime had called Réal, had shown him the paragraph of the letter from Paris, had turned the page over and laid his finger on the signature, “Bonaparte.” Then, after giving him a meaning glance, the admiral locked up the paper in a drawer and put the key in his pocket. Lieutenant Réal had written the passage down from memory directly the notion of consulting Peyrol had occurred to him.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉看着老佩罗尔把那张纸举在一臂之远，又弯起胳膊试图调到适合视力的距离，心里嘀咕着自己誊抄的字号够不够大，老炮长看起来吃不吃力。命令是这样写的：“你将造出一扎公文急件和若干伪造的军官私信，其中须包含一项明确的陈述，并辅以种种暗示，使敌人确信：现正于土伦装配的那支舰队，其目的地为埃及，并笼统地指向东方。该扎文件你须由海路以一艘小船送往那不勒斯（Naples），并须确保该船落入敌军之手。”海军省长（Préfet Maritime）召见了雷亚尔，把巴黎来函的那一段指给他看，随即翻过那一页，用手指按在签名上——“波拿巴”（Bonaparte）。而后，意味深长地瞟了他一眼，便把文件锁进抽屉，钥匙揣进了自己口袋。雷亚尔中尉一萌生向佩罗尔讨主意的念头，便凭记忆把那一段默写了下来。

===Original===
The rover, screwing his eyes and pursing his lips, had come to the end of it. The lieutenant extended his hand negligently and took the paper away: “Well, what do you think?” he asked. “You understand that there can be no question of any ship of war being sacrificed to that dodge. What do you think of it?”

“Easier said than done,” opined Peyrol curtly.

“That’s what I told my admiral.”

“Is he a lubber, so that you had to explain it to him?”

“No, gunner, he is not. He listened to me, nodding his head.”

“And what did he say when you finished?”

“He said: ‘*Parfaitement.* Have you got any ideas about it?’ And I said—listen to me, gunner—I said: ‘*Oui, mon Amiral*, I think I’ve got a man,’ and the admiral interrupted me at once: ‘All right, you don’t want to talk to me about him, I put you in charge of that affair and give you a week to arrange it. When it’s done report to me. Meantime you may just as well take this packet.’ They were already prepared, Peyrol, all those faked letters and dispatches. I carried it out of the admiral’s room, a parcel done up in sailcloth, properly corded and sealed. I have had it in my possession for three days. It’s upstairs in my valise.”

===Chinese===
漫游者眯起眼、抿着嘴，读到了末尾。中尉漫不经心地伸出手，把纸收了回去：“怎么样，你怎么看？”他问，“你该明白，绝谈不上拿哪一条军舰去为这个法子作牺牲。你觉得如何？”

“说来容易做来难。”佩罗尔简短地评了一句。

“我也是这么对我的长官说的。”

“他难道是个旱鸭子（lubber），还要你向他解释这一层？”

“不，炮长，他不是。他听我说着，直点头。”

“你说完了他怎么讲？”

“他说：‘*Parfaitement.*（一点不错。）你可有什么主意？’于是我说——你听我说，炮长——我说：‘*Oui, mon Amiral*（是，将军），我想我找到了一个人。’长官立刻打断了我：‘行了，你不必跟我细说他的事。这件事我交给你全权办理，给你一个星期去张罗。办妥了向我复命。这段时间，你不妨把这个包裹也一并带走。’那些伪造的信件和公文，佩罗尔，他们早已备齐了。我从长官的房间里把那个包裹捧了出来，用帆布包着，捆扎停当，加了火漆印。这东西在我手上已经三天了。就在楼上我的旅行袋里。”

===Original===
“That doesn’t advance you very much,” growled old Peyrol.

“No,” admitted the lieutenant. “I can also dispose of a few thousand francs.”

“Francs,” repeated Peyrol. “Well, you had better get back to Toulon and try to bribe some man to put his head into the jaws of the English lion.”

===Chinese===
“这也没有让你往前挪多少啊。”老佩罗尔咕哝道。

“是没有。”中尉承认道，“我手里还能调拨几千法郎。”

“法郎。”佩罗尔重复道，“好吧，你最好还是回土伦去，试着收买个人，让他把脑袋伸进英国狮子的嘴里去。”

===Original===
Réal reflected, then said slowly, “I wouldn’t tell any man that. Of course a service of danger, that would be understood.”

“It would be. And if you could get a fellow with some sense in his *caboche*, he would naturally try to slip past the English fleet and maybe do it, too. And then where’s your trick?”

“We could give him a course to steer.”

“Yes. And it may happen that your course would just take him clear of all Nelson’s fleet, for you never can tell what the English are doing. They might be watering in Sardinia.”

“Some cruisers are sure to be out and pick him up.”

“Maybe. But that’s not doing the job, that’s taking a chance. Do you think you are talking to a toothless baby—or what?”

“No, my gunner. It will take a strong man’s teeth to undo that knot.” A moment of silence followed. Then Peyrol assumed a dogmatic tone.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔思忖了一阵，缓缓说道：“我不会这么跟任何人挑明。当然，这是一桩有性命危险的差事，这层意思是不言自明的。”

“那是。可你要是能弄到一个脑瓜子里还有点数的人，他自然而然会想方设法溜过英国舰队，说不定还真叫他溜过去了。到那时候，你的套子又套在哪儿了？”

“我们可以给他指定一条航线。”

“是啊。可没准儿你给的那条航线，正好让他从纳尔逊的整支舰队旁边擦身而过、毫发无伤——因为你永远吃不准英国人在干什么。说不定他们正停在撒丁岛（Sardinia）上补水呢。”

“总会有几艘巡洋舰在海上巡逻，会把他拿下的。”

“也许吧。可那不是在办事，那是在碰运气。你以为你在跟一个没牙的奶娃娃说话呢——还是怎么的？”

“不是，我的炮长。要解开这个结，非得有一副硬汉的好牙口不可。”随后是一阵沉默。佩罗尔换上了一副说教的口吻。

===Original===
“I will tell you what it is, lieutenant. This seems to me just the sort of order that a landlubber would give to good seamen. You daren’t deny that.”

“I don’t deny it,” the lieutenant admitted. “And look at the whole difficulty. For supposing even that the tartane blunders right into the English fleet, as if it had been indeed arranged, they would just look into her hold or perhaps poke their noses here and there, but it would never occur to them to search for dispatches, would it? Our man, of course, would have them well hidden, wouldn’t he? He is not to know. And if he were ass enough to leave them lying about the decks, the English would at once smell a rat there. But what I think he would do would be to throw the dispatches overboard.”

“Yes—unless he is told the nature of the job,” said Peyrol.

“Evidently. But where’s the bribe big enough to induce a man to taste of the English pontoons?”

===Chinese===
“我告诉你这是怎么回事，中尉。在我看来，这恰恰是那种旱鸭子给一帮好水手下的命令。这一点你不敢否认吧。”

“我不否认。”中尉承认道，“你且看看整桩的难处吧。因为，就算那艘塔尔塔纳当真稀里糊涂地一头撞进了英国舰队——仿佛真像是事先安排好了似的——他们至多不过朝她货舱里瞅一眼，也许东嗅嗅西探探，可断不会想到要搜什么公文，对不对？我们那个人，当然会把文件藏得严严实实的，是不是？他是不知道内情的。可他要是蠢到把文件随手丢在甲板上，英国人马上就会嗅出味儿来。不过我想，他真正会干的，倒是把公文扔到海里去。”

“是啊——除非事先把这件事的底细告诉他。”佩罗尔说。

“那是自然。可上哪儿去找一笔大到足以叫人甘愿去尝一尝英国囚船（pontoons）滋味的好处呢？”

===Original===
“The man will take the bribe all right and then will do his best not to be caught; and if he can’t avoid that, he will take jolly good care that the English should find nothing on board his tartane. Oh no, lieutenant, any damn scallywag that owns a tartane will take a couple of thousand francs from your hand as tame as can be; but as to deceiving the English Admiral, it’s the very devil of an affair. Didn’t you think of all that before you spoke to the big epaulettes that gave you the job?”

“I did see it, and I put it all before him,” the lieutenant said, lowering his voice still more, for their conversation had been carried on in undertones though the house behind them was silent and solitude reigned round the approaches of Escampobar Farm. It was the hour of siesta—for those that could sleep. The lieutenant, edging closer towards the old man, almost breathed the words in his ear.

===Chinese===
“那人是会照收好处的，收了之后就拼命不让自己被逮着；万一躲不过，他也会千方百计让英国人在他那艘塔尔塔纳上一无所获。哦不，中尉，随便哪个拥有塔尔塔纳的混账无赖，都会乖乖地从你手里接下两三千法郎；可说到骗过英国海军上将，这事简直难于上青天。你去找那些肩章辉煌、把这差事派给你的大人物之前，难道就没想过这些吗？”

“我想到了，也一五一十都摆在他面前了。”中尉说，声音压得更低了——他们的对话本就是压着嗓子进行的，尽管身后的屋子悄无声息，埃斯坎波巴农庄四周也是一片阒寂。这正是午睡的时辰——对于那些睡得着的人而言。中尉朝老头儿挪得更近些，几乎是把话直接吹进了对方耳朵里。

===Original===
“What I wanted was to hear you say all those things. Do you understand now what I meant this morning on the lookout? Don’t you remember what I said?”

Peyrol, gazing into space, spoke in a level murmur.

“I remember a naval officer trying to shake old Peyrol off his feet and not managing to do it. I may be *disparu* but I am too solid yet for any *blancbec* that loses his temper, devil only knows why. And it’s a good thing that you didn’t manage it, else I would have taken you down with me, and we would have made our last somersault together for the amusement of an English ship’s company. A pretty end that!”

===Chinese===
“我想要的，正是听你亲口把这些都说出来。你现在明白我今天早上在瞭望处那番话的意思了吧？你不记得我说过什么了？”

佩罗尔凝望着虚空，用一种平平的低语说道：

“我记得的是一个海军军官想要把老佩罗尔摇下脚去，结果没摇成。我也许已经*disparu*了，可对于哪个不知为什么就发了脾气的*blancbec*（黄毛小子）来说，我还硬朗得很呢。幸亏你没摇成，不然我就拉着你一块儿下去，咱俩一道翻最后一个筋斗，好给英国船上的水手们逗个乐子。那才叫个好收场！”

===Original===
“Don’t you remember me saying, when you mentioned that the English would have sent a boat to go through our pockets, that this would have been the perfect way?” In his stony immobility, with the other man leaning towards his ear, Peyrol seemed a mere insensible receptacle for whispers, and the lieutenant went on forcibly: “Well, it was in allusion to this affair, for, look here, gunner, what could be more convincing, if they had found the packet of dispatches on me! What would have been their surprise, their wonder! Not the slightest doubt could enter their heads. Could it, gunner? Of course it couldn’t. I can imagine the captain of that corvette crowding sail on her to get this packet into the admiral’s hands. The secret of the Toulon fleet’s destination found on the body of a dead officer. Wouldn’t they have exulted at their enormous piece of luck! But they wouldn’t have called it accidental. Oh no! They would have called it providential. I know the English a little too. They like to have God on their side—the only ally they never need pay a subsidy to. Come, gunner, would it not have been a perfect way?”

===Chinese===
“你总记得吧，当初你说英国人多半会派一只小艇来搜我们的口袋，我那时就说，那才是最十全十美的法子？”佩罗尔在岩石般的纹丝不动之中，另一个人凑在他耳边，他仿佛成了一只全无知觉的、专门接收耳语的器皿；中尉用力地接着说下去：“嗯，那话指的就是这桩事，因为，你瞧，炮长，要是他们在我的尸体上搜到了那一扎公文，还有什么比这更叫人信服的呢！他们会何等惊奇，何等震骇！他们脑子里绝不会生出半点疑心。会吗，炮长？当然不会。我都能想见那艘轻巡洋舰的舰长张满全帆、全速赶路，好把这一扎公文送到上将手里的样子。土伦舰队目的地的秘密，竟然在一个阵亡军官的尸体上被发现了。他们岂不是要为自己的鸿运当头而欢欣鼓舞！可他们不会把这叫作碰巧。哦不！他们会把这叫作天意。我对英国人也算是略有了解的。他们喜欢让上帝站在自己那一边——那是他们唯一用不着付津贴的盟友。你说，炮长，那岂不是最十全十美的法子？”

===Original===
Lieutenant Réal threw himself back, and Peyrol, still like a carven image of grim dreaminess, growled softly:

“Time yet. The English ship is still in the Passe.” He waited a little in his uncanny living-statue manner before he added viciously: “You don’t seem in a hurry to go and take that leap.”

“Upon my word, I am almost sick enough of life to do it,” the lieutenant said in a conversational tone.

“Well, don’t forget to run upstairs and take that packet with you before you go,” said Peyrol as before. “But don’t wait for me, I am not sick of life. I am *disparu*, and that’s good enough. There’s no need for me to die.”

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉往凳背上一仰，佩罗尔却仍如一尊阴沉出神的雕像，轻声咕哝道：

“还来得及。那条英国船还在帕斯水道里呢。”他以那种叫人发毛的活雕像姿态又等了一阵，才恶狠狠地补了一句：“瞧你这模样，倒不像急着要去纵身一跳啊。”

“说实在的，我都对活着厌倦到几乎要照办的地步了。”中尉用闲谈般的口吻说道。

“好吧，那你走之前可别忘了上楼去把那个包裹带上。”佩罗尔照旧那样说道，“不过你不必等我，我对活着可没厌倦。我已经*disparu*了，这也就够了。我没必要去死。”

===Original===
And at last he moved in his seat, swung his head from side to side as if to make sure that his neck had not been turned to stone, emitted a short laugh, and grumbled: “*Disparu! Hein!* Well, I am damned!” as if the word “vanished” had been a gross insult to enter against a man’s name in a register. It seemed to rankle, as Lieutenant Réal observed with some surprise; or else it was something inarticulate that rankled, manifesting itself in that funny way. The lieutenant, too, had a moment of anger which flamed and went out at once in the deadly cold philosophic reflection: “We are victims of the destiny which has brought us together.” Then again his resentment flamed. Why should he have stumbled against that girl or that woman, he didn’t know how he must think of her, and suffer so horribly for it? He who had endeavoured almost from a boy to destroy all the softer feelings within himself. His changing moods of distaste, of wonder at himself and at the unexpected turns of life, wore the aspect of profound abstraction, from which he was recalled by an outburst of Peyrol’s, not loud but fierce enough.

“No,” cried Peyrol, “I am too old to break my bones for the sake of a lubberly soldier in Paris who fancies he has invented something clever.”

===Chinese===
终于，他在座位上动了动，把脑袋左右晃了几晃，仿佛要确认自己的脖子还没有变成石头，接着短促地笑了一声，嘟囔道：“*Disparu! Hein!*（失踪！嘿！）好嘛，活见鬼了！”那口气，倒好像“失踪”这个词是一桩记在某人名下的奇耻大辱似的。这似乎是根刺，扎在心里拔不出来——雷亚尔中尉不免有些诧异地注意到了；又或许，扎着的本是一种说不出口的东西，借这古怪的方式发作出来罢了。中尉自己也有过一瞬的怒火，可那火刚窜起来便灭了，熄灭在一种冰冷彻骨的哲思之中：“我们都是那把我们凑到一起的命运的牺牲品。”随即怨愤之火又蹿了上来。他为什么偏偏会撞上那个姑娘——或者说那个女人，他自己都不知道该怎么想她——并为此苦成这副模样？他可是一个几乎从少年时代起便着力摧毁自身一切柔情的人啊。他那变幻不定的心绪——厌恶、对自己的惊异、对人生出人意料的转折的惊异——带着一种深沉出神的神情，直到佩罗尔的一声爆发把他从中唤回：那声音不高，却够凶的。

“不，”佩罗尔嚷道，“我太老了，犯不着为了巴黎那个自以为想出了什么聪明主意的笨手笨脚的军人，去摔断我这把老骨头。”

===Original===
“I don’t ask you to,” the lieutenant said, with extreme severity, in what Peyrol would call an epaulette-wearer’s voice. “You old sea-bandit. And it wouldn’t be for the sake of a soldier anyhow. You and I are Frenchmen after all.”

“You have discovered that, have you?”

“Yes,” said Réal. “This morning, listening to your talk on the hillside with that English corvette within one might say a stone’s throw.”

“Yes,” groaned Peyrol. “A French-built ship!” He struck his breast a resounding blow. “It hurts one there to see her. It seemed to me I could jump down on her deck single-handed.”

===Chinese===
“我也没要你去。”中尉说，语气极其严厉，用的是佩罗尔所谓的“佩肩章者的嗓音”。“你这个老海盗。况且那也不是为了哪个军人。你和我，说到底是法兰西人。”

“这你才看出来，是吗？”

“是的。”雷亚尔说，“今天早上在山坡上听你说话，那艘英国轻巡洋舰就在一箭之遥的地方。”

“是啊。”佩罗尔沉痛地哼了一声，“一艘法兰西造的船！”他砰地一拳捶在自己胸口上，捶得山响。“瞧着她，这儿就疼。我当时觉得自己一个人就能跳到她甲板上去。”

===Original===
“Yes, there you and I understood each other,” said the lieutenant. “But look here, this affair is a much bigger thing than getting back a captured corvette. In reality it is much more than merely playing a trick on an admiral. It’s a part of a deep plan, Peyrol! It’s another stroke to help us on the way towards a great victory at sea.”

“Us!” said Peyrol. “I am a sea-bandit and you are a sea-officer. What do you mean by us?”

“I mean all Frenchmen,” said the lieutenant. “Or, let us say simply France, which you too have served.”

===Chinese===
“是的，在这一层上，你和我彼此是心照不宣的。”中尉说，“可是你瞧，这桩事可比夺回一艘被掳的轻巡洋舰要大得多。实际上，它远不只是捉弄一位海军上将。这是一盘大棋里的一着，佩罗尔！是助我们朝着一场海上大胜再进一步的又一击。”

“我们！”佩罗尔说，“我是个海盗，你是个海军军官。你说的‘我们’是什么意思？”

“我指的是全体法兰西人。”中尉说，“或者干脆就说——法兰西。你也曾为之效力过的那个法兰西。”

===Original===
Peyrol, whose stone-effigy bearing had become humanized almost against his will, gave an appreciative nod and said: “You’ve got something in your mind. Now what is it? If you will trust a sea-bandit.”

“No, I will trust a gunner of the Republic. It occurred to me that for this great affair we could make use of this corvette that you have been observing so long. For to count on the capture of any old tartane by the fleet in a way that would not arouse suspicion is no use.”

“A lubberly notion,” assented Peyrol, with more heartiness than he had ever displayed towards Lieutenant Réal.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔那一副石像般的仪态，几乎是不由自主地染上了几分人情味，他赞许地点了点头，说道：“你心里是有盘算了。是什么？要是你信得过一个海盗的话。”

“不，我信得过的是一位共和国的炮长。我想到的是，为这桩大事，我们不妨利用一下这艘你观察了这么久的轻巡洋舰。因为，指望舰队去拿捕哪条破塔尔塔纳、而且拿捕得不露破绽，那是没有用的。”

“倒是个笨主意。”佩罗尔赞同道，热诚的程度是他对待雷亚尔中尉前所未有的。

===Original===
“Yes, but there’s that corvette. Couldn’t something be arranged to make them swallow the whole thing, somehow, some way. You laugh.… Why?”

“I laugh because it would be a great joke,” said Peyrol, whose hilarity was very short-lived. “That fellow on board, he thinks himself very clever. I never set my eyes on him, but I used to feel that I knew him as if he were my own brother; but now.…”.

He stopped short. Lieutenant Réal, after observing the sudden change on his countenance, said in an impressive manner:

“I think you have just had an idea.”

“Not the slightest,” said Peyrol, turning suddenly into stone, as if by enchantment. The lieutenant did not feel discouraged, and he was not surprised to hear the effigy of Peyrol pronounce: “All the same one could see.” Then very abruptly: “You meant to stay here tonight?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，可有那艘轻巡洋舰在。难道不能想个什么法子，变着法儿地让他们把整件事囫囵吞下去吗？你笑了……笑什么？”

“我笑，是因为那会是一桩天大的笑话。”佩罗尔说，他的兴致来得快去得也快。“船上那个家伙自以为精明得很。我从来没有亲眼见过他，可从前我总觉得我了解他，就像了解我亲兄弟一样；可是现在……”

他戛然而止。雷亚尔中尉留意到他脸上神色的骤变，以一种郑重其事的方式说道：

“我想，你刚才想到了一个主意。”

“丝毫没有。”佩罗尔说着，仿佛中了魔法一般，骤然又化作了石头。中尉并不气馁，听见佩罗尔那尊雕像吐出一句“不管怎样，也能瞧出些名堂”，也不觉得意外。紧接着便是一句突如其来的话：“你本来打算今晚留宿的？”

===Original===
“Yes. I will only go down to Madrague and leave word with the sailing barge which was to come today from Toulon to go back without me.”

“No, lieutenant. You must return to Toulon today. When you get there you must turn out some of those damned quill-drivers at the Port Office if it were midnight and have papers made out for a tartane—oh, any name you like. Some sort of papers. And then you must come back as soon as you can. Why not go down to Madrague now and see whether the barge isn’t already there? If she is, then by starting at once you may get back here some time about midnight.”

===Chinese===
“是的。我只消下到马德拉格去一趟，跟今天要从土伦开来的那条帆驳船留个话，让它不必等我、自行回去。”

“不成，中尉。你今天必须回土伦去。到了那儿，哪怕是半夜，你也得把港口办事处那帮该死的摇笔杆子的（quill-drivers）从被窝里拖出来，让他们给一条塔尔塔纳办齐文书——哦，叫什么名字随你的便。总之弄出那么一套文书来。然后你必须尽快赶回来。你何不现在就下到马德拉格去看看那条驳船到了没有？要是到了，你马上动身，大约半夜前后就能赶回这儿了。”

===Original===
He got up impetuously, and the lieutenant stood up too. Hesitation was imprinted on his whole attitude. Peyrol’s aspect was not animated, but his Roman face with its severe aspect gave him a great air of authority.

“Won’t you tell me something more,” asked the lieutenant.

“No,” said the rover. “Not till we meet again. If you return during the night don’t you try to get into the house. Wait outside. Don’t rouse anybody. I will be about, and if there is anything to say I will say it to you then. What are you looking about you for? You don’t want to go up for your valise. Your pistols up in your room too? What do you want with pistols, only to go to Toulon and back with a naval boat’s crew?” He actually laid his hand on the lieutenant’s shoulder and impelled him gently towards the track leading to Madrague. Réal turned his head at the touch and their eyes met with the strained closeness of a wrestler’s hug. It was the lieutenant who gave way before the unflinchingly direct stare of the old Brother of the Coast. He gave way under the cover of a sarcastic smile and a very airy “I see you want me out of the way for some reason or other,” which produced not the slightest effect upon Peyrol, who stood with his arm pointing towards Madrague. When the lieutenant turned his back on him Peyrol’s pointing arm fell down by his side; but he watched the lieutenant out of sight before he turned too and moved in a contrary direction.

===Chinese===
他蓦地站起身来，中尉也跟着站起。他整个人的姿态都印着犹豫二字。佩罗尔的神情并不热烈，可他那张轮廓严峻的罗马式脸庞，赋予他一种极大的威严气度。

“你不能再多告诉我一点吗？”中尉问。

“不能。”漫游者说，“等咱们再见的时候再说吧。你要是夜里赶回来，千万别想进屋。就在外头等着。别惊动任何人。我会在附近的，要是有什么要说的，到时候我自会跟你说。你东张西望什么？你不必上楼去拿你的旅行袋了。你房间里的手枪也搁在那儿吧？不过是带着一队海军船员去土伦跑个来回，你要手枪做什么？”他竟伸出手按在中尉肩上，轻轻地把他往通往马德拉格的那条小道上一推。雷亚尔被这一碰触引得回过头来，两人的目光相接，带着摔跤手紧抱在一起时那种紧绷的贴近。最终是中尉先在这位老海岸兄弟会会员那毫不回避的直视之下让了步。他是借着一丝讥诮的微笑和一句轻飘飘的“我看你是想找别的是什么缘由把我支开吧”来让步的——这话对佩罗尔没有产生丝毫效用，佩罗尔只管伸着胳膊指向马德拉格的方向。待到中尉转过身去背对着他，佩罗尔那只指引的手才垂回身侧；可他目送中尉走得看不见了，才也转过身，朝相反的方向走去。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
On losing sight of the perplexed lieutenant, Peyrol discovered that his own mind was a perfect blank. He started to get down to his tartane after one sidelong look at the face of the house which contained quite a different problem. Let that wait. His head feeling strangely empty, he felt the pressing necessity of furnishing it with some thought without loss of time. He scrambled down steep places, caught at bushes, stepped from stone to stone, with the assurance of long practice, with mechanical precision and without for a moment relaxing his efforts to capture some definite scheme which he could put into his head. To his right the cove lay full of pale light, while the rest of the Mediterranean extended beyond it in a dark, unruffled blue. Peyrol was making for the little basin where his tartane had been hidden for years, like a jewel in a casket meant only for the secret rejoicing of his eye, of no more practical use than a miser’s hoard﻿—and as precious! Coming upon a hollow in the ground where grew a few bushes and even a few blades of grass, Peyrol sat down to rest. In that position his visible world was limited to a stony slope, a few boulders, the bush against which he leaned and the vista of a piece of empty sea-horizon. He perceived that he detested that lieutenant much more when he didn’t see him. There was something in the fellow. Well, at any rate he had got rid of him for, say, eight or ten hours. An uneasiness came over the old rover, a sense of the endangered stability of things, which was anything but welcome. He wondered at it, and the thought “I am growing old” intruded on him again. And yet he was aware of his sturdy body. He could still creep stealthily like an Indian and with his trusty cudgel knock a man over with a certain aim at the back of his head, and with force enough to fell him like a bullock. He had done that thing no further back than two o’clock the night before, not twelve hours ago, as easy as easy and without an undue sense of exertion. This fact cheered him up. But still he could not find an idea for his head. Not what one could call a real idea. It wouldn’t come. It was no use sitting there.

===Chinese===
那神情迷惑的中尉一消失在视线之外，佩罗尔便发觉自己的脑子里竟是一片彻底的空白。他朝那座屋子的正面斜睨了一眼——那里面藏着的是一个截然不同的问题——便动身朝他的塔尔塔纳走下去。那件事且搁一搁。他的脑袋有一种异样的空荡感，他迫切地觉得非得赶紧给它装进点念头不可。他在陡峭处攀爬而下，一把把抓住灌木，从一块石头跨到另一块石头，带着多年练就的那份笃定，带着机械般的精准，一刻也不曾放松地使劲去抓住某个能塞进脑子里去的明确方案。在他右边，那座小海湾里盈满了苍淡的光，而地中海其余的部分则在它之外铺展成一片幽暗、不兴微澜的湛蓝。佩罗尔正朝着那个小水池走去——他的塔尔塔纳已在那儿藏匿了多年，像一只锦匣里的珠宝，只为着他独自暗中赏玩，其实用处还比不上守财奴的私藏——却同样贵重！走到地上一处洼地，那里竟生着几丛灌木，甚至还长着几茎青草，佩罗尔便坐下来歇口气。以那个姿势坐着，他那看得见的世界只局限在一片石坡、几块巨砾、他倚靠着的那丛灌木，以及一截空荡荡的海天一线的远景。他觉察到，自己不见着那个中尉的时候反倒更厌恶他。那家伙身上是有那么点东西的。也罢，无论如何他总算把他打发走了，比方说，八九个钟头之内是不会再见了。一阵不安袭上这老漫游者的心头，一种万事万物都摇摇欲坠的感觉，而这绝不是什么受欢迎的感受。他对之纳罕起来，“我到底是老了”这个念头又一次钻进了他的脑海。然而他也清楚自己这副结实的身板。他还能像个印第安人那样悄没声息地匍匐潜行，用他那根靠得住的木棍，瞄准一个人的后脑勺，一击便足以叫他像头小公牛似的轰然倒地。这件事他不过就在前一天夜里两点钟干过，满打满算不到十二个钟头之前，轻巧得不能再轻巧，丝毫没有过度费力的感觉。这一桩事实叫他振作了些。可他脑子里仍旧想不出一个主意来。算不上一个能称得上真正主意的念头。它就是不肯来。再这么坐着也是白搭。

===Original===
He got up and after a few strides came to a stony ridge from which he could see the two white blunt mastheads of his tartane. Her hull was hidden from him by the formation of the shore, in which the most prominent feature was a big flat piece of rock. That was the spot on which not twelve hours before Peyrol, unable to rest in his bed and coming to seek sleep in his tartane, had seen by moonlight a man standing above his vessel and looking down at her, a characteristic forked black shape that certainly had no business to be there. Peyrol, by a sudden and logical deduction, had said to himself: “Landed from an English boat.” Why, how, wherefore, he did not stay to consider. He acted at once like a man accustomed for many years to meet emergencies of the most unexpected kind. The dark figure, lost in a sort of attentive amazement, heard nothing, suspected nothing. The impact of the thick end of the cudgel came down on its head like a thunderbolt from the blue. The sides of the little basin echoed the crash. But he could not have heard it. The force of the blow flung the senseless body over the edge of the flat rock and down headlong into the open hold of the tartane, which received it with the sound of a muffled drum. Peyrol could not have done the job better at the age of twenty. No. Not so well. There was swiftness, mature judgment﻿—and the sound of the muffled drum was followed by a perfect silence, without a sigh, without a moan. Peyrol ran round a little promontory to where the shore shelved down to the level of the tartane’s rail and got on board. And still the silence remained perfect in the cold moonlight and amongst the deep shadows of the rocks. It remained perfect because Michel, who always slept under the half-deck forward, being woken by the thump which had made the whole tartane tremble, had lost the power of speech. With his head just protruding from under the half-deck, arrested on all fours and shivering violently like a dog that had been washed with hot water, he was kept from advancing further by his terror of this bewitched corpse that had come on board flying through the air. He would not have touched it for anything.

===Chinese===
他站起身，几步跨过，便来到一道石脊上，从那儿能望见他那条塔尔塔纳的两根白生生、钝秃秃的桅杆头。她的船身被海岸的地势遮住了，那地势中最显眼的是一大块平平的岩石。就在不到十二个钟头之前，正是那块地方——佩罗尔在床上辗转难眠，下到塔尔塔纳上来寻个睡意的时候——借着月光看见一个人站在他的船上方，俯身朝下打量着她，一个分明的、叉开两腿的黑色人形，在那儿显然是毫无道理的。佩罗尔凭着一种突然而又合乎逻辑的推断，在心里对自己说道：“从一条英国人的船上登陆过来的。”为什么、怎么样、究竟为的什么，他都没停下来细想。他当即像惯于应付最意想不到的变故的人那样行动起来。那个黑色的身影，正迷失在一种专注的惊奇之中，什么也没听见，什么也没起疑。木棍粗的那一头，像晴天里一声霹雳似的砸在了它的脑袋上。小水池四壁的岩壁回荡着那一声闷响。可他是听不见的了。那一击的力道把那失去知觉的躯体抛出了平岩的边沿，一头栽进了塔尔塔纳敞开的船舱里，船舱接住他时发出闷鼓似的一声。即便在二十岁上，佩罗尔也不能把这活儿干得更漂亮了。不。还不如这般利落。这里头有迅捷，有成熟的判断力——而那闷鼓似的一声过后，便是一片绝对的寂静，没有一声叹息，没有一声呻吟。佩罗尔绕过一道小小的岬角，跑到海岸倾斜下去、与塔尔塔纳船舷齐平的地方，翻上了船。在清冷的月光下，在岩石深深的阴影里，那寂静依然绝对。它之所以依然绝对，是因为总在船头那半截甲板底下睡觉的米歇尔，被那一下震得整条塔尔塔纳直颤的闷响惊醒之后，已经吓得说不出话来了。他只把脑袋从半甲板底下探出来一点，四肢着地定在那里，像一条被热水烫过的狗那样剧烈地哆嗦着，拦住他再往前挪的，是他对于这具从半空中飞上船来的、中了邪的尸首的恐惧。说什么他也不肯去碰它一下。

===Original===
The “You there, Michel,” pronounced in an undertone, acted like a moral tonic. This then was not the doing of the Evil One; it was no sorcery! And even if it had been, now that Peyrol was there, Michel had lost all fear. He ventured not a single question while he helped Peyrol to turn over the limp body. Its face was covered with blood from the cut on the forehead which it had got by striking the sharp edge of the keelson. What accounted for the head not being completely smashed and for no limbs being broken was the fact that on its way through the air the victim of undue curiosity had come in contact with and had snapped like a carrot one of the foremast shrouds. Raising his eyes casually, Peyrol noticed the broken rope, and at once put his hand on the man’s breast.

“His heart beats yet,” he murmured. “Go and light the cabin lamp, Michel.”

“You going to take that thing into the cabin?”

“Yes,” said Peyrol. “The cabin is used to that kind of thing,” and suddenly he felt very bitter. “It has been a deathtrap for better people than this fellow, whoever he is.”

===Chinese===
那一声压低了嗓门说出的“喂，米歇尔，你在那儿吗”，起到了一剂精神强心针的作用。这么说来，这并不是那恶者的作为；这不是什么妖术！退一步说，就算是，如今佩罗尔既已在此，米歇尔也把一切恐惧都丢了个干净。他帮着佩罗尔把那具软绵绵的身子翻过来时，一个字也没敢多问。那张脸满是血，那是额头磕在内龙骨的锋利边缘上划出的口子淌出来的。至于这颗脑袋何以没有完全碎裂、四肢何以没有一根折断，那是因为这个吃了过分好奇之亏的倒霉鬼，在飞过半空时撞上了前桅的一根支索，像拗断一根胡萝卜似的把它拗断了。佩罗尔漫不经心地抬起眼，注意到了那根崩断的绳索，便立刻把手按在了那人的胸口上。

“他的心还在跳呢。”他喃喃道。“去把舱灯点上，米歇尔。”

“你要把这东西弄进舱房里去？”

“对。”佩罗尔说，“这间舱房对这种事儿早就见惯了。”说着他忽然感到一阵深深的苦涩。“它从前做过比这家伙——管他是谁——体面得多的人的坟场呢。”

===Original===
While Michel was away executing that order Peyrol’s eyes roamed all over the shores of the basin, for he could not divest himself of the idea that there must be more Englishmen dodging about. That one of the corvette’s boats was still in the cove he had not the slightest doubt. As to the motive of her coming, it was incomprehensible. Only that senseless form lying at his feet could perhaps have told him: but Peyrol had little hope that it would ever speak again. If his friends started to look for their shipmate there was just a bare chance that they would not discover the existence of the basin. Peyrol stooped and felt the body all over. He found no weapon of any kind on it. There was only a common clasp-knife on a lanyard round its neck.

===Chinese===
趁着米歇尔走开去执行那道命令，佩罗尔的两眼把小水池四周的岸上扫了个遍，因为他怎么也甩不掉一个念头：一定还有别的英国人在这一带鬼鬼祟祟地转悠。那艘轻巡洋舰的小艇还有一艘停在海湾里，这一点他毫不怀疑。至于她此来的目的，却是叫人摸不着头脑。只有躺在他脚边那一具毫无知觉的躯壳，或许能告诉他一点什么：可佩罗尔对它还能不能再说出一句话，几乎不抱什么指望。倘若他的同伙动手寻找他们的伙伴，那么他们不发现这个小水池的存在，倒还有一线微乎其微的可能。佩罗尔弯下腰，在那人浑身上下摸了一遍。他没在他身上找到任何一样武器。脖子上用一根绳子挂着一把普通的折刀，仅此而已。

===Original===
That soul of obedience, Michel, returning from aft, was directed to throw a couple of bucketfuls of salt water upon the bloody head with its face upturned to the moon. The lowering of the body down into the cabin was a matter of some little difficulty. It was heavy. They laid it full length on a locker, and after Michel with a strange tidiness had arranged its arms along its sides it looked incredibly rigid. The dripping head with soaked hair was like the head of a drowned man with a gaping pink gash on the forehead.

===Chinese===
那个以服从为本分的米歇尔从船尾回来，被吩咐去打两桶海水，浇在那颗仰面朝着月亮的血淋淋的脑袋上。把那具身子弄下舱房去，是桩颇费点周折的事。它沉甸甸的。他们把它直挺挺地平放在一张长柜上，等米歇尔以一种古怪的整洁把它的两只胳膊顺在身体两侧摆好之后，它看起来僵硬得难以置信。那颗滴着水的、头发湿透了的脑袋，活像一个溺死之人的头，额头上豁开着一道粉红的口子。

===Original===
“Go on deck to keep a lookout,” said Peyrol. “We may have to fight yet before the night’s out.”

===Chinese===
“上甲板去放哨，”佩罗尔说，“这一夜还没过完，咱们说不定还得打上一仗呢。”

===Original===
After Michel left him Peyrol began by flinging off his jacket and, without a pause, dragging his shirt off over his head. It was a very fine shirt. The Brothers of the Coast in their hours of ease were by no means a ragged crowd, and Peyrol the gunner had preserved a taste for fine linen. He tore the shirt into long strips, sat down on the locker and took the wet head on his knees. He bandaged it with some skill, working as calmly as though he had been practising on a dummy. Then the experienced Peyrol sought the lifeless hand and felt the pulse. The spirit had not fled yet. The rover, stripped to the waist, his powerful arms folded on the grizzled pelt of his bare breast, sat gazing down at the inert face in his lap with the eyes closed peacefully under the white band covering the forehead. He contemplated the heavy jaw combined oddly with a certain roundness of cheek, the noticeably broad nose with a sharp tip and a faint dent across the bridge, either natural or the result of some old injury. A face of brown clay, roughly modelled, with a lot of black eyelashes stuck on the closed lids and looking artificially youthful on that physiognomy forty years old or more. And Peyrol thought of his youth. Not his own youth; that he was never anxious to recapture. It was of that man’s youth that he thought, of how that face had looked twenty years ago. Suddenly he shifted his position, and putting his lips to the ear of that inanimate head, yelled with all the force of his lungs:

===Chinese===
米歇尔走开之后，佩罗尔先把外套一把扯下，跟着毫不耽搁地把衬衣从头上扒了下来。那是一件极讲究的衬衣。海岸兄弟会的弟兄们闲散之时绝非一群衣衫褴褛之辈，炮长佩罗尔本人也一直保留着对上等细麻布的嗜好。他把衬衣撕成一条条长条，在长柜上坐下，把那颗湿漉漉的脑袋搁到自己膝头。他相当有几分手法地给它包扎起来，手底下从容不迫，仿佛是在拿一个假人练手。接着，经验老到的佩罗尔摸到那只毫无生气的手，去搭脉。魂还没走呢。这漫游者光着上身，两条有力的胳膊交叠在裸露胸膛那片花白的胸毛上，坐着俯看膝头那张纹丝不动的脸——那双眼睛在盖住额头的白布底下安详地阖着。他端详着那张沉甸甸的下巴与某种圆润的脸颊奇特地搭配在一起的脸，端详着那只引人注目地宽大、鼻尖却很尖、鼻梁上有一道浅浅的凹痕（也不知是天生的，还是哪一回旧伤留下的）的鼻子。一张棕色黏土般的脸，粗粗地捏就，紧闭的眼睑上却黏着一大把黑睫毛，在这么一张少说也有四十岁的面相上，显得不真实地年轻。于是佩罗尔想起了他的青春。不是他自己的青春；那个他从来也不急于追回。他所想起的，是那个男人的青春，想起二十年前这张脸该是什么模样。忽然他换了个姿势，把嘴唇凑到那颗毫无生气的脑袋的耳边，运足了胸中所有的气力，大吼一声：

===Original===
“Hullo! Hullo! Wake up, shipmate!”

It seemed enough to wake up the dead. A faint “Voilà! Voilà!” was the answer from a distance, and presently Michel put his head into the cabin with an anxious grin and a gleam in the round eyes.

“You called, *maître*?”

“Yes,” said Peyrol. “Come along and help me to shift him.”

“Overboard?” murmured Michel readily.

“No,” said Peyrol, “into that bunk. Steady! Don’t bang his head,” he cried with unexpected tenderness. “Throw a blanket over him. Stay in the cabin and keep his bandages wetted with salt water. I don’t think anybody will trouble you tonight. I am going to the house.”

“The day is not very far off,” remarked Michel.

===Chinese===
“喂！喂！醒醒，老兄！”

这一嗓子仿佛连死人都能吵醒了。远远地传来一声含含糊糊的“*Voilà! Voilà!*（来啦！来啦！）”作为回应，不一会儿米歇尔就把脑袋探进了舱房，脸上挂着一丝焦灼的傻笑，一双圆眼里闪着光。

“你叫我吗，*maître*（老大）？”

“对，”佩罗尔说，“过来搭把手，帮我把这家伙挪一挪。”

“扔到海里去？”米歇尔麻利地低声问。

“不，”佩罗尔说，“挪到那张铺位上去。稳着点！别磕着他的脑袋。”他喊这一句时带着一种出乎意料的温柔。“拿条毯子给他盖上。你就待在舱房里，拿海水把他的绷带淋湿了别叫它干了。我估摸今夜不会有人来扰你。我要上屋里去了。”

“天亮也不远了，”米歇尔说了一句。

===Original===
This was one reason the more why Peyrol was in a hurry to get back to the house and steal up to his room unseen. He drew on his jacket over his bare skin, picked up his cudgel, recommended Michel not to let that strange bird get out of the cabin on any account. As Michel was convinced that the man would never walk again in his life, he received those instructions without particular emotion.

===Chinese===
这一层更叫佩罗尔急着赶回屋里去，好神不知鬼不觉地溜回自己的房间。他把外套套在赤裸的身上，抄起他的木棍，又叮嘱米歇尔无论如何不能让这只怪鸟溜出舱房。米歇尔既已认定那人这辈子再也别想站起来走路了，便不动声色地领受了这道吩咐。

===Original===
The dawn had broken some time before Peyrol, on his way up to Escampobar happened to look round and had the luck to actually see with his own eyes the English man-of-war’s boat pulling out of the cove. This confirmed his surmises but did not enlighten him a bit as to the causes. Puzzled and uneasy, he approached the house through the farmyard. Catherine, always the first up, stood at the open kitchen door. She moved aside and would have let him pass without remark, if Peyrol himself had not asked in a whisper: “Anything new?” She answered him in the same tone: “She has taken to roaming at night.” Peyrol stole silently up to his bedroom, from which he descended an hour later as though he had spent all the night in his bed up there.

===Chinese===
天早已破晓了。佩罗尔在上埃斯坎波巴的路上，偶然回头一望，竟有运气亲眼看见那艘英国军舰的小艇正从海湾里划出去。这证实了他的揣测，可对于其中的缘由却丝毫没能叫他明白。他满心狐疑、惴惴不安地从农庄的院子里走近屋子。总是头一个起床的凯瑟琳，正站在敞开的厨房门口。她侧身让了让，本打算一声不吭地放他过去的，倒是佩罗尔自己压低了声音问了一句：“有什么新动静没有？”她也用同样的声调答他：“她近来夜里又犯了到处游荡的毛病。”佩罗尔悄没声地溜回楼上的卧室，一个钟头之后再下楼来时，那神气仿佛他整夜都睡在那张床上似的。

===Original===
It was this nocturnal adventure which had affected the character of Peyrol’s forenoon talk with the lieutenant. What with one thing and another, he found it very trying. Now that he had got rid of Réal for several hours, the rover had to turn his attention to that other invader of the strained, questionable, and ominous in its origins, peace of the Escampobar Farm. As he sat on the flat rock with his eyes fixed idly on the few drops of blood betraying his last night’s work to the high heaven, and trying to get hold of something definite that he could think about, Peyrol became aware of a faint thundering noise. Faint as it was, it filled the whole basin. He soon guessed its nature, and his face lost its perplexity. He picked up his cudgel, got on his feet briskly, muttering to himself, “He’s anything but dead,” and hurried on board the tartane.

===Chinese===
正是这一桩夜间的奇遇，给佩罗尔上午与中尉的那番谈话定了调子。这事那事加在一处，他只觉得十分难熬。如今他既已把雷亚尔打发走了好几个钟头，这漫游者便不得不把心思转到另一个闯入者身上——那个人闯进了埃斯坎波巴农庄那份本就紧绷、可疑、且来路不祥的安宁。他坐在那块平岩上，两眼无所事事地盯着几滴血迹——那是他昨夜的活计向高天坦白的罪证——一面拼命想抓住点什么实在的东西好让自己去想，这时佩罗尔忽然察觉到一阵隐约的、如雷的闷响。那声音虽轻，却充塞了整个小水池。他很快便猜出了它的来路，脸上的困惑之色随之消失了。他抄起木棍，利索地站起身来，自言自语地嘟囔了一句“他可一点儿也不像要死的样子”，便急忙上了塔尔塔纳。

===Original===
On the afterdeck Michel was keeping a lookout. He had carried out the orders he had received by the well. Besides being secured by the very obvious padlock, the cabin door was shored up by a spar which made it stand as firm as a rock. The thundering noise seemed to issue from its immovable substance magically. It ceased for a moment, and a sort of distracted continuous growling could be heard. Then the thundering began again. Michel reported:

“This is the third time he starts this game.”

“Not much strength in this,” remarked Peyrol gravely.

“That he can do it at all is a miracle,” said Michel, showing a certain excitement. “He stands on the ladder and beats the door with his fists. He is getting better. He began about half an hour after I got back on board. He drummed for a bit and then fell off the ladder. I heard him. I had my ear against the scuttle. He lay there and talked to himself for a long time. Then he went at it again.” Peyrol approached the scuttle while Michel added his opinion: “He will go on like that forever. You can’t stop him.”

“Easy there,” said Peyrol, in a deep authoritative voice. “Time you finish that noise.”

These words brought instantly a deathlike silence. Michel ceased to grin. He wondered at the power of these few words of a foreign language.

===Chinese===
后甲板上，米歇尔正在放哨。他在升降口那儿已经把接到的命令一一照办了。那扇舱门除了用一眼便看得见的挂锁锁牢之外，还用一根小帆桁顶住，叫它稳如磐石。那如雷的闷响仿佛是从它这纹丝不动的躯体里魔法般地发出来的。它停了一小会儿，能听见一种心神错乱似的、绵绵不绝的低吼。接着那闷雷般的响声又起。米歇尔报告道：

“这是他第三回闹这一出了。”

“这里头没什么劲儿。”佩罗尔正色道。

“他能闹得起来简直就是个奇迹，”米歇尔说，显出几分兴奋。“他站到梯子上，拿两只拳头捶门。他好些了。我回船之后大约半个钟头他就开了头。他咚咚地擂了一阵，就从梯子上摔了下来。我听见的。我把耳朵贴在舱口上。他躺在那儿自言自语了好半天。然后他又来劲了。”佩罗尔朝舱口走过去，米歇尔又添上了自己的看法：“他会这样一直闹下去的。你拦不住他。”

“消停点，”佩罗尔用一种低沉而威严的嗓音说道，“该收场了。”

这一句话立刻带来了一阵死一般的寂静。米歇尔不再咧嘴笑了。他纳罕这外国话的几个字竟有这般神力。

===Original===
Peyrol himself smiled faintly. It was ages since he had uttered a sentence of English. He waited complacently until Michel had unbarred and unlocked the door of the cabin. After it was thrown open he boomed out a warning: “Stand clear!” and, turning about, went down with great deliberation, ordering Michel to go forward and keep a lookout.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔自己也不禁微微一笑。他已有不知多少年没说过一句整句的英文了。他怡然自得地等着，直等米歇尔把舱门上的横闩抽了、锁也开了。门一拉开，他便瓮声瓮气地喝了一声“闪开！”，然后转过身，慢条斯理地走下去，一边吩咐米歇尔到船头去放哨。

===Original===
Down there the man with the bandaged head was hanging on to the table and swearing feebly without intermission. Peyrol, after listening for a time with an air of interested recognition, as one would to a tune heard many years ago, stopped it by a deep-voiced:

“That will do.” After a short silence he added: “You look *bien malade, hein*? What you call sick,” in a tone which if not tender was certainly not hostile. “We will remedy that.”

===Chinese===
下面，那个头上缠着绷带的人正扒着桌子，有气无力地一刻不停地骂着脏话。佩罗尔带着一种饶有兴味、似曾相识的神情听了一阵——就像听一支许多年前听过的曲子那样——然后用一声低沉的嗓门止住了它：

“够了。”短短一阵沉默之后，他又补了一句：“你瞧着*bien malade，hein*（病得不轻吧，嗯）？就是你们说的 sick。”说这话的口气纵然算不上温柔，却也绝无敌意。“咱们来补救补救。”

===Original===
“Who are you?” asked the prisoner, looking frightened and throwing his arm up quickly to guard his head against the coming blow. But Peyrol’s uplifted hand fell only on his shoulder in a hearty slap which made him sit down suddenly on a locker in a partly collapsed attitude and unable to speak. But though very much dazed, he was able to watch Peyrol open a cupboard and produce from there a small demijohn and two tin cups. He took heart to say plaintively: “My throat’s like tinder,” and then suspiciously: “Was it you who broke my head?”

“It was me,” admitted Peyrol, sitting down on the opposite side of the table and leaning back to look at his prisoner comfortably.

“What the devil did you do that for?” inquired the other with a sort of faint fierceness which left Peyrol unmoved.

“Because you put your nose where you no business. Understand? I see you there under the moon, *penché*, eating my tartane with your eyes. You never hear me, *hein*?”

“I believe you walked on air. Did you mean to kill me?”

“Yes, in preference to letting you go and make a story of it on board your cursed corvette.”

“Well then, now’s your chance to finish me. I am as weak as a kitten.”

“How did you say that? Kitten? Ha, ha, ha,” laughed Peyrol. “You make a nice *petit chat*.” He seized the demijohn by the neck and filled the mugs. “There,” he went on, pushing one towards the prisoner﻿—it’s good drink﻿—that.”

===Chinese===
“你是谁？”那俘虏问，一脸惊惧，飞快地抬起胳膊护住脑袋，防着接下来的一击。可佩罗尔高高举起的那只手，落下来时只是亲热地在他肩上拍了一掌——这一巴掌拍得他一下子瘫坐在长柜上，半边身子塌了下去，连话也说不出来。不过他虽然晕头转向得厉害，却还能看得见佩罗尔打开一个壁柜，从里头拿出一只小酒坛（demijohn，细颈大肚瓶）和两只白铁杯。他壮起胆子，可怜巴巴地说：“我喉咙干得像火绒。”接着又狐疑地问：“敲破我脑袋的是你吗？”

“是我。”佩罗尔承认道，在桌子对面坐下，往椅背上一靠，舒舒服服地端详起他的俘虏来。

“你到底为的什么要干这种事？”那一个带着一种有气无力的凶狠劲儿追问道，佩罗尔却不为所动。

“因为你把鼻子伸到了不该你伸的地方。懂吗？我瞧见你站在月亮底下，*penché*（倾着身子探着头），用眼睛把我的塔尔塔纳给吃了。你压根儿没听见我过来，*hein*（嗯）？”

“我瞧你简直是在空气中走路。你是不是想要我的命？”

“对，与其放你回去、让你上你那艘该死的轻巡洋舰上去编排一通，倒不如要了你的命。”

“那么好啊，这会儿正是你结果我的好机会。我软得像只小猫咪。”

“你刚才怎么说来着？小猫咪？哈哈哈，”佩罗尔笑了起来。“你可真是一只可爱的*petit chat*（小猫咪）。”他一把攥住小酒坛的脖颈，把两只杯子斟满。“喏，”他把一只推到俘虏跟前，接着说，“这是好东西——喝的。”

===Original===
Symons’ state was as though the blow had robbed him of all power of resistance, of all faculty of surprise and generally of all the means by which a man may assert himself, except bitter resentment. His head was aching, it seemed to him enormous, too heavy for his neck and as if full of hot smoke. He took a drink under Peyrol’s fixed gaze and with uncertain movements put down the mug. He looked drowsy for a moment. Presently a little colour deepened his bronze; he hitched himself up on the locker and said in a strong voice:

“You played a damned dirty trick on me. Call yourself a man, walking on air behind a fellow’s back and felling him like a bullock.”

===Chinese===
西蒙斯这会儿的境地，仿佛那一棍子把他一切抵抗之力、一切惊愕之能、乃至一个人借以立身的种种手段统统剥夺殆尽，只剩下一腔怨愤。他的头阵阵作痛，那脑袋在他感觉中大得不得了，脖子都快撑不住了，里头好像塞满了滚烫的烟雾。他在佩罗尔目不转睛的盯视下喝了一口，动作不稳地把杯子放下。他有一会儿显得昏昏欲睡。不一会儿，一点血色加深了他那张古铜色的脸；他在长柜上挺了挺身子，用粗壮的嗓门说道：

“你对我使的这一手可真够下作的。还自命是个汉子呢——在人背后踩着空气走路，像撂倒一头小公牛似的把人撂倒。”

===Original===
Peyrol nodded calmly and sipped from his mug.

“If I had met you anywhere else but looking at my tartane I would have done nothing to you. I would have permitted you to go back to your boat. Where was your damned boat?”

“How can I tell you? I can’t tell where I am. I’ve never been here before. How long have I been here?”

“Oh, about fourteen hours,” said Peyrol.

“My head feels as if it would fall off if I moved,” grumbled the other.﻿ ﻿… “You are a damned bungler, that’s what you are.”

“What for﻿—bungler?”

“For not finishing me off at once.”

He seized the mug and emptied it down his throat. Peyrol drank too, observing him all the time. He put the mug down with extreme gentleness and said slowly:

“How could I know it was you? I hit hard enough to crack the skull of any other man.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔不慌不忙地点了点头，从自己的杯子里呷了一口。

“我要是在别处碰见你，而不是瞧见你在那儿盯着我的塔尔塔纳，我才不会动你一根毫毛呢。我本会放你回你的小艇去的。你那条该死的小艇在哪儿？”

“我怎么能告诉你？我连自己在哪儿都不知道。我从前根本没到过这地方。我在这儿待了多久了？”

“哦，差不多十四个钟头吧。”佩罗尔说。

“我这脑袋感觉只要一动就要掉下来似的。”那一个嘟囔道。……“你这个该死的笨手笨脚的家伙，就是这么回事。”

“什么——笨手笨脚？”

“因为你没有当场就把我结果了。”

他抓起杯子，一饮而尽。佩罗尔也喝着，眼睛始终盯着他瞧。他极其轻柔地放下杯子，慢吞吞地说道：

“我哪能知道那就是你呢？我下手够重的了，换了随便哪一个别的人，脑袋壳都该裂开了。”

===Original===
“What do you mean? What do you know about my skull? What are you driving at? I don’t know you, you white-headed villain, going about at night knocking people on the head from behind. Did you do for our officer, too?”

“Oh yes! Your officer. What was he up to? What trouble did you people come to make here, anyhow?”

“Do you think they tell a boat’s crew? Go and ask our officer. He went up the gully and our coxswain got the jumps. He says to me: ‘You are lightfooted, Sam,’ says he; ‘you just creep round the head of the cove and see if our boat can be seen across from the other side.’ Well, I couldn’t see anything. That was all right. But I thought I would climb a little higher amongst the rocks.﻿ ﻿…”

He paused drowsily.

“That was a silly thing to do,” remarked Peyrol in an encouraging voice.

===Chinese===
“你这话什么意思？你又知道我这脑袋壳什么啦？你到底想干什么？我不认识你，你这个白头发的恶棍，大半夜的在人背后给人脑袋来一闷棍。我们的长官也是你收拾的吗？”

“哦，对了！你们的长官。他来干什么来着？你们这帮人到底上这儿来捣什么乱来了？”

“你以为他们会把事情告诉一艇的水手吗？你去问我们那位长官好了。他顺着那条山沟上去了，我们那个舵手就犯了怵。他对我说：‘你脚底下轻，萨姆（Sam），’他说，‘你就绕到海湾那头去，悄悄地瞧瞧，咱们这条小艇从对面那边能不能被人瞧见。’唉，我什么也没瞧见。那就没事了。可我想我不妨在乱石堆里再往上爬高一点。……”

他昏昏欲睡地住了嘴。

“这可是件傻事。”佩罗尔用一种怂恿的口气说道。

===Original===
“I would’ve sooner expected to see an elephant inland than a craft lying in a pool that seemed no bigger than my hand. Could not understand how she got there. Couldn’t help going down to find out﻿—and the next thing I knew I was lying on my back with my head tied up, in a bunk in this kennel of a cabin here. Why couldn’t you have given me a hail and engaged me properly, yardarm to yardarm? You would have got me all the same, because all I had in the way of weapons was the clasp-knife which you have looted off me.”

“Up on the shelf there,” said Peyrol, looking round. “No, my friend, I wasn’t going to take the risk of seeing you spread your wings and fly.”

“You need not have been afraid for your tartane. Our boat was after no tartane. We wouldn’t have taken your tartane for a gift. Why, we see them by dozens every day﻿—those tartanes.”

Peyrol filled the two mugs again. “Ah,” he said, “I dare say you see many tartanes, but this one is not like the others. You a sailor﻿—and you couldn’t see that she was something extraordinary.”

“Hellfire and gunpowder!” cried the other. “How can you expect me to have seen anything. I just noticed that her sails were bent before your club hit me on the head.” He raised his hands to his head and groaned. “Oh lord, I feel as though I had been drunk for a month.”

===Chinese===
“我在内陆瞧见一头大象，也比瞧见一条船躺在一个看上去还没我巴掌大的水洼子里来得不那么稀奇。我怎么也想不通她是怎样跑到那儿去的。我忍不住要下去看个究竟——接下来我所知道的事，就是我已经仰面朝天躺着，脑袋给包扎了起来，躺在这间狗窝似的舱房里的一张铺位上。你当时为什么不能吆喝我一声，堂堂正正地、桁端对桁端地跟我正经干一场呢？你照样能拿住我，因为我身上唯一的家伙就只有那把折刀，还被你给搜走了。”

“就在那边的搁板上呢。”佩罗尔说着四下里扫了一眼。“不，我的朋友，我可不想冒那个险，眼睁睁瞧着你张开翅膀飞了。”

“你用不着为你的塔尔塔纳担惊受怕。我们的小艇才不是冲着什么塔尔塔纳来的。白送我们一条塔尔塔纳我们都不要。嗐，这种塔尔塔纳咱们一天能瞧见几十条。”

佩罗尔又把两只杯子斟满。“啊，”他说，“我敢说你们是见得多了，可这一条跟别的不一样。你一个水手——竟看不出她有什么不寻常之处。”

“该死的烈火与火药！”那一个嚷道。“你怎么能指望我瞧见什么。我不过刚瞧见她的帆都已经挂上了，你的棍子就砸上了我的脑袋。”他抬手按住脑袋，呻吟了一声。“老天爷，我感觉自己像足足醉了一个月。”

===Original===
Peyrol’s prisoner did look somewhat as though he had got his head broken in a drunken brawl. But to Peyrol his appearance was not repulsive. The rover preserved a tender memory of his freebooter’s life with its lawless spirit and its spacious scene of action, before the change in the state of affairs in the Indian Ocean, the astounding rumours from the outer world, made him reflect on its precarious character. It was true that he had deserted the French flag when quite a youngster; but at that time that flag was white; and now it was a flag of three colours. He had known the practice of liberty, equality and fraternity as understood in the haunts, open or secret, of the Brotherhood of the Coast. So the change, if one could believe what people talked about, could not be very great. The rover had also his own positive notions as to what these three words were worth. Liberty﻿—to hold your own in the world if you could. Equality﻿—yes! But no body of men ever accomplished anything without a chief. All this was worth what it was worth. He regarded fraternity somewhat differently. Of course brothers would quarrel amongst themselves; it was during a fierce quarrel that flamed up suddenly in a company of Brothers that he had received the most dangerous wound of his life. But for that Peyrol nursed no grudge against anybody. In his view the claim of the Brotherhood was a claim for help against the outside world. And here he was sitting opposite a Brother whose head he had broken on sufficient grounds. There he was across the table looking dishevelled and dazed, uncomprehending and aggrieved, and that head of his proved as hard as ages ago when the nickname of Testa Dura had been given to him by a Brother of Italian origin on some occasion or other, some butting match no doubt; just as he, Peyrol himself, was known for a time on both sides of the Mozambique Channel as Poigne-de-Fer, after an incident when in the presence of the Brothers he played at arm’s length with the windpipe of an obstreperous negro sorcerer with an enormous girth of chest. The villagers brought out food with alacrity, and the sorcerer was never the same man again. It had been a great display.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔的这个俘虏，看上去倒真有点像在一场醉汉斗殴里让人打破了脑袋。但在佩罗尔眼里，他这副模样并不叫人讨厌。这漫游者对早年的海盗生涯——那无法无天的劲头、那辽阔无垠的活动天地——始终保有一段温情的回忆；直到印度洋一带的局势起了变化、直到外面世界传来种种骇人听闻的消息，才使他开始反省那种生活的朝不保夕。不错，他确是在还是个小伙子的时候就开了法国国旗的小差；可在那时候，那面旗还是白色的；而如今它已是一面三色的旗了。对于自由、平等、博爱这三样东西在海岸兄弟会的公开或隐秘的巢穴里是怎样一种实践，他是熟知的。所以，要是人们嘴里传的那些话可以信，那么这变化想必也不至于太大。对于这三个字到底值几个钱，这漫游者自己也有颇为明确的看法。自由——就是在这世上你能立得住脚便立得住脚。平等——不错！可没有哪一伙人没了头领还能办成事的。这一切也就值它所值的那些罢了。对于博爱，他的看法略有不同。兄弟之间当然也会闹别扭；他平生所受最危险的那一处伤，就是在一场于同伙兄弟间陡然燃起的激烈争吵中落下的。可对这一桩，佩罗尔对谁也不记仇。在他看来，兄弟会彼此之间的情分，就是一种在面对外人时彼此援手的情分。而此刻，他就坐在一个被他以充分理由打破了脑袋的兄弟的对面。他就在桌子那头坐着，蓬头垢面、晕头转向，既不明白所以又满腹委屈，而他那一颗脑袋竟和许多年前一般硬——许多年前，一个意大利裔的兄弟在某一个场合之下给他起了 Testa Dura（硬脑袋）这个绰号，那场合想必是一场顶牛比赛吧；一如他佩罗尔自己，曾有一阵子在莫桑比克海峡两岸都以 Poigne-de-Fer（铁拳）之名为人所知，那是因了这么一桩事：有一回，当着众兄弟的面，他隔着一臂的距离，攥着一个蛮横无理、胸膛宽阔得不得了的黑人巫医的喉管，像耍弄似的玩了好一阵子。村民们赶忙把吃食奉上，而那巫医从此再也不是原来那个人了。那可真是一场了不起的炫技。

===Original===
Yes, no doubt it was Testa Dura; the young neophyte of the order (where and how picked up Peyrol never heard), strange to the camp, simple-minded and much impressed by the swaggering cosmopolitan company in which he found himself. He had attached himself to Peyrol in preference to some of his own countrymen, of whom there were several in that band, and used to run after him like a little dog and certainly had acted a good shipmate’s part on the occasion of that wound, which had neither killed nor cowed Peyrol but merely had given him an opportunity to reflect at leisure on the conduct of his own life.

===Chinese===
是的，毫无疑问，这就是 Testa Dura；那个帮会里年轻的新入门弟子（在哪里、又是怎样被收拢进来的，佩罗尔从来没听人说起过），初来营地里，人生地不熟，心思单纯，对他所置身其间的那个神气活现、五方杂处的群体艳羡不已。他宁愿追随佩罗尔，也不追随他自己几个同胞——那一伙人里有好几个他的同胞呢——从前他总是像条小狗似的跟在佩罗尔屁股后头跑，而在佩罗尔挨那处伤的时候，他确实尽了一个好兄弟、好伙伴的本分；那一伤既没有要了佩罗尔的命，也没有叫他吓破了胆，不过是给了他一个机会，让他得以从容地反省自己这一生的所作所为罢了。

===Original===
The first suspicion of that amazing fact had intruded on Peyrol while he was bandaging that head by the light of the smoky lamp. Since the fellow still lived, it was not in Peyrol to finish him off or let him lie unattended like a dog. And then this was a sailor. His being English was no obstacle to the development of Peyrol’s mixed feelings in which hatred certainly had no place. Amongst the members of the Brotherhood it was the Englishmen whom he preferred. He had also found amongst them that particular and loyal appreciation, which a Frenchman of character and ability will receive from Englishmen sooner than from any other nation. Peyrol had at times been a leader, without ever trying for it very much, for he was not ambitious. The lead used to fall to him mostly at a time of crisis of some sort; and when he had got the lead it was on the Englishmen that he used to depend most.

===Chinese===
对这一惊人事实的头一丝疑心，是在佩罗尔借着那盏冒烟的油灯给那颗脑袋包扎的时候，闯进他心里来的。既然这家伙还活着，那么一了百了地结果他、或是像条狗似的任他躺在那儿无人过问，都不是佩罗尔做得出来的事。再者，这是个水手。他是英国人这一层，并不妨碍佩罗尔心里那种种错综的情感滋长起来——在那些情感里，仇恨是断然没有位置的。在兄弟会的众成员当中，他最偏爱的就是英国人。他也曾在他们当中得到过那种特别的、忠实的赏识，而一个有品格、有才干的法国人，从英国人那里得到这种赏识，比从任何别的民族那里都要来得快。佩罗尔有时也会成为一个头领，尽管他从不怎样刻意钻营，因为他本不好名。这个头领的位置多半是在某种危机来临的时候才落到他头上；而一旦他当上了头领，他所最倚重的，往往是那几个英国人。

===Original===
And so that youngster had turned into this English man-of-war’s man! In the fact itself there was nothing impossible. You found Brothers of the Coast in all sorts of ships and in all sorts of places. Peyrol had found one once in a very ancient and hopeless cripple practising the profession of a beggar on the steps of Manila cathedral, and had left him the richer by two broad gold pieces to add to his secret hoard. There was a tale of a Brother of the Coast having become a mandarin in China, and Peyrol believed it. One never knew where and in what position one would find a Brother of the Coast. The wonderful thing was that this one should have come to seek him out, to put himself in the way of his cudgel. Peyrol’s greatest concern had been all through that Sunday morning to conceal the whole adventure from Lieutenant Réal. As against a wearer of epaulettes, mutual protection was the first duty between Brothers of the Coast. The unexpectedness of that claim coming to him after twenty years invested it with an extraordinary strength. What he would do with the fellow he didn’t know. But since that morning the situation had changed. Peyrol had received the lieutenant’s confidence and had got on terms with him in a special way. He fell into profound thought.

===Chinese===
原来那小伙子，竟变成了眼前这个英国军舰上的水手！这件事本身倒没有什么不可能的。你在各式各样的船上、各式各样的地方，都能撞见海岸兄弟会的人。佩罗尔自己就曾在马尼拉大教堂的台阶上，从一个极其老迈、毫无指望的、以乞讨为业的残疾汉身上认出了一个兄弟，临走留下了两枚宽大的金币，让他那秘密的私藏又厚了几分。还有一段传闻，说有一个海岸兄弟会的人在中国当上了大官（mandarin，满大人），佩罗尔对此也信。你永远不知道会在什么地方、以什么身份撞见一个海岸兄弟会的人。真正奇妙的，倒是这一个竟会自己找上门来，一头撞进了他的木棍之下。佩罗尔整个星期天上午最挂心的一件事，就是把这一整桩奇遇瞒住雷亚尔中尉。在面对一个佩带肩章的人时，海岸兄弟会之间彼此回护，乃是头一等的义务。这一份情分，时隔二十年才落到他头上，它的突如其来，赋予了它一种异乎寻常的力量。他该拿这家伙怎么办，他不知道。但自那天上午以来，局面已经变了。佩罗尔已受了中尉的信赖，以一种特殊的方式同他打得火热。他陷入了深深的沉思。

===Original===
“*Sacrée tête dure*,” he muttered without rousing himself. Peyrol was annoyed a little at not having been recognized. He could not conceive how difficult it would have been for Symons to identify this portly deliberate person with a white head of hair as the object of his youthful admiration, the black-ringletted French Brother in the prime of life of whom everybody thought so much. Peyrol was roused by hearing the other declare suddenly:

===Chinese===
“*Sacrée tête dure*（这该死的硬脑袋）。”他没回过神来似的嘟囔了一句。佩罗尔有点恼火自己竟没被认出来。他无从想象，要西蒙斯把眼前这个举止持重、身材发福、一头白发的人，认作他少年时所崇拜的那个满头黑色鬈发、正值盛年、人人敬重的法国兄弟，该是何等之难。佩罗尔被对方忽然冒出的一句话惊回了神：

===Original===
“I am an Englishman, I am. I am not going to knuckle under to anybody. What are you going to do with me?”

“I will do what I please,” said Peyrol, who had been asking himself exactly the same question.

“Well, then, be quick about it, whatever it is. I don’t care a damn what you do, but﻿—be﻿—quick﻿—about﻿—it.”

He tried to be emphatic; but as a matter of fact the last words came out in a faltering tone. And old Peyrol was touched. He thought that if he were to let him drink the mugful standing there, it would make him dead drunk. But he took the risk. So he said only:

“*Allons*﻿—drink!” The other did not wait for a second invitation but could not control very well the movements of his arm extended towards the mug. Peyrol raised his on high.

“*Trinquons, eh?*” he proposed. But in his precarious condition the Englishman remained unforgiving.

“I’m damned if I do,” he said indignantly, but so low that Peyrol had to turn his ear to catch the words. “You will have to explain to me first what you meant by knocking me on the head.”

He drank, staring all the time at Peyrol in a manner which was meant to give offence but which struck Peyrol as so childlike that he burst into a laugh.

“*Sacré imbécile, va!* Did I not tell you it was because of the tartane? If it hadn’t been for the tartane I would have hidden from you. I would have crouched behind a bush like a﻿—what do you call them?﻿—*lièvre*.”

===Chinese===
“我是个英国人，我就是。我决不向任何人服软。你打算拿我怎么样？”

“我爱怎么办就怎么办。”佩罗尔说——他自己刚才也正在问自己这同一个问题。

“那么好吧，管你是什么，你倒是痛快些。我他妈的根本不在乎你怎么着我，可是——你——倒——是——痛——快——些。”

他本想说得更硬气点；可事实上最后那几个字说出口时已是含含糊糊的颤音了。老佩罗尔心里一软。他寻思，要是让他把搁在那儿的那一满杯喝下去，准能把他灌得烂醉如泥。可他还是冒了这个险。于是他只说道：

“*Allons*（来吧）——喝！”那一个也不等第二回邀请，却没能把伸向杯子的那条胳膊控制稳当。佩罗尔把自己那杯高高举起。

“*Trinquons，eh？*（干一杯，嗯？）”他提议道。可这英国人处在那般糟糕的境地，依旧不肯通融。

“我才不干呢。”他愤愤地说，声音却低得佩罗尔不得不侧过耳朵去才听清那几个字。“你得先给我解释清楚，你敲我那一棍子到底是什么意思。”

他喝着，自始至终直瞪瞪地盯着佩罗尔瞧，那副架势原是想冒犯他的，可在佩罗尔看来却孩子气得很，惹得他哈哈大笑起来。

“*Sacré imbécile，va！*（该死的蠢货！）我不是跟你说了吗，那是为的塔尔塔纳？要不是为了塔尔塔纳，我本会躲开你的。我会蜷在一丛灌木后头，像一只——你们管那个叫什么来着？——*lièvre*（野兔）。”

===Original===
The other, who was feeling the effect of the drink, stared with frank incredulity.

“You are of no account,” continued Peyrol. “Ah! if you had been an officer I would have gone for you anywhere. Did you say your officer went up the gully?”

Symons sighed deeply and easily. “That’s the way he went. We had heard on board of a house thereabouts.”

“Oh, he went to the house!” said Peyrol. “Well, if he did get there he must be very sorry for himself. There is half a company of infantry billeted in the farm.”

This inspired fib went down easily with the English sailor. Soldiers were stationed in many parts of the coast as any seaman of the blockading fleet knew very well. To the many expressions which had passed over the face of that man recovering from a long period of unconsciousness there was added the shade of dismay.

“What the devil have they stuck soldiers on this piece of rock for?” he asked.

“Oh, signalling post and things like that. I am not likely to tell you everything. Why! you might escape.”

===Chinese===
那一个正觉着酒劲上来了，坦率地以不信的目光瞪着他。

“你这个人无足轻重。”佩罗尔接着说，“哈！你要是个军官，那我管在哪儿都要冲你下手。你刚才不是说你们那位长官顺着山沟上去了吗？”

西蒙斯舒坦地深深叹了口气。“他是从那条道上去的。我们在船上听说这一带有一户人家。”

“哦，他上那户人家去了！”佩罗尔说，“好啊，他要是当真到了那儿，这会儿一定懊悔得不行。农庄里驻着半个连的步兵呢。”

这个凭空捏出来的瞎话，那英国水手听得毫不费力地就信了。封锁舰队的任何一个水手都清楚，沿岸许多地方都驻着兵。在那张从长久的昏迷中渐渐苏醒的人脸上已经掠过的种种表情之外，此刻又添上了一层沮丧的阴影。

“他们见鬼的在这块石头上驻兵做什么？”他问。

“哦，信号站什么的呗。我总不见得什么都告诉你吧。干吗！说不定你就跑了呢。”

===Original===
That phrase reached the soberest spot in the whole of Symons’ individuality. Things were happening, then. Mr. Bolt was a prisoner. But the main idea evoked in his confused mind was that he would be given up to those soldiers before very long. The prospect of captivity made his heart sink, and he resolved to give as much trouble as he could.

“You will have to get some of these soldiers to carry me up. I won’t walk. I won’t. Not after having had my brains nearly knocked out from behind. I tell you straight! I won’t walk. Not a step. They will have to carry me ashore.”

Peyrol only shook his head deprecatingly.

“Now you go and get a corporal with a file of men,” insisted Symons obstinately. “I want to be made a proper prisoner of. Who the devil are you? You had no right to interfere. I believe you are a civilian. A common *marinero*, whatever you may call yourself. You look to me a pretty fishy *marinero* at that. Where did you learn English? In prison﻿—eh?
