You ain’t going to keep me in this damned dog-hole, on board your rubbishy tartane. Go and get that corporal, I tell you.”

He looked suddenly very tired and only murmured: “I am an Englishman, I am.”

===Chinese===
这一句话触到了西蒙斯整个人身上最清醒的那一处地方。这么说来，事情果真在起变化了。博尔特先生（Mr. Bolt）已经成了俘虏。但他在那糊涂的脑子里所浮现的主要念头却是：用不了多久，他就要被移交给那些当兵的了。一想到要做俘虏，他的心便沉了下去，于是他打定主意，能给他们添多少麻烦就添多少麻烦。

“你得叫几个大兵来把我抬上去。我是不走的。我不走。我让人从后脑勺差点把脑浆都敲了出来，还能走？我直截了当告诉你！我不走。一步也不走。他们要抬我上岸去。”

佩罗尔只是一面摇头，一面表示不以为然。

“这会儿你就去，”西蒙斯固执地坚持道，“去叫一个下士带一队人来。我要求正正经经地被当作俘虏收押起来。你到底算老几？你压根儿没有权力来插手。我看你是个老百姓。不过是个普通的 *marinero*（水手），随你自己怎么叫吧。我看你这个 *marinero* 也水得很呢。你在哪儿学的英国话？在牢里——嗯？你别想把我关在这个见鬼的狗窝里，在你这条破烂的塔尔塔纳上。我叫你去叫那个下士来。”

他忽然显得疲惫不堪，只喃喃地说了一句：“我是个英国人，我就是。”

===Original===
Peyrol’s patience was positively angelic.

“Don’t you talk about the tartane,” he said impressively, making his words as distinct as possible. “I told you she was not like the other tartanes. That is because she is a courier boat. Every time she goes to sea she makes a *pied-de-nez*, what you call thumb to the nose, to all your English cruisers. I do not mind telling you because you are my prisoner. You will soon learn French now.”

“Who are you? The caretaker of this thing or what?” asked the undaunted Symons. But Peyrol’s mysterious silence seemed to intimidate him at last. He became dejected and began to curse in a languid tone all boat expeditions, the coxswain of the gig and his own infernal luck.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔的耐心简直如天使一般。

“你可别说那条塔尔塔纳的闲话，”他郑重其事地说道，把每一个字咬得尽可能清晰。“我跟你说过，她跟别的塔尔塔纳不一样。那是因为她是一条信使船。她每回出海，都要冲着你们英国所有的巡洋舰做一个 *pied-de-nez*——就是你们说的把大拇指按在鼻尖上那种嘲弄的手势。我不妨告诉你，因为你是我的俘虏。这会儿起，你很快就要学会法国话了。”

“你到底是谁？是这玩意儿的看护人，还是怎么的？”毫不气馁的西蒙斯问道。可佩罗尔那讳莫如深的沉默，到末了似乎反倒把他镇住了。他变得垂头丧气起来，开始有气无力地咒骂起一切乘小艇出动的差事，咒骂大舢板上的那个舵手，还有他自己那倒霉透顶的运气。

===Original===
Peyrol sat alert and attentive like a man interested in an experiment, while after a moment Symons’ face began to look as if he had been hit with a club again, but not as hard as before. A film came over his round eyes and the words “fishy *marinero*” made their way out of his lips in a sort of deathbed voice. Yet such was the hardness of his head that he actually rallied enough to address Peyrol in an ingratiating tone.

“Come, grandfather!” He tried to push the mug across the table and upset it. “Come! Let us finish what’s in that tiny bottle of yours.”

“No,” said Peyrol, drawing the demijohn to his side of the table and putting the cork in.

“No?” repeated Symons in an unbelieving voice and looking at the demijohn fixedly﻿ ﻿… “you must be a tinker.”﻿ ﻿… He tried to say something more under Peyrol’s watchful eyes, failed once or twice, and suddenly pronounced the word *cochon* so correctly as to make old Peyrol start. After that it was no use looking at him any more. Peyrol busied himself in locking up the demijohn and the mugs. When he turned round most of the prisoner’s body was extended over the table and no sound came from it, not even a snore.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔端坐着，警觉而专注，像一个对某项实验很感兴趣的人；过了一会儿，西蒙斯的脸开始露出一种仿佛又挨了一闷棍的神情，只不过这一棍没有上一棍那么重罢了。他那一双圆眼蒙上了一层翳，“水得很的 *marinero*”几个字以一种临终似的嗓音从他嘴里挤了出来。然而他那颗脑袋硬到了这步田地，他竟又缓过劲来，用一种讨好巴结的口气对佩罗尔说起话来。

“来吧，老爷爷！”他想把杯子推过桌子，却把它弄翻了。“来吧！咱们把你那小瓶里的玩意儿喝完了它。”

“不。”佩罗尔说着，把小酒坛拽到自己这一边的桌面上，把塞子塞了进去。

“不？”西蒙斯用一种难以置信的声音重复道，直勾勾地盯着那只小酒坛……“那你必定是个小气的补锅匠（tinker）。”……他在佩罗尔那双审视的目光下还想再说点什么，失败了一两回，忽然把 *cochon*（猪）这个词说得字正腔圆，倒叫老佩罗尔吃了一惊。自那以后，再瞧他也无济于事了。佩罗尔自顾把小酒坛和杯子一一收锁起来。等他转过身去，那俘虏大半个身子都已摊在了桌子上，里头没有发出半点声响，连呼噜都不打一声。

===Original===
When Peyrol got outside, pulling to the door of the cuddy behind him, Michel hastened from forward to receive the master’s orders. But Peyrol stood so long on the afterdeck meditating profoundly with his hand over his mouth that Michel became fidgety and ventured a cheerful: “It looks as if he were not going to die.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔出到外头，顺手把身后的舱厢门拉上，米歇尔赶忙从船头跑过来听候主子的吩咐。可佩罗尔却在后甲板上站了老半天，一手捂着嘴，陷入了深深的沉思，弄得米歇尔坐立不安起来，大着胆子欢快地说了一句：“瞧这样子，他像是死不了啦。”

===Original===
“He is dead,” said Peyrol with grim jocularity. “Dead drunk. And you very likely will not see me till tomorrow sometime.”

“But what am I to do?” asked Michel timidly.

“Nothing,” said Peyrol. “Of course you must not let him set fire to the tartane.”

“But suppose,” insisted Michel, “he should give signs of escaping.”

“If you see him trying to escape,” said Peyrol with mock solemnity, “then, Michel, it will be a sign for you to get out of his way as quickly as you can. A man who would try to escape with a head like this on him would just swallow you at one mouthful.”

He picked up his cudgel and, stepping ashore, went off without as much as a look at his faithful henchman. Michel listened to him scrambling amongst the stones, and his habitual amiably vacant face acquired a sort of dignity from the utter and absolute blankness that came over it.

===Chinese===
“他死了，”佩罗尔带着一丝冷峻的打趣说道。“醉死的。你多半要到明天什么时候才能再见到我了。”

“那我可怎么办？”米歇尔怯生生地问。

“什么也不用办。”佩罗尔说，“当然，你可不能让他把塔尔塔纳点着了。”

“可要是，”米歇尔坚持道，“他露出了要逃跑的苗头呢？”

“你要是瞧他想跑，”佩罗尔装出一本正经的样子说道，“那么，米歇尔，那就是叫你赶紧给他让路的信号。顶着一颗这样的脑袋还想跑的人，会一口就把你吞下肚去的。”

他抄起木棍，迈步上岸，连看都没看他那忠实扈从一眼便走开了。米歇尔听着他在乱石堆里磕磕绊绊远去的声音，他那张素来和气而茫然的脸上，因为涌上了一种彻彻底底、干干净净的空白，竟平添了几分庄严。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
It was only after reaching the level ground in front of the farmhouse that Peyrol took time to pause and resume his contact with the exterior world.

===Chinese===
直到走上农舍前那片平整的地面，佩罗尔才得空停下脚步，重新与外面的世界接上联系。

===Original===
While he had been closeted with his prisoner the sky had got covered with a thin layer of cloud, in one of those swift changes of weather that are not unusual in the Mediterranean. This grey vapour, drifting high up, close against the disc of the sun, seemed to enlarge the space behind its veil, add to the vastness of a shadowless world no longer hard and brilliant but all softened in the contours of its masses and in the faint line of the horizon, as if ready to dissolve in the immensity of the infinite.

===Chinese===
在他与那名俘虏闭门密谈的当儿，天空已笼上了一层薄云——地中海一带天气这般骤变，本不算什么稀罕事。这一片灰蒙蒙的水汽在高处飘游，紧贴着日轮，仿佛把面纱背后的天宇也撑开了几分，给那个不再坚硬耀眼、却在一切块块的轮廓上、在那一线隐约的地平线上全都柔和下来的、没有阴影的世界，更添了几分空阔，仿佛随时都要在那无垠的浩渺里消融殆尽。

===Original===
Familiar and indifferent to his eyes, material and shadowy, the extent of the changeable sea had gone pale under the pale sun in a mysterious and emotional response. Mysterious too was the great oval patch of dark water to the west; and also a broad blue lane traced on the dull silver of the waters in a parabolic curve described magistrally by an invisible finger for a symbol of endless wandering. The face of the farmhouse might have been the face of a house from which all the inhabitants had fled suddenly. In the high part of the building the window of the lieutenant’s room remained open, both glass and shutter. By the door of the *salle* the stable fork leaning against the wall seemed to have been forgotten by the sansculotte. This aspect of abandonment struck Peyrol with more force than usual. He had been thinking so hard of all these people, that to find no one about seemed unnatural and even depressing. He had seen many abandoned places in his life, grass huts, mud forts, kings’ palaces—temples from which every white-robed soul had fled. Temples, however, never looked quite empty. The gods clung to their own. Peyrol’s eyes rested on the bench against the wall of the *salle*. In the usual course of things it should have been occupied by the lieutenant, who had the habit of sitting there with hardly a movement, for hours, like a spider watching for the coming of a fly. This paralysing comparison held Peyrol motionless with a twisted mouth and a frown on his brow, before the evoked vision, coloured and precise, of the man, more troubling than the reality had ever been.

===Chinese===
在他眼里既熟悉又漠然、既实在又幽暗的那一片变幻莫测的海面，在那苍白的日头底下也泛作了苍白色，仿佛一种神秘而动情的呼应。西边那一大片椭圆状的深色水域同样神秘；还有一道宽阔的蓝色水径，勾在暗银色的海面上，呈一道抛物线的弧度，仿佛被一根看不见的手指以大家风范描画出来，作为无尽漂泊的象征。农舍的那张脸，活像是所有住户都已仓皇出逃的一幢房子的脸。在房子高处，中尉房间的那扇窗仍旧开着，玻璃窗和百叶窗都大敞着。正厅（*salle*）的门边，那柄靠在墙上的厩叉，像是被那无套裤汉（sansculotte）忘在那儿了。这一派荒弃的景象，比往日更有力地攫住了佩罗尔。他方才满脑子想的尽是这些人，这会儿却连一个人影也瞧不见，显得很不自然，甚至叫人心里发闷。他这一生见过许多被废弃的地方——草棚、土堡、国王的宫殿，还有那一座座连一个披白袍的灵魂也不剩、人人四散奔逃的庙宇。然而庙宇看上去从来不曾真正空荡荡过。神明总是眷恋着属于自己的领地。佩罗尔的目光落到了正厅墙根那条长凳上。照理说，那上头该坐着中尉才是——他有那个习惯，几乎一动不动地在那儿坐上几个钟头，活像一只守候苍蝇上门的蜘蛛。这个叫人瘫痪的比方，把佩罗尔钉在了原地，他嘴角歪扭、眉头紧锁，眼前浮现出那个被召唤来的幻象——色彩鲜明、纤毫毕现，比他亲眼见过的真人还要叫他心绪难宁。

===Original===
He came to himself with a start. What sort of occupation was this, *’cré nom de nom*, staring at a silly bench with no one on it. Was he going wrong in his head? Or was it that he was getting really old? He had noticed old men losing themselves like that. But he had something to do. First of all he had to go and see what the English sloop in the Passe was doing.

===Chinese===
他猛地一激灵，回过神来。*’cré nom de nom*（他娘的），盯着一条空无一人的傻凳子瞧，这算哪门子的营生？是他脑子出了毛病吗？还是说，他真的老了？他曾留意到上了年纪的人就会这般神思恍惚。可他手头还有正经事要办。头一桩，他得去看看帕斯水道（the Passe）里那条英国单桅快速帆船在搞什么名堂。

===Original===
While he was making his way towards the lookout on the hill where the inclined pine hung peering over the cliff as if an insatiable curiosity were holding it in that precarious position, Peyrol had another view from above of the farmyard and of the buildings and was again affected by their deserted appearance. Not a soul, not even an animal seemed to have been left; only on the roofs the pigeons walked with smart elegance. Peyrol hurried on and presently saw the English ship well over on the Porquerolles side with her yards braced up and her head to the southward. There was a little wind in the Passe, while the dull silver of the open had a darkling rim of rippled water far away to the east in that quarter where, far or near, but mostly out of sight, the British fleet kept its endless watch. Not a shadow of a spar or gleam of sail on the horizon betrayed its presence; but Peyrol would not have been surprised to see a crowd of ships surge up, people the horizon with hostile life, come in running, and dot the sea with their ordered groups all about Cape Cicié, parading their damned impudence. Then indeed that corvette, the big factor of everyday life on that stretch of coast, would become very small potatoes indeed; and the man in command of her (he had been Peyrol’s personal adversary in many imaginary encounters fought to a finish in the room upstairs)—then indeed that Englishman would have to mind his steps. He would be ordered to come within hail of the admiral, be sent here and there, made to run like a little dog and as likely as not get called on board the flagship and get a dressing down for something or other.

===Chinese===
他朝山上的瞭望处走去——那儿有一棵倾斜的松树探出崖头窥望，仿佛一股贪得无厌的好奇心把它钉在了那摇摇欲坠的位置上。一路走着，佩罗尔又从高处俯瞰了一回农家庭院和那几栋屋舍，再度被它们那空寂无人的一幕触动了。一个人影也没有，连一头牲口都像是没有留下，只有屋顶上一群鸽子迈着利落优雅的步子走来走去。佩罗尔加快脚步，不一会儿便看见那条英国船已经远远地靠到了波克罗勒岛（Porquerolles）那一侧，帆桁已转正，船头朝南。帕斯水道里还有一点点风，而外海那一片暗银色的水面，远远地在东边镶上了一圈泛着涟漪的幽暗水边——正是在那一带，或远或近、但大半都隐而不见的地方，英国舰队守着它那无休无止的更。地平线上没有一丝帆桁的影子、没有一星帆影的闪光泄露它的踪迹；然而佩罗尔若是看见成群的战舰骤然涌起、把那一片地平线塞满敌意的生机、乘风压来、在西西耶角（Cape Cicié）四面八方撒下一簇簇井然有序的舰群、大肆炫耀他们那该死的放肆，他也不会觉得意外。到那时，那艘轻巡洋舰（corvette）——那段海岸上日常生活的头号角色——可当真要沦为微不足道的小角色了；而指挥她的那个人（在楼上那间屋子里，佩罗尔曾与他无数次地交锋，在想象中一决胜负地打到收场）——到那时，那个英国佬可就得步步留神了。他会被传到那位海军上将跟前听候吩咐，被打发到东到西，像条小狗似的被支使得团团转，十之八九还会被叫上旗舰，为这样那样的事挨一顿臭骂。

===Original===
Peyrol thought for a moment that the impudence of this Englishman was going to take the form of running along the peninsula and looking into the very cove; for the corvette’s head was falling off slowly. A fear for his tartane clutched Peyrol’s heart till he remembered that the Englishman did not know of her existence. Of course not. His cudgel had been absolutely effective in stopping that bit of information. The only Englishman who knew of the existence of the tartane was that fellow with the broken head. Peyrol actually laughed at his momentary scare. Moreover, it was evident that the Englishman did not mean to parade in front of the peninsula. He did not mean to be impudent. The sloop’s yards were swung right round and she came again to the wind but now heading to the northward back from where she came. Peyrol saw at once that the Englishman meant to pass to windward of Cape Esterel, probably with the intention of anchoring for the night off the long white beach which in a regular curve closes the roadstead of Hyères on that side.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔有一阵子甚至以为，这英国佬的放肆就要化作沿着半岛一路巡行、一直窥进那个小海湾的架势了；因为那艘轻巡洋舰的船头正缓缓地向外偏开。一阵对塔尔塔纳（tartane）的揪心惧怕攥住了佩罗尔的心，直到他想起那英国佬并不知道她的存在。当然不知道。他那根棍棒早已把这一星半点的消息堵得死死的了。唯一知道这条塔尔塔纳存在的英国佬，就是那个被他打破了脑袋的家伙。佩罗尔竟为自己这一阵的虚惊笑出了声。况且，那英国佬显然无意在半岛跟前耀武扬威。他无意放肆。那条单桅快速帆船的帆桁整个儿转了过来，她再度迎风转向，可这一回船头朝北，从她来的方向折返。佩罗尔一眼看出，那英国佬打算从埃斯泰雷尔角（Cape Esterel）的上风处绕过去，多半是想在那一道以平缓的弧线合拢耶尔锚地（roadstead of Hyères）的长长白色海滩外头下锚过夜。

===Original===
Peyrol pictured her to himself, on the clouded night, not so very dark, since the full moon was but a day old, lying at anchor within hail of the low shore, with her sails furled and looking profoundly asleep, but with the watch on deck lying by the guns. He gnashed his teeth. It had come to this at last, that the captain of the *Amelia* could do nothing with his ship without putting Peyrol into a rage. Oh, for forty Brothers, or sixty, picked ones, he thought, to teach the fellow what it might cost him taking liberties along the French coast! Ships had been carried by surprise before, on nights when there was just light enough to see the whites of each other’s eyes in a close tussle. And what would be the crew of that Englishman? Something between ninety and a hundred altogether, boys and landsmen included.﻿﻿…… Peyrol shook his fist for a goodbye, just when Cape Esterel shut off the English sloop from his sight. But in his heart of hearts that seaman of cosmopolitan associations knew very well that no forty or sixty, not any given hundred Brothers of the Coast would have been enough to capture that corvette making herself at home within ten miles of where he had first opened his eyes to the world.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔在心里描摹出她的模样：在那个阴云密布的夜晚——也不算太黑，因为满月刚过了一天——她在离低平海岸一箭之遥处下了锚，帆已卷拢，看上去睡得正沉，甲板上的更却守在炮旁待命。他咬得牙根发酸。事情到头来竟到了这一步：阿米莉亚号（Amelia）的舰长哪怕只是动一动他的船，也足以叫佩罗尔勃然大怒。唉，要是能有四十个兄弟，或者六十个，挑精拣壮的，他想，那就能教那家伙懂得，在法国海岸跟前逞放肆要付出多大的代价！从前也有过趁夜奇袭夺船的事，那样的夜里，刚好有微光够得着在一场贴身肉搏中看清彼此的眼白。可那英国佬的船员能有多少？连童子和外行的旱鸭子算在内，总共不过九十到一百人之间。……佩罗尔挥拳作别，正是在埃斯泰雷尔角把那条英国单桅快速帆船挡出他视线的那一刻。可是在他内心深处，这个走遍四海、交游五洲的水手心里再清楚不过：别说四十个、六十个，就是随便哪一百个海岸兄弟会（Brothers of the Coast）的好手，也远远不够去拿下那艘正在离他第一次睁开眼睛看世界的地点不出十里、安之若素的轻巡洋舰。

===Original===
He shook his head dismally at the leaning pine, his only companion. The disinherited soul of that rover ranging for so many years a lawless ocean with the coasts of two continents for a raiding ground, had come back to its crag, circling like a seabird in the dusk and longing for a great sea victory for its people: that inland multitude of which Peyrol knew nothing except the few individuals on that peninsula cut off from the rest of the land by the dead water of a salt lagoon; and where only a strain of manliness in a miserable cripple and an unaccountable charm of a half-crazed woman had found response in his heart.

===Chinese===
他凄然冲着那棵倾斜的松树——他唯一的同伴——摇了摇头。那漫游者被褫夺了继承权的灵魂，多年来出没在一片无法无天的洋面上，以两大洲的海岸为劫掠的猎场，如今已回到了它的巉岩，像一只暮色里的海鸟盘旋着，渴望着为它的族人赢一场海上大捷：那片内地的芸芸众生，佩罗尔对他们一无所知，所认识的不过是那个半岛上的寥寥几人——那半岛被一片盐礁湖的死水同其余的陆地隔开；在那儿，只有一个凄惨的残疾汉身上那一股男子气，和一个半疯女人身上那股说不清道不明的魅力，曾在他心里得到过回应。

===Original===
This scheme of false dispatches was but a detail in a plan for a great, a destructive victory. Just a detail, but not a trifle all the same. Nothing connected with the deception of an admiral could be called trifling. And such an admiral too. It was, Peyrol felt vaguely, a scheme that only a confounded landsman would invent. It behoved the sailors, however, to make a workable thing of it. It would have to be worked through that corvette.

===Chinese===
这桩伪造文书的计谋，不过是一场毁灭性大捷的盘算里的一处细节。区区一处细节，可也绝不是小事。凡是与欺骗一位海军上将相关的事，没有一桩能被称作小事的。况且还是那么一位海军上将。佩罗尔隐隐觉得，这等计谋，只有该死的旱鸭子才想得出来。然而把它做成一桩行得通的事，却是水手们分内的职责。这件事，非得借着那艘轻巡洋舰来做成不可。

===Original===
And here Peyrol was brought up by the question that all his life had not been able to settle for him—and that was whether the English were really very stupid or very acute. That difficulty had presented itself with every fresh case. The old rover had enough genius in him to have arrived at a general conclusion that if they were to be deceived at all it could not be done very well by words but rather by deeds; not by mere wriggling, but by deep craft concealed under some sort of straightforward action. That conviction, however, did not take him forward in this case, which was one in which much thinking would be necessary.

===Chinese===
到了这一步，佩罗尔被一个他一辈子都没能拿准的问题拦住了去路——那就是：英国人到底是当真很蠢呢，还是很精明。每遇一桩新案子，这个难题都要冒出来一回。这老漫游者肚子里颇有几分天才，早已得出过一个笼统的结论：要骗倒他们，靠言辞多半不行，得靠行事；不是靠一味地躲闪腾挪，而是把深沉的诡计藏在某种直截了当的举动底下。然而这一回，这一信念却没能把他往前带半步，因为这一桩案子，非得苦思冥想不可。

===Original===
The *Amelia* had disappeared behind Cape Esterel, and Peyrol wondered with a certain anxiety whether this meant that the Englishman had given up his man for good. “If he has,” said Peyrol to himself, “I am bound to see him pass out again from beyond Cape Esterel before it gets dark.” If, however, he did not see the ship again within the next hour or two, then she would be anchored off the beach, to wait for the night before making some attempt to discover what had become of her man. This could be done only by sending out one or two boats to explore the coast, and no doubt to enter the cove—perhaps even to land a small search party.

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚号已在埃斯泰雷尔角背后消失了，佩罗尔带着几分不安，暗忖这是否意味着那英国佬已经彻底撒手、不再等他的人了。“要是他果真撒了手，”佩罗尔自言自语道，“那么天黑之前，我准会看见他又从埃斯泰雷尔角那一头驶出去。”可要是他在一两个钟头之内再看不见这条船，那她就是已在海滩外头下了锚，等着夜里再设法打听她那人的下落。要做到这一点，唯有派出一两条小艇沿海岸探查，而且十之八九要驶进那个小海湾——说不定还要登上一小股搜索的人马。

===Original===
After coming to this conclusion Peyrol began deliberately to charge his pipe. Had he spared a moment for a glance inland, he might have caught a whisk of a black skirt, the gleam of a white fichu—Arlette running down the faint track leading from Escampobar to the village in the hollow; the same track in fact up which Citizen Scevola, while indulging in the strange freak to visit the church, had been chased by the incensed faithful. But Peyrol, while charging and lighting his pipe, had kept his eyes fastened on Cape Esterel. Then, throwing his arm affectionately over the trunk of the pine, he had settled himself to watch. Far below him the roadstead, with its play of grey and bright gleams, looked like a plaque of mother-of-pearl in a frame of yellow rocks and dark green ravines set off inland by the masses of the hills displaying the tint of the finest purple; while above his head the sun behind a cloud-veil hung like a silver disc.

===Chinese===
得出这个结论之后，佩罗尔便不慌不忙地开始装他的烟斗。要是他肯分出一瞬朝内陆瞥上一眼，他也许能瞥见一抹黑色裙摆的影子、一方白色三角披肩（fichu）的闪光——那是阿尔莱特（Arlette）正顺着那条从埃斯坎波巴（Escampobar）通往洼地里那个村子的小径跑下去；事实上，也正是同一条小径，公民塞沃拉（Citizen Scevola）先前一时心血来潮要去教堂，就是被那一群被激怒的信徒沿着它一路追赶的。可佩罗尔在装烟、点烟的当儿，眼睛始终钉在埃斯泰雷尔角上。随后，他亲昵地把一条胳膊搭上松树的树干，安顿下来守望。在他脚下远远的下方，那一片锚地里，灰色与明亮的闪光交替变幻，活像一块螺钿镶嵌在黄色巉岩与墨绿色沟壑构成的框子里，再往内陆，由一座座山峦托衬着，山色呈现出最上乘的紫色；而在他头顶上方，那一轮藏在云纱背后的日头，则像一只银盘似地悬挂着。

===Original===
That afternoon, after waiting in vain for Lieutenant Réal to appear outside in the usual way, Arlette, the mistress of Escampobar, had gone unwillingly into the kitchen where Catherine sat upright in a heavy capacious wooden armchair, the back of which rose above the top of her white muslin cap. Even in her old age, even in her hours of ease, Catherine preserved the upright carriage of the family that had held Escampobar for so many generations. It would have been easy to believe that like some characters famous in the world Catherine would have wished to die standing up and with unbowed shoulders.

===Chinese===
那天下午，阿尔莱特——埃斯坎波巴的女主人——白等了一场也没见雷亚尔中尉（Lieutenant Réal）照例到外头露面，便老大不情愿地走进了厨房。凯瑟琳（Catherine）正端端正正坐在一把笨重宽敞的木扶手椅里，那椅背高过她那顶白色细布睡帽的顶。凯瑟琳纵然年事已高，纵然是在歇息的时辰，也依旧保持着那个世世代代据有埃斯坎波巴的家族那份挺直的仪态。人们很容易相信，凯瑟琳一如世上某些有名的人物，是情愿站着死去、双肩不屈的。

===Original===
With her sense of hearing undecayed she detected the light footsteps in the *salle* long before Arlette entered the kitchen. That woman, who had faced alone and unaided (except for her brother’s comprehending silence) the anguish of passion in a forbidden love, and of terrors comparable to those of the Judgment Day, neither turned her face, quiet without serenity, nor her eyes, fearless but without fire, in the direction of her niece.

===Chinese===
她的听觉并未衰退，早在阿尔莱特踏进厨房之前许久，便已察觉了正厅里那阵轻轻的脚步声。这个女人，曾独自一人、毫无外助（除了她哥哥那感同身受的沉默）地，扛住了一段禁忌之爱里激情的煎熬，又扛住了堪比末日审判的种种恐怖；此刻她既没有把那张平静却并不安宁的脸转过去，也没有把那双无畏却毫无光彩的眼睛，转向她侄女的方向。

===Original===
Arlette glanced on all sides, even at the walls, even at the mound of ashes under the big overmantel, nursing in its heart a spark of fire, before she sat down and leaned her elbow on the table.

===Chinese===
阿尔莱特四下里扫了一眼，连墙壁也不放过，连那只大壁炉饰架底下那一小堆灰烬——它心里还焐着一星火种——也不放过，这才坐下，把胳膊肘撑在了桌上。

===Original===
“You wander about like a soul in pain,” said her aunt, sitting by the hearth like an old queen on her throne.

===Chinese===
“你这样四处游荡，活像一个痛苦的灵魂，”她的姑母说道，坐在炉边，活像一位老女王坐在她的宝座上。

===Original===
“And you sit here eating your heart out.”

===Chinese===
“您呢，坐在这儿，把心都熬干了。”

===Original===
“Formerly,” remarked Catherine, “old women like me could always go over their prayers, but now.﻿﻿……”

===Chinese===
“从前啊，”凯瑟琳说道，“像我这样的老太婆，总还能一遍遍地念我们的祷告，可是如今……”

===Original===
“I believe you have not been to church for years. I remember Scevola telling me that a long time ago. Was it because you didn’t like people’s eyes? I have fancied sometimes that most people in the world must have been massacred long ago.”

===Chinese===
“我看您好些年没上教堂了。我记得很久以前塞沃拉跟我提过。是因为您不喜欢人家的目光吗？我有时候竟会幻想，这世上大半的人，怕是早就被杀光了。”

===Original===
Catherine turned her face away. Arlette rested her head on her half-closed hand, and her eyes, losing their steadiness, began to tremble amongst cruel visions. She got up suddenly and caressed the thin, half-averted, withered cheek with the tips of her fingers, and in a low voice, with that marvellous cadence that plucked at one’s heartstrings, she said coaxingly:

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳把脸别了过去。阿尔莱特把头靠在半握的拳头上，她的眼睛失去了方才的镇定，开始在种种残忍的幻象之间颤动起来。她猛地站起身，用指尖轻抚那张瘦削的、半偏过去的、枯皱的面颊，压低了嗓音，用那能把人心弦都拨动的绝妙抑扬，柔声哄道：

===Original===
“Those were dreams, weren’t they?”

===Chinese===
“那都是梦，不是吗？”

===Original===
In her immobility the old woman called with all the might of her will for the presence of Peyrol. She had never been able to shake off a superstitious fear of that niece restored to her from the terrors of a Judgment Day in which the world had been given over to the devils. She was always afraid that this girl, wandering about with restless eyes and a dim smile on her silent lips, would suddenly say something atrocious, unfit to be heard, calling for vengeance from heaven, unless Peyrol were by. That stranger come from *par delà les mers* was out of it altogether, cared probably for no one in the world but had struck her imagination by his massive aspect, his deliberation suggesting a mighty force like the reposeful attitude of a lion. Arlette desisted from caressing the irresponsive cheek, exclaimed petulantly, “I am awake now!” and went out of the kitchen without having asked her aunt the question she had meant to ask, which was whether she knew what had become of the lieutenant.

===Chinese===
那老妇人纹丝不动地坐着，倾尽全部意志的气力，呼唤着佩罗尔的到来。这个侄女是从一个把世界拱手让给魔鬼的末日审判的恐怖里归还到她身边的，她对这侄女怀有一种迷信式的恐惧，从来没能摆脱。她总是害怕这个姑娘——一双不安的眼睛四处游荡，沉默的唇角挂着一抹隐约的笑意——会冷不丁说出什么骇人听闻、不堪入耳的话来，从天上招来报复，除非佩罗尔就在跟前。那个从 *par delà les mers*（大海彼岸）来的外乡人，完全置身事外，这世上怕是没有任何一个人能叫他放在心上，可他那一副厚重结实的模样、那一份暗示着伟大力量的沉稳——活像一头狮子那安详的姿态——却触动了她的想象。阿尔莱特不再去抚那张毫无反应的面颊，赌气地嚷了一声“我这会儿可醒着呢！”便走出了厨房，到底没能向她姑母问出她原本想问的那一句——她是否知道那中尉的下落。

===Original===
Her heart had failed her. She let herself drop on the bench outside the door of the *salle*. “What is the matter with them all?” she thought. “I can’t make them out. What wonder is it that I have not been able to sleep?” Even Peyrol, so different from all mankind, who from the first moment when he stood before her had the power to soothe her aimless unrest, even Peyrol would now sit for hours with the lieutenant on the bench, gazing into the air and keeping him in talk about things without sense, as if on purpose to prevent him from thinking of her. Well, he could not do that. But the enormous change implied in the fact that every day had a tomorrow now, and that all the people around her had ceased to be mere phantoms for her wandering glances to glide over without concern, made her feel the need of support from somebody, from somewhere. She could have cried aloud for it.

===Chinese===
她终究是鼓不起那个勇气。她在正厅门外那条长凳上颓然坐下。“他们一个个到底都是怎么了？”她想。“我摸不透他们。我这许多日子以来都睡不着觉，又有什么可奇怪的？”即便是佩罗尔——这个与全人类都截然不同的人，从他在她跟前站定的头一刻起，就有抚平她那无端不安的本事——即便是佩罗尔，如今也会在长凳上和中尉一坐就是几个钟头，望着半空，没完没了地跟他扯些不知所云的事，像是存心不让他想到她似的。哼，他休想办到。可是，每一日如今都有了一个明天，而她身边所有的人对她那游移的目光来说，都已不再是它毫无所谓地一掠而过的纯粹幻影——这一事实所蕴含的巨大转变，让她感到了一种需要：需要某个人、某个地方的某种支撑。她简直要为这个放声喊出来。

===Original===
She sprang up and walked along the whole front of the farm building. At the end of the wall enclosing the orchard she called out in a modulated undertone: “Eugène,” not because she hoped that the lieutenant was anywhere within earshot, but for the pleasure of hearing the sound of the name uttered for once above a whisper. She turned about and at the end of the wall on the yard side she repeated her call, drinking in the sound that came from her lips, “Eugène, Eugène,” with a sort of half-exulting despair. It was in such dizzy moments that she wanted a steadying support. But all was still. She heard no friendly murmur, not even a sigh. Above her head under the thin grey sky a big mulberry-tree stirred no leaf. Step by step, as if unconsciously, she began to move down the track. At the end of fifty yards she opened the inland view, the roofs of the village between the green tops of the platanes overshadowing the fountain, and just beyond the flat blue-grey level of the salt lagoon, smooth and dull like a slab of lead. But what drew her on was the church-tower, where, in a round arch, she could see the black speck of the bell which, escaping the requisitions of the Republican wars, and dwelling mute above the locked-up empty church, had only lately recovered its voice. She ran on, but when she had come near enough to make out the figures moving about the village fountain, she checked herself, hesitated a moment and then took the footpath leading to the presbytery.

===Chinese===
她一跃而起，沿着农舍整道正面的墙走了一遍。在围住果园那道墙的尽头，她用一个抑扬有致的低声唤道：“欧仁（Eugène）”——这并非因为她指望那中尉就在什么听得见的范围之内，而是为了享受一回那名字被人高出耳语地念出声来的快感。她转过身，在院子那一头墙的尽头又唤了一遍，贪婪地饮着自己唇间吐出的那声音——“欧仁，欧仁”——带着一种半是欣喜、半是绝望的心绪。正是在这种天旋地转的时刻，她需要一种能稳住她的支撑。可是一片死寂。她听不见一句友好的低语，甚至连一声叹息也没有。她头顶上，在薄薄的灰色天空底下，一棵大桑树纹丝不动，一片叶子也不颤。她一步一步地，像是无意识地，开始顺着那条小径往下挪去。走出五十步开外，她眼前豁然展开了内陆的景色：村子的屋顶，夹在泉眼上方那几株悬铃木（platanes）翠绿的树冠之间；再往远些，是那片盐礁湖平坦的蓝灰色水面，光溜溜、暗沉沉的，像一块铅板。可真正牵引着她往前走的，是那座教堂的钟楼——在那里的一个圆拱里，她能看见那口钟黑色的一星影子，它躲过了共和国历次战争的征用，在锁闭的空教堂上方缄默地栖息了许久，近来才刚刚找回了自己的嗓音。她奔跑起来，可等她跑到离村子里那眼泉边走动的人影近得能辨认清楚时，她又收住了脚，迟疑了一刻，便踏上了通往神父寓所（presbytery）的那条小径。

===Original===
She pushed open the little gate with the broken latch. The humble building of rough stones, from between which much mortar had crumbled out, looked as though it had been sinking slowly into the ground. The beds of the plot in front were choked with weeds, because the abbé had no taste for gardening. When the heiress of Escampobar opened the door, he was walking up and down the largest room which was his bedroom and sitting-room and where he also took his meals. He was a gaunt man with a long, as if convulsed, face. In his young days he had been tutor to the sons of a great noble, but he did not emigrate with his employer. Neither did he submit to the Republic. He had lived in his native land like a hunted wild beast, and there had been many tales of his activities, warlike and others. When the hierarchy was reestablished he found no favour in the eyes of his superiors. He had remained too much of a royalist. He had accepted, without a word, the charge of this miserable parish, where he had acquired influence quickly enough. His sacerdotalism lay in him like a cold passion. Though accessible enough, he never walked abroad without his breviary, acknowledging the solemnly bared heads by a curt nod. He was not exactly feared, but some of the oldest inhabitants who remembered the previous incumbent, an old man who died in the garden after having been dragged out of bed by some patriots anxious to take him to prison in Hyères, jerked their heads sideways in a knowing manner when their curé was mentioned.

===Chinese===
她推开那扇门栓坏掉了的小门。这幢寒碜的粗糙石块砌成的屋子，石缝间抹的灰泥大半都已剥落，看上去仿佛正缓缓地往下沉入地里。屋前那块小园子里的苗床被野草堵得满满当当，因为这位神父（abbé）毫无莳花弄草的兴致。当埃斯坎波巴的这位女继承人推门进去时，他正在最大的那间屋子里来回踱步——那间屋子既是他的卧房、起居室，也是他用餐的地方。他是个瘦削的人，一张长脸，像是抽搐过似的。他年轻时曾做过一位大贵族几个儿子的家庭教师，但他并没有随东家一同流亡国外。他也没有向共和国低头。他像一头被猎捕的野兽一样生活在自己的故土上，关于他的种种行迹——武力的也好、别的也罢——流传着许多故事。等到教会的等级重新恢复时，他在上司眼里并不得宠。他身上保王党的色彩留得太重了。他二话不说，接下了这个凄清教区的职务，倒是很快就赢得了相当的影响。他那一股神职之气（sacerdotalism），像一股冷冰冰的热忱似的盘踞在他心里。他虽说相当平易近人，出门却从不离身一本日课经（breviary），对那些庄重地脱帽致意的人，只以一个简慢的点头作为还礼。他并不算让人畏惧，但凡还记得上一任本堂神父——一个被几个急于押解到耶尔去坐牢的爱国者从床上拖出来、死在园子里的老人——的那些最老的住户，一提起他们这位现任本堂神父（curé），便会心照不宣地把头往旁边一偏。

===Original===
On seeing this apparition in an Arlesian cap and silk skirt, a white fichu, and otherwise as completely different as any princess could be from the rustics with whom he was in daily contact, his face expressed the blankest astonishment. Then—for he knew enough of the gossip of his community—his straight, thick eyebrows came together inimically. This was no doubt the woman of whom he had heard his parishioners talk with bated breath as having given herself and her property up to a Jacobin, a Toulon sansculotte who had either delivered her parents to execution or had murdered them himself during the first three days of massacres. No one was very sure which it was, but the rest was current knowledge. The abbé, though persuaded that any amount of moral turpitude was possible in a godless country, had not accepted all that tale literally. No doubt those people were Republican and impious, and the state of affairs up there was scandalous and horrible. He struggled with his feelings of repulsion and managed to smooth his brow and waited. He could not imagine what that woman with mature form and a youthful face could want at the presbytery. Suddenly it occurred to him that perhaps she wanted to thank him—it was a very old occurrence—for interposing between the fury of the villagers and that man. He couldn’t call him, even in his thoughts, her husband, for apart from all other circumstances, that connection could not imply any kind of marriage to a priest, had even there been a legal form observed. His visitor was apparently disconcerted by the expression of his face, the austere aloofness of his attitude, and only a low murmur escaped her lips. He bent his head and was not very certain what he had heard.

===Chinese===
一见这个头戴阿尔式女帽、身穿绸裙、披一方白色三角披肩、总之与一位公主一般无异地迥异于他日常打交道的那些乡下人的不速之客，他脸上露出了一种最茫然的惊愕。紧接着——因为他对本堂教民的是非闲话多少知道一些——他那两道又直又浓的眉毛敌意地拧到了一起。这无疑就是他从教民们口中压低了嗓子议论起的那个女人：她把自己和自己的一份家产，全都交给了一个雅各宾派（Jacobin）、一个土伦的无套裤汉，而这个人，要么是把她的父母送上了断头台，要么是在大屠杀的头三天里亲手谋害了他们。究竟属哪一桩，没有谁能说得准，可其余种种已是尽人皆知。这位神父尽管相信，在一个没有上帝的国度里，无论多寡廉鲜耻的伤风败俗都可能发生，却并没有把那一套传闻照单全收。毫无疑问，那些人是共和派，是不敬神的，那上头的情形既可耻又可怖。他按捺住自己的厌恶之情，勉强把眉头舒展开来，等着。他实在想象不出，这样一个身段成熟、面容却年轻的女子，到神父寓所来究竟要做什么。猛然间他想起，也许她是来向他道谢的——那已是很久以前的一桩事了——感谢他当年在那群村民的怒火与那个男人之间插了一手。即便是在心里，他也无法把那个人称作她的丈夫，因为撇开其余一切情形不谈，在一位神父眼里，那种关系算不得任何形式的婚姻，哪怕它曾走过什么合法的形式。他的访客显然被他脸上的神情、他那冷峻疏远的姿态弄得有些不知所措，唇间只漏出一声低低的含糊。他偏下头去，自己也不大听得清她说了什么。

===Original===
“You come to seek my aid?” he asked in a doubting tone.

===Chinese===
“你是来求我帮忙的？”他用一种将信将疑的口气问道。

===Original===
She nodded slightly, and the abbé went to the door she had left half open and looked out. There was not a soul in sight between the presbytery and the village, or between the presbytery and the church. He went back to face her, saying:

===Chinese===
她微微点了点头，神父便走到她留着半开的那扇门前，朝外张望了一下。从神父寓所到村子之间，从神父寓所到教堂之间，一个人影也看不见。他回过身来面对着她，说道：

===Original===
“We are as alone as we can well be. The old woman in the kitchen is as deaf as a post.”

===Chinese===
“我们这儿可以说是再清静不过了。厨房里那个老妇人，聋得跟一根木头桩子似的。”

===Original===
Now that he had been looking at Arlette closer the abbé felt a sort of dread. The carmine of those lips, the pellucid, unstained, unfathomable blackness of those eyes, the pallor of her cheeks, suggested to him something provokingly pagan, something distastefully different from the common sinners of this earth. And now she was ready to speak. He arrested her with a raised hand.

===Chinese===
这会儿他把阿尔莱特端详得更近了些，神父心里竟生出一种近乎恐惧的感觉。那两片嘴唇的殷红，那一双眼睛清透、无瑕、深不可测的黑，那两颊的苍白，在他看来都暗示着某种令人恼怒的异教气息，某种令人不快地有别于这尘世寻常罪人的东西。而此刻她就要开口了。他举起一只手，止住了她。

===Original===
“Wait,” he said. “I have never seen you before. I don’t even know properly who you are. None of you belong to my flock—for you are from Escampobar, are you not?” Sombre under their bony arches, his eyes fastened on her face, noticed the delicacy of features, the naive pertinacity of her stare. She said:

===Chinese===
“且慢，”他说，“我从来没见过你。我连你究竟是谁都还不甚了了。你们这些人，没一个属于我这一群教民——因为你是从埃斯坎波巴来的，不是吗？”他那双眼睛在嶙峋的眉骨底下阴沉沉的，盯着她的脸，留意到她五官的精致、她那一瞥里天真的执拗。她说：

===Original===
“I am the daughter.”

===Chinese===
“我就是那个女儿。”

===Original===
“The daughter!﻿﻿…… Oh! I see.﻿﻿…… Much evil is spoken of you.”

===Chinese===
“那个女儿！……哦！我明白了。……关于你，可是有不少恶话流传呢。”

===Original===
She said a little impatiently: “By that rabble?” and the priest remained mute for a moment. “What do they say? In my father’s time they wouldn’t have dared to say anything. The only thing I saw of them for years and years was when they were yelping like curs on the heels of Scevola.”

===Chinese===
她略带不耐烦地说：“那帮乱民嘴里说出来的？”神父沉默了片刻。“他们都说了些什么？在我父亲在世的时候，他们可连半句都不敢多说。一年又一年，我唯一能瞧见他们的，就是他们像一群恶犬似的汪汪叫着、跟在塞沃拉的脚后头打转的时候。”

===Original===
The absence of scorn in her tone was perfectly annihilating. Gentle sounds flowed from her lips and a disturbing charm from her strange equanimity. The abbé frowned heavily at these fascinations, which seemed to have in them something diabolic.

===Chinese===
她口气里头那种全然没有轻蔑的意味，反倒具有一种彻底的毁灭性。她唇间流出的是柔和的声音，她那奇怪的平静里散发出一种扰人的魅力。神父冲着这些迷人之处重重地皱起眉——它们里头仿佛藏着几分魔鬼的气息。

===Original===
“They are simple souls, neglected, fallen back into darkness. It isn’t their fault. But they have natural feelings of humanity which were outraged. I saved him from their indignation. There are things that must be left to divine justice.”

===Chinese===
“他们都是些单纯的灵魂，无人照管，又退回到了黑暗里。那不是他们的过错。可他们身上天然就有的人之常情，是受了冒犯的。我从他们的义愤底下救过他一命。有些事，是只能交给上帝的公义去裁处的。”

===Original===
He was exasperated by the unconsciousness of that fair face.

===Chinese===
他被那张姣好面庞上那份浑然不觉激怒了。

===Original===
“That man whose name you have just pronounced and which I have heard coupled with the epithet of blood-drinker is regarded as the master of Escampobar Farm. He has been living there for years. How is that?”

===Chinese===
“你刚才提起名字的那个人——我曾听见‘饮血者（blood-drinker）’这个诨名和他连在一起——被当作埃斯坎波巴农庄的主人。他在那儿已经住了好些年。这是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“Yes, it is a long time ago since he brought me back to the house. Years ago. Catherine let him stay.”

===Chinese===
“是的，他把我带回这屋子，那已是很久以前的事了。好多年前了。是凯瑟琳让他留下来的。”

===Original===
“Who is Catherine?” the abbé asked harshly.

===Chinese===
“凯瑟琳是谁？”神父厉声问。

===Original===
“She is my father’s sister who was left at home to wait. She had given up all hope of seeing any of us again, when one morning Scevola came with me to the door. Then she let him stay. He is a poor creature. What else could Catherine have done? And what is it to us up there how the people in the village regard him?” She dropped her eyes and seemed to fall into deep thought, then added, “It was only later that I discovered that he was a poor creature, even quite lately. They call him blood-drinker, do they? What of that? All the time he was afraid of his own shadow.”

===Chinese===
“她是我父亲的姐妹，被留在家里守着。她早已不再指望还能再见到我们中的任何一个了，直到有一天早上，塞沃拉带着我来到了门口。于是她让他留了下来。他是个没出息的可怜虫。凯瑟琳还能有什么别的法子？再说了，我们在上头，村里人怎么看他，与我们有什么相干？”她垂下眼睛，像是陷入了深深的思索，然后又补了一句，“我是到后来才发现他是个没出息的可怜虫的，甚至就在不久之前。他们管他叫饮血者，是吗？那又怎样？他这一辈子，连自己的影子都怕。”

===Original===
She ceased but did not raise her eyes.

===Chinese===
她住了口，却没有抬起眼睛来。

===Original===
“You are no longer a child,” began the abbé in a severe voice, frowning at her downcast eyes, and he heard a murmur: “Not very long.” He disregarded it and continued: “I ask you, is this all that you have to tell me about that man? I hope that at least you are no hypocrite.”

===Chinese===
“你已经不是个孩子了，”神父用严厉的声音开口道，冲着她低垂的眼睛皱着眉头，他听见一声含糊的低语：“没多久。”他没有理会，接着说下去，“我问你，关于那个人，你要告诉我的就只有这些吗？我盼着，你至少不是个伪君子。”

===Original===
“*Monsieur l’Abbé*,” she said, raising her eyes fearlessly, “what more am I to tell you about him? I can tell you things that will make your hair stand on end, but it wouldn’t be about him.”

===Chinese===
“*Monsieur l’Abbé*（神父先生），”她说着，毫无惧色地抬起了眼睛，“关于他，我还能多告诉您些什么呢？我能跟您说些叫您头发都竖起来的事，可那些事，说的并不是他。”

===Original===
For all answer the abbé made a weary gesture and turned away to walk up and down the room. His face expressed neither curiosity nor pity, but a sort of repugnance which he made an effort to overcome. He dropped into a deep and shabby old armchair, the only object of luxury in the room, and pointed to a wooden straight-backed stool. Arlette sat down on it and began to speak. The abbé listened, but looking far away; his big bony hands rested on the arms of the chair. After the first words he interrupted her: “This is your own story you are telling me?”

===Chinese===
神父只疲惫地做了个手势作为回答，便转过身去，在屋里来回踱起步来。他脸上既没有好奇，也没有怜悯，只有一种他正努力压下去的厌恶。他颓然坐进一只又深又旧的破扶手椅里——这是屋里唯一一件称得上舒适的东西——然后指了指一只直背的木凳子。阿尔莱特坐了上去，开始说起话来。神父听着，目光却望向远远的地方；他那双骨节粗大的手搁在椅子扶手上。听了头几句，他便打断了她：“你这是在讲你自己的故事？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Arlette.

===Chinese===
“是的。”阿尔莱特说。

===Original===
“Is it necessary that I should know?”

===Chinese===
“我有知道的必要吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, *Monsieur l’Abbé*.”

===Chinese===
“有的，神父先生。”

===Original===
“But why?”

===Chinese===
“可是，为什么？”

===Original===
He bent his head a little, without, however, ceasing to look far away. Her voice now was very low. Suddenly the abbé threw himself back.

===Chinese===
他微微低下头，却始终没有停止望向远方。此时她的声音已极低。神父忽然向后一仰。

===Original===
“You want to tell me your story because you have fallen in love with a man?”

===Chinese===
“你想把你的故事讲给我听，是因为你爱上了一个男人？”

===Original===
“No, because that has brought me back to myself. Nothing else could have done it.”

===Chinese===
“不，是因为那把我带回到了我自己。换了别的任何事，都做不到这一点。”

===Original===
He turned his head to look at her grimly, but he said nothing and looked away again. He listened. At the beginning he muttered once or twice, “Yes, I have heard that,” and then kept silent, not looking at her at all. Once he interrupted her by a question: “You were confirmed before the convent was forcibly entered and the nuns dispersed?”

===Chinese===
他扭过头来阴沉地看了她一眼，却什么也没说，又把目光挪开了。他听着。开初他嘟囔了一两回：“是的，这事我听说过。”然后便一直沉默，再也不看她一眼。有一次他用一个问题打断了她：“修道院被强行闯入、修女们被驱散之前，你受过坚振礼（confirmation）吗？”

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, “a year before that or more.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”她说，“在那之前一年，也许更久。”

===Original===
“And then two of those ladies took you with them towards Toulon.”

===Chinese===
“后来，那两位嬷嬷带着你一起往土伦去了。”

===Original===
“Yes, the other girls had their relations near by. They took me with them thinking to communicate with my parents, but it was difficult. Then the English came and my parents sailed over to try and get some news of me. It was safe for my father to be in Toulon then. Perhaps you think that he was a traitor to his country?” she asked, and waited with parted lips. With an impassible face the abbé murmured: “He was a good royalist,” in a tone of bitter fatalism, which seemed to absolve that man and all the other men of whose actions and errors he had ever heard.

===Chinese===
“是的，其余的女孩子她们的亲眷都在附近。她们带着我一起走，本想设法和我父母联络上，可那很难。后来英国人来了，我父母便渡海过来，想打听一点我的消息。那时候我父亲待在土伦还是安全的。也许您觉得他是祖国的叛徒？”她问，微张着嘴等着。神父面无表情地低声道：“他是个好保王党。”用的是一种苦涩的宿命论的口气，仿佛要借此宽免那个人，以及他所听说过的所有那些人的所作所为和种种过错。

===Original===
For a long time, Arlette continued, her father could not discover the house where the nuns had taken refuge. He only obtained some information on the very day before the English evacuated Toulon. Late in the day he appeared before her and took her away. The town was full of retreating foreign troops. Her father left her with her mother and went out again to make preparations for sailing home that very night; but the tartane was no longer in the place where he had left her lying. The two Madrague men that he had for a crew had disappeared also. Thus the family was trapped in that town full of tumult and confusion. Ships and houses were bursting into flames. Appalling explosions of gunpowder shook the earth. She spent that night on her knees with her face hidden in her mother’s lap, while her father kept watch by the barricaded door with a pistol in each hand.

===Chinese===
阿尔莱特接着说，有很长一段时间，她父亲都打听不到修女们避难的那户人家。直到英国人撤出土伦的前一天，他才打听到一点消息。那天傍晚，他出现在她面前，把她带走了。满城都是撤退的外国军队。她父亲把她交给母亲，自己又出去张罗当晚便启程回家的事；可那条塔尔塔纳已不在他原先停泊她的位置上了。他雇来当船员的两个马德拉格（Madrague）汉子也不见了踪影。就这样，这一家人被困在了那座一片混乱、人心惶惶的城里。船只和房屋正一团一团地烧起来。火药骇人的爆炸声震得大地发颤。她那一夜都跪在地上，脸埋在母亲的膝头里，而她的父亲则守在那扇堵死的大门边，一手一把手枪。

===Original===
In the morning the house was filled with savage yells. People were heard rushing up the stairs, and the door was burst in. She jumped up at the crash and flung herself down on her knees in a corner with her face to the wall. There was a murderous uproar, she heard two shots fired, then somebody seized her by the arm and pulled her up to her feet. It was Scevola. He dragged her to the door. The bodies of her father and mother were lying across the doorway. The room was full of gunpowder smoke. She wanted to fling herself on the bodies and cling to them, but Scevola took her under the arms and lifted her over them. He seized her hand and made her run with him, or rather dragged her downstairs. Outside on the pavement some dreadful men and many fierce women with knives joined them. They ran along the streets brandishing pikes and sabres, pursuing other groups of unarmed people, who fled round corners with loud shrieks.

===Chinese===
到了早晨，屋子里满是野蛮的叫喊。听得见有人冲上楼梯，紧接着门被撞开了。她在那一声巨响里跳起来，扑到一个墙角跪下，脸贴着墙。一片杀气腾腾的喧闹，她听见响了两枪，然后有人一把抓住她的胳膊，把她拽了起来。是塞沃拉。他把她拖到门口。她父亲和母亲的尸身横七竖八地倒在门口。满屋都是火药的硝烟。她要扑到那两具尸身上去、死死抱住它们，可塞沃拉把她腋下一抄，从尸体上提了过去。他抓住她的手，逼着她跟他一起跑，不如说是把她拖下了楼。外头的人行道上，几个面目狰狞的汉子，还有许多手持刀子、凶神恶煞的女人，加入了他们。他们沿街奔跑，挥舞着长矛和马刀，追打另一群群手无寸铁的人，那些人尖叫着拐过街角四散逃命。

===Original===
“I ran in the midst of them, *Monsieur l’Abbé*,” Arlette went on in a breathless murmur. “Whenever I saw any water I wanted to throw myself into it, but I was surrounded on all sides, I was jostled and pushed and most of the time Scevola held my hand very tight. When they stopped at a wine shop they would offer me some wine. My tongue stuck to the roof of my mouth and I drank. The wine, the pavements, the arms and faces, everything was red. I had red splashes all over me. I had to run with them all day, and all the time I felt as if I were falling down, and down, and down. The houses were nodding at me. The sun would go out at times. And suddenly I heard myself yelling exactly like the others. Do you understand, *Monsieur l’Abbé*? The very same words!”

===Chinese===
“我就在他们中间跑着，神父先生，”阿尔莱特喘着气、低声地接着说，“每回我一看见什么水，就想一头扎进去，可我四面都被围着，我被人推搡、挤撞，大半的时间里塞沃拉把我的手攥得紧紧的。他们在一家酒铺门口停下时，便会塞给我一点酒喝。我的舌头都粘在了上颚上，我便喝了。那酒，那街道的石头，那些胳膊和脸，一切都是红的。我浑身上下溅满了红的斑点。我不得不跟着他们跑了一整天，而自始至终，我都觉得自己像是在往下坠，往下坠，一直地往下坠。那些房子冲我点头。日头有时会熄灭。忽然间，我听见自己正和别人一模一样地尖叫。您听得明白吗，神父先生？一字不差，是同样的话！”

===Original===
The eyes of the priest in their deep orbits glided towards her and then resumed their faraway fixity. Between his fatalism and his faith he was not very far from the belief of Satan taking possession of rebellious mankind, exposing the nakedness of hearts like flint and of the homicidal souls of the Revolution.

===Chinese===
神父那双深陷在眼窝里的眼睛朝她这边滑了一下，随即又恢复了那望向远方的凝定。在他的宿命论和他的信仰之间，他离这样一种看法相去不远：撒旦占据了那反叛的人类，剥露出燧石一般的心，剥露出革命那一颗颗嗜杀的灵魂。

===Original===
“I have heard something of that,” he whispered stealthily.

===Chinese===
“这些事，我多少听说过一点。”他悄悄地低声道。

===Original===
She affirmed with quiet earnestness: “Yet at that time I resisted with all my might.”

===Chinese===
她以平静而恳切的口吻肯定道：“可那个时候，我是拼了命地抗拒过的。”

===Original===
That night Scevola put her under the care of a woman called Perose. She was young and pretty, and was a native of Arles, her mother’s country. She kept an inn. That woman locked her up in her own room, which was next to the room where the patriots kept on shouting, singing and making speeches far into the night. Several times the woman would look in for a moment, make a hopeless gesture at her with both arms, and vanish again. Later, on many other nights, when all the band lay asleep on benches and on the floor, Perose would steal into the room, fall on her knees by the bed on which Arlette sat upright, open-eyed and raving silently to herself, embrace her feet and cry herself to sleep. But in the morning she would jump up briskly and say: “Come. The great affair is to keep our life in our bodies. Come along to help in the work of justice”; and they would join the band that was making ready for another day of traitor-hunting. But after a time the victims, of which the streets were full at first, had to be sought for in back yards, ferreted out of their hiding-places, dragged up out of the cellars or down from the garrets of the houses, which would be entered by the band with howls of death and vengeance.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，塞沃拉把她托付给了一个名叫佩罗丝（Perose）的女人。她年轻貌美，是阿尔勒（Arles）人，也就是她母亲的故乡。她开着一家客栈。那女人把她锁进了自己的房间，那间屋子紧挨着爱国者们彻夜叫喊、唱歌、发表演说的那一间。有好几回，那女人探进头来待上一瞬，冲她绝望地摊开两只胳膊，便又消失了。后来，在许多个别个的夜里，等到那伙人全在长凳上、地板上睡死了过去，佩罗丝便会溜进屋里，跪在阿尔莱特端坐着、双目圆睁、默默地独自胡言乱语的那张床边，抱住她的双脚，哭着哭着睡了过去。可一到早晨，她又精神抖擞地跳起来说道：“来吧。头一桩大事，是把这条命吊在我们的皮囊里。来吧，去帮着干正义的活儿。”于是她们便汇入那一伙人，准备再过一天搜捕叛贼的日子。可过了一阵，起先满街都是的受害的人，得到后院里去找，从他们的藏身处里掏出来，从地窖里拖上来，或者从房子的阁楼上拖下来——那一伙人便会伴着死亡与复仇的嚎叫闯进那些房子。

===Original===
“Then, *Monsieur l’Abbé*,” said Arlette, “I let myself go at last. I could resist no longer. I said to myself: ‘If it is so then it must be right.’ But most of the time I was like a person half asleep and dreaming things that it is impossible to believe. About that time, I don’t know why, the woman Perose hinted to me that Scevola was a poor creature. Next night, while all the band lay fast asleep in the big room, Perose and Scevola helped me out of the window into the street and led me to the quay behind the arsenal. Scevola had found our tartane lying at the pontoon and one of the Madrague men with her. The other had disappeared. Perose fell on my neck and cried a little. She gave me a kiss and said: ‘My time will come soon. You, Scevola, don’t you show yourself in Toulon, because nobody believes in you any more. Adieu, Arlette. *Vive la Nation!*’ and she vanished in the night. I waited on the pontoon shivering in my torn clothes, listening to Scevola and the man throwing dead bodies overboard out of the tartane. Splash, splash, splash. And suddenly I felt I must run away, but they were after me in a moment, dragged me back and threw me down into that cabin which smelt of blood. But when I got back to the farm all feeling had left me. I did not feel myself exist. I saw things round me here and there, but I couldn’t look at anything for long. Something was gone out of me. I know now that it was not my heart, but then I didn’t mind what it was. I felt light and empty, and a little cold all the time, but I could smile at people. Nothing could matter. Nothing could mean anything. I cared for no one. I wanted nothing. I wasn’t alive at all, *Monsieur l’Abbé*. People seemed to see me and would talk to me, and it seemed funny—till one day I felt my heart beat.”

===Chinese===
“于是，神父先生，”阿尔莱特说，“我到头来还是撒手随它去了。我再也抗拒不住了。我对自己说：‘既然如此，那想必就是对的。’可大半的时间里，我像一个半睡半醒的人，做着那些令人难以置信的梦。大约就在那前后，不知为什么，佩罗丝那女人向我暗示，塞沃拉是个没出息的可怜虫。第二天夜里，趁那一伙人在大屋子里全都睡得死沉，佩罗丝和塞沃拉把我从窗户托到了街上，领着我来到军械库（arsenal）后头的码头。塞沃拉找到了我们那条停在浮码头（pontoon）边的塔尔塔纳，还有一个马德拉格汉子在船上。另一个已经不见了。佩罗丝扑到我颈上哭了一会儿。她吻了我一下，说道：‘我的日子也快到了。你，塞沃拉，你可别再在土伦露面了，因为没人再信得过你了。再见了，阿尔莱特。*Vive la Nation!*（国民万岁！）’说罢她便消失在了夜色里。我待在浮码头上，身上破衣烂衫，冻得直打哆嗦，听着塞沃拉和那汉子把一具具尸身从塔尔塔纳上往海里扔。扑通，扑通，扑通。忽然间我感到我非逃跑不可，可他们一转眼就追了上来，把我拖回去，把我摁进了那间散发着血腥气味的船舱。可等我回到农庄，我身上一切知觉都已离我而去了。我感觉不到自己的存在。我看见周围的东西东一件西一件的，可我没法长久地盯着任何一样看。有什么东西从我身上出去了。如今我才知道，那并不是我的心，可当时我也没在意那是什么。我只觉得轻飘飘、空荡荡，一直都有点冷，可我还能冲人微笑。什么都不会要紧。什么都不会有意义。我不在乎任何人。我什么也不想要。我根本就不算活着，神父先生。人们像是看得见我，会跟我说话，而这在我看来可笑得很——直到有一天，我感到我的心跳了一下。”

===Original===
“Why precisely did you come to me with this tale?” asked the abbé in a low voice.

===Chinese===
“你究竟为什么偏偏带着这番话来找我？”神父低声问。

===Original===
“Because you are a priest. Have you forgotten that I have been brought up in a convent? I have not forgotten how to pray. But I am afraid of the world now. What must I do?”

===Chinese===
“因为您是一位神父。您忘了我是修道院里教养大的吗？我并没有忘记怎么祷告。可如今我害怕这个世界了。我该怎么办？”

===Original===
“Repent!” thundered the abbé, getting up. He saw her candid gaze uplifted, and lowered his voice forcibly. “You must look with fearless sincerity into the darkness of your soul. Remember whence the only true help can come. Those whom God has visited by a trial such as yours cannot be held guiltless of their enormities. Withdraw from the world. Descend within yourself and abandon the vain thoughts of what people call happiness. Be an example to yourself of the sinfulness of our nature and of the weakness of our humanity. You may have been possessed. What do I know? Perhaps it was permitted in order to lead your soul to saintliness through a life of seclusion and prayer. To that it would be my duty to help you. Meantime you must pray to be given strength for a complete renunciation.”

===Chinese===
“悔罪吧！”神父霍地站起身来，声如雷霆。他看见她那一双坦荡的目光仰了起来，便着力压低了嗓音。“你必须以无所畏惧的真诚，去正视你自己灵魂里的黑暗。要记住，那唯一真正的救助只能从何处而来。凡是被上帝以像你这样的试炼所造访过的人，都不能被认为对他们自己那穷凶极恶的行径是无辜的。从这世界退出去吧。沉入你自己的内心，抛弃那一切关于世人所谓幸福的虚妄之念。把你自己当作一个范例，去认识我们天性的罪孽、我们人性的软弱。你或许是被附了体。我知道什么呢？也许那是被许可的，为的是借一种隐修与祷告的生活，把你的灵魂引向圣洁。帮助做到这一点，便是我的本分。在此期间，你必须祈求赐予你力量，去做一次彻底的弃绝。”

===Original===
Arlette, lowering her eyes slowly, appealed to the abbé as a symbolic figure of spiritual mystery. “What can be God’s designs on this creature?” he asked himself.

===Chinese===
阿尔莱特缓缓地垂下眼睛，把神父当作一个属灵奥秘的象征性形象来求助。“上帝对这个受造物，能怀有怎样的旨意呢？”他暗自问自己。

===Original===
“*Monsieur le Curé*,” she said quietly, “I felt the need to pray today for the first time in many years. When I left home it was only to go to your church.”

===Chinese===
“*Monsieur le Curé*（本堂神父先生），”她平静地说，“今天，我头一回感到了祷告的需要，这是许多年来的头一遭。我离开家门，为的也只是到您的教堂里去。”

===Original===
“The church stands open to the worst of sinners,” said the abbé.

===Chinese===
“教堂向最不堪的罪人也敞开着。”神父说。

===Original===
“I know. But I would have had to pass before all those villagers: and you, Abbé, know well what they are capable of.”

===Chinese===
“我知道。可我势必得从那群村里人面前经过：而您，神父，再清楚不过他们干得出什么事来。”

===Original===
“Perhaps,” murmured the abbé, “it would be better not to put their charity to the test.”

===Chinese===
“也许，”神父低声道，“还是莫去试探他们的仁爱为好。”

===Original===
“I must pray before I go back again. I thought you would let me come in through the sacristy.”

===Chinese===
“在回去之前，我非祷告不可。我原想，您会准许我从圣器室（sacristy）那头进来的。”

===Original===
“It would be inhuman to refuse your request,” he said, rousing himself and taking down a key that hung on the wall. He put on his broad-brimmed hat and without a word led the way through the wicket-gate and along the path which he always used himself and which was out of sight of the village fountain. After they had entered the damp and dilapidated sacristy he locked the door behind them and only then opened another, a smaller one, leading into the church. When he stood aside, Arlette became aware of the chilly odour as of freshly turned-up earth mingled with a faint scent of incense. In the deep dusk of the nave a single little flame glimmered before an image of the Virgin. The abbé whispered as she passed on:

===Chinese===
“拒绝你的请求，未免太不近人情了。”他说着，振作了一下，从墙上取下一把挂着的钥匙。他戴上那顶宽檐帽，一言不发地引着路，穿过那道小边门，沿着一条他自己一向走的、从村子的泉眼处看不见的小径走去。他俩走进那间潮湿破败的圣器室之后，他随手把身后的门锁上，这才去开另一扇小一些的、通往教堂里面的门。他侧身让开时，阿尔莱特才察觉到一股阴冷的气味，像是新翻起来的泥土，又夹杂着一缕淡淡的焚香。在中殿（nave）深沉的昏暗里，一星细小的火苗在圣母像前闪烁着。她往前走过时，神父低声道：

===Original===
“There before the great altar abase yourself and pray for grace and strength and mercy in this world full of crimes against God and men.”

===Chinese===
“在那边大祭坛前匍匐下来吧，在这充满了触犯上帝、触犯世人的罪行的世间，祈求恩典、力量与怜悯。”

===Original===
She did not look at him. Through the thin soles of her shoes she could feel the chill of the flagstones. The abbé left the door ajar, sat down on a rush-bottomed chair, the only one in the sacristy, folded his arms and let his chin fall on his breast. He seemed to be sleeping profoundly, but at the end of half an hour he got up and, going to the doorway, stood looking at the kneeling figure sunk low on the altar steps. Arlette’s face was buried in her hands in a passion of piety and prayer. The abbé waited patiently for a good many minutes more, before he raised his voice in a grave murmur which filled the whole dark place.

===Chinese===
她没有看他。透过鞋底薄薄的鞋掌，她能感到石板地透上来的寒意。神父把那扇门虚掩着，在圣器室里唯一的一把草编坐面的椅子上坐下，双臂交叠，下巴垂到了胸前。他像是睡得极沉，可半个钟头之后，他站起身来，走到门口，站着端详那个低低地伏在祭坛台阶上的跪着的身影。阿尔莱特把脸埋在双手里，沉浸在一片虔诚与祷告的激切之中。神父又耐心地等了好些分钟，才提高嗓音，用一种庄重的低语开口，那声音注满了整个昏暗的处所。

===Original===
“It is time for you to leave. I am going to ring for vespers.”

===Chinese===
“你该走了。我要打钟召集晚祷（vespers）了。”

===Original===
The view of her complete absorption before the Most High had touched him. He stepped back into the sacristy and after a time heard the faintest possible swish of the black silk skirt of the Escampobar daughter in her Arlesian costume. She entered the sacristy lightly with shining eyes, and the abbé looked at her with some emotion.

===Chinese===
见她在至高无上者面前那般全然的沉湎，他被触动了。他退回圣器室，过了一阵，听见了埃斯坎波巴的这位女儿、一身阿尔式装束的她，那黑色绸裙极其细微的窸窣声。她步履轻盈地走进圣器室，双目闪闪发亮，神父带着几分动情看着她。

===Original===
“You have prayed well, my daughter,” he said. “No forgiveness will be refused to you, for you have suffered much. Put your trust in the grace of God.”

===Chinese===
“你祷告得很好，我的女儿。”他说，“没有任何赦免会被拒绝给你，因为你受的苦够多了。把你的信赖放在上帝的恩典上吧。”

===Original===
She raised her head and stayed her footsteps for a moment. In the dark little place he could see the gleam of her eyes swimming in tears.

===Chinese===
她抬起头，停了一停脚步。在那间昏暗的小屋里，他看得见她眼中那盈盈泪光里的闪动。

===Original===
“Yes, *Monsieur l’Abbé*,” she said in her clear seductive voice. “I have prayed and I feel answered. I entreated the merciful God to keep the heart of the man I love always true to me or else to let me die before I set my eyes on him again.”

===Chinese===
“是的，神父先生。”她用她那清亮诱人的嗓音说道，“我祷告过了，我觉得得到了回应。我恳求那慈悲的上帝，让他把我所爱那个男人的心，永远对我保持忠诚，要么，就让我在再见到他之前死去。”

===Original===
The abbé paled under his tan of a village priest and leaned his shoulders against the wall without a word.

===Chinese===
这位乡村神父那张晒黑的脸底下泛起了一阵苍白，他一言不发地把两肩靠到了墙上。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
After leaving the church by the sacristy door Arlette never looked back. The abbé saw her flit past the presbytery, and the building hid her from his sight. He did not accuse her of duplicity. He had deceived himself. A heathen. White as her skin was, the blackness of her hair and of her eyes, the dusky red of her lips, suggested a strain of Saracen blood. He gave her up without a sigh.

===Chinese===
从圣器室那道门出了教堂之后，阿尔莱特一次也没有回过头。神父瞧见她从神父住宅旁一掠而过，那栋房子便把她挡在了他的视线之外。他并没有责怪她口是心非。是他自己骗了自己。一个异教徒。纵然她的肌肤白皙，那头发与眼珠的漆黑、那嘴唇的暗红，却暗示着她血管里流着几分撒拉逊人（Saracen）的血统。他毫无惋惜地把她放下了。

===Original===
Arlette walked rapidly towards Escampobar as if she could not get there soon enough; but as she neared the first enclosed field her steps became slower and after hesitating awhile she sat down between two olive trees, near a wall bordered by a growth of thin grass at the foot. "And if I have been possessed," she argued to herself, "as the abbé said, what is it to me as I am now? That evil spirit cast my true self out of my body and then cast away the body too. For years I have been living empty. There has been no meaning in anything."

===Chinese===
阿尔莱特快步朝着埃斯坎波巴走去，仿佛恨不得一下子就赶到那里；可她一走近头一片围了篱笆的田地，脚步便慢了下来，犹豫了一阵，她便在一道墙边的两棵橄榄树之间坐了下来，那墙根处长着一蓬稀疏的青草。"就算我曾被附了体，"她暗自辩道，"就像那神父说的那样，那对现在的我又有什么相干？那恶鬼把真正的我从我身子里面赶了出去，然后连这副身子也一并抛弃了。这些年来，我一直空空荡荡地活着。什么都没有意义。"

===Original===
But now her true self had returned matured in its mysterious exile, hopeful and eager for love. She was certain that it had never been far away from that outcast body which Catherine had told her lately was fit for no man's arms. That was all that old woman knew about it, thought Arlette, not in scorn but rather in pity. She knew better, she had gone to heaven for truth in that long prostration with its ardent prayers and its moment of ecstasy, before an unlighted altar.

===Chinese===
可如今，她那真正的自我已然归来，在那场神秘的放逐中成熟了，满怀希望、渴切地盼望着爱。她确信，它从来不曾远离那具被遗弃的躯体——凯瑟琳近来还告诉她，那具身子不配落入任何男人的怀抱。那老妇人所能知晓的，也就仅此而已了，阿尔莱特心想，那并非轻蔑，毋宁说是怜悯。她自己却知道得更清楚，她是在那一场漫长的伏地祈祷中——带着热烈的祷告和那片刻的出神——在一座没有点灯的祭坛前，升登天国去求得了真谛。

===Original===
She knew its meaning well, and also the meaning of another—of a terrestrial revelation which had come to her that day at noon while she waited on the lieutenant. Everybody else was in the kitchen; she and Réal were as much alone together as had ever happened to them in their lives. That day she could not deny herself the delight to be near him, to watch him covertly, to hear him perhaps utter a few words, to experience that strange satisfying consciousness of her own existence which nothing but Réal's presence could give her; a sort of unimpassioned but all-absorbing bliss, warmth, courage, confidence!… She backed away from Réal's table, seated herself facing him and cast down her eyes. There was a great stillness in the *salle* except for the murmur of the voices in the kitchen. She had at first stolen a glance or two, and then peeping again through her eyelashes, as it were, she saw his eyes rest on her with a peculiar meaning. This had never happened before. She jumped up, thinking that he wanted something, and while she stood in front of him with her hand resting on the table he stooped suddenly, pressed it to the table with his lips and began kissing it passionately without a sound, endlessly.… More startled than surprised at first, then infinitely happy, she was beginning to breathe quickly, when he left off and threw himself back in the chair. She walked away from the table and sat down again to gaze at him openly, steadily, without a smile. But he was not looking at her. His passionate lips were set hard now and his face had an expression of stern despair. No word passed between them. Brusquely he got up with averted eyes and went outside, leaving the food before him unfinished.

===Chinese===
她深切地懂得那其中的含义，也懂得另一桩事的含义——那是一桩人世间的启示，就在那天正午、她伺候那中尉用饭时降临到了她身上。其余的人都在厨房里；她和雷亚尔两人，可谓此生从未有过的那般独处。那一天，她无法抑制自己想要靠近他的那份欢愉——偷眼瞧着他，或许还能听见他说上几句话，去体味那种对自己的存在奇妙而满足的觉察，那是除了雷亚尔在跟前，任何东西都给不了她的；那是一种不动声色却全然令人沉醉的极乐，温暖、勇气、信赖！……她从雷亚尔的桌边退开，在他对面坐下，垂下了眼睛。正厅里一片极大的静寂，只有厨房那边传来的人声嗡嗡。起初她偷瞧了一两眼，随后又仿佛从睫毛缝里再瞥过去时，她看见他的目光正落在她身上，带着一种特别的意味。这是从未有过的事。她一跃而起，以为他要什么东西，而当她站在他面前、一只手搁在桌上时，他猛地俯下身去，用嘴唇把手按在桌上，开始无声地、狂热地、没完没了地亲吻起那只手来。……起先与其说是惊讶，不如说是惊吓，随即便是无尽的幸福，她正开始急促地喘息，他却骤然住了手，往椅背上一仰。她从桌边走开，重新坐下，坦然地、定定地凝望着他，脸上没有一丝笑意。可他并没有看她。他那刚才还热烈的嘴唇此刻紧紧抿住，脸上是一种冷峻绝望的神情。两人之间没有说一个字。他蓦地站起身，避开目光，走了出去，把面前没吃完的饭食丢在了那里。

===Original===
In the usual course of things, on any other day, she would have got up and followed him, for she had always yielded to the fascination that had first roused her faculties. She would have gone out just to pass in front of him once or twice. But this time she had not obeyed what was stronger than fascination, something within herself, which at the same time prompted and restrained her. She only raised her arm and looked at her hand. It was true. It had happened. He had kissed it. Formerly she cared not how gloomy he was as long as he remained somewhere where she could look at him—which she would do at every opportunity with an open and unbridled innocence. But now she knew better than to do that. She had got up, had passed through the kitchen, meeting without embarrassment Catherine's inquisitive glance, and had gone upstairs. When she came down after a time, he was nowhere to be seen, and everybody else too seemed to have gone into hiding; Michel, Peyrol, Scevola.… But if she had met Scevola she would not have spoken to him. It was now a very long time since she had volunteered a conversation with Scevola. She guessed, however, that Scevola had simply gone to lie down in his lair, a narrow shabby room lighted by one glazed little window high up in the end wall. Catherine had put him in there on the very day he had brought her niece home, and he had retained it for his own ever since. She could even picture him to herself in there stretched on his pallet. She was capable of that now. Formerly, for years after her return, people that were out of her sight were out of her mind also. Had they run away and left her she would not have thought of them at all. She would have wandered in and out of the empty house and round the empty fields without giving anybody a thought. Peyrol was the first human being she had noticed for years. Peyrol, since he had come, had always existed for her. And as a matter of fact the rover was generally very much in evidence about the farm. That afternoon, however, even Peyrol was not to be seen. Her uneasiness began to grow, but she felt a strange reluctance to go into the kitchen, where she knew her aunt would be sitting in the armchair like a presiding genius of the house taking its rest, and unreadable in her immobility. And yet she felt she must talk about Réal to somebody. This was how the idea of going down to the church had come to her. She would talk of him to the priest and to God. The force of old associations asserted itself. She had been taught to believe that one could tell everything to a priest, and that the omnipotent God who knew everything could be prayed to, asked for grace, for strength, for mercy, for protection, for pity. She had done it and felt she had been heard.

===Chinese===
若在寻常的日子里，换作任何别的一天，她都会起身跟出去，因为她向来屈从于那头一回唤醒了她心智的魔力。她原会走到外头，只为在他面前经过一两个来回。可这一回，她没有听从那比魔力更强有力的东西——那是一种来自她自身内部的、同时驱使着她又约束着她的东西。她只是抬起手臂，看了看自己的手。那是真的。那确确实实发生了。他吻了它。从前，只要他待在她看得见他的某个地方，她就不在乎他有多阴郁——但凡有机会，她便会带着一种坦荡而无所顾忌的天真去瞧他。可如今她已懂事得多，不再那样做了。她站起身，穿过厨房，坦然迎上凯瑟琳那探询的目光，上了楼。过了一阵她再下楼时，他已经不见了踪影，其余的人也似乎都躲了起来：米歇尔、佩罗尔、塞沃拉……可就算她碰见塞沃拉，她也不会同他说话的。她已许久不曾主动找塞沃拉搭过话了。不过她猜想，塞沃拉不过是去他那个窝里躺下了——一间窄小寒酸的屋子，只靠尽头那堵墙高处一扇装了玻璃的小窗透进来一点光。凯瑟琳就在他把侄女带回来的当天把他安顿在了那里，自那以后他便一直把它占为己有。她甚至能在脑海里勾勒出他躺在那张草铺上伸展开来的样子。这种事她如今做得到了。从前，在她回来之后的许多年里，不在她眼前的人，便也不在她的心上。哪怕他们统统跑光了、丢下她一个人，她也不会想起他们中的任何一个。她会在空荡荡的屋子里进进出出，在空荡荡的田野间游荡，不会想起任何人。佩罗尔是她多年来头一个注意到的人。佩罗尔自从来了之后，对她而言便始终存在着。事实上，这漫游者在农庄上通常是十分惹人注目的。然而那天下午，连佩罗尔也不见踪影。她的不安开始滋长，可她又莫名地不愿走进厨房——她知道她姑母会坐在那把扶手椅里，如同这屋子里主事的神灵在歇息，那一副纹丝不动的模样叫人捉摸不透。可她又觉得自己非得找个人说说雷亚尔的事不可。她之所以动了下到教堂去的念头，便是缘由于此。她要把他的事讲给神父听、讲给上帝听。往昔潜移默化的熏陶发挥了它的力量。她从小被教导相信，什么事都可以对神父说，而那位无所不知、全能的上帝，是可以向他祷告、向他求取恩典、力量、怜悯、庇护与慈悲的。她这样做了，而且觉得自己已被听见了。

===Original===
Her heart had quietened down while she rested under the wall. Pulling out a long stalk of grass, she twined it round her fingers absently. The veil of cloud had thickened over her head, early dusk had descended upon the earth, and she had not found out what had become of Réal. She jumped to her feet wildly. But directly she had done that she felt the need of self-control. It was with her usual light step that she approached the front of the house and for the first time in her life perceived how barren and sombre it looked when Réal was not about. She slipped in quietly through the door of the main building and ran upstairs. It was dark on the landing. She passed by the door leading into the room occupied by her aunt and herself. It had been her father and mother's bedroom. The other big room was the lieutenant's during his visits to Escampobar. Without even a rustle of her dress, like a shadow, she glided along the passage, turned the handle without noise and went in. After shutting the door behind her she listened. There was no sound in the house. Scevola was either already down in the yard or still lying open-eyed on his tumbled pallet in raging sulks about something. She had once accidentally caught him at it, down on his face, one eye and cheek of which were buried in the pillow, the other eye glaring savagely, and had been scared away by a thick mutter: "Keep off. Don't approach me." And all this had meant nothing to her then.

===Chinese===
她在墙根下歇了一阵，心便平静了下来。她随手拔起一根长长的草茎，心不在焉地缠绕在手指上。头顶的云幕更厚了，暮色早早地降临在大地上，而她还是没能弄明白雷亚尔究竟怎么样了。她猛地跳了起来。可一跳起来，她便感到非克制自己不可。于是她迈着素常那轻盈的步子，走到屋子的正面，有生以来头一回察觉到，雷亚尔不在近旁的时候，它显得多么荒凉、多么阴沉。她悄无声息地从正屋的门溜了进去，跑上了楼。楼梯口一片漆黑。她从姑母和她自己住的那间屋子的门前经过。那本是她父亲和母亲的卧室。另一间大屋子是中尉到埃斯坎波巴来时住的。她连衣裳的窸窣声都不发一点，像一道影子似的滑过过道，无声地拧开门把手，走了进去。她随手把门带上，侧耳听了听。屋子里没有一点声响。塞沃拉不是已经下到了院子里，便是还睁着眼躺在他那张乱糟糟的草铺上，为着什么事在那里憋着一肚子邪火。有一回她无意间撞见过他那副样子——脸朝下趴着，一只眼睛和半边脸颊埋在枕头里，另一只眼睛凶狠地瞪着——被一声粗重的嘟囔吓跑了："别过来。别靠近我。"而在当时，这一切对她而言毫无意义。

===Original===
Having ascertained that the inside of the house was as still as the grave, Arlette walked across to the window, which, when the lieutenant was occupying the room, stood always open and with the shutter pushed right back against the wall. It was, of course, uncurtained, and as she came near to it Arlette caught sight of Peyrol coming down the hill on his return from the lookout. His white head gleamed like silver against the slope of the ground and by and by passed out of her sight, while her ear caught the sound of his footsteps below the window. They passed into the house, but she did not hear him come upstairs. He had gone into the kitchen. To Catherine. They would talk about her and Eugène. But what would they say? She was so new to life that everything appeared dangerous: talk, attitudes, glances. She felt frightened at the mere idea of silence between those two. It was possible. Suppose they didn't say anything to each other. That would be awful.

===Chinese===
确信了屋子里面已如坟墓一般死寂之后，阿尔莱特走到窗前——中尉住在这间屋子的时候，那扇窗总是敞着的，百叶窗被推回去紧贴着墙。那扇窗当然没有挂帘子，她走近时，阿尔莱特瞥见佩罗尔正从瞭望台上下来、走下山坡。他那颗白发苍苍的脑袋在山坡的映衬下如银子般闪亮，渐渐地消失在了她的视野之外，而她的耳朵捕捉到了窗下他那一阵脚步声。脚步声进了屋子，可她没有听见他上楼。他进了厨房。去找凯瑟琳了。他们一定会谈到她和欧仁（Eugène）。可他们会说些什么呢？她对人生实在太生疏了，以至于样样东西在她看来都充满了危险：言谈、姿态、目光。光是想到那两个人之间可能沉默不语，她就感到害怕。那是可能的。假如他们彼此什么也不说呢。那可就太可怕了。

===Original===
Yet she remained calm like a sensible person, who knows that rushing about in excitement is not the way to meet unknown dangers. She swept her eyes over the room and saw the lieutenant's valise in a corner. That was really what she had wanted to see. He wasn't gone then. But it didn't tell her, though she opened it, what had become of him. As to his return, she had no doubt whatever about that. He had always returned. She noticed particularly a large packet sewn up in sailcloth and with three large red seals on the seam. It didn't, however, arrest her thoughts. Those were still hovering about Catherine and Peyrol downstairs. How changed they were. Had they ever thought that she was mad? She became indignant. "How could I have prevented that?" she asked herself with despair. She sat down on the edge of the bed in her usual attitude, her feet crossed, her hands lying in her lap. She felt on one of them the impress of Réal's lips, soothing, reassuring like every certitude, but she was aware of a still remaining confusion in her mind, an indefinite weariness like the strain of an imperfect vision trying to discern shifting outlines, floating shapes, incomprehensible signs. She could not resist the temptation of resting her tired body, just for a little while.

===Chinese===
然而她仍然保持着冷静，像一个明白事理的人那样——她知道，激动地东奔西跑并非对付未知危险的办法。她的目光扫过房间，看见了角落里中尉的旅行袋。那才是她真正想看到的东西。这么说，他还没有走。可是，尽管她把它打开了，它却没能告诉她他怎么样了。至于他会不会回来，她丝毫也不怀疑。他总是一再回来的。她特别注意到一个用帆布缝起来的大包裹，接缝处盖着三个硕大的红蜡封印。然而这并没有留住她的心思。她的心思还在楼下凯瑟琳和佩罗尔身上打转。他们变了好大啊。他们可曾以为她是疯的吗？她愤愤不平起来。"那又怎么是我拦得住的呢？"她绝望地自问。她照她惯常的姿态在床沿坐下，两脚交叠，双手搁在膝头。她感到其中一只手上还留着雷亚尔嘴唇的印记，安抚的、令人安心的，如同一切确凿无疑的事那样，可她也察觉到自己脑子里仍残存着一片混乱，一种说不清道不明的倦怠，仿佛一双视力不济的眼睛在竭力辨认游移的轮廓、飘浮的形影、费解的符号时所感到的那种吃力。她抵挡不住歇一歇这副疲倦身子的诱惑，哪怕只是一小会儿也好。

===Original===
She lay down on the very edge of the bed, the kissed hand tucked under her cheek. The faculty of thinking abandoned her altogether, but she remained open-eyed, wide awake. In that position, without hearing the slightest sound, she saw the door handle move down as far as it would go, perfectly noiseless, as though the lock had been oiled not long before. Her impulse was to leap right out into the middle of the room, but she restrained herself and only swung herself into a sitting posture. The bed had not creaked. She lowered her feet gently to the ground, and by the time when holding her breath she put her ear against the door, the handle had come back into position. She had detected no sound outside. Not the faintest. Nothing. It never occurred to her to doubt her own eyes, but the whole thing had been so noiseless that it could not have disturbed the lightest sleeper. She was sure that had she been lying on her other side, that is with her back to the door, she would have known nothing. It was some time before she walked away from the door and sat on a chair which stood near a heavy and much-carved table, an heirloom more appropriate to a château than to a farmhouse. The dust of many months covered its smooth oval surface of dark, finely grained wood.

===Chinese===
她在床的最边缘躺了下来，把那只被吻过的手垫在脸颊底下。思考的能力整个儿抛弃了她，可她依然睁着眼，毫无睡意。就在那个姿势下，她没有听见一丝声响，却看见门把手一直往下转到了底，悄无声息，仿佛那把锁不久前刚上过油。她的头一个冲动是直接跳到屋子正中央去，但她克制住了自己，只是翻起身来坐了个端坐。床没有发出半点吱呀声。她轻轻地把脚放到地上，等到她屏住呼吸把耳朵贴到门上时，那把手已经回到了原位。她在外面没有察觉到任何声响。一丝一毫也没有。什么也没有。她从不曾想过要怀疑自己的眼睛，可整件事是如此之轻，轻到不足以惊醒睡得最浅的人。她确信，倘若她是朝着另一边侧卧的——也就是说背对着门——她便会一无所知。过了好一阵，她才从门边走开，在一张椅子坐下。那椅子挨着一张沉重且雕花繁复的桌子，一件传家古物，搁在城堡里比搁在农舍里更相称。好几个月的积尘覆盖着它那深色、木纹细密的椭圆形光滑桌面。

===Original===
"It must have been Scevola," thought Arlette. It could have been no one else. What could he have wanted? She gave herself up to thought, but really she did not care. The absent Réal occupied all her mind. With an unconscious slowness her finger traced in the dust on the table the initials E.A. and achieved a circle round them. Then she jumped up, unlocked the door and went downstairs. In the kitchen, as she fully expected, she found Scevola with the others. Directly she appeared he got up and ran upstairs, but returned almost immediately, looking as if he had seen a ghost, and when Peyrol asked him some insignificant question his lips and even his chin trembled before he could command his voice. He avoided looking anybody in the face. The others too seemed shy of meeting each other's eyes, and the evening meal of the Escampobar seemed haunted by the absent lieutenant. Peyrol, besides, had his prisoner to think of. His existence presented a most interesting problem, and the proceedings of the English ship was another, closely connected with it and full of dangerous possibilities. Catherine's black and ungleaming eyes seemed to have sunk deeper in their sockets, but her face wore its habitual severe aloofness of expression. Suddenly Scevola spoke as if in answer to some thought of his own.

===Chinese===
"那一定是塞沃拉。"阿尔莱特心想。不会是别的人。他想要干什么呢？她纵使由着自己去想，可说实话她并不在意。不在跟前的雷亚尔占据了她全部的心思。她那根手指不自觉地、缓缓地在桌面的积尘上描出了 E.A. 两个首字母，又绕着它们画了一个圈。随后她跳起身来，开了门锁，下楼去了。在厨房里——正如她完全料到的——她发现塞沃拉和其余的人在一起。她一露面，他便跳起身跑上楼去，可几乎立刻又回来了，一副见了鬼的模样，佩罗尔问他一句无关紧要的话时，他的嘴唇乃至下巴都在颤抖，半天才稳住嗓音。他避而不与任何人对视。其余的人似乎也躲闪着彼此的目光，埃斯坎波巴一家人的这顿晚饭，仿佛被那个不在跟前的中尉的鬼魂缠住了。况且佩罗尔还有他那个俘虏要操心。那家伙的存在本身便是一个极有意思的难题，而那条英国船的动向则是另一个，与之紧密相连、且充满了种种凶险的可能。凯瑟琳那双黑黝黝、不反光的眼睛似乎更深地陷进了眼窝，可她的脸上仍是一向那种严峻而疏离的神情。忽然，塞沃拉开口了，仿佛是在回答自己脑子里的某个念头。

===Original===
"What has lost us was moderation."

Peyrol swallowed the piece of bread and butter which he had been masticating slowly, and asked:

"What are you alluding to, *citoyen*?"

===Chinese===
"毁了我们的是仁慈手软。"

佩罗尔把嘴里慢慢嚼着的那块涂了黄油的面包咽了下去，问道：

"你指的是什么，公民？"

===Original===
"I am alluding to the Republic," answered Scevola in a more assured tone than usual. "Moderation I say. We patriots held our hand too soon. All the children of the ci-devants and all the children of traitors should have been killed together with their fathers and mothers. Contempt for civic virtues and love of tyranny were inborn in them all. They grow up and trample on all the sacred principles.… The work of the Terror is undone!"

===Chinese===
"我指的是共和国，"塞沃拉回答，口气比平常笃定。"我说的就是手软。我们爱国者过早地缩回了手。一切 ci-devant（前贵族）的子嗣、一切叛徒的子嗣，本该连同他们的爹娘一道杀个干净。蔑视公民美德、拥戴暴政，这些都是他们与生俱来的天性。他们长大了，就把一切神圣的原则踩在脚下。……恐怖统治的成果，全都付之东流了！"

===Original===
"What do you propose to do about it?" growled Peyrol. "No use declaiming here or anywhere for that matter. You wouldn't find anybody to listen to you—you cannibal," he added in a good-humoured tone. Arlette, leaning her head on her left hand, was tracing with the forefinger of her right invisible initials on the tablecloth. Catherine, stooping to light a four-beaked oil lamp mounted on a brass pedestal, turned her finely carved face over her shoulder. The sansculotte jumped up, flinging his arms about. His hair was tousled from his sleepless tumbling on his pallet. The unbuttoned sleeves of his shirt flapped against his thin hairy forearms. He no longer looked as though he had seen a ghost. He opened a wide black mouth, but Peyrol raised his finger at him calmly.

===Chinese===
"那你打算怎么办呢？"佩罗尔瓮声瓮气地说。"在这儿也好，在哪儿也好，空喊都是白搭。你不会找得到一个人肯听你的——你这吃人生番，"他用一种并不当真的、和气的口气补了一句。阿尔莱特左手支着头，右手的食指正在桌布上描着看不见的首字母。凯瑟琳正弯腰去点一盏装在铜座子上的四嘴油灯，转过那张线条精致的侧脸来从肩头上望了一眼。那无套裤汉跳了起来，两条胳膊乱舞。他的头发因在草铺上彻夜辗转而乱成一团。他衬衫敞开的袖管拍打着那两条长着毛的干瘦前臂。他脸上已经不再是一副见了鬼的模样了。他张开一张黑洞洞的大嘴，可佩罗尔却不慌不忙地朝他竖起了一根手指。

===Original===
"No, no. The time when your own people up La Boyère way—don't they live up there?—trembled at the idea of you coming to visit them with a lot of patriot scallywags at your back is past. You have nobody at your back; and if you started spouting like this at large, people would rise up and hunt you down like a mad dog."

===Chinese===
"不，不。从前你拉布瓦耶尔（La Boyère）那头自家的那些人——他们是住在那上头的吧？——一想到你带着一帮爱国者泼皮上门便吓得发抖的那个年头，已经过去了。你身后已经没有任何人了；你若是这般当众大放厥词，人们会群起而攻之，像追杀一条疯狗那样追杀你。"

===Original===
Scevola, who had shut his mouth, glanced over his shoulder, and as if impressed by his unsupported state went out of the kitchen, reeling, like a man who had been drinking. He had drunk nothing but water. Peyrol looked thoughtfully at the door which the indignant sansculotte had slammed after him. During the colloquy between the two men, Arlette had disappeared into the *salle*. Catherine, straightening her long back, put the oil lamp with its four smoky flames on the table. It lighted her face from below. Peyrol moved it slightly aside before he spoke.

===Chinese===
塞沃拉闭上了嘴，回头瞟了一眼，似乎被自己势单力薄的处境所触动，便踉踉跄跄地走出了厨房，像一个喝醉了酒的人。他其实只喝过水。佩罗尔若有所思地望着那个愤愤不平的无套裤汉在身后砰地摔上的那扇门。在两个男人这番交锋期间，阿尔莱特已消失进了正厅。凯瑟琳挺直了长长的脊背，把那盏燃着四缕冒烟火苗的油灯搁到桌上。灯光从下方照亮了她的脸。佩罗尔把灯稍稍挪开了一点，才开口说话。

===Original===
"It was lucky for you," he said, gazing upwards, "that Scevola hadn't even one other like himself when he came here."

===Chinese===
"算你走运，"他说，目光朝上望着，"塞沃拉当初到这儿来的时候，身边连一个跟他一样的人都没有。"

===Original===
"Yes," she admitted. "I had to face him alone, from first to last. But can you see me between him and Arlette? In those days he raved terribly, but he was dazed and tired out. Afterwards I recovered myself and I could argue with him firmly. I used to say to him, 'Look, she is so young, and she has no knowledge of herself.' Why, for months the only thing she would say that one could understand was 'Look how it spurts, look how it splashes!' He talked to me of his republican virtue. He was not a profligate. He could wait. She was, he said, sacred to him, and things like that. He would walk up and down for hours talking of her and I would sit there listening to him with the key of the room the child was locked in, in my pocket. I temporized, and, as you say yourself, it was perhaps because he had no one at his back that he did not try to kill me, which he might have done any day. I temporized. And after all, why should he want to kill me? He told me more than once he was sure to have Arlette for his own. Many a time he made me shiver explaining why it must be so. She owed her life to him. Oh! that dreadful crazy life. You know he is one of those men that can be patient as far as women are concerned."

===Chinese===
"是啊，"她承认道，"从头到尾，我都只能独自一个对付他。可你能想象我夹在他和阿尔莱特中间的样子吗？那阵子他说起胡话来骇人得很，可他又是懵的、又疲乏得要命。后来我缓过劲来，便能硬气地同他讲道理。我常对他说：'你瞧，她这么小，又不知道自己是怎么回事。'嗐，有好几个月，她嘴里唯一叫人听得懂的话就是'瞧它喷得多高，瞧它溅得多远！'他跟我侈谈他那共和党人的德行。他并不是个好色之徒。他等得起。他说，她对他而言是神圣的，诸如此类的话。他会一连几个钟头来回踱步，谈论着她，而我便坐在那儿听他说，口袋里揣着那孩子被锁的那间屋子的钥匙。我施着拖字诀，而且，正像你自己说的，也许正是因为他背后无人，他才没有动手杀我——他原可以随便哪一天都下得了手的。我施着拖字诀。话说回来，他又何必要杀我呢？他不止一次跟我说过，他笃定能把阿尔莱特据为己有。许多回，他向我解释为什么事情必然如此，直说得我脊背发凉。她的命是他给的。哦！那段骇人的、疯狂的日子。你知道的，在女人这件事上，他是那种能极有耐心的男人。"

===Original===
Peyrol nodded understandingly. "Yes, some are like that. That kind is more impatient sometimes to spill blood. Still I think that your life was one long narrow escape, at least till I turned up here."

===Chinese===
佩罗尔心领神会地点了点头。"是的，有些人就是那样。这种人有时反而更急于见血。不过我看，你这一辈子可谓是一场漫长的死里逃生，至少在我来到这里之前是这样。"

===Original===
"Things had settled down, somehow," murmured Catherine. "But all the same I was glad when you appeared here, a grey-headed man, serious."

"Grey hairs will come to any sort of man," observed Peyrol acidly, "and you did not know me. You don't know anything of me even now."

===Chinese===
"不知怎的，事情渐渐安定了下来，"凯瑟琳低声道。"可你出现在这里时，我还是很高兴——一个头发花白的男人，沉稳持重。"

"白头发什么样的人都会长，"佩罗尔酸溜溜地说，"况且你当初并不认识我。就是到了现在，你也对我一无所知。"

===Original===
"There have been Peyrols living less than half a day's journey from here," observed Catherine in a reminiscent tone.

"That's all right," said the rover in such a peculiar tone that she asked him sharply: "What's the matter? Aren't you one of them? Isn't Peyrol your name?"

===Chinese===
"从前有姓佩罗尔的人家，住得离这儿不到半天的路程。"凯瑟琳用一种追忆的口吻说。

"这倒不假，"漫游者用一种极其特别的口吻说道，引得她尖声追问："怎么啦？难道你不是他们当中的一员？佩罗尔不是你的名字吗？"

===Original===
"I have had many names and this was one of them. So this name and my grey hair pleased you, Catherine? They gave you confidence in me, *hein*?"

===Chinese===
"我有过许多名字，这只是其中一个。这么说，这个名字和我这头白发讨你喜欢了，凯瑟琳？它们让你对我有了信心，*hein*（嗯？）？"

===Original===
"I wasn't sorry to see you come. Scevola too, I believe. He heard that patriots were being hunted down, here and there, and he was growing quieter every day. You roused the child wonderfully."

"And did that please Scevola too?"

===Chinese===
"你来了，我并不难过。我想塞沃拉也是。他听说爱国者正到处遭到追捕，一天比一天安分了。你把那孩子激醒了，真是了不起。"

"那也合了塞沃拉的心意吗？"

===Original===
"Before you came she never spoke to anybody unless first spoken to. She didn't seem to care where she was. At the same time," added Catherine after a pause, "she didn't care what happened to her either. Oh, I have had some heavy hours thinking it all over, in the daytime doing my work, and at night while I lay awake, listening to her breathing. And I growing older all the time, and, who knows, with my last hour ready to strike. I often thought that when I felt it coming I would speak to you as I am speaking to you now."

===Chinese===
"你来之前，她从不主动跟任何人说话，除非别人先跟她开口。她似乎并不在乎自己身在何处。同时，"凯瑟琳顿了一顿，接着说道，"她也不在乎自己会遭逢什么。哦，我度过了多少沉重的时辰，翻来覆去地想这些——白天干着活想，夜里醒着躺在那里、听着她的呼吸时想。而我却一天比一天老了，谁知道呢，我最后那个钟头说不定随时会敲响。我常常想，等我觉着那钟点临近了，我就要像我现在这样跟你谈一谈。"

===Original===
"Oh, you did think," said Peyrol in an undertone. "Because of my grey hairs, I suppose."

"Yes. And because you came from beyond the seas," Catherine said with unbending mien and in an unflinching voice. "Don't you know that the first time Arlette saw you she spoke to you and that it was the first time I heard her speak of her own accord since she had been brought back by that man, and I had to wash her from head to foot before I put her into her mother's bed?"

===Chinese===
"哦，你果然想过，"佩罗尔压低了声音说。"我估摸是因为我这一头白发。"

"是的。还因为你来自大海的那一边，"凯瑟琳神色凛然、声音毫不退让地说。"你难道不知道吗，阿尔莱特头一回见着你，就对你开了口——而自打她被那个男人带回来之后，我从没听她主动说过一句话。当初我不得不把她从头到脚洗得干干净净，才把她安顿到她母亲的床上。"

===Original===
"The first time," repeated Peyrol.

"It was like a miracle happening," said Catherine, "and it was you that had done it."

===Chinese===
"头一回。"佩罗尔重复道。

"那简直像一桩奇迹发生了，"凯瑟琳说，"而这一切是你做到的。"

===Original===
"Then it must be that some Indian witch has given me the power," muttered Peyrol, so low that Catherine could not hear the words. But she did not seem to care, and presently went on again:

"And the child took to you wonderfully. Some sentiment was aroused in her at last."

"Yes," assented Peyrol grimly. "She did take to me. She learned to talk to—the old man."

===Chinese===
"那么，想必是哪个印度洋的女巫给了我这种法力，"佩罗尔嘟囔道，声音低得凯瑟琳听不见他说了什么。可她似乎并不在意，过了一会儿又接着说：

"那孩子对你亲近得了不得。她心里到底有某种感情被唤醒了。"

"是啊，"佩罗尔阴沉地附和道。"她确实跟我亲近。她学会了跟——这个老头子——说话。"

===Original===
"It's something in you that seems to have opened her mind and unloosed her tongue," said Catherine, speaking with a sort of regal composure down at Peyrol, like a chieftainess of a tribe. "I often used to look from afar at you two talking and wonder what she.…"

"She talked like a child," struck in Peyrol abruptly. "And so you were going to speak to me before your last hour came. Why, you are not making ready to die yet?"

===Chinese===
