"是你身上的什么东西，仿佛打开了她的心智、松开了她的舌头，"凯瑟琳说，以一种近乎王者的从容俯视着佩罗尔，像一个部族的女酋长。"我常从远处看着你们两个说话，心想她……"

"她说起话来就像个孩子，"佩罗尔突兀地插嘴道。"所以你本打算在你最后那个钟头到来之前跟我谈一谈。怎么，你还不到准备去死的时候吧？"

===Original===
"Listen, Peyrol. If anybody's last hour is near, it isn't mine. You just look about you a little. It was time I spoke to you."

"Why, I am not going to kill anybody," muttered Peyrol. "You are getting strange ideas into your head."

===Chinese===
"你听我说，佩罗尔。如果谁最后那个钟头近了，那绝不是我的。你只管四下里瞧一瞧。我早该跟你谈谈了。"

"怎么，我又不打算杀谁，"佩罗尔嘟囔道。"你净往脑袋里装些古怪的念头。"

===Original===
"It is as I said," insisted Catherine without animation. "Death seems to cling to her skirts. She has been running with it madly. Let us keep her feet out of more human blood."

Peyrol, who had let his head fall on his breast, jerked it up suddenly. "What on earth are you talking about?" he cried angrily. "I don't understand you at all."

===Chinese===
"我说的没错，"凯瑟琳毫不激动地坚持道。"死亡仿佛黏在她的裙角上。她一直疯了一样地跟死亡赛跑。咱们别让她的脚再踩进更多人的血里去了。"

佩罗尔原本把脑袋垂到了胸前，这时猛地一抬。"你到底在说些什么？"他恼怒地嚷道。"我一点儿也听不懂你的话。"

===Original===
"You have not seen the state she was in when I got her back into my hands," remarked Catherine.… "I suppose you know where the lieutenant is. What made him go off like that? Where did he go to?"

"I know," said Peyrol. "And he may be back tonight."

===Chinese===
"你没有见过我把她重新接到手时她那副样子，"凯瑟琳说道。……"我估摸你知道那中尉在哪儿。他怎么那样跑掉了？他上哪儿去了？"

"我知道，"佩罗尔说。"他今晚说不定就回来了。"

===Original===
"You know where he is! And of course you know why he has gone away and why he is coming back," pronounced Catherine in an ominous voice. "Well, you had better tell him that unless he has a pair of eyes at the back of his head he had better not return here—not return at all; for if he does, nothing can save him from a treacherous blow."

"No man was ever safe from treachery," opined Peyrol after a moment's silence. "I won't pretend not to understand what you mean."

===Chinese===
"你知道他在哪儿！那你自然也知道他为什么走、又为什么要回来，"凯瑟琳用一种不祥的口吻断言道。"好吧，你最好告诉他，除非他后脑勺上也长着一双眼睛，否则他最好不要回到这里来——干脆就别回来；因为只要他回来，就没有什么能救得了他免遭暗算。"

"从来没有人能防得住暗算，"佩罗尔沉默了片刻后说道。"我不必装作听不懂你的意思。"

===Original===
"You heard as well as I what Scevola said just before he went out. The lieutenant is the child of some ci-devant and Arlette of a man they called a traitor to his country. You can see yourself what was in his mind."

"He is a chickenhearted spouter," said Peyrol contemptuously, but it did not affect Catherine's attitude of an old sibyl risen from the tripod to prophesy calmly atrocious disasters. "It's all his republicanism," commented Peyrol with increased scorn. "He has got a fit of it on."

===Chinese===
"塞沃拉出去之前说的那番话，你跟我一样都听见了。那中尉是某个前贵族的孩子，而阿尔莱特是一个被他们叫作祖国叛徒的人的女儿。你自己想得出来他肚子里在盘算什么。"

"他不过是个胆小如鼠的空谈家，"佩罗尔轻蔑地说，可这丝毫没能动摇凯瑟琳那副犹如从三足鼎上站起身来、平静地预言骇人灾祸的古代女先知的姿态。"这全是他的共和主义作祟，"佩罗尔更加不屑地评道。"他那老毛病又犯了。"

===Original===
"No, that's jealousy," said Catherine. "Maybe he has ceased to care for her in all these years. It is a long time since he has left off worrying me. With a creature like that I thought that if I let him be master here.… But no! I know that after the lieutenant started coming here his awful fancies have come back. He is not sleeping at night. His republicanism is always there. But don't you know, Peyrol, that there may be jealousy without love?"

"You think so," said the rover profoundly. He pondered full of his own experience. "And he has tasted blood too," he muttered after a pause. "You may be right."

===Chinese===
"不，那是嫉妒，"凯瑟琳说。"这些年来他说不定早已不在乎她了。他不再来缠我，也有好一阵子了。对那么个东西，我原本想着，若是放任他在此处作主……可不！我知道，自打那中尉开始到这儿来，他那可怕的痴念又回来了。他夜里睡不着觉。他的共和主义也一直都在。可你难道不知道吗，佩罗尔，世上可以有没有爱的嫉妒？"

"你这么认为，"漫游者深沉地说。他满腹自己的阅历，沉思着。"况且他也不是没尝过血的滋味，"他停了一停，嘟囔道。"你说得兴许没错。"

===Original===
"I may be right," repeated Catherine in a slightly indignant tone. "Every time I see Arlette near him I tremble lest it should come to words and to a bad blow. And when they are both out of my sight it is still worse. At this moment I am wondering where they are. They may be together and I daren't raise my voice to call her away for fear of rousing his fury."

"But it's the lieutenant he is after," observed Peyrol in a lowered voice. "Well, I can't stop the lieutenant coming back."

"Where is she? Where is he?" whispered Catherine in a tone betraying her secret anguish.

===Chinese===
"我兴许没错，"凯瑟琳略带愤愤地重复道。"每回我瞧见阿尔莱特在他跟前，我就打哆嗦，生怕两人闹起口角、进而动起手来出大事。而当他们两个都不在我眼前时，那就更糟了。这会儿我正琢磨他们都在哪儿。他们说不定正待在一处，而我却不敢提高嗓门把她叫走，生怕惹翻了他的火气。"

"可他要对付的是那中尉，"佩罗尔压低了声音说。"这个嘛，我可拦不住中尉回来。"

"她在哪儿？他在哪儿？"凯瑟琳低声耳语，口气里流露出她隐秘的痛苦。

===Original===
Peyrol rose quietly and went into the *salle*, leaving the door open. Catherine heard the latch of the outer door being lifted cautiously. In a few moments Peyrol returned as quietly as he had gone out.

"I stepped out to look at the weather. The moon is about to rise and the clouds have thinned down. One can see a star here and there." He lowered his voice considerably. "Arlette is sitting on the bench humming a little song to herself. I really wonder whether she knew I was standing within a few feet of her."

===Chinese===
佩罗尔悄然起身，走进正厅，让门敞着。凯瑟琳听见外头那扇门的门闩被小心翼翼地提起来的声响。不一会儿，佩罗尔像出去时一样悄无声息地回来了。

"我出去看了看天。月亮快升起来了，云也薄了。这儿那儿已经能看见星星了。"他着力压低了声音。"阿尔莱特正坐在长凳上，自己哼着一支小曲儿。我真纳闷，她到底知不知道我就站在离她几步远的地方。"

===Original===
"She doesn't want to hear or see anybody except one man," affirmed Catherine, now in complete control of her voice. "And she was humming a song, did you say? She who would sit for hours without making a sound. And God knows what song it could have been!"

"Yes, there's a great change in her," admitted Peyrol with a heavy sigh. "This lieutenant," he continued after a pause, "has always behaved coldly to her. I noticed him many times turn his face away when he saw her coming towards us. You know what these epaulette-wearers are, Catherine. And then this one has some worm of his own that is gnawing at him. I doubt whether he has ever forgotten that he was a ci-devant boy. Yet I do believe that she does not want to see and hear anybody but him. Is it because she has been deranged in her head for so long?"

===Chinese===
"除了一个男人，她谁也不想听见、谁也不想看见，"凯瑟琳肯定地说，此刻她的嗓音已完全镇定下来。"你说她在哼曲儿？她，那个能一连坐上几个钟头一声不吭的人。天知道那会是怎样一支曲子！"

"是的，她身上起了好大的变化，"佩罗尔叹息了一声，沉沉地承认道。"这个中尉，"他停了一下接着说，"对她一向冷淡。我不止一次注意到，一瞧见她朝我们走来，他就把脸别过去。你晓得那些佩戴肩章的家伙是什么货色，凯瑟琳。况且这一个，心里还有一条自己的蛆虫在啃噬着他。我怀疑他从来没有忘记过自己曾是个前贵族家的小子。可我倒确信，她不想见到、不想听到除他以外的任何人。这是因为她脑子胡涂了那么久的缘故吗？"

===Original===
"No, Peyrol," said the old woman. "It isn't that. You want to know how I can tell? For years nothing could make her either laugh or cry. You know that yourself. You have seen her every day. Would you believe that within the last month she has been both crying and laughing on my breast without knowing why?"

"This I don't understand," said Peyrol.

"But I do. That lieutenant has got only to whistle to make her run after him. Yes, Peyrol. That is so. She has no fear, no shame, no pride. I myself have been nearly like that." Her fine brown face seemed to grow more impassive before she went on much lower and as if arguing with herself: "Only I at least was never blood-mad. I was fit for any man's arms.… But then that man is not a priest."

===Chinese===
"不，佩罗尔，"老妇人说。"不是那样的。你想知道我是怎么看出来的？好些年来，什么都没法叫她笑一笑，也没法叫她哭一哭。这个你自己清楚。你天天见她。你能相信吗，就在这一个月之内，她趴在我怀里又哭又笑，自己却不知道为了什么？"

"这个我就不明白了。"佩罗尔说。

"可我明白。那中尉只消吹一声口哨，就能叫她跟在屁股后头跑。是的，佩罗尔。就是这样。她没有畏惧，没有羞耻，也没有自尊。我自己从前也几乎跟她一样。"她那张清秀的棕色脸庞似乎变得更加无动于衷了，随后她压低了得多地接着说，像是自己在跟自己争辩："只不过，我至少从没有为血发过狂。任何男人的怀抱都配得上我。……可那个男人，毕竟不是神父。"

===Original===
The last words made Peyrol start. He had almost forgotten that story. He said to himself: "She knows, she has had the experience."

"Look here, Catherine," he said decisively, "the lieutenant is coming back. He will be here probably about midnight. But one thing I can tell you: he is not coming back to whistle her away. Oh no! It is not for her sake that he will come back."

"Well, if it isn't for her that he is coming back then it must be because death has beckoned to him," she announced in a tone of solemn, unemotional conviction. "A man who has received a sign from death—nothing can stop him!"

===Chinese===
最后那句话叫佩罗尔一怔。他几乎已经把那桩往事忘了。他心里说："她知道的，她有切身的体会。"

"你听我说，凯瑟琳，"他断然说道，"那中尉是要回来的。他多半半夜就到。但有一件事我可以告诉你：他回来可不是为了把她吹着口哨带走的。哦，可不是！他回来，不是为了她的缘故。"

"那么，如果他不是为了她而回来，那就一定是因为死亡向他招了手，"她以一种庄严而不动感情的笃定口吻宣告。"一个接到了死亡信号的人——什么也拦不住他！"

===Original===
Peyrol, who had seen death face to face many times, looked at Catherine's fine brown profile curiously.

"It is a fact," he murmured, "that men who rush out to seek death do not often find it. So one must have a sign? What sort of sign would it be?"

"How is anybody to know?" asked Catherine, staring across the kitchen at the wall. "Even those to whom it is made do not recognize it for what it is. But they obey all the same. I tell you, Peyrol, nothing can stop them. It may be a glance, or a smile, or a shadow on the water, or a thought that passes through the head. For my poor brother and sister-in-law it was the face of their child."

===Chinese===
佩罗尔曾不止一次与死亡面对面地打过照面，这时好奇地端详着凯瑟琳那清秀的棕色侧脸。

"确有其事，"他低声道，"那些冲出去找死的人，往往反倒找不着死。这么说，非得有一个信号不可？那会是一种什么样的信号呢？"

"谁又能知道呢？"凯瑟琳问道，目光越过厨房直直地盯着那面墙。"即便是那些接到信号的人，也未必认得出它究竟是什么。可他们照样会俯首听命。我告诉你，佩罗尔，什么也拦不住他们。它也许是一个眼神，一个微笑，水面上一道影子，或是脑中闪过的一个念头。对我那可怜的哥哥和嫂子来说，那是他们孩子的脸。"

===Original===
Peyrol folded his arms on his breast and dropped his head. Melancholy was a sentiment to which he was a stranger; for what has melancholy to do with the life of a sea-rover, a Brother of the Coast, a simple, venturesome, precarious life, full of risks and leaving no time for introspection or for that momentary self-forgetfulness which is called gaiety. Sombre fury, fierce merriment, he had known in passing gusts, coming from outside; but never this intimate inward sense of the vanity of all things, that doubt of the power within himself.

"I wonder what the sign for me will be," he thought: and concluded with self-contempt that for him there would be no sign, that he would have to die in his bed like an old yard dog in his kennel. Having reached that depth of despondency, there was nothing more before him but a black gulf into which his consciousness sank like a stone.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔双臂交叠在胸前，垂下了头。忧郁是一种他所全然陌生的情感；因为忧郁同一个海上漫游者、一个海岸兄弟会成员的生活又有什么相干呢？那是一种淳朴的、冒险的、朝不保夕的生活，充满了风险，既不容人反躬自省，也不容人享受那片刻的忘我——那种忘我，人们称之为欢乐。阴沉的狂暴、猛烈的狂欢，他都曾经历过，不过是外头来的、一阵一阵的；可他从来不曾有过这种万事皆空的切身内在之感，不曾有过对自身之内那股力量的怀疑。

"我不知道，属于我的那个信号会是什么，"他想道：随即满怀自鄙地断定，他根本不会有任何信号，他只能像一条老得掉牙的看院狗死在自己的窝里那样，死在自己的床上。一旦沉到了那般沮丧的深处，他面前便别无他物，唯有一个黑洞洞的深渊，他的意识便如同一块石头似的沉了进去。

===Original===
The silence which had lasted perhaps a minute after Catherine had finished speaking was traversed suddenly by a clear high voice saying:

"What are you two plotting here?"

Arlette stood in the doorway of the *salle*. The gleam of light in the whites of her eyes set off her black and penetrating glance. The surprise was complete. The profile of Catherine, who was standing by the table, became, if possible, harder; a sharp carving of an old prophetess of some desert tribe. Arlette made three steps forward. In Peyrol even extreme astonishment was deliberate. He had been famous for never looking as though he had been caught unprepared. Age had accentuated that trait of a born leader. He only slipped off the edge of the table and said in his deep voice:

"Why, *patronne*! We haven't said a word to each other for ever so long."

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳说完之后，那阵大约持续了一分钟的沉寂，忽然被一个清亮高亢的声音划破了：

"你们两个在这儿密谋什么呢？"

阿尔莱特站在正厅的那扇门口。她眼白里的一星反光，越发衬出她那道漆黑的、摄人的目光。这一惊非同小可。站在桌边的凯瑟琳，那张侧脸如果说还能更冷硬的话，此刻便确实更冷硬了；那是一尊某个荒漠部族老女先知的尖锐雕像。阿尔莱特向前迈了三步。即便在极度的震惊之中，佩罗尔的一举一动也是从容不迫的。他向来以从不见措手不及之态而闻名。岁月更把这个天生的领袖所具有的特质放大了。他只是从桌沿上滑下身来，用他那低沉的嗓音说道：

"怎么啦，*patronne*（女主人）！我们俩彼此都有好久没说一句话了。"

===Original===
Arlette moved nearer still. "I know," she cried. "It was horrible. I have been watching you two. Scevola came and dumped himself on the bench close to me. He began to talk to me, and so I went away. That man bores me. And here I find you people saying nothing. It's insupportable. What has come to you both? Say, you, Papa Peyrol—don't you like me any more?" Her voice filled the kitchen. Peyrol went to the *salle* door and shut it. While coming back he was staggered by the brilliance of life within her that seemed to pale the flames of the lamp. He said half in jest:

"I don't know whether I didn't like you better when you were quieter."

"And you would like best to see me still quieter in my grave."

===Chinese===
阿尔莱特又凑近了些。"我知道，"她嚷道。"那太吓人了。我一直瞧着你们两个。塞沃拉过来一屁股坐在我身边的长凳上。他开始跟我说话，于是我便走开了。那个人叫我厌烦透顶。结果我到这儿来一看，你们这些人一个字也不说。这真叫人受不了。你们俩都怎么了？喂，你呀，佩罗尔老爹——你不再喜欢我了吗？"她的声音灌满了整个厨房。佩罗尔走到正厅那扇门前，把它关上了。他转身走回来时，她身上那种生命的璀璨——那璀璨似乎叫灯的火苗都黯然失色——叫他一阵发懵。他半开玩笑地说：

"我也不知道，从前你安安静静的时候，我是不是反而更喜欢你一些。"

"那你最乐意看到的，怕是我安安静静地躺在坟墓里吧。"

===Original===
She dazzled him. Vitality streamed out of her eyes, her lips, her whole person, enveloped her like a halo and… yes, truly, the faintest possible flush had appeared on her cheeks, played on them faintly rosy like the light of a distant flame on the snow. She raised her arms up in the air and let her hands fall from on high on Peyrol's shoulders, captured his desperately dodging eyes with her black and compelling glance, put out all her instinctive seduction—while he felt a growing fierceness in the grip of her fingers.

"No! I can't hold it in! Monsieur Peyrol, Papa Peyrol, old gunner, you horrid sea-wolf, be an angel and tell me where he is."

===Chinese===
她令他目眩。蓬勃的生气从她的眼睛、她的嘴唇、她的整个身躯里奔涌而出，像一圈光晕似的将她裹住，而且……是的，千真万确，一抹最淡最淡的红晕浮上了她的面颊，在那上面若有若无地漾着淡淡的玫瑰色，如同远处一丛火焰照在雪地上的光。她把双臂高高举向空中，然后让双手从高处落在佩罗尔的双肩上，用她那漆黑的、不可违抗的目光逮住了他那拼命躲闪的眼睛，施展出她全部本能的诱惑——而他则感到她手指的力道越攥越紧。

"不行！我忍不住啦！佩罗尔先生，佩罗尔老爹，老炮长啊，你这个可恶的海狼，行行好，做个天使吧，告诉我他在哪儿。"

===Original===
The rover, whom only that morning the powerful grasp of Lieutenant Réal found as unshakable as a rock, felt all his strength vanish under the hands of that woman. He said thickly:

"He has gone to Toulon. He had to go."

"What for? Speak the truth to me!"

"Truth is not for everybody to know," mumbled Peyrol, with a sinking sensation as though the very ground were going soft under his feet. "On service," he added in a growl.

===Chinese===
就在那天早上，雷亚尔中尉那只强有力的手还觉得这漫游者稳如磐石、不可撼动，可如今在那女人双手之下，他却感到自己浑身气力正在消退。他含混地说：

"他到土伦去了。他非去不可。"

"干什么去？跟我说实话！"

"实话可不是人人都配知道的，"佩罗尔嘟囔道，一阵下沉的感觉涌上来，仿佛脚底下的大地正在变软。"公干。"他瓮声瓮气地补了一句。

===Original===
Her hands slipped suddenly from his big shoulders. "On service?" she repeated. "What service?" Her voice sank and the words "Oh, yes! His service" were hardly heard by Peyrol, who as soon as her hands had left his shoulders felt his strength returning to him and the yielding earth grow firm again under his feet. Right in front of him Arlette, silent, with her arms hanging down before her with entwined fingers, seemed stunned because Lieutenant Réal was not free from all earthly connections, like a visiting angel from heaven depending only on God to whom she had prayed. She had to share him with some service that could order him about. She felt in herself a strength, a power, greater than any service.

===Chinese===
她的双手忽然从他宽厚的肩头滑落下来。"公干？"她重复道。"什么公干？"她的声音低了下去，那一句"哦，对了！他的公干"佩罗尔几乎听不见。而她的手一离开他的肩头，他便感到气力重新回到了自己身上，脚下那发软的大地也重新变得坚实了。在他正前方，阿尔莱特默默地站着，双臂垂在身前、十指交缠，似乎惊呆了——因为雷亚尔中尉竟不像一位从天而降、只仰仗她所祷告过的那位上帝的天使那般，不受任何尘世羁绊的束缚。她不得不与某种能向他发号施令的公干分享他。她在自己体内感到一种力量、一种权能，比任何公干都要强大。

===Original===
"Peyrol," she cried low, "don't break my heart, my new heart, that has just begun to beat. Feel how it beats. Who could bear it?" She seized the rover's thick hairy paw and pressed it hard against her breast. "Tell me when he will be back."

"Listen, *patronne*, you had better go upstairs," began Peyrol with a great effort and snatching his captured hand away. He staggered backwards a little while Arlette shouted at him:

"You can't order me about as you used to do." In all the changes from entreaty to anger she never struck a false note, so that her emotional outburst had the heart-moving power of inspired art. She turned round with a tempestuous swish to Catherine, who had neither stirred nor emitted a sound: "Nothing you two can do will make any difference now." The next moment she was facing Peyrol again. "You frighten me with your white hairs. Come!… am I to go on my knees to you?… There!"

===Chinese===
"佩罗尔，"她低低地唤道，"别伤我的心，我那颗崭新的心，那颗刚开始跳动的心。你摸摸它跳得多厉害。谁能受得了呢？"她一把抓起漫游者那只粗大长毛的手掌，用力把它按在自己胸口。"告诉我他什么时候回来。"

"你听我说，女主人，你最好上楼去，"佩罗尔费了好大的劲才开口，一边把被她抓住的手抽了回来。他踉跄着往后退了退，阿尔莱特却冲他嚷道：

"你再也不能像从前那样对我发号施令了。"从恳求到愠怒的种种变化之中，她没有走错过一个调子，因此她这一番情感的迸发具有一种如灵感艺术般打动人心的力量。她一阵旋风似的转过身去面对凯瑟琳——凯瑟琳既没有动弹，也没有发出一点声响："你们两个如今再做什么都无济于事了。"下一刻她又转回来面对佩罗尔了。"你拿你这一头白发吓唬我。来呀！……难道要我给你跪下不成？……这不！"

===Original===
The rover caught her under the elbows, swung her up clear of the ground, and set her down on her feet, as if she had been a child. Directly he had let her go she stamped her foot at him.

"Are you stupid?" she cried. "Don't you understand that something has happened today?"

Through all this scene Peyrol had kept his head as creditably as could have been expected, in the manner of a seaman caught by a white squall in the tropics. But at those words a dozen thoughts tried to rush together through his mind, in chase of that startling declaration. Something had happened! Where? How? Whom to? What thing? It couldn't be anything between her and the lieutenant. He had, it seemed to him, never lost sight of the lieutenant from the first hour when they met in the morning till he had sent him off to Toulon by an actual push on the shoulder; except while he was having his dinner in the next room with the door open and for the few minutes spent in talking with Michel in the yard. But that was only a very few minutes, and directly afterwards the first sight of the lieutenant sitting gloomily on the bench like a lonely crow did not suggest either elation or excitement or any emotion connected with a woman. In the face of these difficulties Peyrol's mind became suddenly a blank.

===Chinese===
漫游者一把抄住她的两肘，把她整个人提得双脚离地，又把她放下来站稳，仿佛她是个小孩子似的。他一松手，她便朝他跺了一脚。

"你是傻的吗？"她嚷道。"你难道不明白今天出了事吗？"

在这一整场风波里，佩罗尔尽可能地保持了镇定，其体面的程度，不亚于一个在热带海域遇上白色飑的海员。可一听到那句话，便有一打念头争先恐后地涌入他的脑海，去追赶那句惊人的宣告。出事了！在哪里？怎么出的？出在谁身上？什么事？那不可能是她和中尉之间的什么事。他自觉从清早两人头一回碰面起，直到他实实在在地推了他一把把他打发去土伦，他一刻也没有让那中尉离开过自己的视线；除了他在隔壁屋里开着门吃饭那阵，以及跟米歇尔在院子里说话的那几分钟之外。可那满共也就几分钟的工夫，而且紧接着之后，他头一眼瞧见中尉孤零零地、像只落单的乌鸦似的阴郁地坐在长凳上时，也看不出他有任何得意、兴奋或任何与女人有关的情绪。面对这些疑难，佩罗尔的脑子里忽然一片空白。

===Original===
"*Voyons, patronne*," he began, unable to think of anything else to say. "What's all this fuss about? I expect him to be back here about midnight."

He was extremely relieved to notice that she believed him. It was the truth. For indeed he did not know what he could have invented on the spur of the moment that would get her out of the way and induce her to go to bed. She treated him to a sinister frown and a terribly menacing "If you have lied.… Oh!"

He produced an indulgent smile. "Compose yourself. He will be here soon after midnight. You may go to sleep with an easy mind."

===Chinese===
"*Voyons*（得啦），女主人，"他开口道，除此之外他想不出别的话来说。"这闹腾的是哪一出啊？我等他大约半夜回到这儿来。"

他无比宽慰地注意到，她信了他的话。这本就是实话。因为说实话，他实在想不出自己能在仓促之间编出什么来，好把她支开、劝她去睡觉。她赏了他一道阴沉的怒目和一个可怕的威胁："你要是骗了我。……哦！"

他挤出一个宽纵的微笑。"你消消气。他过了半夜就到。你可以把心放到肚子里，踏踏实实去睡了。"

===Original===
She turned her back on him contemptuously, and said curtly, "Come along, aunt," and went to the door leading to the passage. There she turned for a moment with her hand on the door handle.

"You are changed. I can't trust either of you. You are not the same people."

She went out. Only then did Catherine detach her gaze from the wall to meet Peyrol's eyes. "Did you hear what she said? We! Changed! It is she herself.…"

Peyrol nodded twice, and there was a long pause during which even the flames of the lamp did not stir.

"Go after her, Mademoiselle Catherine," he said at last with a shade of sympathy in his tone. She did not move. "*Allons—du courage*," he urged her deferentially as it were. "Try to put her to sleep."

===Chinese===
她鄙夷地把背一转对他，简短地说了句"走吧，姑姑"，便朝通往过道的那扇门走去。走到门口，她手按在门把手上，回过头来停了一瞬。

"你们变了。你们两个我都没法信了。你们跟从前不一样了。"

她走了出去。直到那时，凯瑟琳才把目光从墙上移开，迎上了佩罗尔的眼睛。"你听见她说的话了吗？我们！变了！是她自己。……"

佩罗尔点了两次头，随后是一段长长的沉默，连灯上的火苗都纹丝不动。

"去跟着她吧，Mademoiselle Catherine（凯瑟琳小姐），"他终于开口说道，口气里带着一丝同情。她没有动。"*Allons—du courage*（来——拿出勇气来），"他几乎是恭恭敬敬地劝她。"想法子哄她睡吧。"

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
Upright and deliberate, Catherine left the kitchen, and in the passage outside found Arlette waiting for her with a lighted candle in her hand. Her heart was filled with sudden desolation by the beauty of that young face enhaloed in the patch of light, with the profound darkness as of a dungeon for a background. At once her niece led the way upstairs muttering savagely through her pretty teeth: “He thinks I could go to sleep. Old imbecile!”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳身板笔挺、不慌不忙地走出厨房，在外头的过道里碰见阿尔莱特正擎着一支点亮的蜡烛等她。那张年轻的脸庞被一圈光晕环绕，背后却是一片深不见底的黑暗，如同牢狱一般——这副情景使她心中蓦地涌起一阵凄凉。她侄女当即引着她上楼，从那排漂亮的牙缝里恶狠狠地嘟囔着：“他竟以为我睡得着。老糊涂！”

===Original===
Peyrol did not take his eyes off Catherine’s straight back till the door had closed after her. Only then he relieved himself by letting the air escape through his pursed lips and rolling his eyes freely about. He picked up the lamp by the ring on the top of the central rod and went into the *salle*, closing behind him the door of the dark kitchen. He stood the lamp on the very table on which Lieutenant Réal had had his midday meal. A small white cloth was still spread on it, and there was his chair askew as he had pushed it back when he got up. Another of the many chairs in the *salle* was turned round conspicuously to face the table. These things made Peyrol remark to himself bitterly: “She sat and stared at him as if he had been gilt all over, with three heads and seven arms on his body”—a comparison reminiscent of certain idols he had seen in an Indian temple. Though not an iconoclast, Peyrol felt positively sick at the recollection, and hastened to step outside. The great cloud had broken up and the mighty fragments were moving to the westward in stately flight before the rising moon. Scevola, who had been lying extended full length on the bench, swung himself up suddenly, very upright.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔目不转睛地盯着凯瑟琳那挺直的背影，直到她身后的门关上。这才松了口气，鼓着腮帮子把一口气从撮起的嘴唇间呼出去，眼珠子自在地骨碌碌乱转。他捏着灯芯杆顶端的那只环提起油灯，走进正厅（*salle*），随手关上了身后那扇黑魆魆的厨房门。他把灯搁在雷亚尔中尉晌午用饭的那张桌子上。桌上还铺着一块小小的白桌布，他的椅子也还歪斜着，是他起身时推开时的样子。正厅里那许多把椅子中，另有一把显眼地转过来正对着桌子。这些东西惹得佩罗尔酸溜溜地暗自嘀咕：“她就那么坐着盯着他看，活像他通体鎏金、身上长着三个脑袋七条胳膊似的”——这个比方叫他想起在某座印度神庙里见过的某些神像。佩罗尔虽不算个捣毁圣像的人，可一回想起那情景，心里竟当真一阵作呕，便赶紧迈步走到外头。那大片乌云已经散开，硕大的残块在初升的月亮前头，以庄严的飞行姿态向西移去。塞沃拉原本整个人直挺挺地躺在长凳上，这时忽然一翻身坐了起来，腰板挺得笔直。

===Original===
“Had a little nap in the open?” asked Peyrol, letting his eyes roam through the luminous space under the departing rearguard of the clouds jostling each other up there.

===Chinese===
“在外头打个盹了？”佩罗尔问，目光在头顶那片你推我挤、正徐徐退却的云隙下方那片明亮的天宇里游移。

===Original===
“I did not sleep,” said the sansculotte. “I haven’t closed my eyes—not for one moment.”

===Chinese===
“我没睡，”那无套裤汉说，“我压根儿没合过眼——一忽儿也没有。”

===Original===
“That must be because you weren’t sleepy,” suggested the deliberate Peyrol, whose thoughts were far away with the English ship. His mental eye contemplated her black image against the white beach of the Salins describing a sparkling curve under the moon; and meantime he went on slowly, “for it could not have been noise that kept you awake.” On the level of Escampobar the shadows lay long on the ground while the side of the lookout hill remained yet black but edged with an increasing brightness. And the amenity of the stillness was such that it softened for a moment Peyrol’s hard inward attitude towards all mankind, including even the captain of the English ship. The old rover savoured a moment of serenity in the midst of his cares.

===Chinese===
“那想必是你不困，”慢条斯理的佩罗尔揣测道，他的心思早已飞到了那条英国船上。他心眼儿里凝望着她那一团黑色的影子，衬在萨兰（Salins）那片白沙滩的背景上——沙滩在月光下划出一道闪闪发亮的弧线；与此同时他慢吞吞地接着说，“反正不会是吵闹声闹得你睡不着的。”在埃斯坎波巴这一片高度上，阴影长长地拖在地上，而那座瞭望小丘的一侧仍是黑黝黝的，只是镶上了一道渐渐增亮的光边。这万籁俱寂的那份宜人，竟一时软化了佩罗尔对全人类——甚至包括那条英国船的舰长在内——那一向冷硬的内心姿态。这位老漫游者在满腹心事之中，品味了片刻的宁静。

===Original===
“This is an accursed spot,” declared Scevola suddenly.

===Chinese===
“这是个遭天谴的地方。”塞沃拉忽然说道。

===Original===
Peyrol, without turning his head, looked at him sideways. Though he had sprung up from his reclining posture smartly enough, the citizen had gone slack all over and was sitting all in a heap. His shoulders were hunched up, his hands reposed on his knees. With his staring eyes he resembled a sick child in the moonlight.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔头也不回，只拿眼角斜睨着他。那公民虽说起身起得相当利落，这会儿整个人却又松垮了下来，缩成一团坐在那儿。他耸着肩膀，两只手搁在膝盖上。月光下，他那双瞪着的眼睛，活脱脱像个生了病的孩子。

===Original===
“It’s the very spot for hatching treacheries. One feels steeped in them up to the neck.”

===Chinese===
“这地方天生就是孵育阴谋诡计的所在。人待在这儿，简直像被泡在里头，没到了脖子。”

===Original===
He shuddered and yawned a long irresistible nervous yawn with the gleam of unexpected long canines in a retracted, gaping mouth giving away the restless panther lurking in the man.

===Chinese===
他打了个寒颤，接着打了一个长长的、无法抑制的神经质的呵欠，那张咧开的嘴里往后收缩的牙床间，闪出一排出乎意料的长犬齿，泄露了潜伏在这男人骨子里的那头躁动不安的豹子。

===Original===
“Oh yes, there’s treachery about right enough. You couldn’t conceive that, *citoyen*?”

===Chinese===
“哦，可不是嘛，这儿到处都是阴谋诡计，一点儿没错。这个你可想不出来吧，*citoyen*（公民）？”

===Original===
“Of course I couldn’t,” assented Peyrol with serene contempt. “What is this treachery that you are concocting?” he added carelessly, in a social way, while enjoying the charm of a moonlit evening. Scevola, who did not expect that turn, managed, however, to produce a rattling sort of laugh almost at once.

===Chinese===
“我自然是想不出来的。”佩罗尔以一种安详的轻蔑应和道。“你这是在暗地里捣的什么鬼呢？”他又随口补了一句，像闲谈似的，一边仍享受着这月夜的迷人。塞沃拉没料到话头会这么一转，不过他还是几乎立刻就挤出了一阵嘎嘎作响的干笑。

===Original===
“That’s a good one, ha! ha! ha!… Me!… concocting!… Why me?”

===Chinese===
“这话妙得很，哈！哈！哈！……我！……捣鬼！……凭什么是我？”

===Original===
“Well,” said Peyrol carelessly, “there are not many of us to carry out treacheries about here. The women are gone upstairs; Michel is down at the tartane. There’s me, and you would not dare suspect me of treachery. Well, there remains only you.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，”佩罗尔漫不经心地说，“这一带能搞阴谋诡计的，统共也没有几个人。女人们都上了楼；米歇尔在塔尔塔纳那儿。剩下的就是我，你总不敢疑心我吧。那么，就只剩下你了。”

===Original===
Scevola roused himself. “This is not much of a jest,” he said. “I have been a treason-hunter. I.…”

===Chinese===
塞沃拉打起精神。“这种玩笑可不高明，”他说，“我从前是个缉拿叛徒的行家。我……”

===Original===
He checked that strain. He was full of purely emotional suspicions. Peyrol was talking like this only to annoy him and to get him out of the way; but in the particular state of his feelings Scevola was acutely aware of every syllable of these offensive remarks. “Aha,” he thought to himself, “he doesn’t mention the lieutenant.” This omission seemed to the patriot of immense importance. If Peyrol had not mentioned the lieutenant it was because those two had been plotting some treachery together, all the afternoon on board that tartane. That’s why nothing had been seen of them for the best part of the day. As a matter of fact, Scevola too had observed Peyrol returning to the farm in the evening, only he had observed him from another window than Arlette. This was a few minutes before his attempt to open the lieutenant’s door, in order to find out whether Réal was in his room. He had tiptoed away, uncertain, and going into the kitchen had found only Catherine and Peyrol there. Directly Arlette joined them a sudden inspiration made him run upstairs and try the door again. It was open now! A clear proof that it was Arlette who had been locked up in there. The discovery that she made herself at home like this in the lieutenant’s room gave Scevola such a sickening shock that he thought he would die of it. It was beyond doubt now that the lieutenant had been conspiring with Peyrol down on board that tartane; for what else could they have been doing there. But why had not Réal come up in the evening with Peyrol? Scevola asked himself, sitting on the bench with his hands clasped between his knees.… “It’s their cunning,” he concluded suddenly. “Conspirators always avoid being seen together. Ha!”

===Chinese===
他刹住了这个话头。他满心都是些纯然出于情绪的猜疑。佩罗尔这么说话，无非是为了招惹他、把他支开罢了；可在眼下这种心境里，塞沃拉对这番带刺的话每一个字眼都格外敏感。“啊哈，”他暗自思忖，“他没提那个中尉。”在爱国者看来，这一处遗漏关系重大。佩罗尔之所以没有提起中尉，定是因为他们两人一整个下午都在那条塔尔塔纳上密谋着什么诡计。正因如此，这一整天大半的时间里都见不着他们俩的影子。事实上，傍晚佩罗尔回农庄来，塞沃拉也瞧见了，只不过他是从另一扇窗子里瞧见的，不像阿尔莱特。那是在他试图去推中尉的房门、好探明雷亚尔究竟在不在自己屋里之前几分钟的事。他踮着脚悄悄走开了，心里拿不准，走进厨房时却只看见凯瑟琳和佩罗尔两人在那里。阿尔莱特一进来，他心头灵光一闪，便奔上楼去，再去推那扇门。这一回门开了！这分明证明，方才被锁在里头的正是阿尔莱特。她竟这般在中尉的房里安之若素、如在自己房中一般——这一发现给了塞沃拉一阵恶心透顶的震惊，他以为自己会被活活震死。如今是毫无疑问了：中尉和佩罗尔一直在下面那条塔尔塔纳上密谋；要不然他们在那儿还能做什么呢。可雷亚尔傍晚为什么不跟佩罗尔一道上来呢？塞沃拉问自己，坐在长凳上，两手攥在膝盖之间。……“这是他们的狡猾，”他忽然断定，“密谋的人总是避免让人瞧见他们待在一块儿。哈！”

===Original===
“Have I been a slave to those two women, have I waited all those years, only to see that corrupt creature go off infamously with a ci-devant, with a conspiring aristocrat?”

===Chinese===
“难道我给那两个女人当了这么多年的奴才，苦等了这许多年，到头来却眼睁睁看着那个堕落的东西，跟一个 ci-devant（前贵族）、一个暗中搞鬼的贵族，臭名昭著地私奔了不成？”

===Original===
He became giddy with virtuous fury. There was enough evidence there for any revolutionary tribunal to cut all their heads off. Tribunal! There was no tribunal! No revolutionary justice! No patriots! He hit his shoulder against the wall in his distress with such force that he rebounded. This world was no place for patriots.

===Chinese===
他被一种义愤冲得头昏目眩。那里的罪证，足够送交任何一个革命法庭把他们统统砍了脑袋。法庭！哪儿还有什么法庭！没有什么革命的正义！没有什么爱国者了！他在痛苦中用肩膀撞墙，撞得那样重，整个人都被弹了回来。这个世界根本不是爱国者待的地方。

===Original===
“If I had betrayed myself in the kitchen they would have murdered me in there.”

===Chinese===
“要是在厨房里我露了马脚，他们早把我谋害在里头了。”

===Original===
As it was he thought that he had said too much. Too much. “Prudence! Caution!” he repeated to himself, gesticulating with both arms. Suddenly he stumbled, and there was an amazing metallic clatter made by something that fell at his feet.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，他还是觉得自己话说得太多了。太多了。“小心！谨慎！”他一边暗自念叨，一边两手比划着。忽然他绊了一跤，有什么东西掉在他脚边，发出一阵惊人嘁嚓哐啷的金属响声。

===Original===
“They are trying to kill me now,” he thought, shaking with fright. He gave himself up for dead. Profound silence reigned all round. Nothing more happened. He stooped fearfully to look and recognized his own stable fork lying on the ground. He remembered he had left it at noon leaning against the wall. His own foot had made it fall. He threw himself upon it greedily. “Here’s what I need,” he muttered feverishly. “I suppose that by now the lieutenant would think I am gone to bed.”

===Chinese===
“他们这会儿想要我的命了。”他想着，吓得直哆嗦。他自忖必死无疑。四下里一片死寂。什么也没再发生。他战战兢兢地弯腰一看，认出躺在地上的原来是自己的那把马厩草叉。他记起晌午时分自己把它靠在了墙上。是他自己的脚把它碰倒的。他如获至宝地一把抓起它。“这正是我用得着的东西，”他发热地喃喃自语，“我估摸这会儿那中尉准以为我已经上床睡了。”

===Original===
He flattened himself upright against the wall with the fork held along his body like a grounded musket. The moon clearing the hilltop flooded suddenly the front of the house with its cold light, but he didn’t know it; he imagined himself still to be ambushed in the shadow and remained motionless, glaring at the path leading towards the cove. His teeth chattered with savage impatience.

===Chinese===
他把身子笔直地贴着墙站住，那把草叉顺着身子握着，活像一杆立在地上、枪托朝下的火枪。月亮翻过山顶，一下子用它清冷的光注满了屋子的正面，可他并不知道；他自以为仍埋伏在阴影里，纹丝不动地站着，虎视眈眈地瞪着通往小海湾的那条小径。他气急败坏地咬得牙关咯咯作响。

===Original===
He was so plainly visible in his deathlike rigidity that Michel, coming up out of the ravine, stopped dead short, believing him an apparition not belonging to this earth. Scevola, on his side, noticed the moving shadow cast by a man—that man!—and charged forward without reflection, the prongs of the fork lowered like a bayonet. He didn’t shout. He came straight on, growling like a dog, and lunged headlong with his weapon.

===Chinese===
他那死一般僵直的模样，在月光下清晰可见，米歇尔从沟壑里爬上来，猛地收住脚步，只当他是个不属于这尘世的鬼影。塞沃拉那一边，则注意到了一个人投下移动的影子——就是那个人！——他想也不想就冲了上去，草叉的几个齿像刺刀一样压低了平端着。他没有喊叫。他径直冲来，像狗一样低吼着，端着家伙一头猛扎过去。

===Original===
Michel, a primitive untroubled by anything so uncertain as intelligence, executed an instantaneous sideways leap with the precision of a wild animal; but he was enough of a man to become afterwards paralysed with astonishment. The impetus of the rush carried Scevola several yards down the hill, before he could turn round and assume an offensive attitude. Then the two adversaries recognized each other. The terrorist exclaimed: “Michel!” and Michel hastened to pick up a large stone from the ground.

===Chinese===
米歇尔这个原始人，丝毫没有被智力这种靠不住的东西所困扰，像野兽一般精准地朝旁边一跃便闪开了；可他到底还算个人，事后竟惊得呆若木鸡。塞沃拉冲势不减，一下子顺着山坡冲出去好几码远，才得以转过身来摆出进攻的架子。随即两个对手认出了彼此。那恐怖分子叫了一声：“米歇尔！”米歇尔则赶忙从地上抄起一块大石头。

===Original===
“Hey, you, Scevola,” he cried, not very loud but very threatening. “What are these tricks?… Keep away, or I will heave that piece of rock at your head, and I am good at that.”

===Chinese===
“喂，你，塞沃拉，”他喊道，声音不高，却十分狠厉，“你这是耍什么把戏？……别过来，要不我就把这块石头砸到你脑袋上，这一手我可在行。”

===Original===
Scevola grounded the fork with a thud. “I didn’t recognize you,” he said.

===Chinese===
塞沃拉把草叉往地上一顿，咚的一声。“我没认出是你。”他说。

===Original===
“That’s a story. Who did you think I was? Not the other! I haven’t got a bandaged head, have I?”

===Chinese===
“少来这套。你以为我是谁？总不是那一位吧！我头上又没缠绷带，是吧？”

===Original===
Scevola began to scramble up. “What’s this?” he asked. “What head did you say?”

===Chinese===
塞沃拉手脚并用地爬上来。“这是怎么说？”他问，“你说什么头来着？”

===Original===
“I say that if you come near I will knock you over with that stone,” answered Michel. “You aren’t to be trusted when the moon is full. Not recognize! There’s a silly excuse for flying at people like this. You haven’t got anything against me, have you?”

===Chinese===
“我是说，你要是敢靠近，我就用这块石头把你砸翻，”米歇尔回答，“月圆的时候你这个人靠不住。没认出来！朝人这么扑上来，倒是好一个蹩脚的借口。你跟我没什么过不去的吧，啊？”

===Original===
“No,” said the ex-terrorist in a dubious tone and keeping a watchful eye on Michel, who was still holding the stone in his hand.

===Chinese===
“没有。”前恐怖分子语气含糊地说，一边警惕地盯着仍把石头攥在手里的米歇尔。

===Original===
“People have been saying for years that you are a kind of lunatic,” Michel criticized fearlessly, because the other’s discomfiture was evident enough to put heart into the timid hare. “If a fellow cannot come up now to get a snooze in the shed without being run at with a fork, well.…”

===Chinese===
“人家都说你好些年了，说你是个半疯，”米歇尔毫无惧色地数落道，因为对方那副狼狈相已够明显，足以给这只胆小的野兔壮起胆来。“要是这会儿一个人上来，想在棚子里打个盹都不行，还得挨一草叉冲过来，那可真是……”

===Original===
“I was only going to put this fork away,” Scevola burst out volubly. “I had left it leaning against the wall, and as I was passing along I suddenly saw it, so I thought I would put it in the stable before I went to bed. That’s all.”

===Chinese===
“我不过是想把这把草叉放回去，”塞沃拉滔滔不绝地嚷道，“我先前把它靠在墙上了，我打这儿经过，忽然一眼瞧见它，就想着在上床睡觉之前把它送进马厩里去。就这么回事。”

===Original===
Michel’s mouth fell open a bit.

===Chinese===
米歇尔那张嘴微微张开了一点。

===Original===
“Now what do you think I would want with a stable fork at this time of night, if it wasn’t to put it away?” argued Scevola.

===Chinese===
“你说这大半夜的，我要一把马厩的草叉做什么，要不是为了把它放回去？”塞沃拉振振有词地辩道。

===Original===
“What indeed!” mumbled Michel, who began to doubt the evidence of his senses.

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛！”米歇尔嘟囔着，开始对自己亲眼所见也起了疑心。

===Original===
“You go about mooning like a fool and imagine a lot of silly things, you great stupid imbecile. All I wanted to do was to ask whether everything was all right down there, and you, idiot, bound to one side like a goat and pick up a stone. The moon has affected your head, not mine. Now drop it.”

===Chinese===
“你像个傻子似的成天迷迷瞪瞪地瞎转悠，编排出一堆蠢事来，你这个大笨蛋。我不过是想问一声，下头一切可还太平，你这个蠢货，倒像只山羊似的朝旁边一蹦，还捡起一块石头。被月亮搅坏脑子的，是你，不是我。把石头放下。”

===Original===
Michel, accustomed to do what he was told, opened his fingers slowly, not quite convinced but thinking there might be something in it. Scevola, perceiving his advantage, scolded on:

===Chinese===
米歇尔一向听人吩咐，便慢慢地张开了手指，心里虽还不全信，却想着这话兴许也有几分道理。塞沃拉一见自己占了上风，便接着数落下去：

===Original===
“You are dangerous. You ought to have your feet and hands tied every full moon. What did you say about a head just now? What head?”

===Chinese===
“你这个人危险得很。每月月圆的时候，都该把你的手脚捆起来才对。你刚才说什么头来着？什么头？”

===Original===
“I said that I didn’t have a broken head.”

===Chinese===
“我说的是，我的脑袋没破。”

===Original===
“Was that all?” said Scevola. He was asking himself what on earth could have happened down there during the afternoon to cause a broken head. Clearly, it must have been either a fight or an accident, but in any case he considered that it was for him a favourable circumstance, for obviously a man with a bandaged head is at a disadvantage. He was inclined to think it must have been some silly accident, and he regretted profoundly that the lieutenant had not killed himself outright. He turned sourly to Michel.

===Chinese===
“就这些？”塞沃拉说。他心里暗自纳闷，下午下头到底出了什么事，才会弄出一个缠绷带的脑袋来。明摆着，那要么是打了一场架，要么是出了什么意外，可无论如何他都认为，这于他是一桩有利的情形，因为脑袋缠着绷带的人，显然是要吃亏的。他倾向于认为那必是哪桩愚蠢的意外，并且深以为憾的是，那中尉没有当场摔死。他没好气地转向米歇尔。

===Original===
“Now you may go into the shed. And don’t try any of your tricks with me any more, because next time you pick up a stone I will shoot you like a dog.”

===Chinese===
“现在你可以进棚子里去了。往后别再跟我耍什么花招，因为下回你要是再捡石头，我就像打狗一样一枪崩了你。”

===Original===
He began to move towards the yard gate which stood always open, throwing over his shoulder an order to Michel: “Go into the *salle*. Somebody has left a light in there. They all seem to have gone crazy today. Take the lamp into the kitchen and put it out, and see that the door into the yard is shut. I am going to bed.” He passed through the gateway, but he did not penetrate into the yard very far. He stopped to watch Michel obeying the order. Scevola, advancing his head cautiously beyond the pillar of the gate, waited till he had seen Michel open the door of the *salle* and then bounded out again across the level space, and down the ravine path. It was a matter of less than a minute. His fork was still on his shoulder. His only desire was not to be interfered with, and for the rest he did not care what they all did, what they would think and how they would behave. The fixed idea had taken complete possession of him. He had no plan, but he had a principle on which to act; and that was to get at the lieutenant unawares, and if the fellow died without knowing what hand had struck him, so much the better. Scevola was going to act in the cause of virtue and justice. It was not to be a matter of personal contest at all. Meantime, Michel, having gone into the *salle*, had discovered Peyrol fast asleep on a table. Though his reverence for Peyrol was unbounded, his simplicity was such that he shook his master by the shoulder as he would have done any common mortal. The rover passed from a state of inertia into a sitting posture so quickly that Michel stepped back a pace and waited to be addressed. But as Peyrol only stared at him, Michel took the initiative in a concise phrase:

===Chinese===
他开始朝那扇一向敞着的院门走去，扭头冲米歇尔撂下一道吩咐：“进正厅去。有人在里头留了一盏灯。今儿个他们像是都疯了。把灯拎进厨房，灭了它，再看清通往院子的那扇门关上没有。我要睡了。”他穿过了门洞，却没有往院子里走多远。他停下来看米歇尔执行命令。塞沃拉小心翼翼地把脑袋探出门柱之外，等看见米歇尔推开了正厅的门，便又纵身窜了出去，穿过那片平地，顺着沟壑的小径奔了下去。前后不过一分钟的事。他的草叉仍扛在肩上。他唯一的念头，就是别让人碍他的事；至于别的，他们一个个都干了什么、会怎么想、会怎样行事，他全不在乎。那个执拗的念头已把他整个儿占据了。他并没有什么计划，却有一条行事的原则；那就是要趁那中尉猝不及防的时候下手，要是那家伙到死都不知道是哪只手打的他，那就再好不过。塞沃拉这是要替天行道、主持正义。这压根儿不是什么个人的较量。与此同时，米歇尔进了正厅，却发现佩罗尔正趴在一张桌子上睡得死沉。虽说他对佩罗尔的敬畏是无以复加的，可他那副憨直的性子，竟使他去摇主子的肩膀，就像对待任何一个寻常凡人那样。漫游者从一团死沉的状态，一下子变成了坐着的姿势，快得米歇尔不由得倒退一步，等着对方开口。可佩罗尔只是瞪着他，米歇尔便先开了口，话又短又干脆：

===Original===
“He’s at it!”

===Chinese===
“他在动手了！”

===Original===
Peyrol did not seem completely awake: “What is it you mean?” he asked.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔似乎还没有完全清醒：“你这话什么意思？”他问。

===Original===
“He is making motions to escape.”

===Chinese===
“他正在想辙逃跑呢。”

===Original===
Peyrol was wide awake now. He even swung his feet off the table.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔这回彻底清醒了。他甚至把两只脚从桌子上甩了下来。

===Original===
“Is he? Haven’t you locked the cabin door?”

===Chinese===
“是吗？你没把舱门锁上？”

===Original===
Michel, very frightened, explained that he had never been told to do that.

===Chinese===
米歇尔十分惊惶，解释说从没人吩咐他那么做。

===Original===
“No?” remarked Peyrol placidly. “I must have forgotten.” But Michel remained agitated, and murmured: “He is escaping.”

===Chinese===
“没有吗？”佩罗尔平静地说，“那准是我忘了。”可米歇尔仍是心神不宁，嘟囔着：“他要跑了。”

===Original===
“That’s all right,” said Peyrol. “What are you fussing about? How far can he escape, do you think?”

===Chinese===
“没事儿，”佩罗尔说，“你急个什么劲儿？你说他能跑多远？”

===Original===
A slow grin appeared on Michel’s face. “If he tries to scramble over the top of the rocks, he will get a broken neck in no time,” he said. “And he certainly won’t get very far, that’s a fact.”

===Chinese===
米歇尔脸上慢慢绽出一个憨笑。“他要是不自量力想从岩石顶上翻过去，转眼就得摔断脖子，”他说，“他无论如何也跑不了多远，这是实情。”

===Original===
“Well—you see,” said Peyrol.

===Chinese===
“这不就结了——你瞧。”佩罗尔说。

===Original===
“And he doesn’t seem strong either. He crawled out of the cabin door and got as far as the little water cask and he dipped and dipped into it. It must be half empty by now. After that he got on to his legs. I cleared out ashore directly I heard him move,” he went on in a tone of intense self-approval. “I hid myself behind a rock and watched him.”

===Chinese===
“再说他瞧着也不像有多硬朗。他从舱门里爬出来，挪到那只小水桶跟前，一勺一勺地舀着水喝，一勺接一勺。那桶水这会儿怕是去了一半了。之后他才站起来。我一听见他那儿有动静，立马就上了岸，”他用一种极其自我满意的口气接着说，“我躲在一块石头后头盯着他。”

===Original===
“Quite right,” observed Peyrol. After that word of commendation, Michel’s face wore a constant grin.

===Chinese===
“做得对。”佩罗尔评道。得了这一句夸奖，米歇尔的脸上便一直挂着憨笑。

===Original===
“He sat on the afterdeck,” he went on as if relating an immense joke, “with his feet dangling down the hold, and may the devil take me if I don’t think he had a nap with his back against the cask. He was nodding and catching himself up, with that big white head of his. Well, I got tired of watching that, and as you told me to keep out of his way, I thought I would come up here and sleep in the shed. That was right, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“他在后甲板上坐着，”他接着说，仿佛在讲一桩天大的笑话，“两只脚悬在船舱口晃荡，让魔鬼把我收了去吧，我可笃定他是靠着那水桶打了个盹儿。他那颗又大又白的脑袋一点一点地直往下栽，栽下去又猛地一撑。后来嘛，我盯得也腻了，再说你叫过我要躲着他点儿，我就寻思着上来，到棚子里睡一觉。这一桩我没做错吧？”

===Original===
“Quite right,” repeated Peyrol. “Well, you go now into the shed. And so you left him sitting on the afterdeck?”

===Chinese===
“很对。”佩罗尔又说了一遍，“好了，你这会儿进棚子里去吧。这么说，你是把他留在后甲板上坐着了？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Michel. “But he was rousing himself. I hadn’t got away more than ten yards when I heard an awful thump on board. I think he tried to get up and fell down the hold.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”米歇尔说，“可他那时正在醒转过来。我才走出去不到十步远，就听见船上咚的一声闷响。我琢磨他是想站起来，结果一头栽进了船舱里。”

===Original===
“Fell down the hold?” repeated Peyrol sharply.

===Chinese===
“栽进船舱里了？”佩罗尔尖锐地追问。

===Original===
“Yes, *notre maître*. I thought at first I would go back and see, but you had warned me against him, hadn’t you? And I really think that nothing can kill him.”

===Chinese===
“是的，*notre maître*（我们的老大）。我起先想着要回去瞧瞧，可你交代过要防着他，不是吗？再说我实实在在地觉得，什么东西也弄不死他。”

===Original===
Peyrol got down from the table with an air of concern which would have astonished Michel, if he had not been utterly incapable of observing things.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔面带忧色地从桌子上下来——这副神情本会让米歇尔大为惊诧，若不是他根本毫无观察事物的能力的话。

===Original===
“This must be seen to,” murmured the rover, buttoning the waistband of his trousers. “My cudgel there, in the corner. Now you go to the shed. What the devil are you doing at the door? Don’t you know the way to the shed?” This last observation was caused by Michel remaining in the doorway of the *salle* with his head out and looking to right and left along the front of the house. “What’s come to you? You don’t suppose he has been able to follow you so quick as this up here?”

===Chinese===
“这事得去看看。”漫游者嘟囔着，扣上了裤腰。“我的棍子，在那边角落里。现在你进棚子去。你在门口磨蹭什么鬼？你不认得去棚子的路吗？”最后这一句，是因为米歇尔仍站在正厅门口，探着脑袋，朝屋子的正面左右张望。“你是怎么了？你该不是以为他能这么快就跟到你上头来了吧？”

===Original===
“Oh no, *notre maître*, quite impossible. I saw that *sacré* Scevola promenading up and down here. I don’t want to meet him again.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不会的，*notre maître*，绝不可能。我瞧见那个 *sacré*（该死的）塞沃拉在这儿来回溜达。我可不想再撞上他了。”

===Original===
“Was he promenading outside,” asked Peyrol, with annoyance. “Well, what do you think he can do to you? What notions have you got in your silly head? You are getting worse and worse. Out you go.”

===Chinese===
“他在外头溜达？”佩罗尔恼火地问，“那又怎样，你以为他能把你怎么样？你那颗蠢脑袋里装的都是些什么念头？你是越发不像话了。出去。”

===Original===
Peyrol extinguished the lamp and, going out, closed the door without the slightest noise. The intelligence about Scevola being on the move did not please him very much, but he reflected that probably the sansculotte had fallen asleep again, and after waking up was on his way to bed when Michel caught sight of him. He had his own view on the patriot’s psychology and did not think the women were in any danger. Nevertheless he went to the shed and heard the rustling of straw as Michel settled himself for the night.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔灭了灯，走出去，没有发出一丝声响地关上了门。关于塞沃拉还在外头走动的消息，他听得并不十分痛快，可他转念一想，那无套裤汉多半又睡着了，醒来之后正回屋去睡觉，恰被米歇尔撞见。他对那爱国者的心思自有他的看法，并不觉得女人们会有什么危险。不过他还是走到棚子那边，听见米歇尔安顿下来过夜时稻草发出的窸窣声。

===Original===
“*Debout*,” he cried low. “Sh, don’t make any noise. I want you to go into the house and sleep at the bottom of the stairs. If you hear voices, go up, and if you see Scevola about, knock him down. You aren’t afraid of him, are you?”

===Chinese===
“*Debout*（起来），”他压低嗓子喊，“嘘，别出声。我要你进屋里去，睡在楼梯脚下。你要是听见有人说话，就上去，要是瞧见塞沃拉在那儿晃荡，就把他放翻。你不怕他吧？”

===Original===
“No, if you tell me not to be,” said Michel, who, picking up his shoes, a present from Peyrol, walked barefoot towards the house. The rover watched him slipping noiselessly through the *salle* door. Having thus, so to speak, guarded his base, Peyrol proceeded down the ravine with a very deliberate caution. When he got as far as the little hollow in the ground from which the mastheads of the tartane could be seen, he squatted and waited. He didn’t know what his prisoner had done or was doing, and he did not want to blunder into the way of his escape. The day-old moon was high enough to have shortened the shadows almost to nothing and all the rocks were inundated by a yellow sheen, while the bushes by contrast looked very black. Peyrol reflected that he was not very well concealed. The continued silence impressed him in the end. “He has got away,” he thought. And yet he was not sure. Nobody could be sure. He reckoned it was about an hour since Michel had left the tartane; time enough for a man, even on all fours, to crawl down to the shore of the cove. Peyrol wished he had not hit so hard. His object could have been attained with half the force. On the other hand all the proceedings of his prisoner, as reported by Michel, seemed quite rational. Naturally the fellow was badly shaken. Peyrol felt as though he wanted to go on board and give him some encouragement, and even active assistance.

===Chinese===
“不怕，只要您叫我别怕。”米歇尔说，他拾起佩罗尔送他的那双鞋，光着脚朝屋子走去。漫游者望着他无声无息地溜进了正厅的门。如此——不妨说——把自己大本营看守妥当之后，佩罗尔便以一种十分审慎的小心，顺着沟壑走下去。他走到地面上那个能望见塔尔塔纳桅顶的小凹地，便蹲下来等候。他不知道他的囚徒做了什么、正在做什么，也不想莽莽撞撞地撞到对方逃走的路上去。那轮初生方一日的月亮已经升得够高，把影子缩得几近于无，所有的岩石都浸在一片黄澄澄的光晕里，相形之下，丛丛灌木显得漆黑。佩罗尔暗想自己藏得并不十分严实。那持续不断的寂静，到底叫他受了震动。“他跑掉了。”他想。可又拿不准。谁也拿不准。他估算，米歇尔离开塔尔塔纳大约已经有一个钟头了；这点时间，足够一个人哪怕手脚并用、爬到小海湾的岸边去了。佩罗尔暗暗后悔自己下手太重。他的目的，原本只用一半力气便能达到。可话又说回来，据米歇尔所报，他那个囚徒的一举一动，倒显得相当合乎常理。那家伙自然是被摔得不轻。佩罗尔竟觉得，自己想上船去给他打打气，甚至搭把手帮衬一二。

===Original===
The report of a gun from seaward cut his breath short as he lay there meditating. Within a minute there was a second report, sending another wave of deep sound among the crags and hills of the peninsula. The ensuing silence was so profound that it seemed to extend to the very inside of Peyrol’s head, and lull all his thoughts for a moment. But he had understood. He said to himself that after this his prisoner, if he had life enough left in him to stir a limb, would rather die than not try to make his way to the seashore. The ship was calling to her man.

===Chinese===
他正躺在那儿沉思，海上传来一声炮响，惊得他倒吸一口气。不出一分钟，又是第二声，把又一道深沉的声浪送进了半岛的嶙峋礁石和山峦之间。随之而来的那片寂静，深沉得仿佛一直延伸到了佩罗尔的脑壳里头，一时之间把他所有的念头都哄睡了过去。可他已经明白了。他暗自说，这一来他的囚徒，只要还剩得一口气、能动弹一根指头，宁可死，也非得拼着命摸到海边去不可。那条船在召唤她的人。

===Original===
In fact those two guns had proceeded from the *Amelia*. After passing beyond Cape Esterel, Captain Vincent dropped an anchor underfoot off the beach just as Peyrol had surmised he would do. From about six o’clock till nine the *Amelia* lay there with her unfurled sails hanging in the gear. Just before the moon rose the captain came up on deck and after a short conference with his first lieutenant, directed the master to get the ship under way and put her head again for the Petite Passe. Then he went below, and presently word was passed on deck that the captain wanted Mr. Bolt. When the master’s mate appeared in his cabin, Captain Vincent motioned him to a chair.

===Chinese===
事实上，那两声炮响是从阿米莉亚号（*Amelia*）上发出来的。越过埃斯泰雷尔角之后，文森特舰长正如佩罗尔所料想的那样，在海滩外头于船首抛下了一枚锚。从大约六点钟到九点，阿米莉亚号就停在那儿，展开的帆挂在索具上耷拉着。月亮升起之前，舰长来到甲板上，跟大副简短商议了几句之后，便吩咐舵手长起锚开船，把船头重新对准小水道（Petite Passe）。随后他下了舱，不一会儿，甲板上便传下话来，说舰长要见博尔特先生（Bolt）。待那位准尉走进他的舱房，文森特舰长示意他坐下。

===Original===
“I don’t think I ought to have listened to you,” he said. “Still, the idea was fascinating, but how it would strike other people it is hard to say. The losing of our man is the worst feature. I have an idea that we might recover him. He may have been captured by the peasants or have met with an accident. It’s unbearable to think of him lying at the foot of some rock with a broken leg. I have ordered the first and second cutters to be manned, and I propose that you should take command of them and enter the cove and, if necessary, advance a little inland to investigate. As far as we know there have never been any troops on that peninsula. The first thing you will do is to examine the coast.”

===Chinese===
“我本不该听你的，”他说，“不过，这主意确实诱人，只是换了旁人会怎么看，就难说了。丢了我们的人，这是最糟的一桩。我倒是觉着，我们或许还能把他找回来。他也许被乡民们抓住了，也许遇上了什么意外。一想到他正躺在哪块岩石脚下、断了一条腿，实在叫人受不了。我已经下令一号、二号舰载艇各配齐人手，我打算让你来指挥它们，驶进那个小海湾，必要时再往内陆稍稍推进一点去探查一番。就我们所知，那个半岛上从来没有驻过军队。你要做的头一件事，就是巡查一遍海岸。”

===Original===
He talked for some time, giving more minute instructions, and then went on deck. The *Amelia*, with the two cutters towing alongside, reached about halfway down the Passe and then the boats were ordered to proceed. Just before they shoved off two guns were fired in quick succession.

===Chinese===
他又谈了一阵，交代了更为详尽的吩咐，然后上了甲板。阿米莉亚号带着两艘拖在舷侧的舰载艇，驶到水道的大约中段，随后便下令两艇继续前进。就在它们解缆撑开之前，两声炮响接连发了出去。

===Original===
“Like this, Bolt,” explained Captain Vincent, “Symons will guess that we are looking for him; and if he is hiding anywhere near the shore he will be sure to come down where he can be seen by you.”

===Chinese===
“是这样的，博尔特，”文森特舰长解释道，“西蒙斯（Symons）一听见就会猜到我们在找他；要是他正躲在海岸附近的什么地方，他一定会跑到能被你们瞧见的地方来。”

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
The motive force of a fixed idea is very great. In the case of Scevola it was great enough to launch him down the slope and to rob him for the moment of all caution. He bounded amongst the boulders, using the handle of the stable fork for a staff. He paid no regard to the nature of the ground, till he got a fall and found himself sprawling on his face, while the stable fork went clattering down until it was stopped by a bush. It was this circumstance which saved Peyrol's prisoner from being caught unawares. Since he had got out of the little cabin, simply because after coming to himself he had perceived it was open, Symons had been greatly refreshed by long drinks of cold water and by his little nap in the fresh air. Every moment he was feeling in better command of his limbs. As to the command of his thoughts, that was coming to him too, rather quickly. The advantage of having a very thick skull became evident in the fact that as soon as he had dragged himself out of that cabin he knew where he was. The next thing he did was to look at the moon, to judge of the passage of time. Then he gave way to an immense surprise at the fact of being alone aboard the tartane. As he sat with his legs dangling into the open hold he tried to guess how it came about that the cabin had been left unlocked and unguarded.

He went on thinking about this unexpected situation. What could have become of that white-headed villain? Was he dodging about somewhere watching for a chance to give him another tap on the head? Symons felt suddenly very unsafe sitting there on the afterdeck in the full light of the moon. Instinct rather than reason suggested to him that he ought to get down into the dark hold. It seemed a great undertaking at first, but once he started he accomplished it with the greatest ease, though he could not avoid knocking down a small spar which was leaning up against the deck. It preceded him into the hold with a loud crash which gave poor Symons an attack of palpitation of the heart. He sat on the keelson of the tartane and gasped, but after a while reflected that all this did not matter. His head felt very big, his neck was very painful and one shoulder was certainly very stiff. He could never stand up against that old ruffian. But what had become of him? Why! He had gone to fetch the soldiers! After that conclusion Symons became more composed. He began to try to remember things. When he had last seen that old fellow it was daylight, and now—Symons looked up at the moon again—it must be near six bells in the first watch. No doubt the old scoundrel was sitting in a wine shop drinking with the soldiers. They would be here soon enough! The idea of being a prisoner of war made his heart sink a little. His ship appeared to him invested with an extraordinary number of lovable features which included Captain Vincent and the first lieutenant. He would have been glad to shake hands even with the corporal, a surly and malicious marine acting as master-at-arms of the ship. "I wonder where she is now," he thought dismally, feeling his distaste for captivity grow with the increase of his strength.

===Chinese===
执拗念头的驱动力着实惊人。在塞沃拉身上，这股力量大到足以把他推下山坡，一时之间令他忘却了所有的谨慎。他在乱石间纵跃蹦跳，拿马厩草叉的木柄当拐杖使。他全不顾脚下是什么地面，直摔了一跤，趴倒在地上，那把草叉则哐啷哐啷一路滚下去，直到被一丛灌木挡住才停住。正是这一意外，才使佩罗尔的囚徒免遭暗算。西蒙斯（Symons）之所以钻出了那间小舱房，不过是因为他苏醒过来之后发现舱门洞开着罢了。他大口大口灌了一通凉水，又在露天的新鲜空气里打了个盹儿，精神大为恢复。他每时每刻都觉得自己越发能把持住四肢了。至于他的思绪，也恢复得颇为迅速，来得还挺快。长着一颗格外厚的脑壳，好处在这一刻显露无遗——他刚把自己从那间舱房里拖出来，便晓得身在何处了。他接着做的第二件事是抬头看了看月亮，以此判断时辰的推移。继而，他对独自待在这条塔尔塔纳上这一事实，大为诧异起来。他坐在那里，两条腿悬在敞开的船舱口上方晃荡，暗自揣度那间舱房怎会没上锁、无人看守。

他继续琢磨着这一出乎意料的处境。那个白头发的恶棍能上哪儿去了呢？莫不是正躲在什么地方，伺机再给他脑袋来一下？西蒙斯坐在后甲板上，满月的光辉直直地照着他，忽然觉得自己极不安全。是本能而非理智提示他该下到那黑洞洞的船舱里去。起先这似乎是桩了不起的大事，可一旦动手，竟轻而易举地办成了，只是免不了碰倒了一根斜靠在甲板上的小帆桁（spar）。那根帆桁抢在他头里哗啦一声栽进了船舱，可怜的西蒙斯被惊得一阵心悸。他在塔尔塔纳的内龙骨上坐下来直喘，过了一阵才觉得这一切都无关紧要。他的脑袋胀得老大，脖子疼得厉害，一边肩膀也肯定僵硬得很。他万万敌不过那老恶棍。可那人到底怎么了？敢情是去搬兵了！得出这个结论之后，西蒙斯镇定了些。他开始努力回忆种种事情。他上一次瞧见那老头儿时还是大白天，而现在——西蒙斯又抬头望了望月亮——头更怕是快到六响钟了。那老混蛋这会儿八成正坐在哪家酒铺子里跟大兵们喝着酒呢。他们马上就会来的！一想到要当战俘，他心里不免有些发沉。他的船在他眼里忽然平添了数不尽的可爱之处，其中包括文森特舰长（Captain Vincent）和大副。哪怕是跟那个充当船上纠察长的陆战队下士——一个阴鸷刁恶的家伙——握一握手，他也心甘情愿。"她这会儿在哪儿呢，"他凄然想道，只觉得自己随着体力的恢复，越发厌恶起囚禁的日子来。

===Original===
It was at this moment that he heard the noise of Scevola's fall. It was pretty close; but afterwards he heard no voices and footsteps heralding the approach of a body of men. If this was the old ruffian coming back, then he was coming back alone. At once Symons started on all fours for the fore end of the tartane. He had an idea that ensconced under the foredeck he would be in a better position to parley with the enemy and that perhaps he could find there a handspike or some piece of iron to defend himself with. Just as he had settled himself in his hiding-place Scevola stepped from the shore on to the afterdeck.

At the very first glance Symons perceived that this one was very unlike the man he expected to see. He felt rather disappointed. As Scevola stood still in full moonlight Symons congratulated himself on having taken up a position under the foredeck. That fellow, who had a beard, was like a sparrow in body compared with the other; but he was armed dangerously with something that looked to Symons either like a trident or fishgrains on a staff. "A devil of a weapon that," he thought, appalled. And what on earth did that beggar want on board? What could he be after?

===Chinese===
恰在此刻，他听见了塞沃拉摔跤的声响。那声音就在近处；可随后他并没有听见什么人声和脚步声，预示着一群人的逼近。如果回来的真是那老恶棍，那他是独自回来的。西蒙斯当即手脚并用地朝塔尔塔纳的前头爬去。他盘算着，蜷缩在前甲板底下，与敌人周旋起来会更有利，况且也许能在那儿找到一根撬棍或什么铁件来防身。他刚在藏身之处安顿好，塞沃拉便从岸上一脚踏上了后甲板。

西蒙斯第一眼便看出，此人与他料想中要见的那个人大不相同。他颇觉失望。塞沃拉一动不动地站在满月的光辉下，西蒙斯暗自庆幸自己选了前甲板底下这个位置。那家伙长着胡子，身量跟另一个比起来简直像只麻雀；可他手里拿着一件危险的家伙，在西蒙斯看来，那东西不是一柄三股叉，便是一杆多齿的鱼叉。"好厉害的家伙，"他心想，不由得骇然。那要饭的到底上船来做什么？他图的是什么？

===Original===
The newcomer acted strangely at first. He stood stock still, craning his neck here and there, peering along the whole length of the tartane, then crossing the deck he repeated all those performances on the other side. "He has noticed that the cabin door is open. He's trying to see where I've got to. He will be coming forward to look for me," said Symons to himself. "If he corners me here with that beastly pronged affair I am done for." For a moment he debated within himself whether it wouldn't be better to make a dash for it and scramble ashore; but in the end he mistrusted his strength. "He would run me down for sure," he concluded. "And he means no good, that's certain. No man would go about at night with a confounded thing like that if he didn't mean to do for somebody."

Scevola, after keeping perfectly still, straining his ears for any sound from below where he supposed Lieutenant Réal to be, stooped down to the cabin scuttle and called in a low voice: "Are you there, lieutenant?" Symons saw these motions and could not imagine their purport. That excellent able seaman of proved courage in many cutting-out expeditions broke into a slight perspiration. In the light of the moon the prongs of the fork, polished by much use, shone like silver, and the whole aspect of the stranger was weird and dangerous in the extreme. Who could that man be after, but him, himself.

===Chinese===
那新来的人起初举止古怪。他木桩似的纹丝不动地站着，伸着脖子东张西望，顺着塔尔塔纳整个儿的长长船身仔细窥探，然后穿过甲板，在另一侧把那套动作又从头来了一遍。"他注意到舱门开了。他正想瞧出我跑到哪儿去了。他就要上这儿来找我了，"西蒙斯暗自道，"要是他拿着那该死的三齿玩意儿把我堵在这儿，我可就完了。"有那么一阵子，他心里犯嘀咕，不知是不是干脆冲出去爬上岸更划算；但末了他信不过自己的力气。"他准能追上我，"他断定，"再说他来者不善，这是肯定的。谁要不是存心要收拾什么人，大半夜的绝不会拿着那么个混账玩意儿到处转悠。"

塞沃拉一直纹丝不动，竖起耳朵倾听下面——他以为雷亚尔中尉就在下面——的任何声响，然后弯下身去，对着舱口压低嗓子唤道："你在吗，中尉？"西蒙斯瞧见了这些举动，却猜不透是何用意。这个在多次斩缆夺船突击中身经百验的勇悍水兵，竟沁出了一层薄汗。月光之下，那叉子因长久使用而磨得锃亮的几个齿闪着银光，那陌生人整个儿的模样诡异而凶险到了极点。那人要找的，除了他本人，还能有谁呢。

===Original===
Scevola, receiving no answer, remained in a stooping position. He could not detect the slightest sound of breathing down there. He remained in this position so long that Symons became quite interested. "He must think I am still down there," he whispered to himself. The next proceeding was quite astonishing. The man, taking up a position on one side of the cuddy scuttle and holding his horrid weapon as one would a boarding pike, uttered a terrific whoop and went on yelling in French with such volubility that he quite frightened Symons. Suddenly he left off, moved away from the scuttle and looked at a loss what to do next. Anybody who could have then seen Symons' protruded head with his face turned aft would have seen on it an expression of horror. "The cunning beast," he thought. "If I had been down there, with the row he made, I would have surely rushed on deck and then he would have had me." Symons experienced the feeling of a very narrow escape; yet it brought not much relief. It was simply a matter of time. The fellow's homicidal purpose was evident. He was bound before long to come forward. Symons saw him move, and thought, "Now he's coming," and prepared himself for a dash. "If I can dodge past these blamed prongs I might be able to take him by the throat," he reflected, without, however, feeling much confidence in himself.

But to his great relief Scevola's purpose was simply to conceal the fork in the hold in such a manner that the handle of it just reached the edge of the afterdeck. In that position it was of course invisible to anybody coming from the shore. Scevola had made up his mind that the lieutenant was out of the tartane. He had wandered away along the shore and would probably be back in a moment. Meantime it had occurred to him to see if he could discover anything compromising in the cabin. He did not take the fork down with him because in that confined space it would have been useless and rather a source of embarrassment than otherwise should the returning lieutenant find him there. He cast a circular glance around the basin and then prepared to go down.

===Chinese===
塞沃拉没有等到回答，仍弓着身子。他听不出下面哪怕一丝呼吸的声响。他保持着这个姿势那样久，以至于西蒙斯竟生出几分好奇来了。"他一定以为我还在下头呢，"他悄声自语。接下来的一幕则令人瞠目。那人占据小舱口一侧的位置，把那件骇人的家伙像端着接舷长矛一般握着，猛地爆出一声骇人的怪叫，接着便滔滔不绝地用法语嚷了起来，直把西蒙斯吓得够呛。突然他住了嘴，离开舱口，一副不知下一步该怎么办的茫然模样。谁要是这会儿能瞧见西蒙斯探出的脑袋和他朝后扭着的脸，定会看到上面一副惊恐万状的神情。"狡猾的畜生，"他心想，"我要是真在下头，听他这一通闹腾，准会冲上甲板来，那就正中他的下怀了。"西蒙斯体味着这一番千钧一发的侥幸；可这侥幸并没给他带来多少宽慰。说到底不过是个时间问题。那人杀心已露。迟早要往前头来的。西蒙斯看见他动了，心想："来了，"便做好了冲刺的准备。"只要我能躲过那该死的几根齿，说不定能掐住他的脖子，"他暗自盘算，却对自己没多少信心。

然而令他大松一口气的是，塞沃拉不过是把叉子藏进船舱里，藏得恰使其柄刚好够到后甲板的边缘。在那个位置上，任何从岸上来的人当然都瞧不见它。塞沃拉已经断定中尉不在塔尔塔纳上了。他准是沿着岸边走开了，没准儿一会儿就回来。与此同时，他动了心思，想看看能否在舱房里翻出什么见不得人的东西。他没有把叉子带下去，因为在那逼仄的空间里它派不上用场，万一折回来的中尉撞见他在那儿，非但无用，反而添累赘。他朝小水池四周环顾了一圈，便准备下去。

===Original===
Every movement of his was watched by Symons. He guessed Scevola's purpose by his movements, and said to himself: "Here's my only chance, and not a second to be lost either." Directly Scevola turned his back on the forepart of the tartane in order to go down the little cabin ladder, Symons crawled out from his concealment. He ran along the hold on all fours for fear the other should turn his head round before disappearing below, but directly he judged that the man had touched bottom, he stood on his feet and catching hold of the main rigging swung himself on the afterdeck and, as it were in the same movement, flung himself on the doors of the cabin, which came together with a crash. How he could secure them he had not thought, but as a matter of fact he saw the padlock hanging on a staple on one side; the key was in it, and it was a matter of a fraction of a second to secure the doors effectually.

Almost simultaneously with the crash of the cabin door there was a shrill exclamation of surprise down there, and just as Symons had turned the key the man he had trapped made an effort to break out. That, however, did not disturb Symons. He knew the strength of that door. His first action was to get possession of the stable fork. At once he felt himself a match for any single man, or even two men, unless they had firearms. He had no hope, however, of being able to resist the soldiers and really had no intention of doing so. He expected to see them appear at any moment led by that confounded marinero. As to what the farmer man had come for on board the tartane he had not the slightest doubt about it. Not being troubled by too much imagination, it seemed to him obvious that it was to kill an Englishman and for nothing else. "Well, I am jiggered," he exclaimed mentally. "The damned savage! I haven't done anything to him. They must be a murderous lot hereabouts." He looked anxiously up the slope. He would have welcomed the arrival of soldiers. He wanted more than ever to be made a proper prisoner, but a profound stillness reigned on the shore and a most absolute silence down below in the cabin. Absolute. No word, no movement. The silence of the grave. "He's scared to death," thought Symons, hitting in his simplicity on the exact truth. "It would serve him jolly well right if I went down there and ran him through with that thing. I would do it for a shilling, too." He was getting angry. It occurred to him also that there was some wine down there too. He discovered he was very thirsty and he felt rather faint. He sat down on the little skylight to think the matter over while awaiting the soldiers. He even gave a friendly thought to Peyrol himself. He was quite aware that he could have gone ashore and hidden himself for a time, but that meant in the end being hunted among the rocks and, certainly, captured; with the additional risk of getting a musket ball through his body.

===Chinese===
他的每一个动作都被西蒙斯瞧在眼里。他从他的举动中猜出了塞沃拉的意图，心说："这是我唯一的机会，一刻也耽误不得。"塞沃拉刚一背对塔尔塔纳的前半部、准备下那道通往舱房的小梯子，西蒙斯便从藏身处爬了出来。他怕那人在消失下去之前回过头来，便四肢着地沿着船舱一路飞爬，可一旦判断那人已经落了底，他便一跃而起，抓住主桅索具荡上了后甲板，紧接着——几乎就在同一个动作里——朝舱门猛扑过去，两扇门砰的一声合拢。怎样把它们锁牢他还没想过，可事实上他看见一只挂锁就挂在一边的门形钉上，钥匙还插在里头，锁妥两扇门不过是弹指间的事。

几乎与舱门砰然合拢的同时，下面传来一声尖利的惊呼，就在西蒙斯转动钥匙的当口，被他困住的那人使劲想要冲出来。然而这并没有惊扰到西蒙斯。他清楚那扇门的结实程度。他头一个动作便是把那把马厩草叉弄到手。一拿到手，他便觉得自己足以对付任何一个人，甚至两个人，除非他们有火器。不过他并不指望自己能抵挡得住大兵们，也实在无意去抵挡。他随时等着看见他们在那个该死的 *marinero*（水手）的带领下出现。至于那个农人模样的家伙上塔尔塔纳来到底是为什么，他一点也不犯嘀咕。他这人不大富于想象力，因此在他看来，摆明了就是为了杀一个英国人，不为什么别的。"好啊，我算是倒了血霉了，"他在心里嚷道，"这该死的野蛮人！我他又没招惹过。这一带的人准是一帮杀人不眨眼的货色。"他忧心忡忡地朝山坡上张望。他巴不得大兵们赶紧到来。他比任何时候都更想被正正经经地收为俘虏，然而岸上笼罩着一片深沉的死寂，下面的舱房里更是一丝声响也没有。绝对的寂静。没有一句话，没有一丝动静。坟茔一般的寂静。"他八成吓破了胆，"西蒙斯想道，凭他那股憨劲儿倒恰好说中了实情，"这会儿我要是下去拿那玩意儿把他扎个对穿，也算他罪有应得。出一个先令我就能干。"他渐渐来了气。他又想起下面还有些酒。他发觉自己渴得很，身上也有点发虚。他在那个小天窗上坐下来，一边等着大兵们，一边盘算这事。他甚至对佩罗尔本人也生出了一丝善意。他心里清楚，自己本可以上岸躲上一阵子，可到头来无非是在乱石间被搜出来，然后必然被捉；还要多担一份挨一发火枪子弹的风险。

===Original===
The first gun of the Amelia lifted him to his feet as though he had been snatched up by the hair of his head. He intended to give a resounding cheer, but produced only a feeble gurgle in his throat. His ship was talking to him. They hadn't given him up. At the second report he scrambled ashore with the agility of a cat—in fact, with so much agility that he had a fit of giddiness. After it passed off he returned deliberately to the tartane to get hold of the stable fork. Then, trembling with emotion, he staggered off quietly and resolutely with the only purpose of getting down to the seashore. He knew that as long as he kept downhill he would be all right. The ground in this part being a smooth rocky surface and Symons being barefooted, he passed at no great distance from Peyrol without being heard. When he got on rough ground he used the stable fork for a staff. Slowly as he moved, he was not really strong enough to be surefooted. Ten minutes later or so Peyrol, lying ensconced behind a bush, heard the noise of a rolling stone far away in the direction of the cove. Instantly the patient Peyrol got on his feet and started towards the cove himself. Perhaps he would have smiled if the importance and gravity of the affair in which he was engaged had not given all his thoughts a serious cast. Pursuing a higher path than the one followed by Symons, he had presently the satisfaction of seeing the fugitive, made very noticeable by the white bandages about his head, engaged in the last part of the steep descent. No nurse could have watched with more anxiety the adventure of a little boy than Peyrol the progress of his former prisoner. He was very glad to perceive that he had had the sense to take what looked like the tartane's boathook to help himself with. As Symons' figure sank lower and lower in his descent Peyrol moved on, step by step, till at last he saw him from above sitting down on the seashore, looking very forlorn and lonely, with his bandaged head between his hands. Instantly Peyrol sat down too, protected by a projecting rock. And it is safe to say that with that there came a complete cessation of all sound and movement on the lonely head of the peninsula for a full half-hour.

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚号的第一声炮响把他弹了起来，仿佛有人揪着他的头发一把将他提了起来。他本想扯开嗓子欢呼一声，可喉咙里只挤出一丝微弱的咕噜。他的船在对他说话。他们没有抛弃他。第二声炮响时，他猫一般敏捷地爬上了岸——事实上，敏捷得叫他一阵头晕目眩。等这阵眩晕过去，他特意折回塔尔塔纳去取那把马厩草叉。然后，他浑身激动得发抖，却悄无声息地、坚定地踉跄前行，唯一的念头就是下到海边。他知道只要一路往下走就不会出错。这一段地面是光滑的岩石，而西蒙斯又光着脚，所以他经过佩罗尔近旁时并没有被听见。走到崎岖的地面上，他便拿那把草叉当拐杖使。纵然他走得那样慢，可他实在没有足够的力气做到脚下稳当。大约十分钟之后，佩罗尔伏在一丛灌木后面，听见了远处小海湾方向滚石子的声响。耐心的佩罗尔当即站起身来，自己也朝小海湾走去。倘若不是他正从事的那桩事情的重大与严峻使他满脑子都是严肃的心思，他没准儿会微微一笑。他走的路比西蒙斯的那条高一些，没多久便心满意足地望见了那逃亡者——头上缠着的白绷带使他格外显眼——正进行着那段陡坡的最后一段下行。任何一个看护小男孩冒险的保姆，也不会比佩罗尔注视他这位前囚徒的每一步下坡更加焦心了。他很欣慰地看到，对方尚有头脑，顺手抄了一件看起来像是塔尔塔纳的船钩的东西拄着帮忙。随着西蒙斯的身影越降越低，佩罗尔亦步步跟进，直到最后从高处望见他坐在海边，双手捧着缠了绷带的脑袋，一副说不出的凄凉与孤苦。佩罗尔当即也坐了下来，有块突出的岩石替他遮掩着。可以这么说，随着他这一坐，那孤零零的半岛岬角上，一切声响和动静便彻底停歇了足有半个钟头。

===Original===
Peyrol was not in doubt as to what was going to happen. He was as certain that the corvette's boat or boats were now on the way to the cove as though he had seen them leave the side of the Amelia. But he began to get a little impatient. He wanted to see the end of this episode. Most of the time he was watching Symons. "Sacré tête dure," he thought. "He has gone to sleep." Indeed Symons' immobility was so complete that he might have been dead from his exertions: only Peyrol had a conviction that his once youthful chum was not the sort of person that dies easily. The part of the cove he had reached was all right for Peyrol's purpose. But it would have been quite easy for a boat or boats to fail to notice Symons, and the consequence of that would be that the English would probably land in several parties for a search, discover the tartane.… Peyrol shuddered.

Suddenly he made out a boat just clear of the eastern point of the cove. Mr. Bolt had been hugging the coast and progressing very slowly, according to his instructions, till he had reached the edge of the point's shadow where it lay ragged and black on the moonlit water. Peyrol could see the oars rise and fall. Then another boat glided into view. Peyrol's alarm for his tartane grew intolerable. "Wake up, animal, wake up," he mumbled through his teeth. Slowly they glided on, and the first cutter was on the point of passing by the man on the shore when Peyrol was relieved by the hail of "Boat ahoy!" reaching him faintly where he knelt leaning forward, an absorbed spectator.

===Chinese===
对于即将发生的事，佩罗尔没有半点疑虑。他确信那条轻巡洋舰的一艘或两艘舰载艇此刻正朝小海湾驶来，仿佛他亲眼看着它们驶离了阿米莉亚号的舷侧。可他渐渐有些不耐烦了。他想看到这桩事的结局。大多数时候他都在注视着西蒙斯。"*Sacré tête dure*（该死的硬脑壳），"他心想，"他睡着了。"西蒙斯那纹丝不动的模样确实彻底到了可以让人以为他已竭力而亡的地步：只不过佩罗尔有一桩信念——他那昔日的年少伙计不是那种轻易就死得了的人。他所到达的小海湾这一处，于佩罗尔的目的而言倒正合适。然而一艘或两艘艇也很容易注意不到西蒙斯，其后果便是英国人多半会分几拨登岸搜索，从而发现那条塔尔塔纳。……佩罗尔打了个寒颤。

忽然他辨出了一艘小艇，正好掠过小海湾东边的岬角。博尔特先生（Mr. Bolt）一直贴着海岸、按照指示极其缓慢地行进，直到他抵达那道岬角投在月光水面上的参差黑影的边缘。佩罗尔能看见桨叶起起落落。接着又一艘艇滑进了视野。佩罗尔为他那条塔尔塔纳的担忧越发难以忍受。"醒醒，你这畜生，醒醒啊，"他咬着牙嘟囔。两艘艇缓缓滑行，头一艘舰载艇眼看就要从岸上那人身边划过去了，这时佩罗尔总算松了口气——一声"前方小艇"的呼喊隐隐传来，传到他跪着、身子前倾的地方，他是个全神贯注的旁观者。

===Original===
He saw the boat heading for Symons, who was standing up now and making desperate signs with both arms. Then he saw him dragged in over the bows, the boat back out, and then both of them tossed oars and floated side by side on the sparkling water of the cove.

Peyrol got up from his knees. They had their man now. But perhaps they would persist in landing, since there must have been some other purpose at first in the mind of the captain of the English corvette. This suspense did not last long. Peyrol saw the oars fall in the water, and in a very few minutes the boats, pulling round, disappeared one after another behind the eastern point of the cove.

===Chinese===
他看见那艇朝西蒙斯驶去——后者这会儿已站了起来，两条胳膊拼命地打信号。接着他看见他被拽过了船头拽了进去，那艇倒划出去，随后两艘艇一同举桨致敬，并排漂浮在小海湾粼粼的水面上。

佩罗尔从跪姿中站了起来。他们的人现在到手了。但他们也许还会坚持登岸，因为那位英国轻巡洋舰的舰长起初想必还有别的什么目的。这一阵悬念并没有持续多久。佩罗尔看见桨叶重新落入水中，不出几分钟，两艘艇便划转船头，一先一后消失在了小海湾东边岬角的后头。

===Original===
"That's done," muttered Peyrol to himself. "I will never see the silly Hard-head again." He had a strange notion that those English boats had carried off something belonging to him, not a man but a part of his own life, the sensation of a regained touch with the far-off days in the Indian Ocean. He walked down quickly as if to examine the spot from which Testa Dura had left the soil of France. He was in a hurry now to get back to the farmhouse and meet Lieutenant Réal, who would be due back from Toulon. The way by the cove was as short as any other. When he got down he surveyed the empty shore and wondered at a feeling of emptiness within himself. While walking up towards the foot of the ravine he saw an object lying on the ground. It was a stable fork. He stood over it asking himself, "How on earth did this thing come here?" as though he had been too surprised to pick it up. Even after he had done so he remained motionless, meditating on it. He connected it with some activity of Scevola, since he was the man to whom it belonged, but that was no sort of explanation of its presence on that spot, unless.…

"Could he have drowned himself?" thought Peyrol, looking at the smooth and luminous water of the cove. It could give him no answer. Then at arm's length he contemplated his find. At last he shook his head, shouldered the fork, and with slow steps continued on his way.

===Chinese===
"事了。"佩罗尔自言自语地嘟囔，"我再也见不着那个傻硬脑壳了。"他有一种奇怪的感觉，仿佛那几艘英国小艇带走了一件属于他的东西——不是一个人，而是他生命中的一部分，是与印度洋上那些遥远岁月重新触手可及的那份感觉。他快步走了下去，仿佛要去察看一下 Testa Dura（硬脑壳）离开法兰西土地的那个地点。此刻他急于赶回农庄去见雷亚尔中尉，后者按理该从土伦回来了。从小海湾走这条路与其他路一样近。等他走下去，他打量着空荡荡的海岸，纳闷自己心里那一片空落落的感觉。朝沟壑脚下走去时，他瞧见地上躺着一件东西。那是一把马厩草叉。他站在它上方，自问道："这东西到底怎么跑到这儿来的？"仿佛惊异得一时竟没去捡。即便捡了起来，他也还一动不动地站着，对它沉思默想。他把它与塞沃拉的某些活动联系起来，因为草叉本就是他的东西，可这并不能解释它何以出现在这个地点，除非……

"莫不是他投水自尽了？"佩罗尔望着小海湾平滑而明亮的水面，暗自思忖。水面给不了他答案。他随即把那把捡来的东西伸直胳膊端详了一番。末了他摇了摇头，把草叉扛上肩，迈着缓缓的步子，继续赶路了。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
The midnight meeting of Lieutenant Réal and Peyrol was perfectly silent. Peyrol, sitting on the bench outside the *salle*, had heard the footsteps coming up the Madrague track long before the lieutenant became visible. But he did not move. He did not even look at him. The lieutenant, unbuckling his sword-belt, sat down without uttering a word. The moon, the only witness of the meeting, seemed to shine on two friends so identical in thought and feeling that they could commune with each other without words. It was Peyrol who spoke first.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉（Lieutenant Réal）与佩罗尔（Peyrol）在这午夜时分的会面，寂静无声。佩罗尔坐在正厅（*salle*）外头那条长凳上，早在那中尉现身之前许久，便已听见脚步声顺着马德拉格（Madrague）的小道走上来了。可他一动也没动。他甚至连看都没有看他一眼。那中尉解下佩剑的腰带，一言不发地坐了下来。月亮是这场会面唯一的见证者，它仿佛照着两个心思与情怀如此一致的朋友，以至于他们彼此之间无需言语便能心心相印。先开口的是佩罗尔。

===Original===
“You are up to time.”

“I had the deuce of a job to hunt up the people and get the certificate stamped. Everything was shut up. The Port-Admiral was giving a dinner-party, but he came out to speak to me when I sent in my name. And all the time, do you know, gunner, I was wondering whether I would ever see you again in my life. Even after I had the certificate, such as it is, in my pocket, I wondered whether I would.”

“What the devil did you think was going to happen to me?” growled Peyrol, perfunctorily. He had thrown the incomprehensible stable fork under the narrow bench, and with his feet drawn in he could feel it there, lying against the wall.

“No, the question with me was whether I would ever come here again.”

===Chinese===
“你倒是准时的。”

“我费了九牛二虎之力才把人找齐，把那张证书盖上了章。到处都关着门。港务海军上将（Port-Admiral）正在设宴请客，可我递进名帖之后，他还是出来跟我说了几句。你知道吗，炮长，这一路上我一直在想，我这辈子还能不能再见着你一面。哪怕那张证书——管它什么样——已经揣进了我的口袋里，我也还是在想，到底还能不能见着。”

“你以为我会出什么鬼事？”佩罗尔漫不经心地咕哝了一声。他已经把那把叫人摸不着头脑的马厩叉塞到了那条窄凳底下，双脚往里一收，还能觉着它就躺在那儿，贴着墙。

“不，我心里琢磨的问题是，我还能不能再回到这儿来。”

===Original===
Réal drew a folded paper from his pocket and dropped it on the bench. Peyrol picked it up carelessly. That thing was meant only to throw dust into Englishmen’s eyes. The lieutenant, after a moment’s silence, went on with the sincerity of a man who suffered too much to keep his trouble to himself.

“I had a hard struggle.”

“That was too late,” said Peyrol, very positively. “You had to come back here for very shame; and now you have come, you don’t look very happy.”

“Never mind my looks, gunner. I have made up my mind.”

===Chinese===
雷亚尔从口袋里掏出一张折好的纸，丢在凳子上。佩罗尔漫不经心地把它拾了起来。那玩意儿不过是用来往英国人眼里撒沙子的。那中尉沉默了片刻，又接着往下说，那般真诚，是一个人心里苦得太重、再也独自盛不住了才会有的真诚。

“我狠狠地挣扎了一番。”

“那已经太迟了，”佩罗尔斩钉截铁地说。“你是羞也羞得非得回到这儿来不可了；可如今你回来了，瞧着倒并不怎么快活。”

“别管我这副模样，炮长。我主意已定。”

===Original===
A ferocious, not unpleasing thought flashed through Peyrol’s mind. It was that this intruder on the Escampobar sinister solitude in which he, Peyrol, kept order, was under a delusion. Mind! Pah! His mind had nothing to do with his return. He had returned because, in Catherine’s words, “death had made a sign to him.” Meantime, Lieutenant Réal raised his hat to wipe his moist brow.

===Chinese===
一个凶狠却又并不令人不快的念头在佩罗尔脑中一闪而过。那就是：这个闯进埃斯坎波巴（Escampobar）那阴森孤寂之地——而他佩罗尔正是在此维系着秩序——的不速之客，正受着一个妄念的蒙蔽。主意！呸！他这次回来，跟什么主意毫不相干。他之所以回来，是因为，用凯瑟琳（Catherine）的话来说，“死亡已向他打了个招呼。”与此同时，雷亚尔中尉抬起手，摘下帽子擦了擦他那汗津津的额头。

===Original===
“I made up my mind to play the part of dispatch-bearer. As you have said yourself, Peyrol, one could not bribe a man﻿—I mean an honest man﻿—so you will have to find the vessel and leave the rest to me. In two or three days.﻿ ﻿… You are under a moral obligation to let me have your tartane.”

===Chinese===
“我已拿定主意，来扮这个传递公文的角色。正如你自己说过的，佩罗尔，人是收买不了的——我是说，收买不了一个诚实的人——所以你只管去找船，剩下的事交给我。两三天之内。……你在道义上有义务把你的塔尔塔纳（tartane）交给我用。”

===Original===
Peyrol did not answer. He was thinking that Réal had got his sign, but whether it meant death from starvation or disease on board an English prison hulk, or in some other way, it was impossible to say. This naval officer was not a man he could trust; to whom he could, for instance, tell the story of his prisoner and what he had done with him. Indeed, the story was altogether incredible. The Englishman commanding that corvette had no visible, conceivable or probable reason for sending a boat ashore to the cove of all places in the world. Peyrol himself could hardly believe that it had happened. And he thought: “If I were to tell that lieutenant he would only think that I was an old scoundrel who had been in treasonable communication with the English for God knows how long. No words of mine could persuade him that this was as unforeseen to me as the moon falling from the sky.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔没有答话。他心里在想，雷亚尔是得到了他的那个招呼，可这招呼究竟意味着是饿死、病死在英国囚船上，还是以别的什么方式了结，却无从说起。这个海军军官不是他能信得过的人；比方说，他没法把那个囚徒的事、以及他自己是怎么处置那个人的，讲给这样一个人听。况且，这桩事说起来本来就匪夷所思。指挥那条轻巡洋舰的那个英国人，把一艘小艇派上岸、偏偏还要派到全世界所有地方里的这个小海湾来，根本没有一丝可见的、可想的、或可能的理由。连佩罗尔自己都几乎不敢相信这事竟然真的发生过。他暗想：“我要是把这事告诉那中尉，他只会当我是个老混蛋，天知道跟英国人暗通款曲已有多久了。我说什么话也说服不了他，这事在我看来，就如同月亮从天上掉下来一样地始料未及。”

===Original===
“I wonder,” he burst out, but not very loud, “what made you keep on coming back here time after time!” Réal leaned his back against the wall and folded his arms in the familiar attitude of their leisurely talks.

“Ennui, Peyrol,” he said in a faraway tone. “Confounded boredom.”

Peyrol also, as if unable to resist the force of example, assumed the same attitude, and said:

“You seem to be a man that makes no friends.”

===Chinese===
“我倒纳闷，”他忽然开了口，声音并不怎么高，“到底是什么缘故，叫你一趟又一趟地老往这儿跑！”雷亚尔背靠着墙，双臂抱在胸前——这是他们悠闲闲谈时那副熟惯的姿态。

“厌倦，佩罗尔，”他用一种缥缈的声调说道。“那种要命的百无聊赖。”

佩罗尔也仿佛抵挡不住这榜样的力量似的，摆出了一模一样的姿态，说道：

“你这人，似乎是个不交朋友的主儿。”

===Original===
“True, Peyrol. I think I am that sort of man.”

“What, no friends at all? Not even a little friend of any sort?”

Lieutenant Réal leaned the back of his head against the wall and made no answer. Peyrol got on his legs.

“Oh then, it wouldn’t matter to anybody if you were to disappear for years in an English hulk. And so if I were to give you my tartane you would go?”

“Yes, I would go this moment.”

===Chinese===
“不错，佩罗尔。我想我正是那样的人。”

“什么，一个朋友都没有？连个三朋四友、哪怕是随便什么一个小小的相好，都没有？”

雷亚尔中尉把后脑勺靠在墙上，没有作声。佩罗尔站了起来。

“哦，那么，你要是在一艘英国囚船里头销声匿迹上好些年，也不会有谁放在心上了。这么说，要是我把我的塔尔塔纳交给你，你就走？”

“对，我此刻便走。”

===Original===
Peyrol laughed quite loud, tilting his head back. All at once the laugh stopped short and the lieutenant was amazed to see him reel as though he had been hit in the chest. While giving way to his bitter mirth, the rover had caught sight of Arlette’s face at the open window of the lieutenant’s room. He sat heavily on the bench and was unable to make a sound. The lieutenant was startled enough to detach the back of his head from the wall to look at him. Peyrol stooped low suddenly and began to drag the stable fork from its concealment. Then he got on his feet and stood leaning on it, glaring down at Réal, who gazed upwards with languid surprise. Peyrol was asking himself, “Shall I pick him up on that pair of prongs, carry him down and fling him in the sea?” He felt suddenly overcome by a heaviness of arms and a heaviness of heart that made all movement impossible. His stiffened and powerless limbs refused all service.﻿ ﻿… Let Catherine look after her niece. He was sure that the old woman was not very far away. The lieutenant saw him absorbed in examining the points of the prongs carefully. There was something queer about all this.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔仰着脑袋哈哈大笑起来，笑得颇为响亮。笑声戛然而止，那中尉只见他身子一晃，像是当胸挨了一记似的，不由得大为惊愕。原来这漫游者正纵情于他那苦涩的快意之中时，一眼瞥见了中尉那间屋子敞开的窗子边上阿尔莱特（Arlette）的那张脸。他沉重地坐回到凳子上，一个声音也发不出来。那中尉吃了一惊，竟把后脑勺从墙上挪开，朝他望去。佩罗尔猛地深深一弯腰，动手把那把马厩叉从藏匿处拖了出来。随即他站直了身子，拄着那把叉，俯瞰着雷亚尔，雷亚尔则带着一种慵懒的惊讶仰望着他。佩罗尔心里在问自己：“我要不要用这一对叉尖把他挑起来，扛下山去，扔进海里？”他忽然觉得双臂沉、心头也沉，沉得一动也不能动。他那僵硬而无力的四肢，拒绝了一切差使。……让凯瑟琳去照管她的外甥女吧。他确信那老妇人离得并不远。那中尉只见他全神贯注地细细端详着叉尖。这一切，有些蹊跷。

===Original===
“Hallo, Peyrol! What’s the matter?” he couldn’t help asking.

“I was just looking,” said Peyrol. “One prong is chipped a little. I found this thing in a most unlikely place.”

The lieutenant still gazed at him curiously.

“I know! It was under the bench.”

“H’m,” said Peyrol, who had recovered some self-control. “It belongs to Scevola.”

“Does it?” said the lieutenant, falling back again.

His interest seemed exhausted, but Peyrol didn’t move.

===Chinese===
“喂，佩罗尔！怎么啦？”他忍不住问道。

“我不过瞧瞧，”佩罗尔说。“有一根叉尖磕掉了一点。我是在一个万万想不到的地方捡着这玩意儿的。”
