那中尉仍旧好奇地打量着他。

“我知道！是在那条凳子底下。”

“嗯，”佩罗尔应了一声，他已经恢复了几分自持。“这是塞沃拉（Scevola）的。”

“是吗？”那中尉说着，又靠了回去。

他的兴趣似乎已然用尽了，可佩罗尔却动也不动。

===Original===
“You go about with a face fit for a funeral,” he remarked suddenly in a deep voice. “Hang it all, lieutenant, I have heard you laugh once or twice, but the devil take me if I ever saw you smile. It is as if you had been bewitched in your cradle.”

Lieutenant Réal got up as if moved by a spring. “Bewitched,” he repeated, standing very stiff: “in my cradle, eh?﻿ ﻿… No, I don’t think it was so early as that.”

He walked forward with a tense still face straight at Peyrol as though he had been blind. Startled, the rover stepped out of the way and, turning on his heels, followed him with his eyes. The lieutenant paced on, as if drawn by a magnet, in the direction of the door of the house. Peyrol, his eyes fastened on Réal’s back, let him nearly reach it before he called out tentatively: “I say, lieutenant!” To his extreme surprise, Réal swung round as if to a touch.

===Chinese===
“你四处走动，脸上那副神情活像去送殡，”他忽然用低沉的嗓门说道。“见鬼，中尉，我听你笑过那么一两回，可要是说我曾见你露过一丝笑意——叫我见鬼去吧，从没那回事。你这模样，倒像是还在摇篮里就中了邪似的。”

雷亚尔中尉像被弹簧弹起似的霍地站了起来。“中了邪，”他重复道，身子站得笔直，“在摇篮里，嗯？……不，我倒觉得，还没有那么早。”

他绷着一张凝滞的脸，径直朝佩罗尔走去，活像个瞎子似的。漫游者吃了一惊，闪到一旁，脚跟一转，用目光跟随着他。那中尉仿佛被一块磁石吸着一般，朝屋门的方向走去。佩罗尔两眼紧盯着雷亚尔的背影，直到他几乎走到门前，才试探着喊了一声：“我说，中尉！”令他极为诧异的是，雷亚尔像是被一碰似的，猛地转过身来。

===Original===
“Oh yes,” he answered, also in an undertone. “We will have to discuss that matter tomorrow.”

Peyrol, who had approached him close, said in a whisper which sounded quite fierce: “Discuss? No! We will have to carry it out tomorrow. I have been waiting half the night just to tell you that.”

Lieutenant Réal nodded. The expression on his face was so stony that Peyrol doubted whether he had understood. He added:

“It isn’t going to be child’s play.” The lieutenant was about to open the door when Peyrol said: “A moment,” and again the lieutenant turned about silently.

===Chinese===
“哦，对，”他也压低声音答道。“那件事，我们明天还得商量商量。”

已经凑到他跟前的佩罗尔，用一种听来颇为凶狠的耳语说道：“商量？不！明天我们得把它办了。我等了大半夜，就为了跟你说这一句话。”

雷亚尔中尉点了点头。他脸上的神情木然之极，佩罗尔都吃不准他到底听明白了没有。他又补了一句：

“这可不是儿戏。”那中尉正要开门，佩罗尔却说：“等一下，”那中尉便又默默地转过身来。

===Original===
“Michel is sleeping somewhere on the stairs. Will you just stir him up and tell him I am waiting outside. We two will have to finish our night on board the tartane, and start work at break of day to get her ready for sea. Yes, lieutenant, by noon. In twelve hours’ time you will be saying goodbye to *la belle* France.”

Lieutenant Réal’s eyes, staring over his shoulder, seemed glazed and motionless in the moonlight like the eyes of a dead man. But he went in. Peyrol heard presently sounds within of somebody staggering in the passage and Michel projected himself outside headlong, but after a stumble or two pulled up scratching his head and looking on every side in the moonlight without perceiving Peyrol, who was regarding him from a distance of five feet. At last Peyrol said:

“Come, wake up! Michel! Michel!”

“*Voilà, notre maître.*”

===Chinese===
“米歇尔（Michel）正睡在楼梯哪个角落里。你去把他捅醒，告诉他我在外头等着。我们俩今夜剩下的时光就得在塔尔塔纳上头打发了，天一亮就动手，把她收拾齐整好出海。没错，中尉，到正午。再过十二个钟头，你就要跟 *la belle* France（美丽的法兰西）道别了。”

雷亚尔中尉那双越过肩头凝望的眼睛，在月光下显得呆滞而不动，像死人的眼睛一样。可他还是走了进去。不一会儿，佩罗尔便听见里头过道里有人跌跌撞撞的声响，紧接着米歇尔一头栽了出来，可踉跄了一两步便站定了，抓耳挠腮，在月光下四处张望，却没有瞧见离他仅五英尺之遥、正打量着他的佩罗尔。佩罗尔终于开口道：

“来，醒醒！米歇尔！米歇尔！”

“*Voilà, notre maître*（来了，我们的老大）。”

===Original===
“Look at what I have picked up,” said Peyrol. “Take it and put it away.”

Michel didn’t offer to touch the stable fork extended to him by Peyrol.

“What’s the matter with you?” asked Peyrol.

“Nothing, nothing! Only last time I saw it, it was on Scevola’s shoulder.” He glanced up at the sky. “A little better than an hour ago.”

“What was he doing?”

“Going into the yard to put it away.”

===Chinese===
“瞧我捡着了什么，”佩罗尔说，“拿着，把它搁回去。”

佩罗尔把那把马厩叉递向他，米歇尔却没有伸手去接的意思。

“你怎么啦？”佩罗尔问。

“没、没什么！只不过我上一回瞧见它，它还扛在塞沃拉的肩上呢。”他抬头瞥了一眼天色。“大约一个多钟头之前。”

“他在干什么？”

“正要进院子里去把它搁起来。”

===Original===
“Well, now *you* go into the yard to put it away,” said Peyrol, “and don’t be long about it.” He waited with his hand over his chin till his henchman reappeared before him. But Michel had not got over his surprise.

“He was going to bed, you know,” he said.

“Eh, what? He was going.﻿ ﻿… He hasn’t gone to sleep in the stable, perchance? He does sometimes, you know.”

“I know. I looked. He isn’t there,” said Michel, very awake and round-eyed.

===Chinese===
“那好，这回轮到你进院子里去把它搁起来，”佩罗尔说，“手脚麻利点。”他一手托着下巴等着，直到他这位伙伴重新出现在他面前。可米歇尔心里的那股惊讶还没过去。

“他原本是要去睡觉的，你知道。”他说。

“嗯，什么？他正要去睡。……他该不是跑到马厩里睡去了吧？他有时候是那样的，你知道的。”

“我知道。我去瞧过了。他不在里头。”米歇尔说，这会儿人醒透了，瞪圆了双眼。

===Original===
Peyrol started towards the cove. After three or four steps he turned round and found Michel motionless where he had left him.

“Come on,” he cried, “we will have to fit the tartane for sea directly the day breaks.”

Standing in the lieutenant’s room just clear of the open window, Arlette listened to their voices and to the sound of their footsteps diminishing down the slope. Before they had quite died out she became aware of a light tread approaching the door of the room.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔朝小海湾的方向走去。走了三四步，他回过头，发现米歇尔还一动不动地待在原处。

“跟上，”他喊道，“天一亮我们就得动手把塔尔塔纳装配齐整好出海。”

阿尔莱特正站在中尉那间屋子里，紧挨着那扇敞开的窗子，听着他们说话的声音、以及他们的脚步声顺着坡一路渐弱下去。还没等那声音完全消失，她便觉察到有一阵轻巧的脚步声正向这间屋子的门走来。

===Original===
Lieutenant Réal had spoken the truth. While in Toulon he had more than once said to himself that he could never go back to that fatal farmhouse. His mental state was quite pitiable. Honour, decency, every principle, forbade him to trifle with the feelings of a poor creature with her mind darkened by a very terrifying, atrocious and, as it were, guilty experience. And suddenly he had given way to a base impulse and had betrayed himself by kissing her hand! He recognized with despair that this was no trifling, but that the impulse had come from the very depths of his being. It was an awful discovery for a man who on emerging from boyhood had laid for himself a rigidly straight line of conduct amongst the unbridled passions and the clamouring falsehoods of revolution which seemed to have destroyed in him all capacity for the softer emotions. Taciturn and guarded, he had formed no intimacies. Relations he had none. He had kept clear of social connections. It was in his character. At first he visited Escampobar because when he took his leave he had no place in the world to go to, and a few days there were a complete change from the odious town. He enjoyed the sense of remoteness from ordinary mankind. He had developed a liking for old Peyrol, the only man who had nothing to do with the Revolution﻿—who had not even seen it at work. The sincere lawlessness of the ex-Brother of the Coast was refreshing. That one was neither a hypocrite nor a fool. When he robbed or killed, it was not in the name of the sacred revolutionary principles or for the love of humanity.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉说的是真话。还在土伦（Toulon）的时候，他便不止一次地对自己说过，他再也回不到那座要命的农庄去了。他那心境，实属堪怜。荣誉、体面、每一条为人处世的准则，都禁止他拿一个可怜人的感情来开消遣——那人的心智已被一段极其骇人、残忍、且不妨说是带着罪孽的遭遇所蒙蔽。可他却蓦地放纵了一桩卑下的冲动，竟去吻了她的手，由此把自己出卖了！他怀着绝望地意识到，这绝非什么消遣，那股冲动是从他生命最深处涌上来的。对于一个自打脱离少年时起、便在大革命那放纵无度的种种激情与聒噪不休的种种谎言之间——这一切似乎已将他对温柔情感的全部感受力毁损殆尽——为自己立下了一条刚正不阿、寸步不移的行止之道的人而言，这是一桩可怕的发现。他沉默寡言、处处设防，不曾与人结下任何亲近之交。亲戚，他一个也没有。他自觉地与一切社交往来绝缘。这本就是他的性情。起初他到埃斯坎波巴来，是因为一旦告了假，这世上竟没有一个他可以去的地方，在那儿待上几天，与那座可憎的城镇相比，是完全两样的光景。他享受那种远离寻常人众的疏离之感。他对老佩罗尔生出了一份好感，那是唯一一个与大革命毫无瓜葛的人——甚至不曾亲眼瞧见过它是怎样运转的。这位前海岸兄弟会成员那真诚的、不守法纪的劲头，令人神清气爽。那一位既不是伪君子，也不是蠢货。他抢劫也好、杀人也好，从不打着神圣的革命原则的旗号，也不是出于对人类的爱。

===Original===
Of course Réal had remarked at once Arlette’s black, profound and unquiet eyes and the persistent dim smile on her lips, her mysterious silences and the rare sound of her voice which made a caress of every word. He heard something of her story from the reluctant Peyrol who did not care to talk about it. It awakened in Réal more bitter indignation than pity. But it stimulated his imagination, confirmed him in that scorn and angry loathing for the Revolution he had felt as a boy and had nursed secretly ever since. She attracted him by her unapproachable aspect. Later he tried not to notice that, in common parlance, she was inclined to hang about him. He used to catch her gazing at him stealthily. But he was free from masculine vanity. It was one day in Toulon that it suddenly dawned on him what her mute interest in his person might mean. He was then sitting outside a café sipping some drink or other with three or four officers, and not listening to their uninteresting conversation. He marvelled that this sort of illumination should come to him like this, under these circumstances; that he should have thought of her while seated in the street with these men round him, in the midst of more or less professional talk! And then it suddenly dawned on him that he had been thinking of nothing but that woman for days.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔当然一眼就留意到了阿尔莱特那双乌黑的、深沉的、不得安宁的眼睛，以及她唇上那道久久不散的隐约笑意，她那些神秘的沉默，还有她那难得一闻的嗓音——那嗓音把每一个字都化作了爱抚。关于她的身世，他从不大愿意提起此事的佩罗尔嘴里，断断续续地听到了一些。这事在雷亚尔心中唤起的，是比怜悯更为苦涩的愤慨。可它也激发了他的想象，坐实了他自少年时起便怀有的、对那场大革命的蔑视与愤恨——这份情怀他一直暗暗揣在心底。她那不可攀近的神情吸引着他。后来，他竭力装作不曾察觉——用大白话说——她颇有在他跟前流连不去的倾向。他时常撞见她偷偷地凝望自己。可他身上并无半分男子汉的虚荣。直到有一天在土伦，他才蓦地恍然：她对他这个人那份无言的关注，究竟意味着什么。那时他正坐在一家咖啡馆外头，跟三四个军官一起啜着这种那种饮料，并没有去听他们那毫无趣味的谈话。他暗暗称奇：这样一种彻悟，竟会这般、在这样的情形下降临到他头上；他竟会在坐在街上、被这些人围着、置身于多半是些行伍事务的闲谈之中时，想起了她！紧接着他又忽然省悟到，连日来他心里所想的，除了那个女人之外，竟再没有别的。

===Original===
He got up brusquely, flung the money for his drink on the table, and without a word left his companions. But he had the reputation of an eccentric man and they did not even comment on his abrupt departure. It was a clear evening. He walked straight out of town, and that night wandered beyond the fortifications, not noticing the direction he took. All the countryside was asleep. There was not a human being stirring, and his progress in that desolate part of the country between the forts could have been traced only by the barking of dogs in the rare hamlets and scattered habitations.

===Chinese===
他猛地站起身，把那杯饮料的钱往桌上一扔，一言不发地撇下了同伴。可他本就有古怪之名，他们对他这唐突的离去甚至连一句议论都没有。那是个清朗的傍晚。他径直走出了城，那天夜里，他一路徜徉到了城墙之外，全然没留意自己走的是什么方向。整片乡野都已沉睡。没有一个人在走动，他在各座炮台之间那一片荒凉的乡间一路穿行，能追踪到他行迹的，唯有那稀落的村落和零星的住户里的犬吠声。

===Original===
“What has become of my rectitude, of my self-respect, of the firmness of my mind?” he asked himself pedantically. “I have let myself be mastered by an unworthy passion for a mere mortal envelope stained with crime and without a mind!”

===Chinese===
“我的正直、我的自重、我心灵的坚毅，都到哪里去了？”他带着一种学究气地自问道。“我竟任由自己被一桩不配的激情所征服——为的不过是一具沾着罪孽、且没有心智的凡胎肉身！”

===Original===
His despair at this awful discovery was so profound that if he had not been in uniform he would have tried to commit suicide with the small pistol he had in his pocket. He shrank from the act, and the thought of the sensation it would produce, from the gossip and comments it would raise, the dishonouring suspicions it would provoke. “No,” he said to himself, “what I will have to do is to unmark my linen, put on civilian clothes and walk out much farther away, miles beyond the forts, hide myself in some wood or in an overgrown hollow and put an end to my life there. The gendarmes or a *garde-champêtre* discovering my body after a few days, a complete stranger without marks of identity, and being unable to find out anything about me, will give me an obscure burial in some village churchyard.”

===Chinese===
他对这桩可怕发现的绝望，深沉到了这般地步：若不是身上穿着军装，他真要用口袋里那把小手枪结果了自己。可他畏缩于这一举动，畏缩于它将引起的轰动，畏缩于它将招来的闲话与议论、以及那些有辱名声的猜疑。“不，”他对自己说，“我所要做的，是抹去我亚麻衣物上的标记，换上便服，走得远远的，到那几座炮台之外好几里开外的地方，在某个树林里、或哪个荒草丛生的洼地里把自己藏起来，在那里了结此生。宪兵也好、*garde-champêtre*（乡间巡守）也好，过几天发现了我的尸首——一个没有任何身份标识的、彻头彻尾的陌生人——又查不出我的半点来历，便会在某个村子的教堂墓地里，给我一口草草的无名坟。”

===Original===
On that resolution he turned back abruptly and at daybreak found himself outside the gate of the town. He had to wait till it was opened, and then the morning was so far advanced that he had to go straight to work at his office at the Toulon Admiralty. Nobody noticed anything peculiar about him that day. He went through his routine tasks with outward composure, but all the same he never ceased arguing with himself. By the time he returned to his quarters he had come to the conclusion that as an officer in wartime he had no right to take his own life. His principles would not permit him to do that. In this reasoning he was perfectly sincere. During a deadly struggle against an irreconcilable enemy his life belonged to his country. But there were moments when his loneliness, haunted by the forbidden vision of Escampobar with the figure of that distracted girl, mysterious, awful, pale, irresistible in her strangeness, passing along the walls, appearing on the hill-paths, looking out of the window, became unbearable. He spent hours of solitary anguish shut up in his quarters, and the opinion amongst his comrades was that Réal’s misanthropy was getting beyond all bounds.

===Chinese===
打定这个主意之后，他蓦地折返，天亮时分，他发现自己已站在了城门外头。他只得等着城门开启，待到门开，时辰已然不早，他只好径直赶到土伦海军部（Toulon Admiralty）的办公处去当值。那天谁也没有察觉他有什么异样。他外表镇定地照例办完了日常公务，可心里那番与自己的争辩却一刻也不曾停歇。等他回到寓所，他已得出结论：身为战时的军官，他没有权利了结自己的性命。他的原则不允许他那样做。在这番推论里，他是完完全全真诚的。在一场与势不两立之敌的生死搏斗当中，他的生命属于他的国家。可也有些时刻，他那独处的孤寂，被一道禁戒的幻影所纠缠——埃斯坎波巴的幻影，以及那个神魂颠倒的姑娘的身影：神秘、可怖、苍白、在她那份古怪之中透着不可抗拒，她沿着墙根走过，出现在山间小径上，从窗口探望出来——这孤寂便叫人再也无法忍受。他把自己关在寓所里，熬过一个个钟头独自的煎熬，而他那些同僚之间的看法是：雷亚尔的厌世，已经到了无可救药的地步。

===Original===
One day it dawned upon him clearly that he could not stand this. It affected his power of thinking. “I shall begin to talk nonsense to people,” he said to himself. “Hasn’t there been once a poor devil who fell in love with a picture or a statue? He used to go and contemplate it. His misfortune cannot be compared with mine! Well, I will go to look at her as at a picture too; a picture as untouchable as if it had been under glass.” And he went on a visit to Escampobar at the very first opportunity. He made up for himself a repellent face, he clung to Peyrol for society, out there on the bench, both with their arms folded and gazing into space. But whenever Arlette crossed his line of sight it was as if something had moved in his breast. Yet these visits made life just bearable; they enabled him to attend to his work without beginning to talk nonsense to people. He said to himself that he was strong enough to rise above temptation, that he would never overstep the line; but it had happened to him upstairs in his room at the farm, to weep tears of sheer tenderness while thinking of his fate. These tears would put out for a while the gnawing fire of his passion. He assumed austerity like an armour and in his prudence he, as a matter of fact, looked very seldom at Arlette for fear of being caught in the act.

===Chinese===
终于有一天，他清楚地意识到，自己再也撑不住了。这已影响到他思考的能力。“我要开始跟人说胡话了，”他对自己说。“从前不是有过一个倒霉蛋，爱上了一幅画、或者一尊雕像么？他常常跑去凝望它。他那点不幸，岂能与我相提并论！好，那我也去看她，就像看一幅画一样；一幅仿佛罩在玻璃底下、碰也碰不得的画。”于是他一得着机会，便往埃斯坎波巴走了一趟。他给自己摆出一副拒人千里的面孔，到了外头那条长凳上，便黏着佩罗尔做伴，两人都抱着胳膊，凝望着虚空。可每当阿尔莱特从他视线下方走过，他的胸中便仿佛有什么东西动了一下。然而这一趟趟的造访，总算让日子勉强可以过下去了；它们使他能够对付着办差，而不至于开口就跟人说胡话。他对自己说，他够坚强，足以凌驾于诱惑之上，他绝不会越过那条线；可事情也曾在农庄楼上他那间屋子里发生过：他一边想着自己的命数，一边流下纯粹出于柔情的泪水。这些泪水，会把他那股嗜啃人心的情欲之火暂时浇灭一阵。他把严峻当成一副铠甲披在身上，而出于谨慎，事实上他极少去看阿尔莱特，生怕被人当场撞见。

===Original===
The discovery that she had taken to wandering at night had upset him all the same, because that sort of thing was unaccountable. It gave him a shock which unsettled, not his resolution, but his fortitude. That morning he had allowed himself, while she was waiting on him, to be caught looking at her, and then, losing his self-control, had given her that kiss on the hand. Directly he had done it he was appalled. He had overstepped the line. Under the circumstances this was an absolute moral disaster. The full consciousness of it came to him slowly. In fact, this moment of fatal weakness was one of the reasons why he had let himself be sent off so unceremoniously by Peyrol to Toulon. Even while crossing over he thought the only thing was not to come back any more. Yet while battling with himself he went on with the execution of the plan. A bitter irony presided over his dual state. Before leaving the Admiral, who had received him in full uniform in a room lighted by a single candle, he was suddenly moved to say: “I suppose if there is no other way I am authorized to go myself,” and the Admiral had answered: “I didn’t contemplate that, but if you are willing I don’t see any objection. I would only advise you to go in uniform in the character of an officer entrusted with dispatches. No doubt in time the Government would arrange for your exchange. But bear in mind that it would be a long captivity, and you must understand it might affect your promotion.”

===Chinese===
然而，得知她竟养成了夜里游荡的习惯，这终究叫他心绪大乱，因为那种事实在无从解释。这给了他一个震动，动摇的不是他的决心，而是他的坚毅。那天早上，趁她伺候他用饭时，他竟听任自己被她撞见自己在瞧她，紧接着，一失了自持，便在她手上印下了那一吻。事一做完，他便骇然。他越过了那条线。眼下的情形，这简直是一场彻底的道德灾祸。对这一点的完整意识，是慢慢才来到他心头的。事实上，这致命软弱的一刻，正是他何以听任佩罗尔那么毫不客气地把他打发到土伦去的原因之一。即便在渡海途中，他还一心想着，唯一的办法就是再也不回来了。然而他一边与自我搏斗，一边却仍把那计划推行了下去。一种苦涩的讽刺，主宰着他这般双重的状态。在向那位海军上将辞行之前——上将是在一间只点着一支蜡烛的屋子里、身着全套军礼服接见他的——他忽然冲动地开口道：“我想，若是别无他法，我是得到授权可以亲自去的。”上将答道：“我并未作此设想，但若你愿意，我亦不见有何异议。我只想劝你身着军装、以一名受命传递公文的军官的身份前去。假以时日，政府自会安排交换战俘。但你得当心，那将是一段漫长的囚禁，你也得当明白，这或许会影响你的晋升。”

===Original===
At the foot of the grand staircase in the lighted hall of the official building Réal suddenly thought: “And now I must go back to Escampobar.” Indeed he had to go to Escampobar because the false dispatches were there in the valise he had left behind. He couldn’t go back to the Admiral and explain that he had lost them. They would look on him as an unutterable idiot or a man gone mad. While walking to the quay where the naval boat was waiting for him he said to himself: “This, in truth, is my last visit for years﻿—perhaps for life.”

===Chinese===
在那官署灯火通明的大厅里，大楼梯的脚下，雷亚尔忽然想道：“而如今，我必须回埃斯坎波巴去一趟。”事实上他也非得回埃斯坎波巴不可，因为那份假公文还留在他那只旅行袋（valise）里，搁在那农庄没带走。他总不能再回去见那海军上将，解释说自己把它弄丢了。他们会把他看作一个不可救药的蠢材，或是一个发了疯的人。他一路朝那艘等着他的海军小艇停靠的码头走去，一边对自己说：“这一趟，千真万确，是我数年之内——也许是一生之中——的最后一次了。”

===Original===
Going back in the boat, notwithstanding that the breeze was very light, he would not let the men take to the oars. He didn’t want to return before the women had gone to bed. He said to himself that the proper and honest thing to do was not to see Arlette again. He even managed to persuade himself that his uncontrolled impulse had had no meaning for that witless and unhappy creature. She had neither started, nor exclaimed; she had made no sign. She had remained passive and then she had backed away and sat down quietly. He could not even remember that she had coloured at all. As to himself, he had enough self-control to rise from the table and go out without looking at her again. Neither did she make a sign. What could startle that body without mind? She had made nothing of it, he thought with self-contempt. “Body without mind! Body without mind!” he repeated with angry derision directed at himself. And all at once he thought: “No. It isn’t that. All in her is mystery, seduction, enchantment. And then﻿—what do I care for her mind!”

===Chinese===
乘船回去的路上，尽管风很微弱，他却不许水手们动用船桨。他不想在两位女眷就寝之前赶回去。他对自己说，得体而正派的做法，是再也不去见阿尔莱特。他甚至设法说服了自己：他那失控的冲动，对那个痴傻而不幸的人而言，本没有什么意义。她既不曾一惊，也不曾失声；她没有做出任何表示。她一直是被动的，随后便退了开去，静静地坐了下来。他甚至记不起她脸上可曾泛过一丝红晕。至于他自己，他尚有足够的自持，能从桌边站起、走出去，不再看她一眼。她也没有做出任何表示。一具没有心智的肉身，还有什么能叫它吃惊呢？她全没把这当回事——他带着自鄙地想道。“没有心智的肉身！没有心智的肉身！”他带着愤愤的自我嘲弄，把这话重复了一遍。可蓦地，他又想：“不。不是那样的。她身上的一切，都是神秘、是诱惑、是迷咒。再说——我管她有没有心智！”

===Original===
This thought wrung from him a faint groan, so that the coxswain asked respectfully: “Are you in pain, *mon lieutenant*?” “It’s nothing,” he muttered and set his teeth with the desperation of a man under torture.

===Chinese===
这个念头逼出他一声低低的呻吟，惹得那舵手恭恭敬敬地问道：“您可是身上作痛，*mon lieutenant*（中尉先生）？”“没什么。”他咕哝了一句，便咬紧了牙关，那副样子，如同一个受刑的人那般绝望。

===Original===
While talking with Peyrol outside the house, the words “I won’t see her again,” and “body without mind,” rang through his head. By the time he had left Peyrol and walked up the stairs his endurance was absolutely at an end. All he wanted was to be alone. Going along the dark passage he noticed that the door of Catherine’s room was standing ajar. But that did not arrest his attention. He was approaching a state of insensibility. As he put his hand on the door handle of his room he said to himself: “It will soon be over!”

===Chinese===
在外头与佩罗尔说话的时候，“我不会再去见她了”和“没有心智的肉身”这两句话，在他脑中来回作响。待到他离开佩罗尔、走上楼梯，他的忍耐已经到了头。他一心只想独自待着。顺着那黑暗的过道走去时，他注意到凯瑟琳那间屋子的门虚掩着。可这并没有叫他留神。他正临近一种麻木无感的状态。当他把手按到自己屋门的把手上时，他对自己说：“快了，就要了结了！”

===Original===
He was so tired out that he was almost unable to hold up his head, and on going in he didn’t see Arlette, who stood against the wall on one side of the window, out of the moonlight and in the darkest corner of the room. He only became aware of somebody’s presence in the room as she flitted past him with the faintest possible rustle, when he staggered back two paces and heard behind him the key being turned in the lock. If the whole house had fallen into ruins, bringing him to the ground, he could not have been more overwhelmed and, in a manner, more utterly bereft of all his senses. The first that came back to him was the sense of touch when Arlette seized his hand. He regained his hearing next. She was whispering to him: “At last! At last! But you are careless. If it had been Scevola instead of me in this room you would have been dead now. I have seen him at work.” He felt a significant pressure on his hand, but he couldn’t see her properly yet, though he was aware of her nearness with every fibre of his body. “It wasn’t yesterday though,” she added in a low tone. Then suddenly: “Come to the window so that I may look at you.”

===Chinese===
他累得几乎连头也抬不起来，进屋时根本没有瞧见阿尔莱特——她贴着窗子一侧的墙站着，避在月光之外，躲在屋里最暗的那个角落里。直到她从他身旁掠过、带起一丝极轻极轻的窸窣，他才觉察到屋里另有其人，当即踉跄着倒退了两步，又听见身后钥匙在锁眼里一转。便算整座屋子轰然坍塌、把他压倒在地，他也不会比此刻更为天旋地转，也不妨说，更为彻底地丧失了一切知觉。最先回到他身上的是触觉——阿尔莱特一把攥住了他的手。接着恢复的是听觉。她正对他耳语：“终于！终于！可你也太大意了。今晚要是在这屋里的不是我、而是塞沃拉，你此刻已经是个死人了。我见过他下手的。”他感到手上被意味深长地一握，可他还没法把她看真切，尽管他全身上下每一根神经都意识到了她的贴近。“不过，这可不是昨天才有的。”她又压低嗓门补了一句。接着忽然道：“到窗口来，让我好好看看你。”

===Original===
A great square of moonlight lay on the floor. He obeyed the tug like a little child. She caught hold of his other hand as it hung by his side. He was rigid all over, without joints, and it did not seem to him that he was breathing. With her face a little below his, she stared at him closely, whispering gently: “Eugène, Eugène,” and suddenly the livid immobility of his face frightened her. “You say nothing. You look ill. What is the matter? Are you hurt?” She let go his insensitive hands and began to feel him all over for evidence of some injury. She even snatched off his hat and flung it away in her haste to discover that his head was unharmed; but finding no sign of bodily damage, she calmed down like a sensible, practical person. With her hands clasped round his neck she hung back a little. Her little even teeth gleamed, her black eyes, immensely profound, looked into his, not with a transport of passion or fear, but with a sort of reposeful satisfaction, with a searching and appropriating expression. He came back to life with a low and reckless exclamation, felt horribly insecure at once as if he were standing on a lofty pinnacle above a noise as of breaking waves in his ears, in fear lest her fingers should part and she would fall off and be lost to him forever. He flung his arms round her waist and hugged her close to his breast. In the great silence, in the bright moonlight falling through the window, they stood like that for a long, long time. He looked at her head resting on his shoulder. Her eyes were closed, and the expression of her unsmiling face was that of a delightful dream, something infinitely ethereal, peaceful and, as it were, eternal. Its appeal pierced his heart with a pointed sweetness. “She is exquisite. It’s a miracle,” he thought with a sort of terror. “It’s impossible.”

===Chinese===
地板上铺着一大块方方正正的月光。他像个小孩似的，听凭那一拽便走了过去。她一把抓住他垂在身侧的另一只手。他浑身僵直，仿佛身上全无关节，连自己是不是在呼吸也觉不出来了。她的脸略略低于他的脸，她凑近了端详他，轻轻地低语：“欧仁（Eugène），欧仁，”蓦地，他脸上那青灰色的纹丝不动把她吓了一跳。“你一句话也不说。你气色不好。怎么啦？你受伤了吗？”她松开他那双毫无知觉的手，开始在他周身上下摸索，要找出某种伤痛的蛛丝马迹。她甚至一把掀掉他的帽子、远远地甩开，急切地要弄清楚他的头是否无恙；可既没找到任何身体创伤的痕迹，她便像一个明白事理、讲求实际的人那样安下了心。她双手搂住他的脖颈，身子稍稍向后仰去。她那口细小齐整的牙齿闪着微光，她那双深邃之极的黑眼睛凝视着他的眼睛——那神情既非激情或惊惧的激动，而是一种安详自足的满足，一种探究而据为己有的神情。他低低地、不顾一切地呼了一声，这才活了过来，随即感到一种可怖的摇摇欲坠，仿佛自己正立在一座高耸的尖顶之上，耳畔是如惊涛拍岸般的轰鸣，生怕她的手指一松、她便会跌落下去、永远地从他手里失去。他张开双臂搂住她的腰，把她紧紧拥到自己胸前。在那一片宏大的寂静之中，在从窗口倾泻而下的明亮月光里，他们就这样站了很久很久。他望着她那颗靠在他肩头的脑袋。她的双目紧闭，她那不笑的脸上，是一种甜美的梦境般的神情，一种无限空灵、安宁、且不妨说是永恒的东西。它那撩动人心之处，以一种尖利的甘甜刺穿了他的心。“她是精美的。这是个奇迹，”他带着一种惊恐想道。“这不可能。”

===Original===
She made a movement to disengage herself, and instinctively he resisted, pressing her closer to his breast. She yielded for a moment and then tried again. He let her go. She stood at arm’s length, her hands on his shoulders, and her charm struck him suddenly as funny in the seriousness of expression as of a very capable, practical woman.

“All this is very well,” she said in a businesslike undertone. “We will have to think how to get away from here. I don’t mean now, this moment,” she added, feeling his slight start. “Scevola is thirsting for your blood.” She detached one hand to point a finger at the inner wall of the room, and lowered her voice. “He’s there, you know. Don’t trust Peyrol either. I was looking at you two out there. He has changed. I can trust him no longer.” Her murmur vibrated. “He and Catherine behave strangely. I don’t know what came to them. He doesn’t talk to me. When I sit down near him he turns his shoulder to me.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
她动了动，要从他怀里挣出来，他本能地不肯依，反而把她搂得更紧。她顺从了片刻，接着又试了一次。他便放开了她。她站到一臂之遥的地方，双手搭在他肩上，她这份魅力忽地给了他一种滑稽之感——她那副神情，竟如同一个十分干练、讲求实际的妇人那样郑重其事。

“这一切都很好，”她以一种办事般的低声说道。“我们得想想怎么离开这里。我不是说现在，不是说此刻，”她感觉到他轻轻一颤，便补了一句。“塞沃拉正渴着你的血呢。”她抽出一只手，朝屋里那堵内墙竖起一根指头，压低了声音。“他就在那边，你知道。也别信佩罗尔。我刚才在外头瞧着你们俩呢。他变了。我再也不能信他了。”她的低语颤动起来。“他跟凯瑟琳的举动都很古怪。我不知道他们是怎么了。他不跟我说话。我坐到他身边去，他就把肩膀别过去。……”

===Original===
She felt Réal sway under her hands, paused in concern and said: “You are tired.” But as he didn’t move, she actually led him to a chair, pushed him into it, and sat on the floor at his feet. She rested her head against his knees and kept possession of one of his hands. A sigh escaped her. “I knew this was going to be,” she said very low. “But I was taken by surprise.”

“Oh, you knew it was going to be,” he repeated faintly.

“Yes! I had prayed for it. Have you ever been prayed for, Eugène?” she asked, lingering on his name with delight.

“Not since I was a child,” answered Réal in a sombre tone.

===Chinese===
她感到雷亚尔在她两手之下晃了一下，关切地顿住，说道：“你累了。”见他一动不动，她竟真的把他领到一张椅子跟前，把他按了进去，自己则在他脚边坐到了地板上。她把头靠在他的膝盖上，一只手仍攥在他手里不肯放。她叹了口气。“我就知道这事是会发生的，”她极低声地说。“可我还是着了慌。”

“哦，你就知道这事是会发生的，”他有气无力地重复道。

“对！我为此祈祷过。你可曾被人为你祈祷过，欧仁？”她问道，在他的名字上流连，满心欢喜。

“自从我是个孩子以后，就没有了。”雷亚尔阴郁地答道。

===Original===
“Oh yes! You have been prayed for today. I went down to the church.﻿ ﻿…” Réal could hardly believe his ears.﻿ ﻿… “The abbé let me in by the sacristy door. He told me to renounce the world. I was ready to renounce anything for you.” Réal, turning his face to the darkest part of the room, seemed to see the spectre of fatality awaiting its time to move forward and crush that calm, confident joy. He shook off the dreadful illusion, raised her hand to his lips for a lingering kiss, and then asked:

“So you knew that it was going to be? Everything? Yes! And of me, what did you think?”

She pressed strongly the hand to which she had been clinging all the time. “I thought this.”

===Chinese===
“哦，有的！你今天就被人祈祷过了。我去了那座教堂……”雷亚尔几乎不敢相信自己的耳朵。……“那位神父（abbé）从法衣室的门把我领了进去。他叫我弃绝尘世。为了你，我什么都肯弃绝。”雷亚尔把脸转向屋里最暗的地方，仿佛看见命运的幽灵正候着它的时辰、要走上前来碾碎这份沉静而自信的欢喜。他甩掉这可怖的幻象，捧起她的手，送到唇边久久地一吻，然后问道：

“这么说，你早就知道这事是会发生的？一切？对！那么对于我，你是怎么想的？”

她紧紧一握那只她始终攥着不放的手。“我想的就是这样。”

===Original===
“But what did you think of my conduct at times? You see, I did not know what was going to be. I﻿ ﻿… I was afraid,” he added under his breath.

“Conduct? What conduct? You came, you went. When you were not here I thought of you, and when you were here I could look my fill at you. I tell you I knew how it was going to be. I was not afraid then.”

===Chinese===
“那你又觉得我有时候的举止如何呢？你要知道，我并不知道这事是会发生的。我……我害怕来着，”他压低嗓门补了一句。

“举止？什么举止？你来，你去。你不在的时候，我思念你；你在的时候，我可以把你瞧个够。我告诉你，我早知道这事是会怎样的。那时候，我并不害怕。”

===Original===
“You went about with a little smile,” he whispered, as one would mention an inconceivable marvel.

“I was warm and quiet,” murmured Arlette, as if on the borders of dreamland. Tender murmurs flowed from her lips describing a state of blissful tranquillity in phrases that sounded like the veriest nonsense, incredible, convincing and soothing to Réal’s conscience.

“You were perfect,” it went on. “Whenever you came near me everything seemed different.”

“What do you mean? How different?”

“Altogether. The light, the very stones of the house, the hills, the little flowers amongst the rocks! Even Nanette was different.”

===Chinese===
“你四下里走动，唇边总带着一丝浅笑，”他悄声道，仿佛在提及一桩不可想象的奇事。

“我那时心里又暖又静，”阿尔莱特喃喃着，仿佛身在梦乡的边缘。温柔的喃喃低语从她唇间流泻而出，描绘着一种极乐般的宁静，那些话听着活像是十足的胡言乱语，不可思议，却又令人信服，抚慰着雷亚尔的良心。

“你那时是完美的，”那低语继续着。“每逢你走到我跟前，一切仿佛都变了样。”

“你这话什么意思？怎么个变法？”

“全变了。光，这屋子的每一块石头，山峦，岩石间的小花！连娜奈特（Nanette）也变了样。”

===Original===
Nanette was a white Angora with long silken hair, a pet that lived mostly in the yard.

“Oh, Nanette was different too,” said Réal, whom delight in the modulations of that voice had cut off from all reality, and even from a consciousness of himself, while he sat stooping over that head resting against his knee, the soft grip of her hand being his only contact with the world.

===Chinese===
娜奈特是一只白色的安哥拉猫，长着一身丝绸般的长毛，是个多半待在院子里的宠物。

“哦，娜奈特也变了样，”雷亚尔说。那嗓音的种种抑扬顿挫带给他的欢愉，已把他与一切现实隔绝开来，甚至与他自身的意识隔绝开来——他就这样弓着身子坐在那儿，俯临着那颗靠在他膝上的脑袋，她那只手柔软的握持，是他与这个世界唯一的牵连。

===Original===
“Yes. Prettier. It’s only the people.﻿ ﻿…” She ceased on an uncertain note. The crested wave of enchantment seemed to have passed over his head, ebbing out faster than the sea, leaving the dreary expanses of the sand. He felt a chill at the roots of his hair.

“What people?” he asked.

“They are so changed. Listen, tonight while you were away﻿—why did you go away?﻿—I caught those two in the kitchen, saying nothing to each other. That Peyrol﻿—he is terrible.”

===Chinese===
“是的。更漂亮了。只有那些人……”她在一个不确定的尾音上住了口。那道浪头般涌起的迷咒，仿佛已从他头顶漫了过去，退得比海潮还快，留下一片阴郁苍茫的沙地。他感到发根处一阵发凉。

“哪些人？”他问。

“他们都变了。你听，今晚你不在的时候——你为什么要走开？——我撞见他们两个在厨房里，彼此一言不发。那个佩罗尔——他真叫人害怕。”

===Original===
He was struck by the tone of awe, by its profound conviction. He could not know that Peyrol, unforeseen, unexpected, inexplicable, had given by his mere appearance at Escampobar a moral and even a physical jolt to all her being, that he was to her an immense figure, like a messenger from the unknown entering the solitude of Escampobar; something immensely strong, with inexhaustible power, unaffected by familiarity and remaining invincible.

===Chinese===
他被那敬畏的口气、被其中那深沉的确信打动了。他无从知晓的是：佩罗尔，这始料未及的、不期而至的、无可解释的存在，单凭他出现在埃斯坎波巴这一桩事，便给了她整个身心一道精神上的、乃至肉体上的震动；于她而言，他是一座巨大的身影，像一个来自未知的信使，踏入了埃斯坎波巴的孤寂；某种强大之极、拥有无穷力量、不为亲近所磨损、且始终不可战胜的存在。

===Original===
“He will say nothing, he will listen to nothing. He can do what he likes.”

“Can he?” muttered Réal.

She sat up on the floor, moved her head up and down several times as if to say that there could be no doubt about that.

“Is he, too, thirsting for my blood?” asked Réal bitterly.

===Chinese===
“他什么也不肯说，什么也不肯听。他爱做什么就做什么。”

“是吗？”雷亚尔咕哝了一声。

她从地板上坐直了身子，把脑袋上下点了好几下，仿佛在说，这一点是不容置疑的。

“那么，他也渴着我的血么？”雷亚尔苦涩地问。

===Original===
“No, no. It isn’t that. You could defend yourself. I could watch over you. I have been watching over you. Only two nights ago I thought I heard noises outside and I went downstairs, fearing for you; your window was open but I could see nobody, and yet I felt.﻿ ﻿… No, it isn’t that! It’s worse. I don’t know what he wants to do. I can’t help being fond of him, but I begin to fear him now. When he first came here and I saw him he was just the same﻿—only his hair was not so white﻿—big, quiet. It seemed to me that something moved in my head. He was gentle, you know. I had to smile at him. It was as if I had recognized him. I said to myself: ‘That’s he, the man himself.’ ”

===Chinese===
“不，不。不是那样。你能自卫。我能看护你。我一直在看护着你。就在两个晚上之前，我以为听见外头有动静，便下了楼，为你担心；你那扇窗开着，可我什么人也没瞧见，然而我感觉得到。……不，不是那样的！更要命。我不知道他要做什么。我由不得自己对他心生好感，可如今我也开始怕他了。当初他刚到这里、我头一回瞧见他的时候，他就是这副模样——只不过那时他的头发还没有这么白——高大、沉静。我只觉着脑子里有什么东西动了一下。他那时很温存，你知道。我忍不住对他笑。就仿佛我认出了他似的。我对自己说：‘就是他，那个人本人。’”

===Original===
“And when I came?” asked Réal with a feeling of dismay.

“You! You were expected,” she said in a low tone, with a slight tinge of surprise at the question, but still evidently thinking of the Peyrol mystery. “Yes, I caught them at it last evening, he and Catherine, in the kitchen, looking at each other and as quiet as mice. I told him he couldn’t order me about. Oh, *mon chéri, mon chéri*, don’t you listen to Peyrol﻿—don’t let him.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“那我来的那会儿呢？”雷亚尔问，心里一阵沮丧。

“你！你是被盼着的，”她低声说，对这一问略带一点惊讶，但显然还沉浸在佩罗尔那团迷雾里。“是啊，就在昨天傍晚，我撞见他们两个——他跟凯瑟琳——在厨房里，彼此对望着，安静得像两只耗子。我对他说，他休想对我发号施令。哦，*mon chéri, mon chéri*（我亲爱的，我亲爱的），你可别听佩罗尔的——别由着他。……”

===Original===
With only a slight touch on his knee she sprang to her feet. Réal stood up too.

“He can do nothing to me,” he mumbled.

===Chinese===
她只在他膝上轻轻一按，便一跃而起。雷亚尔也站了起来。

“他奈何我不得。”他咕哝道。

===Original===
“Don’t tell him anything. Nobody can guess what he thinks, and now even I cannot tell what he means when he speaks. It was as if he knew a secret.” She put an accent into those words which made Réal feel moved almost to tears. He repeated that Peyrol could have no influence over him, and he felt that he was speaking the truth. He was in the power of his own word. Ever since he had left the Admiral in a gold-embroidered uniform, impatient to return to his guests, he was on a service for which he had volunteered. For a moment he had the sensation of an iron hoop very tight round his chest. She peered at his face closely, and it was more than he could bear.

===Chinese===
“什么也别告诉他。谁也猜不透他在想什么，如今就连他开口说话，连我也听不出他是什么意思了。就仿佛他知道一个秘密似的。”她在那几个字上落下重音，那语气叫雷亚尔感动得几乎落泪。他重又说了一遍，佩罗尔对他不可能有什么影响，而他也觉得自己说的是真话。他已被自己的诺言所束缚。自从他离开那位身着一身绣金军礼服、急于回去陪客的海军上将之后，他便身处一桩他自愿请缨的差使之中了。有那么一瞬，他感到一道铁箍紧紧勒在胸口。她凑近了端详他的脸，这叫他再也承受不住了。

===Original===
“All right. I’ll be careful,” he said. “And Catherine, is she also dangerous?”

In the sheen of the moonlight Arlette, her neck and head above the gleams of the fichu, visible and elusive, smiled at him and moved a step closer.

===Chinese===
“好吧。我会当心的，”他说。“那么凯瑟琳呢，她也危险么？”

在月光的清辉里，阿尔莱特——她的脖颈和脑袋露在 *fichu*（三角披巾）的微光之上，看得见、又捉摸不定——朝他微微一笑，又走近了一步。

===Original===
“Poor Aunt Catherine,” she said.﻿ ﻿… “Put your arm round me, Eugène.﻿ ﻿… She can do nothing. She used to follow me with her eyes always. She thought I didn’t notice, but I did. And now she seems unable to look me in the face. Peyrol too, for that matter. He used to follow me with his eyes. Often I wondered what made them look at me like that. Can you tell, Eugène? But it’s all changed now.”

===Chinese===
“可怜的凯瑟琳姑妈。”她说。……“用你的胳膊搂着我，欧仁。……她什么也做不了。从前她总是用眼睛跟着我。她以为我没留意，可我留意到了。可如今她似乎连正眼也不敢看我。佩罗尔也是，说来也是一样。他从前也老拿眼睛跟着我。我常纳闷，是什么叫他们那样看着我。你说得上来么，欧仁？可这一切如今全变了。”

===Original===
“Yes, it is all changed,” said Réal in a tone which he tried to make as light as possible. “Does Catherine know you are here?”

“When we went upstairs this evening I lay down all dressed on my bed and she sat on hers. The candle was out, but in the moonlight I could see her quite plainly with her hands on her lap. When I could lie still no longer I simply got up and went out of the room. She was still sitting at the foot of her bed. All I did was to put my finger on my lips and then she dropped her head. I don’t think I quite closed the door.﻿ ﻿… Hold me tighter, Eugène, I am tired.﻿ ﻿… Strange, you know! Formerly, a long time ago, before I ever saw you, I never rested and never felt tired.” She stopped her murmur suddenly and lifted a finger recommending silence. She listened and Réal listened too, he did not know for what; and in this sudden concentration on a point, all that had happened since he had entered the room seemed to him a dream in its improbability and in the more than lifelike force dreams have in their inconsequence. Even the woman letting herself go on his arm seemed to have no weight as it might have happened in a dream.

===Chinese===
“是的，全变了，”雷亚尔说，竭力把话说得尽可能轻松。“凯瑟琳知道你在这儿吗？”

“今晚我们上楼的时候，我衣服也没脱就躺到了自己床上，她坐在她的床沿。蜡烛已经灭了，可是在月光底下，我能清清楚楚地看见她，双手搁在膝上。等到我再也躺不住了，我便索性起身走出了屋子。她还坐在自己床脚那儿。我只把一根手指按在唇上，她便垂下了头。我想我并没有把门关严。……把我搂紧些，欧仁，我累了。……真奇怪，你知道！从前，很久很久以前，在我还从未见过你的时候，我从不歇息，也从不觉得累。”她忽然止住了喃喃低语，竖起一根手指示意噤声。她侧耳倾听，雷亚尔也侧耳倾听，他不知在听什么；而在这骤然凝聚于一点的当儿，自打他踏进这屋子以来所发生的一切，在他看来都像是一场梦——梦那般的匪夷所思，又带着梦那般的、在它毫无因果之中却比真还要逼真的力道。便是这个任由自己倚在他臂弯里的女人，仿佛也没有分量，一如在梦里可能发生的那样。

===Original===
“She is there,” breathed Arlette suddenly, rising on tiptoe to reach up to his ear. “She must have heard you go past.”

“Where is she?” asked Réal with the same intense secrecy.

“Outside the door. She must have been listening to the murmur of our voices.﻿ ﻿…” Arlette breathed into his ear as if relating an enormity. “She told me one day that I was one of those who are fit for no man’s arms.”

===Chinese===
“她在那儿，”阿尔莱特忽然吐出这一声，踮起脚尖凑到他耳边。“她一定听见了你走过。”

“她在哪儿？”雷亚尔同样以一种极度的隐秘低声问。

“在门外。她一定一直在听我们说话的喃喃声……”阿尔莱特对着他耳畔吐气，仿佛在叙述一桩骇人之事。“有一天她对我说，我是那种配不上任何男人怀抱的人。”

===Original===
At this he flung his other arm round her and looked into her enlarged as if frightened eyes, while she clasped him with all her strength and they stood like that a long time, lips pressed on lips without a kiss and breathless in the closeness of their contact. To him the stillness seemed to extend to the limits of the universe. The thought “Am I going to die?” flashed through that stillness and lost itself in it like a spark flying in an everlasting night. The only result of it was the tightening of his hold on Arlette.

===Chinese===
听到这话，他又伸出另一只臂膀把她搂住，凝视着她那双睁大了的、仿佛受了惊的眼睛，而她也使出全部力气紧紧抱住他，他们就这样站了很久，唇贴着唇，却没有一个吻，在那贴合的紧密里连气也透不出来。于他而言，那一片静止仿佛一直延伸到了宇宙的尽头。“我可是要死了么？”这个念头掠过那片静止，又消融在其中，像一颗火星飞进了一个永恒的黑夜里。它唯一的结果，是叫他更紧地搂住了阿尔莱特。

===Original===
An aged and uncertain voice was heard uttering the word “Arlette.” Catherine, who had been listening to their murmurs, could not bear the long silence. They heard her trembling tones as distinctly as though she had been in the room. Réal felt as if it had saved his life. They separated silently.

===Chinese===
一个苍老而迟疑的声音，吐出了“阿尔莱特”这个名字。一直在倾听他们低语的凯瑟琳，再也承受不住这长长的沉默。他们把她那颤抖的声调听得一清二楚，就仿佛她也在屋里似的。雷亚尔觉得，这声音仿佛救了他的命。他们默默地分开了。

===Original===
“Go away,” called out Arlette.

“Arl.﻿ ﻿…”

“Be quiet,” she cried louder. “You can do nothing.”

“Arlette,” came through the door, tremulous and commanding.

“She will wake up Scevola,” remarked Arlette to Réal in a conversational tone. And they both waited for sounds that did not come. Arlette pointed her finger at the wall. “He is there, you know.”

===Chinese===
“你走开，”阿尔莱特扬声喊道。

“阿尔……”

“安静些，”她放大了声音喝道，“你什么也做不了。”

“阿尔莱特，”门那边传来的声音，颤抖而又带着威严。

“她要把塞沃拉吵醒了。”阿尔莱特用一种闲谈的口吻对雷亚尔说。他们俩都等着那些并没有传来的声响。阿尔莱特朝那堵墙竖起一根指头。“他就在那边，你知道。”

===Original===
“He is asleep,” muttered Réal. But the thought “I am lost” which he formulated in his mind had no reference to Scevola.

“He is afraid,” said Arlette contemptuously in an undertone. “But that means little. He would quake with fright one moment and rush out to do murder the next.”

===Chinese===
“他睡着了。”雷亚尔咕哝道。然而他在心里盘桓的那一句“我完了”，与塞沃拉并无干系。

“他胆子小着呢，”阿尔莱特轻蔑地低声说。“可那也算不了什么。他上一刻还吓得直哆嗦，下一刻就会冲出去杀人。”

===Original===
Slowly, as if drawn by the irresistible authority of the old woman, they had been moving towards the door. Réal thought with the sudden enlightenment of passion: “If she does not go now I won’t have the strength to part from her in the morning.” He had no image of death before his eyes but of a long and intolerable separation. A sigh verging upon a moan reached them from the other side of the door and made the air around them heavy with sorrow against which locks and keys will not avail.

“You had better go to her,” he whispered in a penetrating tone.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，仿佛被那老妇人不可抗拒的威严所牵引，他们一直朝门口挪去。雷亚尔带着激情骤然醒豁时的心念想道：“要是她这会儿不走，到了早晨我便没有气力跟她分别了。”他眼前浮现的并非死亡的影子，而是一场漫长而难以忍受的别离。一声近乎呻吟的叹息从门的那边传来，使他们周遭的空气都因哀愁而变得沉重——再怎样的锁和钥匙，也抵挡不住这种哀愁。

“你还是到她那儿去吧。”他用一种穿透人心的低声说。

===Original===
“Of course I will,” said Arlette with some feeling. “Poor old thing. She and I have only each other in the world, but I am the daughter here, she must do what I tell her.” With one of her hands on Réal’s shoulder she put her mouth close to the door and said distinctly:

“I am coming directly. Go back to your room and wait for me,” as if she had no doubt of being obeyed.

===Chinese===
“我自然要去的。”阿尔莱特带了几分动情地说。“可怜的老太太。这世上她和我只有彼此了，可我才是这一辈的女儿，她得听我的。”她一只手搭在雷亚尔肩上，把嘴凑近门，清清楚楚地说道：

“我就来。你回你屋里去，等着我。”那口气，仿佛她毫不怀疑自己会被听从。

===Original===
A profound silence ensued. Perhaps Catherine had gone already. Réal and Arlette stood still for a whole minute as if both had been changed into stone.

“Go now,” said Réal in a hoarse, hardly audible voice.

===Chinese===
随后是一片深沉的寂静。也许凯瑟琳已经走了。雷亚尔和阿尔莱特一动不动地站了整整一分钟，仿佛两个都被变成了石头。

“走吧。”雷亚尔用一种沙哑的、几乎听不见的声音说。

===Original===
She gave him a quick kiss on the lips, and again they stood like a pair of enchanted lovers bewitched into immobility.

“If she stays on,” thought Réal, “I shall never have the courage to tear myself away, and then I shall have to blow my brains out.” But when at last she moved he seized her again and held her as if she had been his very life. When he let her go he was appalled by hearing a very faint laugh of her secret joy.

===Chinese===
她飞快地在他唇上一吻，于是他们又站定了，像一对中了魔咒、被定住不动的恋人。

“她要是不走，”雷亚尔心想，“我便再也没勇气挣脱开去，那我就只好拿枪打碎自己的脑壳了。”可等到她终于动了，他又一把抓住她，把她抱住，仿佛她便是他的性命。待他放开她时，他听见一声极轻的、属于她那秘密欢喜的轻笑，不禁为之愕然。

===Original===
“Why do you laugh?” he asked in a scared tone.

She stopped to answer him over her shoulder.

“I laughed because I thought of all the days to come. Days and days and days. Have you thought of them?”

“Yes,” Réal faltered, like a man stabbed to the heart, holding the door half open. And he was glad to have something to hold on to.

===Chinese===
“你笑什么？”他问，声音里透着惊惶。

她停下脚步，回过头来答他。

“我笑，是因为我想到了今后所有的日子。一天又一天，又一天。你可曾想过它们？”

“想过。”雷亚尔嗫嚅道，像一个被一刀捅进心窝的人，半开着门。他很高兴手里总算有样东西可以攀住。

===Original===
She slipped out with a soft rustle of her silk skirt, but before he had time to close the door behind her she put back her arm for an instant. He had just time to press the palm of her hand to his lips. It was cool. She snatched it away and he had the strength of mind to shut the door after her. He felt like a man chained to the wall and dying of thirst, from whom a cold drink is snatched away. The room became dark suddenly. He thought, “A cloud over the moon, a cloud over the moon, an enormous cloud,” while he walked rigidly to the window, insecure and swaying as if on a tight rope. After a moment he perceived the moon in a sky on which there was no sign of the smallest cloud anywhere. He said to himself: “I suppose I nearly died just now. But no,” he went on thinking with deliberate cruelty, “oh no, I shall not die. I shall only suffer, suffer, suffer.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
她溜了出去，绸裙带起一阵柔软的窸窣，可他还来不及在她身后把门关上，她便把胳膊伸了回来，停留了一瞬。他刚好来得及把她的手心按到唇边。那只手是凉的。她猛地抽了回去，而他也定下心神，在她身后关上了门。他觉得自己像一个被铁链锁在墙上、渴得要死的人，被人一把夺走了那杯凉水。屋子忽然暗了下来。他一边僵直地朝窗口走去，一边想：“一片云遮住了月亮，一片云遮住了月亮，一片好大的云。”他脚下虚浮，摇摇晃晃，仿佛走在一条绷紧的绳索上。过了一会儿，他望见月亮悬在天上，那片天空上，到处看不见一丝一毫最小的云。他对自己说：“我想，我方才差点就死了。可不对，”他带着一种存心的残忍，继续想下去，“哦不，我不会死的。我只会受苦，受苦，受苦。……”

===Original===
“Suffer, suffer.” Only by stumbling against the side of the bed did he discover that he had gone away from the window. At once he flung himself on it violently with his face buried in the pillow, which he bit to restrain the cry of distress about to burst through his lips. Natures schooled into insensibility, when once overcome by a mastering passion are, like vanquished giants, ready for despair. He, a man on service, felt himself shrinking from death and that doubt contained in itself all possible doubts of his own fortitude. The only thing he knew was that he would be gone tomorrow morning. He shuddered along his whole extended length, then lay still gripping a handful of bedclothes in each hand to prevent himself from leaping up in panicky restlessness. He was saying to himself pedantically, “I must lie down and rest, I must rest to have strength for tomorrow, I must rest,” while the tremendous struggle to keep still broke out in waves of perspiration on his forehead. At last sudden oblivion must have descended on him because he turned over and sat up suddenly with the sound of the word “*Ecoutez*” in his ears.

===Chinese===
“受苦，受苦。”直到撞上了床沿，他才发现自己已经离开了窗口。他当即猛地扑倒在床上，把脸埋进枕头里，咬住枕头，好压住那即将冲出唇间的一声凄厉之喊。那些被磨砺得麻木无感的天性，一旦被一股主宰的激情所压倒，便如同被征服的巨人，随时听任绝望摆布。他，一个身在任上的人，发觉自己竟然畏缩于死亡，而这一疑虑本身，便已蕴含了对自身坚毅的一切可能的怀疑。他唯一知道的是，明天一早他便要走了。他伸直了身子，从头到脚一阵战栗，随后便静静地躺着，每只手各攥着一把被褥，好叫自己不至于在惊惶不安中一跃而起。他带着学究气地对自己说：“我必须躺下来歇息，我必须歇息，好攒足力气对付明天，我必须歇息。”然而那拼命保持静止的巨大搏斗，化作一阵阵冷汗从他额头上沁出。末了，一种骤然的昏沉想必是降临到了他头上，因为他翻了个身，猛地坐了起来，耳畔还响着那个词的余音——“*Ecoutez*（听着）”。

===Original===
A strange, dim, cold light filled the room; a light he did not recognize for anything he had known before, and at the foot of his bed stood a figure in dark garments with a dark shawl over its head, with a fleshless predatory face and dark hollows for its eyes, silent, expectant, implacable.﻿ ﻿… “Is this death?” he asked himself, staring at it terrified. It resembled Catherine. It said again: “*Ecoutez.*” He took away his eyes from it, and glancing down noticed that his clothes were torn open on his chest. He would not look up at that thing, whatever it was, spectre or old woman, and said:

“Yes, I hear you.”

===Chinese===
一种古怪的、朦胧的、冰冷的光充满了这间屋子；一种他从前不曾见识过的光；而在他床脚，立着一个身影，一身深色的衣裳，头上蒙着一条深色的披巾，一张皮肉干瘪、如猛禽般的脸，一双眼睛是两个黑黢黢的凹陷，沉默着、等待着、不留情面。……“这就是死亡么？”他暗问自己，惊恐地盯着它。它像凯瑟琳。它又说了一遍：“*Ecoutez*（听着）。”他把目光从它身上移开，往下一瞥，发觉自己胸前的衣襟已被撕开。他不愿抬头去看那东西——无论它是什么，是幽灵也好，是老妇人也罢——说道：

“是，我听见你了。”

===Original===
“You are an honest man.” It was Catherine’s unemotional voice. “The day has broken. You will go away.”

“Yes,” he said without raising his head.

“She is asleep,” went on Catherine or whoever it was, “exhausted, and you would have to shake her hard before she would wake. You will go. You know,” the voice continued inflexibly, “she is my niece, and you know that there is death in the folds of her skirt and blood about her feet. She is for no man.”

===Chinese===
“你是个诚实的人。”那是凯瑟琳不带感情的声音。“天已经亮了。你要走了。”

“是。”他说，没有抬起头来。

“她睡着了，”凯瑟琳——或者说无论那是谁——继续说道，“她已筋疲力尽，要把她摇醒，非得下狠劲不可。你要走。你知道，”那声音毫不通融地继续道，“她是我的外甥女，你也知道，她裙裾的褶皱里藏着死亡，她的脚边沾着鲜血。她不属任何男人。”

===Original===
Réal felt all the anguish of an unearthly experience. This thing that looked like Catherine and spoke like a cruel fate had to be faced. He raised his head in this light that seemed to him appalling and not of this world.

“Listen well to me, you too,” he said. “If she had all the madness of the world and the sin of all the murders of the Revolution on her shoulders I would still hug her to my breast. Do you understand?”

===Chinese===
雷亚尔感到一种超乎尘世的经历所带来的全部痛楚。这个看起来像凯瑟琳、说起话来却像残酷命运的东西，是必须面对的。他在那于他而言可怖之极、且不似人间的光里抬起了头。

“你也好好听着我说，”他说，“哪怕她肩上扛着这世上全部的疯狂、扛着大革命所有杀戮的罪孽，我仍要把她拥进怀里。你明白么？”

===Original===
The apparition which resembled Catherine lowered and raised its hooded head slowly. “There was a time when I could have hugged *l’enfer même* to my breast. He went away. He had his vow. You have only your honesty. You will go.”

===Chinese===
那个酷似凯瑟琳的身影，缓缓地垂下、又抬起它那蒙着兜帽的脑袋。“曾经有一段时候，我也能把 *l'enfer même*（地狱本身）拥进怀里。他走了。他有他的誓愿。你所有的，不过是你的诚实。你要走。”

===Original===
“I have my duty,” said Lieutenant Réal in measured tones, as if calmed by the excess of horror that old woman inspired him with.

“Go without disturbing her, without looking at her.”

===Chinese===
“我有我的职责。”雷亚尔中尉用一种不疾不徐的声调说道，仿佛是被那老妇人激起的无以复加的恐怖反倒叫他平静了下来。

“走罢，不要惊动她，不要看她。”

===Original===
“I will carry my shoes in my hand,” he said. He sighed deeply and felt as if sleepy. “It is very early,” he muttered.

“Peyrol is already down at the well,” announced Catherine. “What can he be doing there all this time?” she added in a troubled voice. Réal, with his feet now on the ground, gave her a side glance; but she was already gliding away, and when he looked again she had vanished from the room and the door was shut.

===Chinese===
“我会把鞋拎在手里。”他说。他深深地叹了口气，觉得自己像是发困。“天还早得很呢。”他咕哝道。

“佩罗尔已经在井边了。”凯瑟琳宣布道。“他在那儿待了这半天，能做什么呢？”她用一种不安的声音补了一句。雷亚尔双脚此刻已踏在地上，斜眼瞥了她一下；可她已经悄然滑了出去，待他再看时，她已从屋里消失了，门也关上了。

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
Catherine, going downstairs, found Peyrol still at the well. He seemed to be looking into it with extreme interest.

“Your coffee is ready, Peyrol,” she shouted to him from the doorway.

He turned very sharply like a man surprised and came along smiling.

“That’s pleasant news, Mademoiselle Catherine,” he said. “You are down early.”

“Yes,” she admitted, “but you too, Peyrol. Is Michel about? Let him come and have some coffee too.”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳下楼时，发现佩罗尔还在井边。他似乎正以极大的兴致往井里探望着。

“你的咖啡好了，佩罗尔。”她从门口朝他喊道。

他猛地一转身，像个被撞见的人似的，随即笑着走了过来。

“这可是个好消息，凯瑟琳小姐（Mademoiselle Catherine），”他说，“你起得真早。”

“是啊，”她承认道，“可你也不晚啊，佩罗尔。米歇尔在吗？让他也来喝点咖啡吧。”

===Original===
“Michel’s at the tartane. Perhaps you don’t know that she is going to make a little voyage.” He drank a mouthful of coffee and took a bite out of a slice of bread. He was hungry. He had been up all night and had even had a conversation with Citizen Scevola. He had also done some work with Michel after daylight; however, there had not been much to do because the tartane was always kept ready for sea. Then after having locked up again Citizen Scevola, who was extremely concerned as to what was going to happen to him but was left in a state of uncertainty, he had come up to the farm, had gone upstairs, where he was busy with various things for a time, and then had stolen down very cautiously to the well, where Catherine, whom he had not expected downstairs so early, had seen him before she went into Lieutenant Réal’s room. While he enjoyed his coffee he listened without any signs of surprise to Catherine’s comments upon the disappearance of Scevola. She had looked into his den. He had not slept on his pallet last night, of that she was certain, and he was nowhere to be seen, not even in the most distant field, from the points of vantage around the farm. It was inconceivable that he should have slipped away to Madrague, where he disliked to go, or to the village, where he was afraid to go. Peyrol remarked that whatever happened to him he was no great loss, but Catherine was not to be soothed.

===Chinese===
“米歇尔在塔尔塔纳上。您也许还不知道，她要出一趟小小的海。”他喝了一口咖啡，又咬了一口面包。他饿了。他整宿没合眼，甚至跟公民塞沃拉还谈过一番话。天亮之后他又和米歇尔干了些活；不过也没什么可干的，因为塔尔塔纳一向保持着随时可以出海的状态。然后，他把满心挂念自己下场、却被蒙在鼓里不得其详的公民塞沃拉重新锁好，便上了农庄，到楼上去忙了一阵种种杂事，接着又蹑手蹑脚地溜到井边——不料凯瑟琳比他预想的下楼更早，在他进雷亚尔中尉（Lieutenant Réal）的房间之前就瞧见了他。他一边喝着咖啡，一边听着凯瑟琳对塞沃拉失踪的议论，脸上毫无诧异的表示。她已经去他的窝里看过了。他昨夜没有睡在那张草铺上，这一点她拿得准，而农庄四下里各处高点的视野所及，连最远的田里也见不着他的人影。他不可能溜到马德拉格去——他讨厌去那儿；也不可能是去了村子——他害怕去那儿。佩罗尔说，不管他出了什么事，都算不得什么大损失，可凯瑟琳的心绪怎么也平不下来。

===Original===
“It frightens a body,” she said. “He may be hiding somewhere to jump on one treacherously. You know what I mean, Peyrol.”

“Well, the lieutenant will have nothing to fear, as he’s going away. As to myself, Scevola and I are good friends. I had a long talk with him quite recently. You two women can manage him perfectly; and then, who knows, perhaps he has gone away for good.”

Catherine stared at him, if such a word as stare can be applied to a profound contemplative gaze. “The lieutenant has nothing to fear from him,” she repeated cautiously.

“No, he is going away. Didn’t you know it?” The old woman continued to look at him profoundly. “Yes, he is on service.”

===Chinese===
“这叫人心里发毛，”她说，“他没准儿正藏在什么地方，打算冷不防扑上来害人。你明白我的意思，佩罗尔。”

“嗳，中尉就要走了，没什么可怕他的。至于我嘛，塞沃拉跟我是好朋友。前不久我还跟他长谈了一次。你们两个女人对付他绰绰有余；再者说，谁知道呢，没准儿他已经一去不回了。”

凯瑟琳凝目望着他——如果“凝目”这个词能用来形容那样一种深沉出神的注视的话。“中尉用不着怕他。”她审慎地重复了一遍。

“是啊，他要走了。你不知道吗？”老妇人继续深沉地望着他。“是的，他在执行公务。”

===Original===
For another minute or so Catherine continued silent in her contemplative attitude. Then her hesitation came to an end. She could not resist the desire to inform Peyrol of the events of the night. As she went on Peyrol forgot the half-full bowl of coffee and his half-eaten piece of bread. Catherine’s voice flowed with austerity. She stood there, imposing and solemn like a peasant-priestess. The relation of what had been to her a soul-shaking experience did not take much time, and she finished with the words, “The lieutenant is an honest man.” And after a pause she insisted further: “There is no denying it. He has acted like an honest man.”

For a moment longer Peyrol continued to look at the coffee in the bowl, then without warning got up with such violence that the chair behind him was thrown back upon the flagstones.

“Where is he, that honest man?” he shouted suddenly in stentorian tones which not only caused Catherine to raise her hands, but frightened himself, and he dropped at once to a mere forcible utterance. “Where is that man? Let me see him.”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳又保持了一分钟光景那副出神沉思的姿态，没有作声。随后她的踌躇到了头。她按捺不住要把昨夜的事情告诉佩罗尔。她说下去，佩罗尔便忘了那半碗咖啡和啃了一半的面包。凯瑟琳的声音带着一种肃穆流淌出来。她站在那儿，端庄凝重，像一位农妇出身的女祭司。讲述那段于她而言惊心动魄的遭遇并没花多少时间，她以这句话收尾：“中尉是个诚实的人。”停了片刻她又强调了一遍：“这是否认不了的。他的所作所为，是个诚实的人。”

佩罗尔又对着碗里的咖啡看了一会儿，忽然毫无预兆地霍然起身，动作之猛，把身后的椅子撞翻在石板地上。

“那个人在哪儿，那个诚实的人？”他忽然用洪钟般的嗓门吼了出来，不仅吓得凯瑟琳抬起了双手，也把自己吓了一跳，于是马上压低了嗓门，只剩下一种强压着的口气。“那个人在哪儿？让我见见他。”

===Original===
Even Catherine’s hieratic composure was disturbed.

“Why?” she said, looking really disconcerted, “he will be down here directly. This bowl of coffee is for him.”

Peyrol made as if to leave the kitchen, but Catherine stopped him. “For God’s sake, Monsieur Peyrol,” she said, half in entreaty and half in command, “don’t wake up the child. Let her sleep. Oh, let her sleep! Don’t wake her up. God only knows how long it is since she has slept properly. I could not tell you. I daren’t think of it.” She was shocked by hearing Peyrol declare: “All this is confounded nonsense.” But he sat down again, seemed to catch sight of the coffee bowl and emptied what was left in it down his throat.

“I don’t want her on my hands more crazy than she has been before,” said Catherine in a sort of exasperation but in a very low tone. This phrase in its selfish form expressed a real and profound compassion for her niece. She dreaded the moment when that fatal Arlette would wake up and the dreadful complications of life which her slumbers had suspended would have to be picked up again. Peyrol fidgeted on his seat.

===Chinese===
就连凯瑟琳那祭司般的镇定也被搅动了。

“怎么啦？”她说，脸上当真有些发窘，“他一会儿就下来。这碗咖啡就是给他的。”

佩罗尔做出要离开厨房的样子，凯瑟琳却拦住了他。“看在老天爷的份上，佩罗尔先生，”她半是恳求、半是命令地说，“别吵醒那孩子。让她睡吧。哦，让她睡！别把她吵醒。天知道她有多久没有好好睡过了。我说不上来。我都不敢去想。”她听见佩罗尔宣称“这全是他妈的胡扯”，不由得大吃一惊。可他又坐了下来，像是才注意到那碗咖啡，把里头剩下的东西一仰脖灌了下去。

“我不想她到了我手里，比从前疯得更厉害。”凯瑟琳带着几分恼怒、却把声音压得极低地说。她这一句以自私的口吻说出的话，表达的却是对外甥女真切而深沉的怜惜。她害怕的是，那个命根子似的阿尔莱特（Arlette）一旦醒来，那些因她睡着而暂时搁置的可怕的人生纠葛便又得重新拾起。佩罗尔在座位上局促地挪动着。

===Original===
“And so he told you he was going? He actually did tell you that?” he asked.

“He promised to go before the child wakes up.… At once.”

“But, *sacré nom d’un chien*, there is never any wind before eleven o’clock,” Peyrol exclaimed in a tone of profound annoyance, yet trying to moderate his voice, while Catherine, indulgent to his changing moods, only compressed her lips and nodded at him soothingly. “It is impossible to work with people like that,” he mumbled.

“Do you know, Monsieur Peyrol, that she has been to see the priest?” Catherine was heard suddenly towering above her end of the table. The two women had had a talk before Arlette had been induced by her aunt to lie down. Peyrol gave a start.

“What? Priest?… Now look here, Catherine,” he went on with repressed ferocity, “do you imagine that all this interests me in the least?”

===Chinese===
“那么，他跟你说过他要走了？他当真跟你说了？”他问。

“他答应在孩子醒来之前就走。……马上就走。”

“可是，*sacré nom d’un chien*（他妈的），十一点钟以前从来没有风。”佩罗尔带着极深的恼怒嚷道，却又竭力压着嗓门，而凯瑟琳对他阴晴不定的脾气颇为宽容，只是抿紧了嘴唇，安抚地朝他点点头。“跟这种人没法共事。”他嘟囔道。

“佩罗尔先生，您可知道，她去找过神父了？”忽然传来凯瑟琳的声音，她在桌子那头一下子威严起来。在阿尔莱特被姑母劝着躺下之前，两个女人曾有过一番交谈。佩罗尔吃了一惊。

“什么？神父？……我说，凯瑟琳，”他按捺着一股狠劲往下说，“你以为这些事我有一丁点兴趣吗？”

===Original===
“I can think of nothing but that niece of mine. We two have nobody but each other in the world,” she went on, reproducing the very phrase Arlette had used to Réal. She seemed to be thinking aloud, but noticed that Peyrol was listening with attention. “He wanted to shut her up from everybody,” and the old woman clasped her meagre hands with a sudden gesture. “I suppose there are still some convents about the world.”

“You and the *patronne* are mad together,” declared Peyrol. “All this only shows what an ass the curé is. I don’t know much about these things, though I have seen some nuns in my time, and some very queer ones too, but it seems to me that they don’t take crazy people into convents. Don’t you be afraid. I tell you that.” He stopped, because the inner door of the kitchen came open and Lieutenant Réal stepped in. His sword hung on his forearm by the belt, his hat was on his head. He dropped his little valise on the floor and sat down in the nearest chair to put on his shoes, which he had brought down in his other hand. Then he came up to the table. Peyrol, who had kept his eyes on him, thought: “Here is one who looks like a moth scorched in the fire.” Réal’s eyes were sunk, his cheeks seemed hollowed, and the whole face had an arid and dry aspect.

===Chinese===
“我心里搁得下的，只有我那个外甥女。这世上我们两个除了彼此，再没有别的人了。”她接着说，把阿尔莱特对雷亚尔说过的那句话几乎原样搬了过来。她像是在自言自语，却注意到佩罗尔正用心听着。“他想要把她跟所有人都隔绝开来。”老妇人忽然一把攥紧了自己那双干瘪的手。“我估摸着这世上总还有几座修道院吧。”

“您跟女主人（*patronne*）一块儿都疯了。”佩罗尔断言道。“这一切只说明那个本堂神父是个蠢驴。这些事我懂得不多，虽说这辈子也见过些修女，还有些古怪得很的修女，不过据我看，修道院并不收疯子。您尽管放心。这话我跟您说了。”他住了口，因为厨房里头那扇门开了，雷亚尔中尉走了进来。他的佩剑用腰带挎在小臂上，帽子已戴在头上。他把那只小旅行袋（valise）往地上一搁，便在最近的一把椅子上坐下，穿起他另一只手拎下来的鞋子来。然后他走到桌前。佩罗尔一直盯着他看，心想：“这副模样，活像一只被火燎焦了的飞蛾。”雷亚尔眼窝深陷，双颊似乎凹陷下去，整张脸带着一种枯干焦灼的神气。

===Original===
“Well, you are in a fine state for the work of deceiving the enemy,” Peyrol observed. “Why, to look at you, nobody would believe a word you said. You are not going to be ill, I hope. You are on service. You haven’t got the right to be ill. I say, Mademoiselle Catherine, produce the bottle, you know, my private bottle.…” He snatched it from Catherine’s hand, poured some brandy into the lieutenant’s coffee, pushed the bowl towards him and waited. “*Nom de nom!*” he said forcibly, “don’t you know what this is for? It’s for you to drink.” Réal obeyed with a strange, automatic docility. “And now,” said Peyrol, getting up, “I will go to my room and shave. This is a great day—the day we are going to see the lieutenant off.”

===Chinese===
“嗬，你这副尊容，去干迷惑敌人的活计可真够瞧的。”佩罗尔说道。“说真的，瞧你这副模样，谁也不会相信你嘴里吐出半个字。你总不至于要生病吧，我希望。你在执行公务。你没有生病的权利。我说，凯瑟琳小姐，把那瓶酒拿出来，您知道，我那瓶私藏的。……”他从凯瑟琳手里一把夺过酒瓶，往中尉的咖啡里倒了些白兰地，把碗推到他面前，等着。“*Nom de nom!*（我的天！）”他用力地说，“你难道不知道这是干什么的？这是给你喝的。”雷亚尔带着一种古怪而机械的顺从照做了。“那么现在，”佩罗尔站起身来说，“我回屋刮脸去了。今天是个大日子——咱们送中尉上路的日子。”

===Original===
Till then Réal had not uttered a word, but directly the door closed behind Peyrol he raised his head.

“Catherine!” His voice was like a rustle in his throat. She was looking at him steadily, and he continued: “Listen, when she finds I am gone you tell her I will return soon. Tomorrow. Always tomorrow.”

“Yes, my good Monsieur,” said Catherine in an unmoved voice but clasping her hands convulsively. “There is nothing else I would dare tell her!”

“She will believe you,” whispered Réal wildly.

“Yes! She will believe me,” repeated Catherine in a mournful tone.

Réal got up, put the sword-belt over his head, picked up the valise. There was a little flush on his cheeks.

“Adieu,” he said to the silent old woman. She made no answer, but as he turned away she raised her hand a little, hesitated, and let it fall again. It seemed to her that the women of Escampobar had been singled out for divine wrath. Her niece appeared to her like the scapegoat charged with all the murders and blasphemies of the Revolution. She herself, too, had been cast out from the grace of God. But that had been a long time ago. She had made her peace with Heaven since. Again she raised her hand and, this time, made in the air the sign of the cross at the back of Lieutenant Réal.

===Chinese===
在那之前雷亚尔一个字也没说，可佩罗尔身后那扇门一关上，他便抬起了头。

“凯瑟琳！”他的声音像是从喉咙里挤出来的一阵沙沙声。她定定地望着他，他接着说：“听着，等她发觉我不在了，你就告诉她我很快就会回来。明天。永远是明天。”

“好的，好先生。”凯瑟琳用一种不动声色的声音说，双手却不由自主地攥紧了。“别的我哪敢跟她说！”

“她会信你的。”雷亚尔发狂似地低声说。

“是的！她会信我的。”凯瑟琳用一种凄凉的声调重复道。

雷亚尔站起身，把剑带从头上套过去，拎起旅行袋。他双颊微微泛起一抹红晕。

“Adieu（永别了）。”他对那沉默的老妇人说。她没有回答，可当他转身离去时，她微微抬起手，犹豫了一下，又垂了下去。在她看来，埃斯坎波巴的女人们似乎被挑出来承受天谴了。她的外甥女在她眼里就像一只替罪羊，背负着大革命所有杀戮与亵渎的罪愆。而她自己，也曾被逐出上帝的恩宠之外。不过那是很久以前的事了。从那以后她早已与上天和解。她又抬起手来，这一回，在雷亚尔中尉的背影上方，朝空中画了一个十字。

===Original===
Meanwhile, upstairs Peyrol, scraping his big flat cheek with an English razor-blade at the window, saw Lieutenant Réal on the path to the shore; and high above there, commanding a vast view of sea and land, he shrugged his shoulders impatiently with no visible provocation. One could not trust those epaulette-wearers. They would cram a fellow’s head with notions either for their own sake or for the sake of the service. Still, he was too old a bird to be caught with chaff; and besides, that long-legged stiff beggar going down the path, with all his officer airs, was honest enough. At any rate he knew a seaman when he saw one, though he was as cold-blooded as a fish. Peyrol had a smile which was a little awry.

===Chinese===
与此同时，楼上窗前，佩罗尔正用一片英国剃刀刮着他那宽大扁平的面颊，瞧见雷亚尔中尉正走在通往海岸的小径上；而在那高高之处，俯瞰着海陆一片辽阔的景致，他无缘无故地不耐烦地耸了耸肩。那些佩戴肩章的军官（epaulette-wearer）是不能信任的。他们要么为了自己、要么为了公务，会把一桩桩念头塞进人家的脑袋。不过，他这只老鸟可不是拿点谷糠就能套住的；况且，那个大长腿、直挺挺地顺着小径走下去、摆着一身军官派头的家伙，为人倒是够诚实。至少他识货，一眼能看出谁是好水手，尽管他冷血得像条鱼。佩罗尔的笑意有一丝歪斜。

===Original===
Cleaning the razor-blade (one of a set of twelve in a case) he had a vision of a brilliantly hazy ocean and an English Indiaman with her yards braced all ways, her canvas blowing loose above her bloodstained decks overrun by a lot of privateersmen, and with the island of Ceylon swelling like a thin blue cloud on the far horizon. He had always wished to own a set of English blades and there he had got it, fell over it as it were, lying on the floor of a cabin which had been already ransacked. “For good steel—it was good steel,” he thought, looking at the blade fixedly. And there it was, nearly worn out. The others too. That steel! And here he was, holding the case in his hand as though he had just picked it up from the floor. Same case. Same man. And the steel worn out.

He shut the case brusquely, flung it into his sea-chest, which was standing open, and slammed the lid down. The feeling which was in his breast, and had been known to more articulate men than himself, was that life was a dream less substantial than the vision of Ceylon lying like a cloud on the sea. Dream left astern. Dream straight ahead. This disenchanted philosophy took the shape of fierce swearing. “*Sacré nom de nom de nom.… Tonnerre de bon Dieu!*”

===Chinese===
他擦净那片剃刀（一整盒十二片里的一片），眼前浮现出一片辉煌朦胧的大洋，一艘英国东印度商船（East Indiaman），她所有的帆桁四面撑开，松散的帆篷在沾满鲜血的甲板上方猎猎飘荡，甲板上满是私掠船的水手，而锡兰岛（Ceylon）像一团淡淡的蓝云，在遥远的天际隆起。他一直巴望拥有一整套英国刀片，而那一回他竟撞上了——简直像是绊倒在上面——那盒子就搁在一间已被洗劫一空的舱房地板上。“好钢啊——那可真是好钢，”他盯着那片刀片想道。可如今，它已快磨尽了。其余的也一样。那些钢啊！而此刻，他手里攥着那个盒子，仿佛刚从地板上捡起来似的。同一个盒子。同一个人。而钢已经磨尽了。

他啪地合上盒子，把它扔进敞着盖的水手衣物箱里，砰一声摔上箱盖。他胸中那股感受——比他更善于言辞的人也曾体味过——便是：人生不过一场梦，甚至还不如那座像云一样浮在海上的锡兰岛来得真切。梦被甩在了船尾。梦就在正前方。这种大彻大悟的虚无之感，化作了一通凶狠的咒骂。“*Sacré nom de nom de nom.（该死该死该死）……Tonnerre de bon Dieu!*（好上帝的雷劈啊！）”

===Original===
While tying his neckcloth he handled it with fury as though he meant to strangle himself with it. He rammed a soft cap on to his venerable locks recklessly, seized his cudgel—but before leaving the room walked up to the window giving on the east. He could not see the Petite Passe on account of the lookout hill, but to the left a great portion of the Hyères Roadstead lay spread out before him, pale grey in the morning light, with the land about Cape Blanc swelling in the distance with all its details blurred as yet and only one conspicuous object presenting to his sight something that might have been a lighthouse by its shape, but which Peyrol knew very well was the English corvette already under way and with all her canvas set.

===Chinese===
系领巾的时候他使着蛮力，像是要把自己勒死似的。他把一顶软帽不管不顾地扣到他那头令人起敬的白发上，抄起他的木棍——可还没出屋，就走到朝东的那扇窗前。因为那座瞭望小山的遮挡，他看不见小水道（Petite Passe），但左边一大片耶尔锚地（Hyères Roadstead）在他眼前铺展开来，在晨光中呈淡灰色，远处布朗角（Cape Blanc）一带的陆地隆起着，一切细节都还模模糊糊，唯有一个显眼的东西映入眼帘，看形状可能是一座灯塔，但佩罗尔心里清楚得很，那是那艘英国轻巡洋舰，已经起锚开航，张满了帆。

===Original===
This sight pleased Peyrol mainly because he had expected it. The Englishman was doing exactly what he had expected he would do, and Peyrol looked towards the English cruiser with a smile of malicious triumph as if he were confronting her captain. For some reason or other he imagined Captain Vincent as long-faced, with yellow teeth and a wig, whereas that officer wore his own hair and had a set of teeth which would have done honour to a London belle, and was really the hidden cause of Captain Vincent appearing so often wreathed in smiles.

That ship at this great distance and steering in his direction held Peyrol at the window long enough for the increasing light of the morning to burst into sunshine, colouring and filling-in the flat outline of the land with tints of wood and rock and field, with clear dots of buildings enlivening the view. The sun threw a sort of halo around the ship. Recollecting himself, Peyrol left the room and shut the door quietly. Quietly too he descended the stairs from his garret. On the landing he underwent a short inward struggle, at the end of which he approached the door of Catherine’s room and opening it a little, put his head in. Across the whole width of it he saw Arlette fast asleep. Her aunt had thrown a light coverlet over her. Her low shoes stood at the foot of the bed. Her black hair lay loose on the pillow; and Peyrol’s gaze became arrested by the long eyelashes on her pale cheek. Suddenly he fancied she moved, and he withdrew his head sharply, pulling the door to. He listened for a moment as if tempted to open it again, but judging it too risky, continued on his way downstairs. At his reappearance in the kitchen Catherine turned sharply. She was dressed for the day, with a big white cap on her head, a black bodice and a brown skirt with ample folds. She had a pair of varnished sabots on her feet over her shoes.

===Chinese===
这番景象令佩罗尔颇为满意，主要是因为他早已料到了。那英国人做的正是他预料之中会做的事，佩罗尔朝那艘英国巡洋舰投去一抹幸灾乐祸的得意的笑，仿佛他正面对着她的舰长。不知出于什么缘故，他把文森特舰长（Captain Vincent）想象成一张长脸、一口黄牙、戴着一顶假发，而事实上那位军官留的是自己的头发，一口牙齿即便放在伦敦的美人儿脸上也毫不逊色——这其实才是文森特舰长脸上常常挂满笑容的隐秘缘由。

那艘船在如此远的距离上朝他的方向驶来，使佩罗尔在窗前驻足了好一会儿，直到越来越亮的晨光一下子迸射成阳光，给那原本平板单调的陆地轮廓染上并填满了林木、岩石和田野的色彩，一处处清晰的建筑小白点使景色生动起来。阳光在那艘船周围投下了一圈光晕。佩罗尔回过神来，离开房间，轻轻带上了门。他也蹑手蹑脚地从他那间阁楼下楼来。在楼梯口他经历了一番短暂的内心挣扎，挣扎完了便走到凯瑟琳的房门前，把门推开一道缝，探进头去。隔着整个房间的宽度，他看见阿尔莱特正熟睡着。她姑母给她盖了一条薄夹被。她的矮帮鞋摆在床脚。她的黑发散落在枕头上；佩罗尔的目光落到了她苍白脸颊上那长长的睫毛上，便移不开了。忽然他仿佛觉得她动了一下，赶紧把头缩了回来，拉上了门。他驻足听了一会儿，像是又想推开门，但觉得太冒险，便继续下楼去了。他再次出现在厨房里时，凯瑟琳猛地转过身来。她已经穿戴整齐，头上戴着一顶白色大软帽，上身一件黑色紧身胸衣，下面一条棕色裙子，裙摆宽大多褶。她脚上的鞋外头还套着一双上了漆的木鞋（sabots）。

===Original===
“No signs of Scevola,” she said, advancing towards Peyrol. “And Michel, too, has not been here yet.”

Peyrol thought that if she had been only shorter, what with her black eyes and slightly curved nose she would have looked like a witch. But witches can read people’s thoughts, and he looked openly at Catherine with the pleasant conviction that she could not read his thoughts. He said:

“I took good care not to make any noise upstairs, Mademoiselle Catherine. When I am gone the house will be empty and quiet enough.”

She had a curious expression. She struck Peyrol suddenly as if she were lost in that kitchen in which she had reigned for many years. He continued:

“You will be alone all the morning.”

She seemed to be listening to some distant sound, and after Peyrol had added, “Everything is all right now,” she nodded, and after a moment said in a manner that for her was unexpectedly impulsive:

“Monsieur Peyrol, I am tired of life.”

He shrugged his shoulders and with somewhat sinister jocosity remarked:

“I will tell you what it is; you ought to have been married.”

She turned her back on him abruptly.

“No offence,” Peyrol excused himself in a tone of gloom rather than of apology. “It is no use to attach any importance to things. What is this life? Phew! Nobody can remember one-tenth of it. Here I am; and, you know, I would bet that if one of my old-time chums came along and saw me like this, here with you—I mean one of those chums that stand up for a fellow in a scrimmage and look after him should he be hurt—well, I bet,” he repeated, “he wouldn’t know me. He would say to himself, perhaps, ‘Hullo! here’s a comfortable married couple.’”

===Chinese===
“塞沃拉一点影子也没有，”她朝佩罗尔迎上来说，“还有米歇尔，也还没上来过。”

佩罗尔心想，她要是再矮一些，凭她那双黑眼睛和微微弯曲的鼻子，看着就像个巫婆了。可巫婆能看穿人家的心思，于是他大大方方地瞧着凯瑟琳，心里美滋滋地笃定她读不懂他的心思。他说道：

“我楼上可是着意没弄出一点声响来，凯瑟琳小姐。等我走了，这屋子空下来，可就清净透了。”

她脸上有一种古怪的神情。佩罗尔忽然觉得她像是迷失在这间她当家作主了许多年的厨房里似的。他接着说：

“您一上午都会是独个儿。”

她似乎在倾听某种远处的声响，等佩罗尔补了一句“现在一切都妥帖了”之后，她点了点头，过了一会儿以一种在她而言出人意料地冲动的方式说：

“佩罗尔先生，我活腻了。”

他耸了耸肩，带着几分不怀好意的调侃说道：

“我跟您说吧；您本该嫁过人的。”

她蓦地转过身去，背对着他。

“别见怪，”佩罗尔用一种与其说是道歉、不如说是阴郁的口气为自己开脱。“什么事都别太当真。这条命算什么？呸！谁也记不住它的十分之一。我呢，就在这儿；您要知道，我敢打赌，要是我从前的一个老伙计这会儿走过来，瞧见我这个样子，跟您在一块儿——我是说那种在混战里肯替你撑腰、你受了伤会照料你的伙计——嗐，我敢打赌，”他重复了一遍，“他保准认不出我来。他没准儿会暗自嘀咕：‘嚯！这对夫妻倒过得舒舒服服的。’”

===Original===
He paused. Catherine, with her back to him and calling him, not Monsieur, but Peyrol, *tout court*, remarked, not exactly with displeasure, but rather with an ominous accent, that this was no time for idle talk. Peyrol, however, continued, though his tone was very far from being that of idle talk:

“But you see, Mademoiselle Catherine, you were not like the others. You allowed yourself to be struck all of a heap, and at the same time you were too hard on yourself.”

Her long thin frame, bent low to work the bellows under the enormous overmantel, she assented: “Perhaps! We Escampobar women were always hard on ourselves.”

“That’s what I say. If you had had things happen to you which happened to me.…

“But you men, you are different. It doesn’t matter what you do. You have got your own strength. You need not be hard on yourselves. You go from one thing to another thoughtlessly.”

He remained looking at her searchingly, with something like a hint of a smile on his shaven lips, but she turned away to the sink, where one of the women working about the farm had deposited a great pile of vegetables. She started on them with a broken-bladed knife, preserving her sibylline air, even in that homely occupation.

“It will be a good soup, I see, at noon today,” said the rover suddenly. He turned on his heels and went out through the *salle*.

===Chinese===
他停了下来。凯瑟琳背对着他，对他不再称先生，而是径直（*tout court*）叫他佩罗尔，她说这会儿可不是闲扯的时候——那口气与其说是不悦，不如说是带着一种不祥的意味。然而佩罗尔还是说了下去，尽管他的口气离闲扯差了十万八千里：

“可是您瞧，凯瑟琳小姐，您跟旁人不一样。您由着自己一下子被击垮了，与此同时您又对自己太苛刻了。”

她那瘦长的身子在巨大的炉罩下面弯得低低的，正拉着风箱，闻言应道：“也许吧！我们埃斯坎波巴的女人，一向是对自己苛刻的。”

“我说的就是这个。要是您遇上过我遇上的那些事。……”

“可你们男人，不一样。你们做什么都无所谓。你们有自己的气力。你们用不着对自己苛刻。你们浑浑噩噩地从一桩事转到另一桩事。”

他定定地瞧着她，刮过的嘴唇上似乎挂着一丝若有若无的笑意，可她转身走向水槽，那儿有一个在农庄帮工的女人放下了一大堆蔬菜。她拿起一把断了刃的菜刀对付起它们来，即便在这种家常的活计里，也保持着她那先知般莫测的神气。

“我看今天正午的汤一定好喝。”漫游者忽然说道。他一转身，穿过正厅（*salle*）走了出去。

===Original===
The whole world lay open to him, or at any rate the whole of the Mediterranean, viewed down the ravine between the two hills. The bell of the farm’s milch-cow, which had a talent for keeping herself invisible, reached him from the right, but he could not see as much as the tips of her horns, though he looked for them. He stepped out sturdily. He had not gone twenty yards down the ravine when another sound made him stand still as if changed into stone. It was a faint noise resembling very much the hollow rumble an empty farm-cart would make on a stony road, but Peyrol looked up at the sky, and though it was perfectly clear, he did not seem pleased with its aspect. He had a hill on each side of him and the placid cove below his feet. He muttered “H’m! Thunder at sunrise. It must be in the west. It only wanted that!” He feared it would first kill the little breeze there was and then knock the weather up altogether. For a moment all his faculties seemed paralysed by that faint sound. On that sea ruled by the gods of Olympus he might have been a pagan mariner subject to Jupiter’s caprices; but like a defiant pagan he shook his fist vaguely at space, which answered him by a short and threatening mutter. Then he swung on his way till he caught sight of the two mastheads of the tartane, when he stopped to listen. No sound of any sort reached him from there, and he went on his way thinking, “‘Go from one thing to another thoughtlessly’! Indeed!… That’s all old Catherine knows about it.”

===Chinese===
整个世界在他面前敞开着，至少整个地中海是如此，从两座山之间的那条沟壑望下去便可尽收眼底。农庄那头奶牛的铃铛声从右边传来——那头牛很有把自己藏得无影无踪的天赋——可他哪怕找也瞧不见它哪怕一点犄角尖。他迈开结实的步子走了出去。他顺着沟壑才走了不到二十码，另一阵声响便使他像石头一样定住了。那是一阵微弱的声响，极像一个空荡荡的农庄大车碾过石头路面时发出的那种空洞的隆隆声，可佩罗尔抬头望了望天，虽然天色一片晴朗，他似乎对自己的所见并不满意。他两边各有一座山，脚下是那片平静的小海湾。他嘟囔道：“哼！日出时分就打雷。一定是在西边。就差这一出了！”他担心它会先把那仅存的一丝微风扼杀掉，然后把整个天气搅得一塌糊涂。有那么一瞬间，他浑身的官能似乎都被那微弱的声响麻痹了。在那片由奥林匹斯众神主宰的大海上，他或许就像一个听凭朱庇特反复无常脾性摆布的异教水手；可他像一个桀骜不驯的异教徒那样，朝虚空愤愤地挥了挥拳，而虚空回敬了他一声短促而充满威胁的闷响。然后他大步流星地继续赶路，直到望见塔尔塔纳的那两根桅杆顶端，便停下脚步侧耳倾听。那边没有传来任何声响，他便继续往前走，心里想着：“‘浑浑噩噩地从一桩事转到另一桩事’！哼！……老凯瑟琳懂的也就这么多了。”

===Original===
He had so many things to think of that he did not know which to lay hold of first. He just let them lie jumbled up in his head. His feelings too were in a state of confusion, and vaguely he felt that his conduct was at the mercy of an internal conflict. The consciousness of that fact accounted perhaps for his sardonic attitude towards himself and outwardly towards those whom he perceived on board the tartane, and especially towards the lieutenant, whom he saw sitting on the deck leaning against the head of the rudder, characteristically aloof from the two other persons on board. Michel, also characteristically, was standing on the top of the little cabin scuttle, obviously looking out for his *maître*. Citizen Scevola, sitting on deck, seemed at first sight to be at liberty, but as a matter of fact he was not. He was loosely tied up to a stanchion by three turns of the mainsheet with the knot in such a position that he could not get at it without attracting attention; and that situation seemed also somewhat characteristic of Citizen Scevola with its air of half liberty, half suspicion and, as it were, contemptuous restraint. The sansculotte, whose late experiences had nearly unsettled his reason, first by their utter incomprehensibility and afterwards by the enigmatical attitude of Peyrol, had dropped his head and folded his arms on his breast. And that attitude was dubious, too. It might have been resignation or it might have been profound sleep. The rover addressed himself first to the lieutenant.

“*Le moment approche*,” said Peyrol with a queer twitch at a corner of his lip, while under his soft woollen cap his venerable locks stirred in the breath of a suddenly warm air. “The great moment—eh?”

He leaned over the big tiller, and seemed to be hovering above the lieutenant’s shoulder.

“What’s this infernal company?” murmured the latter without even looking at Peyrol.

===Chinese===
他有太多的事情要想，竟不知先抓住哪一桩。他索性由着它们乱七八糟地堆在脑子里。他的情绪也是一团乱麻，他隐约觉得自己的所作所为正听凭一场内心冲突的摆布。也许正是对这一点的自觉，才造就了他对自己、以及对外——对他在塔尔塔纳上看到的那些人，尤其是对那位中尉——所表现出的那种讥诮的态度；他看见中尉坐在甲板上，靠着舵柱头，一如既往地与船上另外两个人疏离着。米歇尔也同样一如既往地站在小舱口顶上，分明是在张望他的师傅（*maître*）。公民塞沃拉坐在甲板上，乍一看像是自由的，实则不然。他被主帆索松松地绕在一根立柱上捆了三圈，绳结所处的位置使他无法不引人注意地去够它；而这一处境似乎也颇能代表公民塞沃拉其人——半是自由、半是嫌疑，以及一种仿佛是不屑一顾的拘束。这个无套裤汉，近来的遭遇几乎搅乱了他的神志——先是因为那些遭遇的完全不可理解，后来又因为佩罗尔那令人费解的态度——此刻他垂着头，双臂交叠在胸前。这一姿态同样可疑。它可能是认命，也可能是沉沉睡去。漫游者先对中尉开了口。

“*Le moment approche*（时刻临近了）。”佩罗尔说，嘴角怪怪地抽动了一下，他那顶软羊毛帽底下，令人起敬的白发在一股忽然变暖的气流中飘动。“伟大的时刻——嗯？”

他俯身撑在那根大舵柄上，像是悬在中尉的肩头上方。

“这一帮子是什么乌合之众？”后者嘟囔道，连看都没看佩罗尔一眼。

===Original===
“All old friends—*quoi*?” said Peyrol in a homely tone. “We will keep that little affair amongst ourselves. The fewer the men, the greater the glory. Catherine is getting the vegetables ready for the noonday soup and the Englishman is coming down towards the Passe, where he will arrive about noon too, ready to have his eye put out. You know, lieutenant, that will be your job. You may depend on me for sending you off when the moment comes. For what is it to you? You have no friends, you have not even a *petite amie*. As to expecting an old rover like me—oh no, lieutenant! Of course liberty is sweet, but what do you know of it, you epaulette-wearers? Moreover, I am no good for quarterdeck talks and all that politeness.”

“I wish, Peyrol, you would not talk so much,” said Lieutenant Réal, turning his head slightly. He was struck by the strange expression on the old rover’s face. “And I don’t see what the actual moment matters. I am going to look for the fleet. All you have to do is to hoist the sails for me and then scramble ashore.”

“Very simple,” observed Peyrol through his teeth, and then began to sing:

“Quoique leurs chapeaux sont bien laids

Goddam! Moi, j’aime les Anglais

Ils ont un si bon caractère!”

===Chinese===
“都是老朋友——*quoi*（可不是嘛）？”佩罗尔用一种家常的口气说。“这件小事咱们自己人之间处理了。人越少，功劳越大。凯瑟琳正给正午的汤备着菜呢，而那个英国人正朝水道这边过来，差不多也是正午到达，准备让人把眼睛给戳瞎。您知道的，中尉，那便是您的活计。到时候您尽管放心，我会把您送走的。这对您又算得了什么呢？您没有朋友，您连个小情人（*petite amie*）都没有。至于指望我这么个老漫游者——哦不，中尉！自由当然是甜美的，可你们这些佩戴肩章的军官又懂得什么？再说了，在后甲板上那一套寒暄客套，我可不来。”

“佩罗尔，我倒希望你别这么多嘴。”雷亚尔中尉微微转过头来说。老漫游者脸上那副古怪的神情给了他一击。“我也看不出眼下这一刻有什么要紧。我要去找舰队。你只需替我把帆升起来，然后摸上岸去就完了。”

“简单得很。”佩罗尔咬着牙说了一句，随即便唱了起来：

“*Quoique leurs chapeaux sont bien laids*

（虽说他们的帽子丑得很）

*Goddam! Moi, j'aime les Anglais*

（该死的！我倒喜欢英国人）

*Ils ont un si bon caractère!*

（他们的脾气可真好啊！）”

===Original===
but interrupted himself suddenly to hail Scevola:

“*Hé! Citoyen!*” and then remarked confidentially to Réal: “He isn’t asleep, you know, but he isn’t like the English, he has a *sacré mauvais caractère*. He got into his head,” continued Peyrol, in a loud and innocent tone, “that you locked him up in this cabin last night. Did you notice the venomous glance he gave you just now?”

Both Lieutenant Réal and the innocent Michel appeared surprised at his boisterousness; but all the time Peyrol was thinking: “I wish to goodness I knew how that thunderstorm is getting on and what course it is shaping. I can’t find that out unless I go up to the farm and get a view to the westward. It may be as far as the Rhône Valley; no doubt it is and it will come out of it too, curses on it. One won’t be able to reckon on half an hour of steady wind from any quarter.” He directed a look of ironic gaiety at all the faces in turn. Michel met it with a faithful-dog gaze and innocently open mouth. Scevola kept his chin buried on his chest. Lieutenant Réal was insensible to outward impressions, and his absent stare made nothing of Peyrol. The rover himself presently fell into thought. The last stir of air died out in the little basin, and the sun clearing Porquerolles inundated it with a sudden light, in which Michel blinked like an owl.

“It’s hot early,” he announced aloud, but only because he had formed the habit of talking to himself. He would not have presumed to offer an opinion unless asked by Peyrol.

===Chinese===
却又猛地住了口，转而招呼塞沃拉：

“*Hé, Citoyen!*（喂，公民！）”然后煞有介事地对雷亚尔耳语道：“他没睡着呢，您要知道，可他不像英国人，他有一副*sacré mauvais caractère*（该死的坏脾气）。他脑子里认定了，”佩罗尔继续用一种大声而天真的口气说道，“是您昨夜把他锁在这间舱房里的。您注意到他刚才恶狠狠地瞪您那一眼了吗？”

雷亚尔中尉和老实的米歇尔似乎都对他这番喧闹感到诧异；可佩罗尔心里一直在想：“我巴不得知道那场雷雨怎么样了，朝哪个方向走。除非我上农庄去朝西望一望，否则没法弄明白。它也许远在罗讷河谷（Rhône Valley）一带；没错，一定是，而且它还会从那边滚出来，该死的。这样一来，哪个方向也别想指望有半个钟头的稳当风。”他把一抹带着讥诮快意的目光依次投向每一张脸。米歇尔以一条忠犬般的目光和天真地张着的嘴迎接它。塞沃拉把下巴埋在胸口。雷亚尔中尉对外界的印象无动于衷，他那心不在焉的茫然凝视根本没把佩罗尔当回事。不久漫游者自己也陷入了沉思。最后一丝空气的骚动在小水池里消逝了，太阳越过波克罗勒岛（Porquerolles），一下子把阳光倾泻下来，米歇尔在那片光里像猫头鹰一样直眨眼。

“热得倒早。”他大声宣布道，但这不过是因为他养成了自言自语的习惯。除非佩罗尔问他，他可不敢贸然发表意见。

===Original===
His voice having recalled Peyrol to himself, he proposed to masthead the yards, and even asked Lieutenant Réal to help in that operation, which was accomplished in silence, except for the faint squeaking of the blocks. The sails, however, were kept hauled up in the gear.

“Like this,” said Peyrol, “you have only to let go the ropes and you will be under canvas at once.”

Without answering Réal returned to his position by the rudder-head. He was saying to himself: “I am sneaking off. No, there is honour, duty. And of course I will return. But when? They will forget all about me and I shall never be exchanged. This war may last for years—” and illogically he wished he could have had a God to whom he could pray for relief in his anguish. “She will be in despair,” he thought, writhing inwardly at the mental picture of a distracted Arlette.
