Life, however, had embittered his spirit early, and he said to himself: “But in a month’s time will she even give me a thought?” Instantly he felt remorseful with a remorse strong enough to lift him to his feet as if he were morally obliged to go up again and confess to Arlette this sacrilegious cynicism of thought. “I am mad,” he muttered, perching himself on the low rail. His lapse from faith plunged him into such a depth of unhappiness that he felt all his strength of will go out of him. He sat there apathetic and suffering. He meditated dully: “Young men have been known to die suddenly; why should not I? I am, as a matter of fact, at the end of my endurance. I am half dead already. Yes! but what is left of that life does not belong to me now.”

===Chinese===
他的声音把佩罗尔唤了回来，佩罗尔便提议把帆桁升上桅顶，甚至请雷亚尔中尉也来搭把手。这事在沉默中完成了，只有滑轮发出微弱的吱嘎声。不过帆仍然卷在索具里没有放下来。

“这样，”佩罗尔说，“您只要松开绳子，马上就能扬帆了。”

雷亚尔没有回答，回到舵柱头旁自己的位置上。他暗自对自己说：“我在溜走。不，还有荣誉，还有职责。我当然会回来的。可什么时候呢？他们会把我忘得一干二净，而我永远也不会被交换回去。这场仗也许会打上好些年——”他不合逻辑地希望自己能有一个上帝，好让他在痛苦中向其祈求慰藉。“她会绝望的。”他想道，一想到失魂落魄的阿尔莱特，他内心便如刀绞。然而生活早已使他的心灵变得苦涩，他对自己说：“可一个月之后她还会想到我吗？”刹那间他涌起一阵悔恨，那悔恨之强烈足以使他站起身来，仿佛他在道义上必须重新上去、向阿尔莱特坦白这亵渎般愤世嫉俗的心思。“我疯了。”他咕哝着，坐到低矮的栏杆上。他这一念之失的背信使他陷入如此深重的不幸，觉得自己全部的意志力都从他身上流泻殆尽。他坐在那里，麻木而痛苦。他木然地冥想着：“年轻人也有猝死的先例；我为什么不能呢？事实上，我已是强弩之末了。我已经半死不活了。是的！可那条命剩下的一切，如今已不属于我了。”

===Original===
“Peyrol,” he said, in such a piercing tone that even Scevola jerked his head up; but he made an effort to reduce his shrillness and went on speaking very carefully: “I have left a letter for the Secretary-General at the Majorité to pay twenty-five hundred francs to Jean—you are Jean, are you not?—Peyrol, price of the tartane in which I sail. Is that right?”

“What did you do that for?” asked Peyrol with an extremely stony face. “To get me into trouble?”

“Don’t be a fool, gunner, nobody remembers your name. It is buried under a stack of blackened paper. I must ask you to go there and tell them that you have seen with your own eyes Lieutenant Réal sail away on his mission.”

The stoniness of Peyrol persisted, but his eyes were full of fury. “Oh yes, I see myself going there. Twenty-five hundred francs! Twenty-five hundred fiddlesticks.” His tone changed suddenly. “I heard someone say that you were an honest man, and I suppose this is a proof of it. Well, to the devil with your honesty.” He glared at the lieutenant, and then thought: “He doesn’t even pretend to listen to what I say”—and another sort of anger, partly contemptuous and with something of dim sympathy in it, replaced his downright fury. “Pah!” he said, spat over the side, and walking up to Réal with great deliberation, slapped him on the shoulder. The only effect of this proceeding was to make Réal look up at him without any expression whatever.

===Chinese===
“佩罗尔。”他说，嗓音尖锐得连塞沃拉都猛地抬起头来；可他努力压低了那尖锐的声调，一字一句小心翼翼地说下去：“我给军港司令部（Majorité）的秘书长留了一封信，让他付给让——你就是让（Jean），是吧？——佩罗尔两千五百法郎，作为我搭乘的这条塔尔塔纳的船价。这样行吗？”

“你这是干什么？”佩罗尔问道，一张脸板得像石头。“想给我惹麻烦？”

“别犯傻了，炮长，没人记得你的名字。它埋在一大堆发黑的纸堆底下呢。我得请你去那儿一趟，告诉他们你亲眼看见雷亚尔中尉出海执行任务去了。”

佩罗尔脸上那副石头般的神情依旧，可他两眼满是怒火。“哦，是啊，我倒看得见自己上那儿去。两千五百法郎！两千五百个屁。”他的口气忽然一变。“我听人说过你是个诚实的人，我估摸这便是个明证了。好吧，让你的诚实见鬼去吧。”他瞪着中尉，继而想道：“他甚至装都不装作在听我说话”——另一种愤怒取代了他那直截了当的怒火，那愤怒里一半是鄙夷，一半是隐隐的同情。“呸！”他说了一声，朝舷外吐了口唾沫，然后极其从容地走到雷亚尔跟前，在他肩上拍了一掌。这一举动的唯一效果，是让雷亚尔抬起头来看了他一眼，脸上什么表情也没有。

===Original===
Peyrol then picked up the lieutenant’s valise and carried it down into the cuddy. As he passed by, Citizen Scevola uttered the word “*Citoyen*,” but it was only when he came back again that Peyrol condescended to say “Well?”

“What are you going to do with me?” asked Scevola.

“You would not give me an account of how you came on board this tartane,” said Peyrol in a tone that sounded almost friendly, “therefore I need not tell you what I will do with you.”

===Chinese===
佩罗尔随即拎起中尉的旅行袋，拎到下面的小舱房（cuddy）里去了。他经过时，公民塞沃拉吐出一句“*Citoyen*（公民）”，可直到他再回来，佩罗尔才屈尊回了一句“怎么？”

“你要拿我怎么办？”塞沃拉问。

“你不肯跟我说你是怎么上了这条塔尔塔纳的，”佩罗尔用一种近乎友好的口气说道，“因此我也用不着告诉你我要拿你怎么办。”

===Original===
A low muttering of thunder followed so close upon his words that it might have come out of Peyrol’s own lips. The rover gazed uneasily at the sky. It was still clear overhead, and at the bottom of that little basin surrounded by rocks there was no view in any other direction; but even as he gazed there was a sort of flicker in the sunshine, succeeded by a mighty but distant clap of thunder. For the next half-hour Peyrol and Michel were busy ashore taking a long line from the tartane to the entrance of the little basin, where they fastened the end of it to a bush. This was for the purpose of hauling the tartane out into the cove. Then they came aboard again. The bit of sky above their heads was still clear, but while walking with the hauling line near the cove Peyrol had got a glimpse of the edge of the cloud. The sun grew scorching all of a sudden, and in the stagnating air a mysterious change seemed to come over the quality and the colour of the light. Peyrol flung his cap on the deck, baring his head to the subtle menace of the breathless stillness of the air.

“Phew! *Ca chauffe*,” he muttered, rolling up the sleeves of his jacket. He wiped his forehead with his mighty forearm upon which a mermaid with an immensely long fishtail was tattooed. Perceiving the lieutenant’s belted sword lying on the deck, he picked it up and without any ceremony threw it down the cabin stairs. As he was passing again near Scevola, the sansculotte raised his voice.

“I believe you are one of those wretches corrupted by English gold,” he cried like one inspired. His shining eyes, his red cheeks, testified to the fire of patriotism burning in his breast, and he used that conventional phrase of revolutionary time, a time when, intoxicated with oratory, he used to run about dealing death to traitors of both sexes and all ages. But his denunciation was received in such profound silence that his own belief in it wavered. His words had sunk into an abysmal stillness and the next sound was Peyrol speaking to Réal.

“I am afraid you will get very wet, lieutenant, before long,” and then, looking at Réal, he thought with great conviction: “Wet! He wouldn’t mind getting drowned.” Standing stock still, he fretted and fumed inwardly, wondering where precisely the English ship was by this time and where the devil that thunderstorm had got to: for the sky had become as mute as the oppressed earth. Réal asked:

“Is it not time to haul out, gunner?”

And Peyrol said:

“There is not a breath of wind anywhere for miles.” He was gratified by the fairly loud mutter rolling apparently along the inland hills. Over the pool a little ragged cloud torn from the purple robe of the storm floated, arrested and thin like a bit of dark gauze.

===Chinese===
一阵低沉的雷鸣紧跟着他的话滚过，紧得简直像是从佩罗尔自己嘴里吐出来的。漫游者不安地凝视着天空。头顶上依然一片晴朗，而在那四面岩石环绕的小水池底部，别的方向什么也看不见；可就在他凝望的当儿，阳光里闪了一下，紧接着是一声沉闷而遥远的巨雷。此后的半个钟头里，佩罗尔和米歇尔忙着在岸上把一条长缆绳从塔尔塔纳拉到小水池的入口处，把绳头拴在一丛灌木上。这是为了把塔尔塔纳拽到外面的海湾里去。然后他们重新上了船。头顶那巴掌大一块天仍旧是晴的，可佩罗尔拿着缆绳在海湾附近走动时，瞥见了那团云的边缘。日头忽然变得灼烤起来，凝滞的空气里，光线的质地和颜色似乎发生了某种神秘的变化。佩罗尔把帽子往甲板上一甩，光着脑袋承受那无风死寂中微妙的威胁。

“呸！*Ca chauffe*（真够呛）。”他嘟囔着，卷起外衣的袖子。他用那条粗壮的前臂擦了擦额头——那前臂上刺着一条拖着极长鱼尾的美人鱼。他瞥见中尉那把连腰带的佩剑搁在甲板上，便一把拎起来，毫不客气地丢下了舱梯。他再次从塞沃拉身边经过时，那无套裤汉提高了嗓门。

“我看你就是那种被英国人的金子收买的败类。”他像受了神启似地叫道。他那放光的眼睛、通红的双颊，都证明了他胸中燃烧着的爱国之火，他说出的是革命时期那句套话——那个年月里，他被雄辩的辞藻灌得醉醺醺的，到处乱跑，给男女老幼的叛徒送去死亡。然而他的控诉被一片深沉的寂静接住了，寂静得连他自己对它都动摇了。他的话沉入了一口无底的寂静之井，下一个声响是佩罗尔在对雷亚尔说话。

“我看您用不了多久就要淋个透湿了，中尉。”然后他看着雷亚尔，十分笃定地想：湿！淹死他都不在乎。他一动不动地站着，心里焦躁翻腾，揣度着那艘英国船这会儿究竟到了哪里，而那场该死的雷雨又跑到哪儿去了：因为天空已变得和那被压迫的大地一样哑默无声了。雷亚尔问道：

“该把船拽出去了吧，炮长？”

佩罗尔说：

“方圆几里之内连一丝风也没有。”一阵相当响亮的闷雷显然沿着内陆的山丘滚滚传来，这使他颇为满意。小水池上方飘着一片被从暴风雨的紫色袍子上撕下来的碎云，停滞着，薄得像一缕黑色的轻纱。

===Original===
Above at the farm Catherine had heard, too, the ominous mutter and came to the door of the *salle*. From there she could see the purple cloud itself, convoluted and solid, and its sinister shadow lying over the hills. The oncoming of the storm added to her sense of uneasiness at finding herself all alone in the house. Michel had not come up. She would have welcomed Michel, to whom she hardly ever spoke, simply as a person belonging to the usual order of things. She was not talkative, but somehow she would have liked somebody to speak to just for a moment. This cessation of all sound, voices or footsteps, around the buildings was not welcome; but looking at the cloud, she thought that there would be noise enough presently. However, stepping back into the kitchen, she was met by a sound that made her regret the oppressive silence, by its piercing and terrifying character; it was a shriek in the upper part of the house, where, as far as she knew, there was only Arlette asleep. In her attempt to cross the kitchen to the foot of the stairs the weight of her accumulated years fell upon the old woman. She felt suddenly very feeble and hardly able to breathe. And all at once the thought “Scevola! Was he murdering her up there?” paralysed the last remnant of her physical powers. What else could it be? She fell, as if shot, into a chair under the first shock and found herself unable to move. Only her brain remained active, and she raised her hands to her eyes as if to shut out the image of the horrors upstairs. She heard nothing more from above. Arlette was dead. She thought that now it was her turn. While her body quailed before the brutal violence, her weary spirit longed ardently for the end. Let him come! Let all this be over at last, with a blow on the head or a stab in the breast. She had not the courage to uncover her eyes. She waited. But after about a minute—it seemed to her interminable—she heard rapid footsteps overhead. Arlette was running here and there. Catherine uncovered her eyes and was about to rise when she heard at the top of the stairs the name of Peyrol shouted with a desperate accent. Then again, after the shortest of pauses, the cry of “Peyrol, Peyrol!” and then the sound of feet running downstairs. There was another shriek, “Peyrol!” just outside the door before it flew open. Who was pursuing her? Catherine managed to stand up. Steadying herself with one hand on the table, she presented an undaunted front to her niece, who ran into the kitchen with loose hair flying and the appearance of wildest distraction in her eyes.

===Chinese===
上头农庄里，凯瑟琳也听见了那不祥的闷雷，走到正厅门口。从那儿她能看见那团紫色的云本身，翻卷着，凝实而厚重，它那不祥的阴影笼罩着群山。风暴的逼近，使她独自一人在屋里的那种不安更甚了。米歇尔没有上来。她会乐意看到米歇尔的——她几乎从不跟他说话——仅仅因为他是属于日常秩序中的人。她不爱说话，可不知怎的，她忽然很想有个人哪怕跟她说上一句话。四周——屋舍周围——一切声响、人声、脚步声——的骤然消失并不令人舒畅；可望着那团云，她想，要不了多久就会吵个够的。然而，她退回厨房时，迎面撞上一个声音，那声音以其尖锐骇人的特质，反倒叫她怀念起方才那沉闷的寂静来；那是屋子楼上一声尖叫，而据她所知，楼上只有阿尔莱特在睡觉。她想要穿过厨房走到楼梯脚下，积年的沉重一下子压到了这老妇人身上。她忽然觉得自己虚弱极了，几乎喘不上气来。紧接着，一个念头——“塞沃拉！他是不是在上头杀她？”——把她最后一点残存的体力也麻痹了。还能是别的什么呢？她在最初的打击下像中弹一样跌进一把椅子里，发现自己动弹不得了。只有脑子还在转动，她抬起双手捂住眼睛，仿佛要挡住楼上那些惨象。她再也没有听见楼上传来任何声响。阿尔莱特死了。她想，现在轮到她了。她的肉身在蛮横的暴力面前瑟缩，她那疲惫的灵魂却热切地盼着终局的到来。让他来吧！让这一切终于了结吧，头上挨一下子也好，胸口捅一刀也罢。她没有勇气把手从眼睛上挪开。她等着。可大约过了一分钟——她觉得漫长得没有尽头——她听见头顶响起了急促的脚步声。阿尔莱特在到处跑。凯瑟琳挪开手，正要起身，便听见楼梯顶上以绝望的声调喊出了佩罗尔的名字。然后，仅仅停了最短的一瞬，又是一声“佩罗尔，佩罗尔！”接着是跑下楼的脚步声。门被撞开之前，门外又是一声尖叫：“佩罗尔！”是谁在追她？凯瑟琳勉强站了起来。她一只手撑着桌子稳住身子，迎面朝着她外甥女——那姑娘一头散发飞扬，眼里是近乎癫狂的神色，冲进了厨房。

===Original===
The staircase door had slammed to behind her. Nobody was pursuing her; and Catherine, putting forth her lean brown arm, arrested Arlette’s flight with such a jerk that the two women swung against each other. She seized her niece by the shoulders.

“What is this, in Heaven’s name? Where are you rushing to?” she cried, and the other, as if suddenly exhausted, whispered:

“I woke up from an awful dream.”

The kitchen grew dark under the cloud that hung over the house now. There was a feeble flicker of lightning and a faint crash, far away.

The old woman gave her niece a little shake. “Dreams are nothing,” she said. “You are awake now.…” And indeed Catherine thought that no dream could be so bad as the realities which kept hold of one through the long waking hours.

“They were killing him,” moaned Arlette, beginning to tremble and struggle in her aunt’s arms. “I tell you they were killing him.”

“Be quiet. Were you dreaming of Peyrol?”

She became still in a moment and then whispered: “No, Eugène.”

===Chinese===
楼梯间的门在她身后砰地关上了。并没有人在追她；凯瑟琳伸出她那只干瘦的棕色胳膊，一把拽住了阿尔莱特的奔逃，那股猛劲使两个女人撞到了一起。她抓住外甥女的肩膀。

“这是怎么了，老天爷？你这是要往哪儿冲？”她喊道，另一个像是忽然被抽空了力气，低声说：

“我从一个可怕的梦里醒过来。”

此刻，笼罩在屋顶上的那团乌云使厨房暗了下来。有一道微弱的闪电，和一声遥远的低沉雷响。

老妇人轻轻摇了摇外甥女。“梦算不了什么，”她说，“你现在醒了。……”凯瑟琳心里确实这么想：再没有哪个梦能比那些漫长的清醒时辰里攥住人的现实更可怕了。

“他们在杀他。”阿尔莱特呻吟道，开始在姑母怀里发抖、挣扎。“我告诉你，他们在杀他。”

“别说了。你梦到的是佩罗尔吗？”

她一下子静了下来，然后低声道：“不是，是欧仁（Eugène）。”

===Original===
She had seen Réal set upon by a mob of men and women, all dripping with blood, in a livid cold light, in front of a stretch of mere shells of houses with cracked walls and broken windows, and going down in the midst of a forest of raised arms brandishing sabres, clubs, knives, axes. There was also a man flourishing a red rag on a stick, while another was beating a drum which boomed above the sickening sound of broken glass falling like rain on the pavement. And away round the corner of an empty street came Peyrol, whom she recognized by his white head, walking without haste, swinging his cudgel regularly. The terrible thing was that Peyrol looked straight at her, not noticing anything, composed, without a frown or a smile, unseeing and deaf, while she waved her arms and shrieked desperately to him for help. She woke up with the piercing sound of his name in her ears and with the impression of the dream so powerful that even now, looking distractedly into her aunt’s face, she could see the bare arms of that murderous crowd raised above Réal’s sinking head. Yet the name that had sprung to her lips on waking was the name of Peyrol. She pushed her aunt away with such force that the old woman staggered backwards, and to save herself had to catch hold of the overmantel above her head. Arlette ran to the door of the *salle*, looked in, came back to her aunt and shouted: “Where is he?”

Catherine really did not know which path the lieutenant had taken. She understood very well that “he” meant Réal.

She said: “He went away a long time ago”; grasped her niece’s arm and added with an effort to steady her voice: “He is coming back, Arlette—for nothing will keep him away from you.”

Arlette, as if mechanically, was whispering to herself the magic name, “Peyrol, Peyrol!” then cried: “I want Eugène now. This moment.”

Catherine’s face wore a look of unflinching patience. “He has departed on service,” she said. Her niece looked at her with enormous eyes, coal-black, profound, and immovable, while in a forcible and distracted tone she said: “You and Peyrol have been plotting to rob me of my reason. But I will know how to make that old man give him up. He is mine!” She spun round wildly, like a person looking for a way of escape from a deadly peril, and rushed out blindly.

===Chinese===
她梦见雷亚尔被一群男女围攻，那些人浑身滴着血，在一片惨白冰冷的光里，在一大片只剩下空壳、墙裂窗碎的房屋前面，他在一片高举的胳膊的丛林中倒了下去——那些胳膊挥舞着马刀、棍棒、刀子、斧头。还有一个男人挥舞着棍子上扎的一块红布，另一个人在敲鼓，鼓声盖过了碎玻璃像雨一样落在石板路上那种令人作呕的声响。而在一条空荡荡的街道拐角那边，走来了佩罗尔——她凭他那头白发认出了他——他不慌不忙地走着，有规律地甩着他的木棍。可怕的是，佩罗尔直直地望着她，却什么也没注意到，从容镇定，不蹙眉也不微笑，视而不见，充耳不闻，而她拼命挥舞着双臂、绝望地尖叫着向他呼救。她在耳畔那声尖锐的自己的呼喊中醒来——那是他的名字——而梦境的印象如此强烈，以至于此刻她失魂落魄地望着姑母的脸，眼前仍然浮现着那群凶手赤裸的胳膊高举在雷亚尔下沉的头顶上方的情景。然而她醒来时脱口而出的名字，却是佩罗尔。她用力把姑母推开，推得老妇人踉跄后退，为了稳住自己不得不一把抓住头顶上方的炉罩。阿尔莱特冲到正厅门口，探头张望，又折回姑母身边，喊道：“他在哪儿？”

凯瑟琳确实不知道中尉走的是哪条路。她心里很明白，“他”指的是雷亚尔。

她说：“他走了很久了。”抓住外甥女的胳膊，竭力稳住自己的声音补了一句：“他会回来的，阿尔莱特——什么也拦不住他回到你身边。”

阿尔莱特像是机械地喃喃念着那个有魔力的名字：“佩罗尔，佩罗尔！”然后叫道：“我现在就要欧仁。这会儿就要。”

凯瑟琳脸上是一副不屈不挠的忍耐。“他执行公务去了。”她说。她外甥女用一双乌黑、深沉、纹丝不动的大眼睛望着她，用力而失魂落魄地说：“你和佩罗尔一直在合谋夺走我的理智。可我知道怎么让那个老头儿把他交出来。他是我的！”她发狂似地转过身，像一个在致命危险中寻找生路的人，不管不顾地冲了出去。

===Original===
About Escampobar the air was murky but calm and the silence was so profound that it was possible to hear the first heavy drops of rain striking the ground. In the intimidating shadow of the storm-cloud, Arlette stood irresolute for a moment, but it was to Peyrol, the man of mystery and power, that her thoughts turned. She was ready to embrace his knees, to entreat and to scold. “Peyrol, Peyrol!” she cried twice, and lent her ear as if expecting an answer. Then she shouted: “I want him back.”

Catherine, alone in the kitchen, moving with dignity, sat down in the armchair with the tall back, like a senator in his curule chair awaiting the blow of a barbarous fate.

Arlette flew down the slope. The first sign of her coming was a faint thin scream which really the rover alone heard and understood. He pressed his lips in a particular way, showing his appreciation of the coming difficulty. The next moment he saw, poised on a detached boulder and thinly veiled by the first perpendicular shower, Arlette, who, catching sight of the tartane with the men on board of her, let out a prolonged shriek of mingled triumph and despair: “Peyrol! Help! Pey—rol!”

===Chinese===
埃斯坎波巴四周，空气浑浊而平静，寂静如此深沉，以至于能听见最初几滴沉重的雨点砸在地上。在暴风云那令人胆寒的阴影里，阿尔莱特犹豫地站了片刻，可她心思所向的，是佩罗尔——那个神秘而有权势的人。她准备去抱住他的膝盖，去恳求，去责骂。“佩罗尔，佩罗尔！”她喊了两声，侧耳倾听，仿佛期待着一个回答。然后她高喊道：“我要他回来。”

凯瑟琳独自在厨房里，举止庄重地走到那张高背扶手椅前坐下，像一个坐在执政官座椅里等候蛮族命运一击的元老。

阿尔莱特飞也似地冲下山坡。她到来的第一个迹象，是一声微弱而尖细的尖叫，其实只有漫游者一个人听见并听懂了。他以一种特别的方式抿紧了嘴唇，表示他意识到了即将到来的麻烦。下一刻，他看见阿尔莱特伫立在一块孤零零的大圆石上，被第一阵垂直的雨幕薄薄地遮掩着——她一眼瞧见了塔尔塔纳和船上的人，便发出一声悠长的、交织着胜利与绝望的尖叫：“佩罗尔！救命！佩——罗尔！”

===Original===
Réal jumped to his feet with an extremely scared face, but Peyrol extended an arresting arm. “She is calling to me,” he said, gazing at the figure poised on the rock. “Well leaped! *Sacré nom!*… Well leaped!” And he muttered to himself soberly: “She will break her legs or her neck.”

“I see you, Peyrol,” screamed Arlette, who seemed to be flying through the air. “Don’t you dare.”

“Yes, here I am,” shouted the rover, striking his breast with his fist.

Lieutenant Réal put both his hands over his face. Michel looked on open-mouthed, very much as if watching a performance in a circus; but Scevola cast his eyes down. Arlette came on board with such an impetus that Peyrol had to step forward and save her from a fall which would have stunned her. She struggled in his arms with extreme violence. The heiress of Escampobar, with her loose black hair, seemed the incarnation of pale fury. “*Misérable!* Don’t you dare!” A roll of thunder covered her voice, but when it had passed away she was heard again in suppliant tones. “Peyrol, my friend, my dear old friend. Give him back to me,” and all the time her body writhed in the arms of the old seaman. “You used to love me, Peyrol,” she cried without ceasing to struggle, and suddenly struck the rover twice in the face with her clenched fist. Peyrol’s head received the two blows as if it had been made of marble, but he felt with fear her body become still, grow rigid in his arms. A heavy squall enveloped the group of people on board the tartane. Peyrol laid Arlette gently on the deck. Her eyes were closed, her hands remained clenched; every sign of life had left her white face. Peyrol stood up and looked at the tall rocks streaming with water. The rain swept over the tartane with an angry swishing roar to which was added the sound of water rushing violently down the folds and seams of the precipitous shore, vanishing gradually from his sight, as if this had been the beginning of a destroying and universal deluge—the end of all things.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔满脸骇然地跳了起来，佩罗尔却伸出一条胳膊拦住了他。“她在叫我。”他说，凝望着那伫立在岩石上的身影。“跳得好！*Sacré nom!*（该死）……跳得好！”他冷静地自言自语道：“她非摔断腿或摔断脖子不可。”

“我看见你了，佩罗尔，”阿尔莱特尖叫着，像是凌空飞来一般。“你敢。”

“是的，我在这儿。”漫游者喊道，一拳捶在自己胸口。

雷亚尔中尉双手捂住了脸。米歇尔张着嘴看得目瞪口呆，活像在看马戏团的表演；塞沃拉却垂下了眼睛。阿尔莱特冲上船来的那股冲劲太猛，佩罗尔不得不抢步上前，才使她免于一个会摔得昏死过去的跟头。她在他的怀里拼命挣扎。这个埃斯坎波巴的继承人，披散着一头黑发，像是苍白愤怒的化身。“*Misérable!*（你这恶徒）你敢！”一阵滚雷淹没了她的声音，可雷声过后，她又以恳求的口气开了口：“佩罗尔，我的朋友，我亲爱的老朋友。把他还给我吧。”而她的身体一直在老水手的怀里扭动。“你从前是爱我的，佩罗尔。”她不停地挣扎着喊道，忽然攥紧拳头在漫游者脸上捶了两下。佩罗尔的脑袋承受了这两拳，像是大理石做的一样，可他惊恐地感到她的身体静了下来，在他怀里变得僵硬。一阵猛烈的飑裹住了塔尔塔纳上这群人。佩罗尔把阿尔莱特轻轻放到甲板上。她双目紧闭，双手仍攥着拳；她苍白的脸上已没有一丝生命的迹象。佩罗尔站起身来，望着那些流水如注的高耸岩石。大雨扫过塔尔塔纳，发出愤怒的嘶嘶轰鸣，加上水流沿陡岸的褶皱和缝隙狂暴地冲刷而下的声响，渐渐地从他的视野中隐去，仿佛这是一场毁灭一切的滔天洪水的开端——万物的终结。

===Original===
Lieutenant Réal, kneeling on one knee, contemplated the pale face of Arlette. Distinct, yet mingling with the faint growl of distant thunder, Peyrol’s voice was heard saying:

“We can’t put her ashore and leave her lying in the rain. She must be taken up to the house.” Arlette’s soaked clothes clung to her limbs while the lieutenant, his bare head dripping with rainwater, looked as if he had just saved her from drowning. Peyrol gazed down inscrutably at the woman stretched on the deck and at the kneeling man. “She has fainted from rage at her old Peyrol,” he went on rather dreamily. “Strange things do happen. However, lieutenant, you had better take her under the arms and step ashore first. I will help you. Ready? Lift.”

The movements of the two men had to be careful and their progress was slow on the lower, steep part of the slope. After going up more than two-thirds of the way, they rested their insensible burden on a flat stone. Réal continued to sustain the shoulders, but Peyrol lowered the feet gently.

“Ha!” he said. “You will be able to carry her yourself the rest of the way and give her up to old Catherine. Get a firm footing and I will lift her and place her in your arms. You can walk the distance quite easily. There.… Hold her a little higher, or her feet will be catching on the stones.”

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉单膝跪地，凝视着阿尔莱特那张苍白的脸。佩罗尔的声音传了过来，清晰，却又与远处隐约的雷声交织在一起，只听他说道：

“咱们不能把她搁在岸上淋雨。得把她抬上屋去。”阿尔莱特湿透的衣裳紧贴着四肢，而中尉光着的脑袋淋着雨水往下滴，看着倒像他刚刚把她从溺水中救起来似的。佩罗尔高深莫测地俯视着那个瘫在甲板上的女人和那个跪着的男人。“她是冲着她老佩罗尔发火才晕过去的。”他近乎梦呓似地接着说。“怪事真是有的。不过，中尉，您最好从腋下架住她，先下船上岸。我来帮您。准备好了吗？抬。”

两人的动作必须小心，在坡下那段陡峭处走得也慢。走了全程的三分之二开外之后，他们把失去知觉的负担搁在一块平整的石头上。雷亚尔继续托着肩头，佩罗尔却轻轻放下了她的双脚。

“哈！”他说，“剩下的路您自个儿就能把她扛上去，交给老凯瑟琳了。站稳了，我把她托起来，搁到您怀里。这段路您轻轻松松就走到了。好。……把她举高一点，不然她的脚要被石头绊住的。”

===Original===
Arlette’s hair was hanging far below the lieutenant’s arm in an inert and heavy mass. The thunderstorm was passing away, leaving a cloudy sky. And Peyrol thought with a profound sigh: “I am tired.”

“She is light,” said Réal.

“*Parbleu*, she is light. If she were dead you would find her heavy enough. *Allons, mon lieutenant.* No! I am not coming. What’s the good? I’ll stay down here. I have no mind to listen to Catherine’s scolding.”

The lieutenant, looking absorbed into the face resting in the hollow of his arm, never averted his gaze—not even when Peyrol, stooping over Arlette, kissed the white forehead near the roots of the hair, black as a raven’s wing.

“What am I to do?” muttered Réal.

“Do? Why, give her up to old Catherine. And you may just as well tell her that I will be coming along directly. That will cheer her up. I used to count for something in that house. *Allez!* For our time is very short.”

With these words he turned away and walked slowly down to the tartane. A breeze had sprung up. He felt it on his wet neck and was grateful for the cool touch which recalled him to himself, to his old wandering self which had known no softness and no hesitation in the face of any risk offered by life.

===Chinese===
阿尔莱特的头发沉甸甸地、毫无生气地从雷亚尔的胳膊下面垂下来，长长的一大缕。雷雨正在过去，留下一片多云的天空。佩罗尔深深地叹了口气，心想：“我累了。”

“她很轻。”雷亚尔说。

“*Parbleu*（可不是嘛），她是轻。她要是死了，您就知道她有多沉了。*Allons, mon lieutenant*（走吧，中尉）。不！我不去了。去干什么？我就待在下面。我可不想听凯瑟琳数落。”

中尉出神地凝望着靠在他臂弯里的那张脸，目光一刻也没有移开——甚至当佩罗尔俯身凑近阿尔莱特、在她那乌黑如乌鸦羽翼的发根旁的苍白额头上亲了一下时，也没有。

“我该怎么办？”雷亚尔咕哝道。

“怎么办？嗐，把她交给老凯瑟琳啊。您索性再告诉她，我一会儿就上去。这能叫她宽宽心。从前我在那屋子里多少还算号人物呢。*Allez!*（走吧！）咱们的时间不多了。”

说完这些话他便转过身去，慢慢地走下塔尔塔纳。一阵微风吹了起来。他湿漉漉的脖颈感觉到了它，很是感激那一缕清凉的触感——它把他唤回到了自己，唤回到了他那个旧的、漂泊的自我，那个面对生活所提出的一切风险都不知温柔为何物、也不知犹豫为何物的自我。

===Original===
As he stepped on board, the shower passed away, Michel, wet to the skin, was still in the very same attitude gazing up the slope. Citizen Scevola had drawn his knees up and was holding his head in his hands; whether because of rain or cold or for some other reason, his teeth were chattering audibly with a continuous and distressing rattle. Peyrol flung off his jacket, heavy with water, with a strange air as if it was of no more use to his mortal envelope, squared his broad shoulders, and directed Michel, in a deep, quiet voice, to let go the lines holding the tartane to the shore. The faithful henchman was taken aback and required one of Peyrol’s authoritative “*Allez!*” to put him in motion. Meantime the rover cast off the tiller lines and laid his hand with an air of mastery on the stout piece of wood projecting horizontally from the rudder-head about the level of his hip. The voices and the movements of his companions caused Citizen Scevola to master the desperate trembling of his jaw. He wriggled a little in his bonds, and the question that had been on his lips for a good many hours was uttered again.

“What are you going to do with me?”

“What do you think of a little promenade at sea?” Peyrol asked in a tone that was not unkindly.

Citizen Scevola, who had seemed totally and completely cast down and subdued, let out a most unexpected screech.

“Unbind me. Put me ashore.”

Michel, busy forward, was moved to smile as though he possessed a cultivated sense of incongruity. Peyrol remained serious.

“You shall be untied presently,” he assured the blood-drinking patriot, who had been for so many years the reputed possessor not only of Escampobar but of the Escampobar heiress that, living on appearances, he had almost come to believe in that ownership himself. No wonder he screeched at this rude awakening. Peyrol raised his voice: “Haul on the line, Michel.”

===Chinese===
他踏上船时，那阵雨过去了。浑身湿透的米歇尔仍保持着原来的姿势，凝望着山坡。公民塞沃拉已蜷起双膝，双手抱住脑袋；不知是淋了雨还是受了冻，抑或别的什么缘故，他的牙齿格格地打着战，发出连续不断的、令人难受的咯咯声。佩罗尔甩掉了那件吸饱了水的外衣，那副神情仿佛这衣裳对他的肉身已毫无用处了，他端起宽阔的肩膀，压低沉稳的嗓音吩咐米歇尔松开把塔尔塔纳系在岸上的缆绳。这忠心的随从吃了一惊，非得佩罗尔再发出一声威严的“*Allez!*（走！）”才使他动起来。与此同时，漫游者解开舵柄的绳索，以一种主宰者的姿态把手按在那根从舵柱头水平伸出的、与他胯骨齐平的粗实木柄上。同伴们的声音和动静使公民塞沃拉勉强压住了下巴那绝望的颤抖。他在绳索里扭了扭身子，那句在他唇边悬了好几个钟头的问题又吐了出来。

“你要拿我怎么办？”

“到海上去兜一圈，你看怎么样？”佩罗尔用一种并不严厉的口气问道。

公民塞沃拉原本看上去已彻底颓丧驯服，却发出了一声谁也料不到的尖叫。

“给我松绑。把我送上岸去。”

正在船头忙活的米歇尔不禁莞尔，仿佛他也有一种训练有素的、对荒诞事物的感受力。佩罗尔却面容严肃。

“一会儿就给你松绑。”他向那个嗜血的爱国者保证——这家伙多年来一直被公认为不仅拥有埃斯坎波巴，还拥有埃斯坎波巴的继承人，以至于靠着这些表象过活，他自己也几乎信以为真了。难怪这一回被如此粗暴地惊醒，他要尖叫了。佩罗尔提高了嗓门：“拉缆绳，米歇尔。”

===Original===
As, directly the ropes had been let go, the tartane had swung clear of the shore, the movement given her by Michel carried her towards the entrance by which the basin communicated with the cove. Peyrol attended to the helm, and in a moment, gliding through the narrow gap, the tartane carrying her way, shot out almost into the middle of the cove.

A little wind could be felt, running light wrinkles over the water, but outside the overshadowed sea was already speckled with white caps. Peyrol helped Michel to haul aft the sheets and then went back to the tiller. The pretty spick-and-span craft that had been lying idle for so long began to glide into the wide world. Michel gazed at the shore as if lost in admiration. Citizen Scevola’s head had fallen on his knees while his nerveless hands clasped his legs loosely. He was the very image of dejection.

“Hé, Michel! Come here and cast loose the citizen. It is only fair that he should be untied for a little excursion at sea.”

When his order had been executed, Peyrol addressed himself to the desolate figure on the deck.

“Like this, should the tartane get capsized in a squall, you will have an equal chance with us to swim for your life.”

Scevola disdained to answer. He was engaged in biting his knee with rage in a stealthy fashion.

“You came on board for some murderous purpose. Who you were after, unless it was myself, God only knows. I feel quite justified in giving you a little outing at sea. I won’t conceal from you, citizen, that it may not be without risk to life or limb. But you have only yourself to thank for being here.”

===Chinese===
绳索一松开，塔尔塔纳便荡离了岸，米歇尔给她的那一推把她带向了水池通向海湾的出口。佩罗尔管着舵，一眨眼的工夫，塔尔塔纳便带着惯性滑过那道窄缝，几乎射到了海湾的正中央。

能感觉到一点风，在水面上拂起细细的皱褶，但在阴云笼罩的外头，海面上已布满了白色的浪花帽。佩罗尔帮米歇尔把帆索拉向船尾，然后回到舵柄旁。这条一直闲置了那么久的、干干净净、漂漂亮亮的小船，开始向广阔的世界滑去。米歇尔凝望着海岸，像是陶醉其中。公民塞沃拉的脑袋已垂到了膝盖上，两只绵软的手松松地抱着自己的腿。他简直是沮丧的化身。

“*Hé, Michel!*（喂，米歇尔！）过来把这位公民松开。到海上溜达一圈，给他松绑也算是公平。”

等他的命令被执行之后，佩罗尔便对甲板上那个凄惶的身影说道：

“这样一来，万一塔尔塔纳在飑里翻了船，你也跟我们一样有同等的机会游命。”

塞沃拉不屑于回答。他正偷偷地、满腔怒火地啃着自己的膝盖。

“你上这条船来，是带着杀人的目的。你冲着谁来的，除非是我本人，只有天知道。我觉得请你到海上兜一圈，完全名正言顺。我不瞒你，公民，这趟也许免不了有伤及性命或手足的风险。可你落到这一步，只能怪你自己。”

===Original===
As the tartane drew clear of the cove, she felt more the weight of the breeze and darted forward with a lively motion. A vaguely contented smile lighted up Michel’s hairy countenance.

“She feels the sea,” said Peyrol, who enjoyed the swift movement of his vessel. “This is different from your lagoon, Michel.”

“To be sure,” said Michel with becoming gravity.

“Doesn’t it seem funny to you, as you look back at the shore, to think that you have left nothing and nobody behind?”

Michel assumed the aspect of a man confronted by an intellectual problem. Since he had become Peyrol’s henchman he had lost the habit of thinking altogether. Directions and orders were easy things to apprehend; but a conversation with him whom he called *notre maître* was a serious matter demanding great and concentrated attention.

“Possibly,” he murmured, looking strangely selfconscious.

“Well, you are lucky, take my word for it,” said the rover, watching the course of his little vessel along the head of the peninsula. “You have not even a dog to miss you.”

“I have only you, Maître Peyrol.”

“That’s what I was thinking,” said Peyrol half to himself, while Michel, who had good sea-legs, kept his balance to the movements of the craft without taking his eyes from the rover’s face.

“No,” Peyrol exclaimed suddenly, after a moment of meditation, “I could not leave you behind.” He extended his open palm towards Michel.

“Put your hand in there,” he said.

Michel hesitated for a moment before this extraordinary proposal. At last he did so, and Peyrol, holding the bereaved fisherman’s hand in a powerful grip, said:

“If I had gone away by myself, I would have left you marooned on this earth like a man thrown out to die on a desert island.” Some dim perception of the solemnity of the occasion seemed to enter Michel’s primitive brain. He connected Peyrol’s words with the sense of his own insignificant position at the tail of all mankind, and, timidly, he murmured with his clear, innocent glance unclouded, the fundamental axiom of his philosophy:

“Somebody must be last in this world.”

“Well, then, you will have to forgive me all that may happen between this and the hour of sunset.”

The tartane, obeying the helm, fell off before the wind, with her head to the eastward.

Peyrol murmured: “She has not forgotten how to walk the seas.” His unsubdued heart, heavy for so many days, had a moment of buoyancy—the illusion of immense freedom.

===Chinese===
塔尔塔纳一驶出海湾，便更真切地感受到了风的分量，轻快地向前窜去。米歇尔那毛茸茸的脸上浮现出一丝隐约满足的笑意。

“她感应到海了。”佩罗尔说，他正享受着他的船飞快的航速。“这跟你的礁湖可不一样，米歇尔。”

“可不是。”米歇尔以应有的庄重说道。

“你回头望望岸上，想到你什么也没留下、什么人也没留下，是不是觉得怪怪的？”

米歇尔做出一副面对智力难题的人的神情。自从成了佩罗尔的随从，他已全然失去了思考的习惯。命令和吩咐是容易领会的东西；可是跟那个被他称作我们老大（*notre maître*）的人谈话，却是一件严肃的事，需要极大的、全神贯注的注意力。

“也许吧。”他嘟囔道，神情古怪地忸怩起来。

“嗐，你是幸运的，信我的话。”漫游者说，一面注视着他的小船沿着半岛岬头的航向。“你连一条会惦记你的狗都没有。”

“我只有你了，佩罗尔师傅（Maître Peyrol）。”

“我刚才也正是这么想的。”佩罗尔半自言自语地说，而有着一双好海脚的米歇尔，随着船的摇晃保持着平衡，眼睛一刻也不离开漫游者的脸。

“不，”佩罗尔沉思了片刻忽然说道，“我不能把你留下。”他朝米歇尔伸出摊开的手掌。

“把手放进来。”他说。

米歇尔面对这个非同寻常的提议犹豫了一阵。终于他还是照做了，佩罗尔紧紧攥住这个丧亲的渔夫的手，说道：

“要是我自个儿走了，我就会把你一个人撂在这世上，像个人被扔到荒岛上等死一样。”某种对这一刻庄严性的朦胧感知，似乎钻进了米歇尔那原始的脑子。他把佩罗尔的话和自己处于全人类最末尾那个无足轻重的位置之感联系了起来，怯怯地，他那清澈无辜的目光一尘不染地咕哝出了他那条人生哲学的基本信条：

“这世上总得有人殿在最后。”

“那么好吧，从现在到日落之前，无论发生什么，你都得原谅我。”

塔尔塔纳听从舵的号令，顺风转向，船头朝东。

佩罗尔喃喃道：“她没有忘记怎样行走在大海上。”他那颗多日来沉重不堪的不屈之心，有了一刻的轻盈——一种巨大自由的幻觉。

===Original===
At that moment Réal, amazed at finding no tartane in the basin, was running madly towards the cove, where he was sure Peyrol must be waiting to give her up to him. He ran out on to the very rock on which Peyrol’s late prisoner had sat after his escape, too tired to care, yet cheered by the hope of liberty. But Réal was in a worse plight. He could see no shadowy form through the thin veil of rain which pitted the sheltered piece of water framed in the rocks. The little craft had been spirited away. Impossible! There must be something wrong with his eyes! Again the barren hillsides echoed the name of “Peyrol,” shouted with all the force of Réal’s lungs. He shouted it only once, and about five minutes afterwards appeared at the kitchen door, panting, streaming with water as if he had fought his way up from the bottom of the sea. In the tall-backed armchair Arlette lay, with her limbs relaxed, her head on Catherine’s arm, her face white as death. He saw her open her black eyes, enormous and as if not of this world; he saw old Catherine turn her head, heard a cry of surprise, and saw a sort of struggle beginning between the two women. He screamed at them like a madman: “Peyrol has betrayed me!” and in an instant, with a bang of the door, he was gone.

===Chinese===
就在那一刻，雷亚尔惊愕地发现水池里已没有塔尔塔纳了，正发疯似地朝海湾跑去——他确信佩罗尔一定在那儿等着把船交给他。他一直跑到了佩罗尔那个前囚徒逃脱之后坐过的那块岩石上——那人当时累得什么都顾不上了，却还因自由的希望而振奋着。可雷亚尔的处境更糟。隔着那层细密的雨幕，雨点在四面岩石环抱的那片平静水面上打出一个又一个小坑，他看不到任何船的影子。那条小船像是凭空消失了。不可能！一定是他的眼睛出了毛病！光秃秃的山坡再一次回荡起“佩罗尔”的名字，那是雷亚尔用尽肺里的全部力气喊出来的。他只喊了这一声，大约五分钟之后便出现在厨房门口，气喘吁吁，浑身流水，像是刚从海底一路挣扎着爬上来似的。高背扶手椅里躺着阿尔莱特，四肢瘫软，头枕在凯瑟琳的臂弯里，脸色白得像死人。他看见她睁开那双黑眼睛，大得异乎寻常，仿佛不属于这个世界；他看见老凯瑟琳转过头来，听见一声惊呼，又看见两个女人之间似乎爆发了一场争夺。他像疯子一样冲她们尖叫道：“佩罗尔出卖了我！”转眼之间，砰的一声门响，他已没了踪影。

===Original===
The rain had ceased. Above his head the unbroken mass of clouds moved to the eastward, and he moved in the same direction, as if he too were driven by the wind up the hillside, towards the lookout. When he reached the spot and, gasping, flung one arm round the trunk of the leaning tree, the only thing he was aware of during the sombre pause in the unrest of the elements was the distracting turmoil of his thoughts. After a moment he perceived through the rain the English ship with her topsails lowered on the caps, forging ahead slowly across the northern entrance of the Petite Passe. His distress fastened insanely on the notion of there being a connection between that enemy ship and Peyrol’s inexplicable conduct. That old man had always meant to go himself! And when a moment after, looking to the southward, he made out the shadow of the tartane coming round the land in the midst of another squall, he muttered to himself a bitter “Of course!” She had both her sails set. Peyrol was indeed pressing her to the utmost in his shameful haste to traffic with the enemy. The truth was that from the position in which Réal first saw him, Peyrol could not yet see the English ship, and held confidently on his course up the middle of the strait. The man-of-war and the little tartane saw each other quite unexpectedly at a distance that was very little over a mile. Peyrol’s heart flew into his mouth at finding himself so close to the enemy. On board the *Amelia* at first no notice was taken. It was simply a tartane making for shelter on the north side of Porquerolles. But when Peyrol suddenly altered his course, the master of the man-of-war noticing the manoeuvre, took up the long glass for a look. Captain Vincent was on deck and agreed with the master’s remark that “there was a craft acting suspiciously.” Before the *Amelia* could come round in the heavy squall, Peyrol was already under the battery of Porquerolles and, so far, safe from capture. Captain Vincent had no mind to bring his ship within reach of the battery and risk damage to his rigging or hull for the sake of a small coaster. However, the tale brought on board by Symons of his discovery of a hidden craft, of his capture, and his wonderful escape, had made every tartane an object of interest to the whole ship’s company. The *Amelia* remained hove to in the strait while her officers watched the lateen sails gliding to and fro under the protecting muzzles of the guns. Captain Vincent himself had been impressed by Peyrol’s manoeuvre. Coasting craft, as a rule, were not afraid of the *Amelia*. After taking a few turns on the quarterdeck he ordered Symons to be called aft.

===Chinese===
雨已经停了。头顶上方那一整块绵密的云向东移动着，他也朝同一方向移动，仿佛他也被风驱赶着上了山坡，朝瞭望台走去。等他到了那儿，气喘吁吁地一把抱住那棵歪斜树的树干时，在天地万物的躁动之中这一段阴沉的停歇里，他唯一意识到的，便是自己脑子里那纷乱翻腾的折磨。过了一会儿，他透过雨幕辨出了那艘英国船，她的中桅帆已收降在桅肩上，正缓缓地驶过小水道的北口。他的痛苦发疯似地抓住了这么一个念头：那艘敌船和佩罗尔那不可理喻的举动之间一定有某种联系。那个老头子一直都打算自己去！而紧接着，当他朝南望去，辨出塔尔塔纳的影子在另一阵飑中绕过海岬而来时，他苦涩地自言自语了一句：“果然！”她张着两面帆。佩罗尔确实在可耻地急于和敌人做交易而把她逼到了极限。事实是，从雷亚尔最初看到他的那个位置，佩罗尔还看不见那艘英国船，正信心十足地沿着海峡中间的航道前进。那艘军舰和那条小塔尔塔纳彼此在仅一英里多一点的距离上毫无防备地发现了对方。发现自己离敌人这么近，佩罗尔的心一下子提到了嗓子眼。在阿米莉亚号（*Amelia*）上，起初谁也没在意。那不过是一条驶向波克罗勒岛北面避风的塔尔塔纳罢了。可当佩罗尔忽然改变航向时，军舰的航海长注意到了这一举动，拿起长筒望远镜望了望。文森特舰长在甲板上，赞同航海长“有一条船举止可疑”的说法。还没等阿米莉亚号在这场猛烈的飑中转过来，佩罗尔已驶到了波克罗勒岛炮台的掩护之下，暂且安全了。文森特舰长无意为了一条沿海小船而把他的军舰开进炮台的射程，冒着索具或船体受损的风险。然而，西蒙斯（Symons）带回船上的那段故事——他如何发现一条藏匿的船、如何被俘、又如何神奇地逃脱——已使每一条塔尔塔纳都成了全船上下关注的目标。阿米莉亚号停舵飘留在海峡里，军官们注视着那三角帆在炮口掩护下来回滑行。文森特舰长本人对佩罗尔的操纵也印象深刻。沿海的小船通常是不怕阿米莉亚号的。他在后甲板来回踱了几圈之后，下令把西蒙斯叫到船尾来。

===Original===
The hero of a unique and mysterious adventure, which had been the only subject of talk on board the corvette for the last twenty-four hours, came along rolling, hat in hand, and enjoying a secret sense of his importance.

“Take the glass,” said the captain, “and have a look at that vessel under the land. Is she anything like the tartane that you say you have been aboard of?”

Symons was very positive. “I think I can swear to those painted mastheads, your honour. It is the last thing I remember before that murderous ruffian knocked me senseless. The moon shone on them. I can make them out now with the glass.” As to the fellow boasting to him that the tartane was a dispatch-boat and had already made some trips, well, Symons begged his honour to believe that the beggar was not sober at the time. He did not care what he blurted out. The best proof of his condition was that he went away to fetch the soldiers and forgot to come back. The murderous old ruffian! “You see, you honour,” continued Symons, “he thought I was not likely to escape after getting a blow that would have killed nine out of any ten men. So he went away to boast of what he had done before the people ashore; because one of his chums, worse than himself, came down thinking he would kill me with a dam’ big manure fork, saving your honour’s presence. A regular savage he was.”

Symons paused, staring, as if astonished at the marvels of his own tale. The old master, standing at his captain’s elbow, observed in a dispassionate tone that, anyway, that peninsula was not a bad jumping-off place for a craft intending to slip through the blockade. Symons, not being dismissed, waited, hat in hand, while Captain Vincent directed the master to fill on the ship and stand a little nearer to the battery. It was done, and presently there was a flash of a gun low down on the water’s edge and a shot came skipping in the direction of the *Amelia*. It fell very short, but Captain Vincent judged the ship was close enough, and ordered her to be hove to again. Then Symons was told to take a look through the glass once more. After a long interval he lowered it and spoke impressively to his captain:

“I can make out three heads aboard, your honour, and one is white. I would swear to that white head anywhere.”

Captain Vincent made no answer. All this seemed very odd to him; but after all it was possible. The craft had certainly acted suspiciously. He spoke to the first lieutenant in a half-vexed tone.

“He has done a rather smart thing. He will dodge here till dark and then get away. It is perfectly absurd. I don’t want to send the boats too close to the battery. And if I do he may simply sail away from them and be round the land long before we are ready to give him chase. Darkness will be his best friend. However, we will keep a watch on him in case he is tempted to give us the slip late in the afternoon. In that case we will have a good try to catch him. If he has anything aboard, I should like to get hold of it. It may be of some importance, after all.”

===Chinese===
在过去二十四小时里，那场独一无二的神秘奇遇成了这艘轻巡洋舰上唯一的话题，而这场奇遇的主角，这会儿拿着帽子、摇摇晃晃地走了过来，暗暗享受着一种自以为举足轻重的感觉。

“拿望远镜，”舰长说，“看看岸下那条船。她像不像你说你上去过的那条塔尔塔纳？”

西蒙斯十分肯定。“我想我敢凭那些漆过色的桅杆头发誓，长官。那是我被那个杀人不眨眼的恶棍打晕之前记得的最后一件事。月亮照在上面。我现在用望远镜也能认出来。”至于那家伙向他吹嘘说这条塔尔塔纳是一条通信船、已经跑过几个来回了——嗐，西蒙斯请长官相信，那叫花子当时喝得醉醺醺的。他嘴上没把门，什么话都秃噜。他当时醉态的最好证明就是，他跑去找大兵却忘了回来。那个杀人的老恶棍！“您瞧，长官，”西蒙斯继续说道，“他以为我挨了那一记——搁在十个人里能打死九个的一记——之后不可能逃脱。所以他就走开了，到岸上的人面前去吹嘘他的所作所为；因为他有一个同伙，比他还要坏，跑下来想用一把该死的大粪叉子把我捅死，恕我冒昧。一个地地道道的野蛮人。”

西蒙斯顿了顿，瞪着眼，仿佛对自己故事里的那些奇迹也感到惊讶。站在舰长肘旁的老航海长用一种不动声色的口吻说，不管怎么说，那个半岛对于一条打算溜过封锁的船来说，倒不失为一个不错的跳板。西蒙斯未被遣退，便手拿帽子在一旁候着，而文森特舰长吩咐航海长让船转向上风、稍稍靠近炮台一些。命令执行了，不一会儿，水边低处闪了一炮，一发炮弹一路蹦跳着朝阿米莉亚号飞来。落点差得很远，可文森特舰长判断船已够近了，便下令再次停舵飘留。然后西蒙斯被吩咐再用望远镜看一遍。隔了好一阵他才放下望远镜，郑重其事地对舰长说：

“我能辨出船上三个人头，长官，其中一个是白的。那颗白头我到哪儿都认得出来。”

文森特舰长没有回答。这一切在他看来十分蹊跷；但毕竟也不是没有可能。那条船的举动确实可疑。他用一种半是恼火的口气对大副说道：

“他干了一件相当漂亮的事。他会在这里磨蹭到天黑，然后溜之大吉。这简直荒唐。我不想把小艇派到离炮台太近的地方。就算我派了，他没准儿也能直接从他们面前扬长而去，赶在我们准备追击之前老早就绕过海岬了。黑暗是他最好的帮手。不过，我们盯着他，以防他受诱惑在傍晚时分溜走。真到了那一步，我们就好好试一把抓住他。要是他船上有什么东西，我倒想弄到手。说不定终究还是有些要紧的。”

===Original===
On board the tartane Peyrol put his own interpretation on the ship’s movements. His object had been attained. The corvette had marked him for her prey. Satisfied as to that, Peyrol watched his opportunity and taking advantage of a long squall, with rain thick enough to blur the form of the English ship, he left the shelter of the battery to lead the Englishman a dance and keep up his character of a man anxious to avoid capture.

Réal, from his position on the lookout, saw in the thinning downpour the pointed lateen sails glide round the north end of Porquerolles and vanish behind the land. Some time afterwards the *Amelia* made sail in a manner that put it beyond doubt that she meant to chase. Her lofty canvas was shut off too presently by the land of Porquerolles. When she had disappeared Réal turned to Arlette.

“Let us go,” he said.

Arlette, stimulated by the short glimpse of Réal at the kitchen door, whom she had taken for a vision of a lost man calling her to follow him to the end of the world, had torn herself out of the old woman’s thin, bony arms which could not cope with the struggles of her body and the fierceness of her spirit. She had run straight to the lookout, though there was nothing to guide her there except a blind impulse to seek Réal wherever he might be. He was not aware of her having found him until she seized hold of his arm with a suddenness, energy and determination of which no one with a clouded mind could have been capable. He felt himself being taken possession of in a way that tore all his scruples out of his breast. Holding on to the trunk of the tree, he threw his other arm round her waist, and when she confessed to him that she did not know why she had run up there, but that if she had not found him she would have thrown herself over the cliff, he tightened his clasp with sudden exultation, as though she had been a gift prayed for instead of a stumbling-block for his pedantic conscience. Together they walked back. In the failing light the buildings awaited them, lifeless, the walls darkened by rain and the big slopes of the roofs glistening and sinister under the flying desolation of the clouds. In the kitchen Catherine heard their mingled footsteps, and rigid in the tall armchair awaited their coming. Arlette threw her arms round the old woman’s neck, while Réal stood on one side, looking on. Thought after thought flew through his mind and vanished in the strong feeling of the irrevocable nature of the event handing him to the woman whom, in the revulsion of his feelings, he was inclined to think more sane than himself. Arlette, with one arm over the old woman’s shoulders, kissed the wrinkled forehead under the white band of linen that, on the erect head, had the effect of a rustic diadem.

“Tomorrow you and I will have to walk down to the church.”

===Chinese===
在塔尔塔纳上，佩罗尔对那艘军舰的举动做出了自己的解读。他的目的已经达到。那艘轻巡洋舰已把他锁定为猎物。对此确认无疑之后，佩罗尔便窥准时机，趁着一阵绵长的飑——雨密得足以模糊英国船的身形——离开了炮台的庇护，去牵着那英国人的鼻子兜圈子，维持住自己那副急于逃避捕获的人设。

雷亚尔从瞭望台上，在渐稀的雨幕中看见那尖尖的三角帆绕过波克罗勒岛的北端，消失在陆地后面。过了一阵，阿米莉亚号张帆的方式毫无疑问地表明她要追击了。她高耸的帆幕不久也被波克罗勒岛的陆地遮住了。她消失之后，雷亚尔转向阿尔莱特。

“咱们走吧。”他说。

阿尔莱特——她在厨房门口匆匆瞥见雷亚尔的身影，把他当作一个失踪之人显灵、在召唤她追随他到天涯海角，受了这一刺激——从老妇人那瘦骨嶙峋的臂膀里挣脱了出来，那双胳膊既对付不了她身体的挣扎，也招架不住她精神的暴烈。她径直跑到了瞭望台，尽管指引她到那儿去的不过是一种盲目的冲动——无论雷亚尔在哪里都要找到他。直到她一把抓住他的胳膊——那突如其来的劲头、气力和决断，不是一个神志昏乱的人所能有的——他才发觉她找到了自己。他觉得自己正以一种把他胸中所有的踟蹰顾虑连根拔起的方式被占有了。他一手抓着树干，另一条胳膊搂住了她的腰；当她向他坦白说自己也不知为什么会跑到这儿来，但要是没找到他，她就会从悬崖上跳下去——他骤然狂喜地收紧了搂抱，仿佛她是一件祈求来的恩赐，而不是横在他那迂腐良心面前的一块绊脚石。他们一道往回走。在渐暗的天光里，那些屋舍等待着他们，死气沉沉，墙壁被雨水洇得深暗，宽大的屋顶坡面在飞掠的凄惨云幕下闪着阴冷的光。厨房里，凯瑟琳听见了他们交叠的脚步声，在高背扶手椅里挺直了身子等候他们到来。阿尔莱特一把搂住老妇人的脖子，雷亚尔则站在一旁看着。一个念头接着一个念头掠过他的脑海，又消融在一种强烈的感觉之中——这件事已无可挽回地把他交到了这个女人手里，而在感情的激变中，他反倒觉得她比自己更加清醒。阿尔莱特一只胳膊搭在老妇人的肩上，亲吻着她那布满皱纹的额头——额头之上扎着一道白色的亚麻布带子，在那颗挺直的头颅上，像一顶朴素的冠冕。

“明天，你跟我得步行到下面的教堂去一趟。”

===Original===
The austere dignity of Catherine’s pose seemed to be shaken by this proposal to lead before the God with whom she had made her peace long ago that unhappy girl chosen to share in the guilt of impious and unspeakable horrors which had darkened her mind.

Arlette, still stooping over her aunt’s face, extended a hand towards Réal, who, making a step forward, took it silently into his grasp.

“Oh yes, you will, Aunt,” insisted Arlette. “You will have to come with me to pray for Peyrol, whom you and I shall never see any more.”

Catherine’s head dropped, whether in assent or grief; and Réal felt an unexpected and profound emotion, for he, too, was convinced that none of the three persons in the farm would ever see Peyrol again. It was as though the rover of the wide seas had left them to themselves on a sudden impulse of scorn, of magnanimity, of a passion weary of itself. However come by, Réal was ready to clasp forever to his breast that woman touched by the red hand of the Revolution; for she, whose little feet had run ankle-deep through the terrors of death, had brought to him the sense of triumphant life.

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳那姿态中的肃穆尊严似乎被这个提议所撼动了——竟要把这个被选中来分担那些玷污了她心智的、不敬而不可名状的恐怖罪孽的不幸姑娘，领到那个她早已与之和解的上帝面前。

阿尔莱特仍俯身在姑母的脸前，朝雷亚尔伸出一只手，雷亚尔向前迈了一步，默默地把它握在掌中。

“哦，你会的，姑姑，”阿尔莱特坚持道，“你得跟我一起去，为佩罗尔祈祷——你和我，都再也见不着他了。”

凯瑟琳垂下了头，不知是应允还是悲伤；而雷亚尔感到一阵出乎意料的、深沉的激动，因为他同样确信，农庄里这三个人谁也不会再见到佩罗尔了。仿佛那个漫游了广阔海洋的漫游者，在一阵鄙夷的、宽宏大量的、一种对自身厌倦已极的激情的突然冲动之下，把他们丢下，由他们自去了。不管这份际遇从何而来，雷亚尔已准备好永远把那个被大革命的血手触碰过的女人拥在胸前；因为正是她，那双小脚曾在死亡的恐怖中没过脚踝地奔走过，给他带来了胜利的生命的意味。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
Astern of the tartane, the sun, about to set, kindled a streak of dull crimson glow between the darkening sea and the overcast sky. The peninsula of Giens and the islands of Hyères formed one mass of land detaching itself very black against the fiery girdle of the horizon; but to the north the long stretch of the Alpine coast continued beyond sight its endless sinuosities under the stooping clouds.

===Chinese===
在塔尔塔纳（tartane，地中海单桅三角帆船）的船尾方向，行将沉落的太阳在渐暗的海面与阴沉的天穹之间点燃了一道暗淡的绯红霞光。吉安（Giens）半岛与耶尔群岛（islands of Hyères）连成了一整片陆地，衬着那燃烧般环峙的地平线，剪影黑得格外分明；而在北面，阿尔卑斯沿岸那道漫长的海岸线，却在低垂的云幕之下，把无穷无尽的曲折蜿蜒延伸到了目力之外。

===Original===
The tartane seemed to be rushing together with the run of the waves into the arms of the oncoming night. A little more than a mile away on her lee quarter, the *Amelia*, under all plain sail, pressed to the end of the chase. It had lasted now for a good many hours, for Peyrol, when slipping away, had managed to get the advantage of the *Amelia* from the very start. While still within the large sheet of smooth water which is called the Hyères Roadstead, the tartane, which was really a craft of extraordinary speed, managed to gain positively on the sloop. Afterwards, by suddenly darting down the eastern passage between the two last islands of the group, Peyrol actually got out of sight of the chasing ship, being hidden by the Ile du Levant for a time. The *Amelia*, having to tack twice in order to follow, lost ground once more. Emerging into the open sea, she had to tack again, and then the position became that of a stern chase, which proverbially is known as a long chase. Peyrol’s skilful seamanship had twice extracted from Captain Vincent a low murmur accompanied by a significant compression of lips. At one time the *Amelia* had been near enough the tartane to send a shot ahead of her. That one was followed by another, which whizzed extraordinarily close to the mastheads, but then Captain Vincent ordered the gun to be secured again. He said to his first lieutenant, who, his speaking-trumpet in hand, kept at his elbow: “We must not sink that craft on any account. If we could get only an hour’s calm, we would carry her with the boats.”

===Chinese===
塔尔塔纳仿佛正随波飞驰，一同扑向那渐渐合拢双臂、迎面逼来的夜色。在她左后舷一英里开外，阿米莉亚号（*Amelia*）满张着常用风帆，紧追不舍，直扑这场追逐的终点。这一追已经持续了好几个钟头，因为佩罗尔（Peyrol）当初悄然溜走时，从一开始便占了阿米莉亚号的上风。还在那片被称作耶尔锚地（Hyères Roadstead）的辽阔平静海面之内时，这艘本就快得出奇的塔尔塔纳，竟当真在单桅快速帆船面前也拉出了距离。随后，佩罗尔猛然从群岛最末两座岛之间的东边水道一头扎了下去，借着黎凡特岛（Ile du Levant）一时遮蔽，竟当真叫追船失了它的踪影。阿米莉亚号为了跟上来不得不两次抢风转向，又落了下风。待她驶入开阔海面，只得再次抢风，于是局面便成了尾追——俗话说了，尾追便是一场漫长的追击。佩罗尔精湛的船艺，两度从文森特舰长（Captain Vincent）口中逼出一声低低的沉吟，伴着意味深长的抿唇。有那么一阵子，阿米莉亚号已逼近塔尔塔纳到足以朝她前方打上一炮的距离。那一炮之后又跟了一炮，嗖地从桅顶近旁擦过，近得异乎寻常；但文森特舰长随即下令将那门炮重新固牢。他对手执传话筒、寸步不离守在他肘边的大副说道：“无论如何也不能把那条船打沉。只要能碰上哪怕一个钟头的无风，我们就能放小艇去把她拿下。”

===Original===
The lieutenant remarked that there was no hope of a calm for the next twenty-four hours at least.

“No,” said Captain Vincent, “and in about an hour it will be dark, and then he may very well give us the slip. The coast is not very far off and there are batteries on both sides of Fréjus, under any of which he will be as safe from capture as though he were hove up on the beach. And look,” he exclaimed after a moment’s pause, “this is what the fellow means to do.”

“Yes, sir,” said the lieutenant, keeping his eyes on the white speck ahead, dancing lightly on the short Mediterranean waves, “he is keeping off the wind.”

“We will have him in less than an hour,” said Captain Vincent, and made as if he meant to rub his hands, but suddenly leaned his elbow on the rail. “After all,” he went on, “properly speaking, it is a race between the *Amelia* and the night.”

“And it will be dark early today,” said the first lieutenant, swinging the speaking-trumpet by its lanyard. “Shall we take the yards off the backstays, sir?”

“No,” said Captain Vincent. “There is a clever seaman aboard that tartane. He is running off now, but at any time he may haul up again. We must not follow him too closely, or we shall lose the advantage which we have now. That man is determined on making his escape.”

===Chinese===
大副说道，至少未来二十四小时内，无风是没有指望的。

“是啊，”文森特舰长说，“再过一个来钟头天就黑了，那时他大有可能叫我们扑个空。海岸离此不远，弗雷瑞斯（Fréjus）两侧都设有炮台，他只要躲进其中任何一座的火力之下，便如同被拖上了海滩一般，万无一失。你瞧，”他顿了片刻又嚷道，“那家伙打的就是这个主意。”

“是的，长官。”大副答道，目光仍盯着前方那一点在短促的地中海浪头上轻盈跳动的白斑，“他在向下风偏离。”

“不出一个钟头，我们就能拿住他。”文森特舰长说着，做出一副像是要搓手的样子，却忽然把肘一撑，靠在了栏杆上。“说到底，”他接着道，“说白了，这是阿米莉亚号跟夜色之间的一场赛跑。”

“今儿天黑得早。”大副说着，甩动着挂在绳带上的传话筒，“要不要把桁桁从后支索上挪开，长官？”

“不。”文森特舰长说，“那条塔尔塔纳上有个厉害的行家。这会儿他是顺风跑，可随时都会再抢风向上。我们不能跟得太紧，否则眼下这点优势反倒要丢。那人铁了心要逃出去。”

===Original===
If those words by some miracle could have been carried to the ears of Peyrol, they would have brought to his lips a smile of malicious and triumphant exultation. Ever since he had laid his hand on the tiller of the tartane every faculty of his resourcefulness and seamanship had been bent on deceiving the English captain, that enemy whom he had never seen, the man whose mind he had constructed for himself from the evolutions of his ship. Leaning against the heavy tiller he addressed Michel, breaking the silence of the strenuous afternoon.

===Chinese===
倘若这几句话能借某种奇迹传到佩罗尔耳中，他唇边必会浮起一抹既狠辣又得意扬扬的笑。自打他的手按上塔尔塔纳的舵柄那一刻起，他那满腹的机变与一身船艺，便全都扑在了一件事上——蒙骗那位英国舰长，那个他素未谋面的敌手，那个他凭对方军舰的一招一式、在自己心中一寸寸勾画出来的心思。他倚着那沉重的舵柄，开口对米歇尔（Michel）说话，打破了这紧绷一下午的沉寂。

===Original===
“This is the moment,” his deep voice uttered quietly. “Ease off the mainsheet, Michel. A little now, only.”

When Michel returned to the place where he had been sitting to windward, the rover noticed his eyes fixed on his face wonderingly. Some vague thoughts had been forming themselves slowly, incompletely, in Michel’s brain. Peyrol met the utter innocence of the unspoken inquiry with a smile that, beginning sardonically on his manly and sensitive mouth, ended in something resembling tenderness.

“That’s so, camarade,” he said with particular stress and intonation, as if those words contained a full and sufficient answer. Most unexpectedly Michel’s round and generally staring eyes blinked, as if dazzled. He too produced from somewhere in the depths of his being a queer, misty smile from which Peyrol averted his gaze.

===Chinese===
“就是现在。”他那低沉的嗓音平静地说道。“把主帆帆脚索松一松，米歇尔。松一点儿就行，就一点儿。”

待米歇尔回到他上风处原本坐着的位置，漫游者发觉他的眼睛正怔怔地盯着自己的脸看。一些模糊的念头正在米歇尔的脑子里慢慢成形，残缺不全。佩罗尔迎上那毫无心机的无声探问，绽出一个微笑——那笑从他刚毅而敏感的唇边起初带着讥诮，到末了却化作了某种近乎温柔的东西。

“是这么回事，*camarade*（伙计）。”他用一种别样的重音和腔调说道，仿佛这几个字便是一个完满而充分的回答。最出乎意料的是，米歇尔那双圆睁着的、平日总直愣愣瞪着的眼睛竟眨了一眨，像是被晃了眼。他也从自己身心深处某个地方挤出了一个古怪、朦胧的笑，引得佩罗尔把目光移了开去。

===Original===
“Where is the citizen?” he asked, bearing hard against the tiller and staring straight ahead. “He isn’t gone overboard, is he? I don’t seem to have seen him since we rounded the land near Porquerolles Castle.”

Michel, after craning his head forward to look over the edge of the deck, announced that Scevola was sitting on the keelson.

“Go forward,” said Peyrol, “and ease off the foresheet now a little. This tartane has wings,” he added to himself.

===Chinese===
“那公民呢？”他问道，用力顶住舵柄，直直地望着前方，“他没掉下海去罢？自打我们从波克罗勒城堡（Porquerolles Castle）附近绕过那片陆地起，我好像就没再见过他。”

米歇尔伸长脖子往甲板边沿外探看了一番，回报说塞沃拉（Scevola）正坐在内龙骨上。

“到前面去，”佩罗尔说，“把前帆帆脚索也稍稍松一松。这条塔尔塔纳长了翅膀啦。”他暗自补了一句。

===Original===
Alone on the afterdeck Peyrol turned his head to look at the *Amelia*. That ship, in consequence of holding her wind, was now crossing obliquely the wake of the tartane. At the same time she had diminished the distance. Nevertheless, Peyrol considered that had he really meant to escape, his chances were as eight to ten﻿ ﻿—practically an assured success. For a long time he had been contemplating the lofty pyramid of canvas towering against the fading red belt on the sky, when a lamentable groan made him look round. It was Scevola. The citizen had adopted the mode of progression on all fours, and while Peyrol looked at him he rolled to leeward, saved himself rather cleverly from going overboard, and holding on desperately to a cleat, shouted in a hollow voice, pointing with the other hand as if he had made a tremendous discovery: “*La terre! La terre!*”

“Certainly,” said Peyrol, steering with extreme nicety. “What of that?”

“I don’t want to be drowned!” cried the citizen in his new hollow voice. Peyrol reflected a bit before he spoke in a serious tone:

“If you stay where you are, I assure you that you will﻿ ﻿…” he glanced rapidly over his shoulder at the *Amelia*﻿ ﻿… “not die by drowning.” He jerked his head sideways. “I know that man’s mind.”

===Chinese===
独自留在艉甲板上的佩罗尔转过头去，望了望阿米莉亚号。那条船因为抱着风不放，此刻正斜斜地横穿塔尔塔纳的尾迹。与此同时，她已缩短了两船间的距离。尽管如此，佩罗尔估量着，倘若自己当真一心要逃，胜算乃是八对十——几乎等于稳操胜券。他久久地凝望着那在天空渐褪的红霞映衬下高高耸起的一座帆布金字塔，忽然一声凄惨的呻吟引得他回过头来。是塞沃拉。这公民已然改用了四脚着地的行进方式，就在佩罗尔望着他的当口，他滚向下风，颇为机灵地没让自己翻下船去，死死抓着一个系索栓，用一种空洞的嗓音嚷了起来，另一只手往前一指，仿佛有了什么惊天动地的发现：“*La terre! La terre!*（陆地！陆地！）”

“那是自然。”佩罗尔说着，把舵掌得精细到了极处，“那又怎样？”

“我不想被淹死！”公民用他那种新生的空洞嗓音喊道。佩罗尔沉吟片刻，才以郑重的口吻说道：

“你只要待在原地不动，我向你保证，你会……”他飞快地回头瞥了一眼阿米莉亚号……“不是被淹死。”他把头往旁边一甩。“那个人的心思，我摸得透。”

===Original===
“What man? Whose mind?” yelled Scevola with intense eagerness and bewilderment. “We are only three on board.”

But Peyrol’s mind was contemplating maliciously the figure of a man with long teeth, in a wig and with large buckles to his shoes. Such was his ideal conception of what the captain of the *Amelia* ought to look like. That officer, whose naturally good-humoured face wore then a look of severe resolution, had beckoned his first lieutenant to his side again.

===Chinese===
“什么人？谁的心思？”塞沃拉急切得近乎癫狂、又满头雾水地嚷道，“船上统共就咱们三个。”

可佩罗尔的心思，正心怀叵测地描画着一个人的模样——长着长牙，戴着假发，鞋上镶着大大的鞋扣。这便是他心目中阿米莉亚号舰长理当长成的样子。而那位军官——一张天生和善的脸此刻却带着严峻决然之色——已再次招手把大副唤到了身边。

===Original===
“We are gaining,” he said quietly. “I intend to close with him to windward. We won’t risk any of his tricks. It is very difficult to outmaneuver a Frenchman, as you know. Send a few armed marines on the forecastle ahead. I am afraid the only way to get hold of this tartane is to disable the men on board of her. I wish to goodness I could think of some other. When we close with her, let the marines fire a well-aimed volley. You must get some marines to stand by aft as well. I hope we may shoot away his halliards; once his sails are down on his deck he is ours for the trouble of putting a boat over the side.”

===Chinese===
“我们追近了。”他平静地说，“我打算从上风靠上去。不能冒他耍花样的险。你要知道，要在一艘法国人面前占尽上风，可实在不容易。派几名持枪的陆战队员到前面的艏楼上去。恐怕要拿住这条塔尔塔纳，唯一的办法就是把船上的人打倒。老天在上，我但愿还能想出别的法子。等我们一靠上去，就让陆战队员放一阵瞄准好的排枪。艉楼这边你也得安排些陆战队员待命。我指望能把他的升降索打断；只要他的帆一塌到甲板上，他便是我们的了——只消放一条小艇下水便成。”

===Original===
For more than half an hour Captain Vincent stood silent, elbow on rail, keeping his eye on the tartane, while on board the latter Peyrol steered silent and watchful but intensely conscious of the enemy ship holding on in her relentless pursuit. The narrow red band was dying out of the sky. The French coast, black against the fading light, merged into the shadows gathering in the eastern board. Citizen Scevola, somewhat soothed by the assurance that he would not die by drowning, had elected to remain quiet where he had fallen, not daring to trust himself to move on the lively deck. Michel, squatting to windward, gazed intently at Peyrol in expectation of some order at any minute. But Peyrol uttered no word and made no sign. From time to time a burst of foam flew over the tartane, or a splash of water would come aboard with a scurrying noise.

It was not till the corvette had got within a long gunshot from the tartane that Peyrol opened his mouth.

“No!” he burst out, loud in the wind, as if giving vent to long anxious thinking, “no! I could not have left you behind with not even a dog for company. Devil take me if I don’t think you would not have thanked me for it either. What do you say to that, Michel?”

A half-puzzled smile dwelt persistently on the guileless countenance of the ex-fisherman. He stated what he had always thought in respect of Peyrol’s every remark: “I think you are right, *maître*.”

“Listen then, Michel. That ship will be alongside of us in less than half an hour. As she comes up they will open on us with musketry.”

“They will open on us﻿ ﻿…” repeated Michel, looking quite interested. “But how do you know they will do that, *maître*?”

“Because her captain has got to obey what is in my mind,” said Peyrol, in a tone of positive and solemn conviction. “He will do it as sure as if I were at his ear telling him what to do. He will do it because he is a first-rate seaman, but I, Michel, I am just a little bit cleverer than he.” He glanced over his shoulder at the *Amelia* rushing after the tartane with swelling sails, and raised his voice suddenly. “He will do it because no more than half a mile ahead of us is the spot where Peyrol will die!”

Michel did not start. He only shut his eyes for a time, and the rover continued in a lower tone:

“I may be shot through the heart at once,” he said; “and in that case you have my permission to let go the halliards if you are alive yourself. But if I live I mean to put the helm down. When I do that you will let go the foresheet to help the tartane to fly into the wind’s eye. This is my last order to you. Now go forward and fear nothing. Adieu.” Michel obeyed without a word.

===Chinese===
足足半个多钟头，文森特舰长一声不吭地站着，肘撑栏杆，目光始终不离那条塔尔塔纳；而在塔尔塔纳上，佩罗尔也默然警醒地掌着舵，心头却无比清晰地感知着那条敌舰如何穷追不舍、死死咬住不放。天空中那一道窄窄的红霞正在熄灭。法国那边的海岸，在将褪的天光里黑黢黢的，已融入了东边海面上渐渐聚拢的暗影之中。公民塞沃拉得了那句“不会被淹死”的保证，稍稍安了些心，索性就安分地待在他摔倒的地方不动了，再不敢冒险在这颠簸活泛的甲板上挪动半步。米歇尔蹲在上风处，目不转睛地盯着佩罗尔，随时等着他下达什么命令。可佩罗尔一言不发，也不做任何示意。时不时地，一阵白沫飞过塔尔塔纳的船舷，又或是一汪海水哗地溅上甲板，发出一阵细碎急促的声响。

直到那艘轻巡洋舰逼近到离塔尔塔纳只剩一门长炮射程之内时，佩罗尔才开了口。

“不！”他在风里大声迸出这一句，像是把憋了许久的焦灼心绪一股脑儿倾吐出来，“不！我总不能把你丢在身后，连条做伴的狗都不留。我敢赌咒，你要是不信——我反倒觉得你不会为此谢我呢。这事你怎么说，米歇尔？”

半是迷惘的笑意，固执地留在这老渔夫那张毫无城府的脸上。他说出了他对佩罗尔每一句话向来所持的那一个看法：“我想您是对的，*maître*（师傅）。”

“那你就听着，米歇尔。不出半个钟头，那条船就要靠到我们船边了。她一靠上来，他们就要用火枪朝我们开火。”

“他们就要朝我们开火……”米歇尔重复道，一副颇为感兴趣的模样，“可您怎么知道他们会那么干，师傅？”

“因为她的舰长，非得照我心里想的去做不可。”佩罗尔用一种斩钉截铁、近乎庄严的笃定口吻说道，“他一定会那么做，笃定得就像我正贴在他耳边吩咐他该怎么做一样。他之所以会那么做，是因为他是个一流的航海人，可是我呀，米歇尔，我比他稍稍更精那么一点儿。”他回头瞥了一眼鼓满风帆、紧追塔尔塔纳而来的阿米莉亚号，忽然提高了嗓门。“他之所以会那么做，是因为在咱们前方不出半英里的地方，便是佩罗尔的葬身之地！”

米歇尔并未一惊。他只是闭了一会儿眼睛。漫游者放低了声音，接着说道：

“我也许会被一枪打穿心脏，当场了账，”他说，“那样的话，只要你自己还活着，我就准你松开升降索。可要是我还活着，我便要把舵往下风一推。我这么一推，你就松开前帆帆脚索，好帮这条塔尔塔纳迎着风眼飞起来。这是我给你的最后一道命令。这便到前面去罢，什么都不用怕。*Adieu*（别了）。”米歇尔一言不发地照办了。

===Original===
Half a dozen of the *Amelia*’s marines stood ranged on the forecastle-head ready with their muskets. Captain Vincent walked into the lee waist to watch his chase. When he thought that the jibboom of the *Amelia* had drawn level with the stern of the tartane he waved his hat and the marines discharged their muskets. Apparently no gear was cut. Captain Vincent observed the white-headed man, who was steering, clap his hand to his left side, while he hove the tiller to leeward and brought the tartane sharply into the wind. The marines on the poop fired in their turn, all the reports merging into one. Voices were heard on the decks crying that they “had hit the white-haired chap.” Captain Vincent shouted to the master:

“Get the ship round on the other tack.”

The elderly seaman who was the master of the *Amelia* took a critical look before he gave the necessary orders; and the *Amelia* closed on her chase with her decks resounding to the piping of boatswain’s mates and the hoarse shout: “Hands shorten sail. About ship.”

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚号上的六七名陆战队员在艏楼上排成了一列，端着火枪严阵以待。文森特舰长走到下风舷的舯部，去观望他的猎物。当他判定阿米莉亚号的艏斜桅已经追得与塔尔塔纳的船尾齐平时，他便挥了挥帽子，陆战队员一齐扣动了扳机。看样子并没有打断什么索具。文森特舰长瞧见那个白头发的掌舵人一手捂住左胁，同时把舵柄往下一推，让塔尔塔纳猛地迎风一转。艉楼上的陆战队员接着也开了火，所有枪声汇成了一响。甲板上听得有人嚷着“打中那白头发的家伙了”。文森特舰长朝航海长高喊：

“把船转到另一舷的抢风向上去。”

那位年迈的海员——也就是阿米莉亚号的航海长——先挑剔地端详了一番，才下达了必要的号令；于是阿米莉亚号朝她的猎物靠去，甲板上回荡着水手长助手们尖锐的哨音和粗哑的吆喝：“各就各位，收帆。掉头转向。”

===Original===
Peyrol, lying on his back under the swinging tiller, heard the calls shrilling and dying away; he heard the ominous rush of the *Amelia*’s bow wave as the sloop foamed within ten yards of the tartane’s stern; he even saw her upper yards coming down, and then everything vanished out of the clouded sky. There was nothing in his ears but the sound of the wind, the wash of the waves buffeting the little craft left without guidance, and the continuous thrashing of its foresail the sheet of which Michel had let go according to orders. The tartane began to roll heavily, but Peyrol’s right arm was sound and he managed to put it round a bollard to prevent himself from being flung about. A feeling of peace sank into him, not unmingled with pride. Everything he had planned had come to pass. He had meant to play that man a trick, and now the trick had been played. Played by him better than by any other old man on whom age had stolen, unnoticed, till the veil of peace was torn down by the touch of a sentiment unexpected like an intruder and cruel like an enemy.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔仰面躺在来回摆荡的舵柄之下，听见了那尖锐的哨音一声声尖啸着又渐次消歇；他听见了阿米莉亚号船首浪那不祥的奔涌——那条单桅快速帆船正吐着白沫，从离塔尔塔纳船尾不出十码的地方掠过；他甚至看见了它上桁的桁杆正在落下来，随后一切便都从那阴云密布的天空中消失了。他耳边只剩下了风声、浪头拍击这条失去了指引的小船的哗哗声，以及那面前帆不住地猛烈扑打——帆脚索已被米歇尔遵令松开。塔尔塔纳开始剧烈地横摇起来，好在佩罗尔的右臂尚还灵便，他设法把它绕上了一根系缆桩，免得自己被甩得东倒西歪。一种安宁之感沉入了他的心胸，其中也不无骄傲。他所筹谋的一切，都已一一应验。他本要给那人耍一个花招，如今这花招已经耍成了。耍得比任何一个被暮年悄然潜入、却浑然不觉的老人都要漂亮——直到有一天，那重安宁的帷幔被一种感情触破，那感情来得出人意料，像个不速之客，又冷酷得如同仇敌。

===Original===
Peyrol rolled his head to the left. All he could see were the legs of Citizen Scevola sliding nervelessly to and fro to the rolling of the vessel as if his body had been jammed somewhere. Dead, or only scared to death? And Michel? Was he dead or dying, that man without friends whom his pity had refused to leave behind marooned on the earth without even a dog for company. As to that, Peyrol felt no compunction; but he thought he would have liked to see Michel once more. He tried to utter his name, but his throat refused him even a whisper. He felt himself removed far away from that world of human sounds, in which Arlette had screamed at him: “Peyrol, don’t you dare!” He would never hear anybody’s voice again! Under that grey sky there was nothing for him but the swish of breaking seas and the ceaseless furious beating of the tartane’s foresail. His plaything was knocking about terribly under him, with her tiller flying madly to and fro just clear of his head, and solid lumps of water coming on board over his prostrate body. Suddenly, in a desperate lurch which brought the whole Mediterranean with a ferocious snarl level with the slope of the little deck, Peyrol saw the *Amelia* bearing right down upon the tartane. The fear, not of death, but of failure, gripped his slowing-down heart. Was this blind Englishman going to run him down and sink the dispatches together with the craft? With a mighty effort of his ebbing strength Peyrol sat up and flung his arm round the shroud of the mainmast.

===Chinese===
佩罗尔把头往左一滚。他所能看见的，不过是公民塞沃拉那两条腿随着船的摇晃软塌塌地来回滑动，仿佛他的身子已被卡在了什么地方。是死了，还是仅仅被吓得魂飞魄散？那么米歇尔呢？他死了没有，是不是正在死去——那个没有朋友的人，正是出于怜悯，他才不忍心把他撇下，让他像个被放逐在荒岛上、连条做伴的狗都没有的人那样孤独终老。这一点上，佩罗尔心头并无悔恨；但他想，他本愿意再看米歇尔一眼的。他试着唤他的名字，可他的喉咙却连一丝耳语也不肯给他了。他觉得自己已被移到了远离那个属人声响的世界之外——在那个世界里，阿尔莱特（Arlette）曾冲他尖叫：“佩罗尔，你敢！”他再也听不见任何人的声音了！在这一片灰色的天穹之下，留给他的，唯有碎浪的刷刷声和塔尔塔纳面前帆那无休无止、狂暴至极的扑打。他的这件玩物在他身下可怕地颠簸着，舵柄就在他头顶上方不远处疯狂地来回飞荡，一块块实打实的水团越过他俯卧的身躯砸上甲板。忽然，在一个猛烈的倾侧之中——那一侧把整片地中海都带着凶狠的咆哮抬到了与这小甲板斜面齐平的高度——佩罗尔看见阿米莉亚号正径直朝塔尔塔纳压了下来。攫住他那颗渐渐迟滞的心的，不是对死亡的恐惧，而是对功亏一篑的恐惧。这个瞎了眼的英国人，难道要撞上来、连人带船把那些急件一同沉进海里去么？佩罗尔拼尽他那正一点点流尽的力气，坐起身来，一把将手臂搂住了主桅的护桅索。

===Original===
The *Amelia*, whose way had carried her past the tartane for a quarter of a mile before sail could be shortened and her yards swung on the other tack, was coming back to take possession of her chase. In the deepening dusk and amongst the foaming seas it was a matter of difficulty to make out the little craft. At the very moment when the master of the man-of-war, looking out anxiously from the forecastle-head, thought that she might perhaps have filled and gone down, he caught sight of her rolling in the trough of the sea, and so close that she seemed to be at the end of the *Amelia*’s jibboom. His heart flew in his mouth. “Hard a starboard!” he yelled, his order being passed along the decks.

Peyrol, sinking back on the deck, in another heavy lurch of his craft, saw for an instant the whole of the English corvette swing up into the clouds as if she meant to fling herself upon his very breast. A blown seatop flicked his face noisily, followed by a smooth interval, a silence of the waters. He beheld in a flash the days of his manhood, of strength and adventure. Suddenly an enormous voice like the roar of an angry sea-lion seemed to fill the whole of the empty sky in a mighty and commanding shout: “Steady!”﻿ ﻿… And with the sound of that familiar English word ringing in his ears Peyrol smiled to his visions and died.

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚号凭着余冲，已掠过塔尔塔纳四分之一英里之远，这才得以收帆、把桁桁转到另一舷的抢风向上，如今又折了回来，要来接收她的猎物。在愈来愈深的暮色里，在一片白沫翻腾的海面之间，要辨清那条小船可实在不易。就在这艘军舰的航海长从艏楼焦灼地向外张望、正以为她怕是已经灌水沉没的那一刻，他忽然瞥见她在波谷里打滚，而且近得就像是悬在阿米莉亚号艏斜桅的尽头似的。他的心一下子提到了嗓子眼。“右满舵！”他厉声大喊，这道号令沿着甲板一路传了下去。

佩罗尔在又一次沉重的倾侧中颓然倒回甲板上，一瞥之间，只见那艘英国轻巡洋舰整个儿荡上了云端，仿佛要朝他的胸口直压下来。一蓬飞溅的浪花啪地甩上了他的脸，接着是一段平顺的间歇，一片海水的沉寂。他在电光石火间望见了自己壮年的岁月，那些气力与冒险的岁月。忽然，一个巨大的嗓音，宛如一头愤怒海狮的咆哮，似乎充塞了整个空旷的天空，发出一声威严而洪亮的断喝：“Steady!（稳住！）”——伴随着那个他所熟悉的英文词在耳畔回响，佩罗尔朝他的种种幻象微微一笑，死去了。

===Original===
The *Amelia*, stripped down to her topsails and hove to, rose and fell easily, while on her quarter about a cable’s length away Peyrol’s tartane tumbled like a lifeless corpse amongst the seas. Captain Vincent, in his favourite attitude of leaning over the rail, kept his eyes fastened on his prize. Mr. Bolt, who had been sent for, waited patiently till his commander turned round.

“Oh, here you are, Mr. Bolt. I have sent for you to go and take possession. You speak French, and there may still be somebody alive in her. If so, of course you will send him on board at once. I am sure there can be nobody unwounded there. It will anyhow be too dark to see much, but just have a good look round and secure everything in the way of papers you can lay your hands on. Haul aft the foresheet and sail her up to receive a towline. I intend to take her along and ransack her thoroughly in the morning; tear down the cuddy linings and so on, should you not find at once what I expect.﻿ ﻿…” Captain Vincent, his white teeth gleaming in the dusk, gave some further orders in a lower tone, and Mr. Bolt departed in a hurry. Half an hour afterwards he was back on board, and the *Amelia*, with the tartane in tow, made sail to the eastward in search of the blockading fleet.

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚号已收帆只剩中桅帆，顶风停住了船，轻轻地随波起伏；而在她艉后约一链之遥的地方，佩罗尔的塔尔塔纳像一具没有生命的尸首似地在海浪间翻滚。文森特舰长以他那惯常的姿态——俯身靠着栏杆——目不转睛地盯着他的战利品。被人去叫来的博尔特先生（Mr. Bolt）耐心地等着，直到舰长转过身来。

“哦，你来了，博尔特先生。我叫你来，是要你过去接收那条船。你懂法语，那船上说不定还有人活着。要是有的话，当然你立刻把他送到我船上来。我敢说那上面不可能还有没负伤的人。反正天色也太暗，看不出多少名堂，不过你只管好好四处搜一搜，凡是能到手的文件之类，一律收好。把前帆帆脚索拉向艉部，把船开上来接一根拖缆。我打算把她一路拖着，到明天早上再把她彻底搜个底朝天；要是你一时找不到我指望的那些东西，就把后舱的衬板都拆下来，诸如此类——”文森特舰长在暮色里露出一口白牙，又压低了嗓门交代了几句别的命令，博尔特先生便匆匆去了。半个钟头之后他回到了船上，阿米莉亚号拖着塔尔塔纳，扬帆向东驶去，去寻找那支执行封锁的舰队。

===Original===
Mr. Bolt, introduced into a cabin strongly lighted by a swinging lamp, tendered to his captain across the table a sailcloth package corded and sealed, and a piece of paper folded in four, which, he explained, seemed to be a certificate of registry, strangely enough mentioning no name. Captain Vincent seized the grey canvas package eagerly.

“This looks like the very thing, Bolt,” he said, turning it over in his hands. “What else did you find on board?”

Bolt said that he had found three dead men, two on the afterdeck and one lying at the bottom of the open hold with the bare end of the foresheet in his hand﻿—“shot down, I suppose, just as he had let it go,” he commented. He described the appearance of the bodies and reported that he had disposed of them according to orders. In the tartane’s cabin there was half a demijohn of wine and a loaf of bread in a locker; also, on the floor, a leather valise containing an officer’s uniform coat and a change of clothing. He had lighted the lamp and saw that the linen was marked “E. Réal.” An officer’s sword on a broad shoulder-belt was also lying on the floor. These things could not have belonged to the old chap with the white hair, who was a big man. “Looks as if somebody had tumbled overboard,” commented Bolt. Two of the bodies looked nondescript, but there was no doubt about that fine old fellow being a seaman.

“By heavens!” said Captain Vincent, “he was that! Do you know, Bolt, that he nearly managed to escape us. Another twenty minutes would have done it. How many wounds had he?”

“Three I think, sir. I did not look closely,” said Bolt.

“I hated the necessity of shooting brave men like dogs,” said Captain Vincent. “Still, it was the only way; and there may be something here,” he went on, slapping the package with his open palm, “that will justify me in my own eyes. You may go now.”

===Chinese===
博尔特先生被领进一间被一盏摇晃的吊灯照得通亮的舱房，隔着桌子向舰长呈上一只用绳扎紧、加了封印的帆布包裹，还有一张折成四折的纸片——据他解释，那似乎是一份登记证书，奇怪的是上面竟没有署名。文森特舰长急切地一把抓过那只灰白色的帆布包。

“看来正是这玩意儿，博尔特。”他说着，在手里把那包翻来翻去。“你在船上还查到了些什么？”

博尔特说，他发现了三具尸体，两具在艉甲板上，还有一具俯卧在敞开的底舱底下，手里还攥着前帆帆脚索光秃秃的末端——“我估摸，是就在他松手的那一刻被打倒的。”他评道。他描述了几具尸首的模样，并报告说自己已按命令处置了它们。塔尔塔纳的舱房里，有一只柜子，里面搁着半坛酒和一块面包；此外，地板上还有一只皮质的旅行袋（valise），里头装着一件军官的制服上衣和一套换洗衣物。他点上了灯，看见那内衣上标着“E. Réal”的字样。地板上还躺着一把系在宽肩带上的军官佩剑。这些东西，不可能属于那个白头发的老头儿——那人是个大块头。“瞧着像是有人翻下海去了。”博尔特评道。那三具尸首里有两具看上去说不清来路，但那位出色的老头儿毫无疑问是个航海人。

“老天在上！”文森特舰长说，“他正是那样的人！你可知道，博尔特，他差点儿就从我们手里溜走了。再给他二十分钟，就成了。他身上有几处伤？”

“三处，我想是，长官。我没细看。”博尔特说。

“我真不愿意非得像打狗一样去枪杀勇敢的人。”文森特舰长说，“可这也是没办法的事；说不定这里头会有什么，”他接着说，张开手掌拍了拍那只包裹，“能让我在自己眼里也给自己一个交代。你现在可以去了。”

===Original===
Captain Vincent did not turn in, but only lay down fully dressed on the couch till the officer of the watch, appearing at the door, told him that a ship of the fleet was in sight away to windward. Captain Vincent ordered the private night signal to be made. When he came on deck the towering shadow of a line-of-battle ship that seemed to reach to the very clouds was well within hail and a voice bellowed from her through a speaking-trumpet:

“What ship is that?”

“His Majesty’s sloop *Amelia*,” hailed back Captain Vincent. “What ship is that, pray?”

Instead of the usual answer, there was a short pause, and another voice spoke boisterously through the trumpet:

“Is that you, Vincent? Don’t you know the *Superb* when you see her?”

“Not in the dark, Keats. How are you? I am in a hurry to speak the Admiral.”

“The fleet is lying by,” came the voice, now with painstaking distinctness, across the murmurs, whispers and splashes of the black lane of water dividing the two ships. “The Admiral bears S.S.E. If you stretch on till daylight as you are, you will fetch him on the other tack in time for breakfast on board the *Victory*. Is anything up?”

===Chinese===
文森特舰长没有就寝，只是和衣在卧榻上躺了一会儿，直到值更官出现在门口，报告说上风方向望见了一条本舰队的船。文森特舰长下令打出夜间的专用识别信号。待他走上甲板，一艘战列舰那高耸入云般的庞大阴影已在传话可及的距离之内，一个嗓门透过传话筒从那条船上隆隆地吼了过来：

“那是哪条船？”

“国王陛下的单桅快速帆船阿米莉亚号。”文森特舰长回喊道，“请问那是哪条船？”

回他的不是惯常的那句答话，而是短暂的停顿，随后另一个嗓门透过传话筒大大咧咧地嚷道：

“是你吗，文森特？你瞧见苏珀布号（*Superb*）都认不出来了？”

“黑灯瞎火的，认不出来呀，基茨（Keats）。你好吗？我急着要见总司令说话。”

“舰队正停船待命。”那嗓音传来，此刻一字一句刻意咬得清清楚楚，隔着那条把两舰隔开、夹着呢喃、低语和水花飞溅的黑色水道——“总司令在 S.S.E. 方位。你要是照这样直驶到天亮，在另一舷抢风一转，正好赶上胜利号（*Victory*）上的早饭。出什么事了么？”

===Original===
At every slight roll the sails of the *Amelia*, becalmed by the bulk of the seventy-four, flapped gently against the masts.

“Not much,” hailed Captain Vincent. “I made a prize.”

“Have you been in action?” came the swift inquiry.

“No, no. Piece of luck.”

“Where’s your prize?” roared the speaking-trumpet with interest.

“In my desk,” roared Captain Vincent in reply.﻿ ﻿… “Enemy dispatches.﻿ ﻿… I say, Keats, fill on your ship. Fill on her, I say, or you will be falling on board of me.” He stamped his foot impatiently. “Clap some hands at once on the towline and run that tartane close under our stern,” he called to the officer of the watch, “or else the old *Superb* will walk over her without ever knowing anything about it.”

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚号的帆被那艘七十四炮舰的庞大船身遮得没了风，随着船身每一次轻微的摇晃，便轻轻地拍打着桅杆。

“没什么大不了。”文森特舰长喊道，“我抓了一桩战利品。”

“你交过火了么？”紧接着便是一句迅疾的追问。

“没有，没有。走了运。”

“你的战利品在哪儿？”传话筒饶有兴味地吼道。

“在我书桌里。”文森特舰长也扯着嗓子回吼道……“敌方的急件。……我说，基茨，让你那条船转向上风满帆罢。我说，让她满帆，不然你可要撞到我船上来啦。”他不耐烦地顿了顿脚。“马上叫几个人去拽那根拖缆，把那条塔尔塔纳拽到我们艉下紧紧靠着，”他吩咐值更官道，“要不然老苏珀布号连个招呼都不打，就从她身上碾过去了。”

===Original===
When Captain Vincent presented himself on board the *Victory* it was too late for him to be invited to share the Admiral’s breakfast. He was told that Lord Nelson had not been seen on deck yet that morning; and presently word came that he wished to see Captain Vincent at once in his cabin. Being introduced, the captain of the *Amelia*, in undress uniform, with a sword by his side and his hat under his arm, was received kindly, made his bow and with a few words of explanation laid the packet on the big round table at which sat a silent secretary in black clothes, who had been obviously writing a letter from his Lordship’s dictation. The Admiral had been walking up and down, and after he had greeted Captain Vincent he resumed his pacing of a nervous man. His empty sleeve had not yet been pinned on his breast, and swung slightly every time he turned in his walk. His thin locks fell lank against the pale cheeks, and the whole face in repose had an expression of suffering with which the fire of his one eye presented a startling contrast. He stopped short and exclaimed while Captain Vincent towered over him in a respectful attitude:

“A tartane! Captured on board a tartane! How on earth did you pitch upon that one out of the hundreds you must see every month?”

“I must confess that I got hold accidentally of some curious information,” said Captain Vincent. “It was all a piece of luck.”

===Chinese===
待文森特舰长登上胜利号时，已是太晚，赶不上被邀与总司令共进早餐了。有人告诉他，那天早上纳尔逊勋爵（Lord Nelson）还没在甲板上露过面；不一会儿传话来说，勋爵要即刻在舱中见他。被引见进去之后，这位阿米莉亚号的舰长身着便服，腰间佩剑，臂下挟帽，受到了和气的接待，他行过礼，三言两语说明了来意，便把那只包裹放到一张大圆桌上——桌前坐着一位穿黑衣、默不作声的秘书，显见得方才正在依勋爵口授笔录一封信。总司令原本正在屋里来回踱步，与文森特舰长见过礼之后，便又恢复了一个神经质之人那种踱步。他那截空荡荡的衣袖尚未别到胸前，每回转身踱步时便微微晃荡。他稀疏的头发软塌塌地贴在苍白的两颊上，整张脸在静默之时带着一种受苦的神情，与他那只独眼里的火焰恰成一种触目的反差。他蓦地收住脚步，惊呼出声，而文森特舰长则怀着敬意，高大地俯身在他之上：

“一条塔尔塔纳！在一条塔尔塔纳上缴获的！你每个月少说也要看见上百条那样的船，究竟是怎么偏偏挑中了这一条的？”

“我必须承认，我是偶然弄到了一些不同寻常的情报。”文森特舰长说，“这全是运气。”

===Original===
While the secretary was ripping open with a penknife the cover of the dispatches Lord Nelson took Captain Vincent out into the stern gallery. The quiet and sunshiny morning had the added charm of a cool, light breeze; and the *Victory*, under her three topsails and lower staysails, was moving slowly to the southward in the midst of the scattered fleet carrying for the most part the same sail as the Admiral. Only far away two or three ships could be seen covered with canvas, trying to close with the flag. Captain Vincent noted with satisfaction that the first lieutenant of the *Amelia* had been obliged to brace by his afteryards in order not to overrun the Admiral’s quarter.

“Why!” exclaimed Lord Nelson suddenly, after looking at the sloop for a moment, “you have that tartane in tow!”

“I thought that your Lordship would perhaps like to see a 40-ton lateen craft which has led such a chase to, I dare say, the fastest sloop in His Majesty’s service.”

“How did it all begin?” asked the Admiral, continuing to look at the *Amelia*.

===Chinese===
趁秘书用削笔刀拆开急件的封套，纳尔逊勋爵把文森特舰长带到了艉部的廊台上去。这个宁静、阳光明媚的早晨，又添了一阵凉爽微风的迷人意趣；胜利号张着三面中桅帆和下支索帆，正在那支稀疏分布、多半张着与旗舰相同帆装的舰队之中，缓缓向南驶去。唯有远处两三条船满张着帆，可以看出正努力向旗舰靠拢。文森特舰长满意地注意到，阿米莉亚号的大副为了不致冲过总司令的艉部，不得不把后桅的桁桁转了向。

“咦！”纳尔逊勋爵打量了那条单桅快速帆船片刻，忽然嚷道，“你把那条塔尔塔纳拖着走哪！”

“我想，勋爵阁下也许愿意看看一条四十吨的三角帆船——它领着陛下麾下恐怕要数最快的一条单桅快速帆船，打了这么一场好追击。”

“这到底是怎么起的头？”总司令问道，目光仍不离阿米莉亚号。

===Original===
“As I have already hinted to your Lordship, certain information came in my way,” began Captain Vincent, who did not think it necessary to enlarge upon that part of the story. “This tartane, which is not very different to look at from the other tartanes along the coast between Cette and Genoa, had started from a cove on the Giens peninsula. An old man with a white head of hair was entrusted with the service, and really they could have found nobody better. He came round Cape Esterel intending to pass through the Hyères Roadstead. Apparently he did not expect to find the *Amelia* in his way. And it was there that he made his only mistake. If he had kept on his course, I would probably have taken no more notice of him than of two other craft that were in sight then. But he acted suspiciously by hauling up for the battery on Porquerolles. This manoeuvre in connection with the information of which I spoke decided me to overhaul him and see what he had on board.” Captain Vincent then related concisely the episodes of the chase. “I assure your Lordship that I never gave an order with greater reluctance than to open musketry fire on that craft; but the old man had given such proofs of his seamanship and determination that there was nothing else for it. Why! at the very moment he had the *Amelia* alongside of him he still made a most clever attempt to prolong the chase. There were only a few minutes of daylight left, and in the darkness we might very well have lost him. Considering that they all could have saved their lives simply by striking their sails on deck, I cannot refuse them my admiration, and especially to the white-haired man.”

===Chinese===
“正如我已经向勋爵阁下暗示过的那样，有一份情报落入了我手里。”文森特舰长开口道，他并不觉得有必要把那段情节再加以铺陈。“这条塔尔塔纳，看上去与塞特（Cette）和热那亚（Genoa）之间沿海的其他塔尔塔纳并无多大不同，它是从吉安半岛上的一个小海湾出发的。一个白头发的老头儿被委以这趟差使，说实在的，他们也再找不出更好的人选了。他绕过埃斯泰雷尔角（Cape Esterel），打算穿过耶尔锚地。显然他没料到会在路上撞见阿米莉亚号。而正是在这一点上，他犯下了唯一的错误。倘若他照原航向走下去，我对他的注意，大概也不会比当时望得见的另外两条船更多。可他却行迹可疑地抢风向上，朝波克罗勒的炮台靠去。这一手机动，再联系到我说过的那份情报，便叫我下定决心要上前去查他一查，看看他船上装的什么。”文森特舰长随即简明扼要地讲述了追逐中的几个回合。“我向勋爵阁下保证，我下达过的所有命令里，再没有比下令对那条船开火更叫我勉为其难的了；可那老头儿既已那样地证明了他的船艺和决心，便再没有第二条路可走。哎！就在阿米莉亚号已经贴到他船边的那一刻，他居然还做了个极为巧妙的手脚，想把这场追击再拖长一些。当时所剩的日光不过几分钟了，天一黑，我们极有可能就把他丢了。想想他们只要肯把帆落到甲板上，便人人都能保住性命，我不能不向他们，尤其是向那位白头发的老人，献上我的钦佩。”

===Original===
The Admiral, who had been all the time looking absently at the *Amelia* keeping her station with the tartane in tow, said:

“You have a very smart little ship, Vincent. Very fit for the work I have given you to do. French built, isn’t she?”

“Yes, my Lord. They are great shipbuilders.”

“You don’t seem to hate the French, Vincent,” said the Admiral, smiling faintly.

“Not that kind, my Lord,” said Captain Vincent, with a bow. “I detest their political principles and the characters of their public men, but your Lordship will admit that for courage and determination we could not have found worthier adversaries anywhere on this globe.”
