“I never said that they were to be despised,” said Lord Nelson. “Resource, courage, yes.﻿ ﻿… If that Toulon fleet gives me the slip, all our squadrons from Gibraltar to Brest will be in jeopardy. Why don’t they come out and be done with it? Don’t I keep far enough out of their way?” he cried.

===Chinese===
总司令一直心不在焉地望着阿米莉亚号拖着塔尔塔纳守着自己的阵位，这时说道：

“你这条小船很利索，文森特。很配我派给你的那桩差事。它是法国造的吧？”

“是的，勋爵。他们是了不起的造船匠。”

“你看上去并不仇恨法国人哪，文森特。”总司令说着，淡淡地一笑。

“不是恨那一种，勋爵。”文森特舰长一躬到地说道，“我痛恨的是他们的政治原则，是他们那些公众人物的品性；可是勋爵阁下也得承认，论胆识与决断，我们在这世上再也找不到比他们更配做敌手的人了。”

“我可从没说过他们可以小瞧。”纳尔逊勋爵说，“机变、胆识，是有的。……土伦那支舰队要是再从我手底下溜走，我们从直布罗陀（Gibraltar）到布雷斯特（Brest）的所有分舰队就都要陷入险境了。他们为什么不出来、痛痛快快了结呢？难道我离他们还不够远么？”他嚷道。

===Original===
Vincent remarked the nervous agitation of the frail figure with a concern augmented by a fit of coughing which came on the Admiral. He was quite alarmed by its violence. He watched the Commander-in-Chief in the Mediterranean choking and gasping so helplessly that he felt compelled to turn his eyes away from the painful spectacle; but he noticed also how quickly Lord Nelson recovered from the subsequent exhaustion.

“This is anxious work, Vincent,” he said. “It is killing me. I aspire to repose somewhere in the country, in the midst of fields, out of reach of the sea and the Admiralty and dispatches and orders, and responsibility too. I have been just finishing a letter to tell them at home I have hardly enough breath in my body to carry me on from day to day.﻿ ﻿… But I am like that white-headed man you admire so much, Vincent,” he pursued, with a weary smile, “I will stick to my task till perhaps some shot from the enemy puts an end to everything.﻿ ﻿… Let us see what there may be in those papers you have brought on board.”

===Chinese===
文森特留意到那羸弱身躯上神经质的激动，正自忧心，总司令又发作起一阵咳嗽，更叫他忧心忡忡。那咳嗽之猛烈，简直把他吓着了。他眼看着这位地中海舰队总司令憋闷、喘不过气来，那般无可奈何，只觉得不得不把目光从这幅痛苦的景象上移开；但他也注意到，纳尔逊勋爵从随后的虚脱之中恢复过来，又是何等之快。

“这是叫人焦虑的活儿啊，文森特。”他说，“它正在要我的命。我向往能到乡间某个地方去休养，置身田野之中，远离大海、海军部、急件、号令，也远离那一身责任。我方才正写完一封信，要告诉国内的人，我身子里的气力已所剩无几，连一天一天捱下去都勉强。……可是我也跟你所钦佩的那位白头发的老人一样啊，文森特，”他带着倦乏的微笑接着说，“我要死守我的职分，直到也许某一颗敌人的炮弹把这一切都了结。……来，让我们瞧瞧你带上船来的那些文件里，都有些什么。”

===Original===
The secretary in the cabin had arranged them in separate piles.

“What is it all about?” asked the Admiral, beginning again to pace restlessly up and down the cabin.

“At the first glance, the most important, my Lord, are the orders for marine authorities in Corsica and Naples to make certain dispositions in view of an expedition to Egypt.”

“I always thought so,” said the Admiral, his eye gleaming at the attentive countenance of Captain Vincent. “This is a smart piece of work on your part, Vincent. I can do no better than send you back to your station. Yes﻿ ﻿… Egypt﻿ ﻿… the East.﻿ ﻿… Everything points that way,” he soliloquised under Vincent’s eyes, while the secretary, picking up the papers with care, rose quietly and went out to have them translated and to make an abstract for the Admiral.

“And yet who knows!” exclaimed Lord Nelson, standing still for a moment. “But the blame or the glory must be mine alone. I will seek counsel from no man.” Captain Vincent felt himself forgotten, invisible, less than a shadow in the presence of a nature capable of such vehement feelings. “How long can he last?” he asked himself with sincere concern.

===Chinese===
舱里那位秘书已把它们分成了几摞。

“这都是关于什么的？”总司令问道，又开始在舱中焦躁地来回踱起步来。

“乍一看，最要紧的，勋爵，是给科西嘉（Corsica）和那不勒斯（Naples）两地海军当局的命令，要他们为一次远征埃及（Egypt）作某些部署。”

“我一向就是这么想的。”总司令说，那只独眼在文森特专注的脸上一亮。“这一手干得漂亮，文森特。我能做的，莫过于把你打发回你的阵位上去。对……埃及……东方……一切都指向那个方向。”他在文森特眼前这样自语着，而那位秘书则小心翼翼地收起那些文件，悄没声地起身出去了，要去把它们译出，并为总司令拟一份摘要。

“可也难说啊！”纳尔逊勋爵蓦地站住脚嚷道，“但无论是过是功，都得由我一人来承当。我绝不向任何人讨主意。”文森特舰长觉得自己被遗忘了，被看不见了，在一个能怀有这般激烈情感的天性面前，他比一道影子还不如。“他还能撑多久呢？”他由衷地忧心着，暗暗自问。

===Original===
The Admiral, however, soon remembered his presence, and at the end of another ten minutes Captain Vincent left the *Victory*, feeling, like all officers who approached Lord Nelson, that he had been speaking with a personal friend; and with a renewed devotion for the great sea-officer’s soul dwelling in the frail body of the Commander-in-Chief of His Majesty’s ships in the Mediterranean. While he was being pulled back to his ship a general signal went up in the *Victory* for the fleet to form line, as convenient, ahead and astern of the Admiral; followed by another to the *Amelia* to part company. Vincent accordingly gave his orders to make sail, and, directing the master to shape a course for Cape Cicié, went down into his cabin. He had been up nearly the whole of the last three nights, and he wanted to get a little sleep. His slumbers, however, were short and disturbed. Early in the afternoon he found himself broad awake and reviewing in his mind the events of the day before. The order to shoot three brave men in cold blood, terribly distasteful at the time, was lying heavily on him. Perhaps he had been impressed by Peyrol’s white head, his obstinacy to escape him, the determination shown to the very last minute; by something in the whole episode that suggested a more than common devotion to duty and a spirit of daring defiance. With his robust health, simple good nature, and sanguine temperament touched with a little irony, Captain Vincent was a man of generous feelings and of easily moved sympathies.

“Yet,” he reflected, “they have been asking for it. There could be only one end to that affair. But the fact remains that they were defenceless and unarmed and particularly harmless-looking, and at the same time as brave as any. That old chap now.﻿ ﻿…” He wondered how much of exact truth there was in Symons’ tale of adventure. He concluded that the facts must have been true but that Symons’ interpretation of them made it extraordinarily difficult to discover what really there was under all that. That craft certainly was fit for blockade running. Lord Nelson had been pleased. Captain Vincent went on deck with the kindliest feelings towards all men, alive and dead.

===Chinese===
不过，总司令很快便想起了他还在；又过了十分钟，文森特舰长离开了胜利号。和所有接近过纳尔逊勋爵的军官一样，他觉得自己仿佛是和一位私交挚友谈过了话；他对那位寓于国王陛下地中海舰队总司令这羸弱躯壳之中的伟大海军军官的灵魂，也再度生出一片忠心。正当他用小艇被划回自己船上时，胜利号升起了一道通用信号，命舰队视情况方便，在总司令的前后列成战列；紧接着又对阿米莉亚号发出一道信号，命其离队。文森特据此下令扬帆，又指示航海长设定驶向西西耶角（Cape Cicié）的航向，便下到了自己的舱里。过去三个夜晚他几乎整夜未眠，只想稍微睡一睡。然而他这一觉睡得既短又不稳。午后没多久，他便完全清醒过来，在脑中一遍遍回放着前一天种种。那道把三个勇敢之人冷血击毙的命令，当时便叫他万般不忍，此刻更沉沉地压在他心头。也许是佩罗尔那头白发打动了他，是他那般要摆脱自己的执拗，是那份直撑到最后一刻的决心；也许是整件事里有某种东西，透着一股超乎寻常的尽职之心和一种大胆抗命的气概。凭着健壮的体魄、质朴的好性情，再加上略带几分讥诮的乐观性情，文森特舰长是个胸怀慷慨、易于动怜悯之人。

“可是，”他暗想，“他们也是自找的。那件事只能有那一种结局。可事实终究是：他们手无寸铁、毫无防备，瞧着尤其人畜无害，同时又和任何人一样勇敢。就说那老头儿罢。……”他纳闷西蒙斯（Symons）所讲的那一番历险，究竟有几分是干真万确的实情。他得出的结论是：那些事实想必不假，可西蒙斯对它们的解读，却叫人格外难以看透这一切底下究竟藏着什么。那条船，确乎是适合偷越封锁的。纳尔逊勋爵已然龙颜大悦。文森特舰长走上甲板，对一切人——无论活人死人——都怀着最善意的情肠。

===Original===
The afternoon had turned out very fine. The British fleet was just out of sight with the exception of one or two stragglers, under a press of canvas. A light breeze, in which only the *Amelia* could travel at five knots, hardly ruffled the profundity of the blue waters basking in the warm tenderness of the cloudless sky. To south and west the horizon was empty except for two specks very far apart, of which one shone white like a bit of silver and the other appeared black like a drop of ink. Captain Vincent, with his purpose firm in his mind, felt at peace with himself. As he was easily accessible to his officers, his first lieutenant ventured a question to which Captain Vincent replied:

“He looks very thin and worn out, but I don’t think he is as ill as he thinks he is. I am sure you all would like to know that his Lordship is pleased with our yesterday’s work﻿—those papers were of some importance you know﻿—and generally with the *Amelia*. It was a queer chase, wasn’t it?” he went on. “That tartane was clearly and unmistakably running away from us. But she never had a chance against the *Amelia*.”

===Chinese===
这一日下午的天气好得很。英国舰队除了两三条满张着帆掉在后面的落伍船之外，都已驶出视野之外。微风轻拂，只有阿米莉亚号能在其中走到五节航速，几乎吹不皱那在无云长空温暖的柔情里懒洋洋舒展着的深蓝海水。南面和西面的地平线上一片空旷，只有相隔极远的两个小点，其中一个白得像一小片银子，另一个则黑得像一滴墨。文森特舰长心头既已抱定了主意，便觉得自己与自己和解了。他平日里对军官们向来平易近人，于是大壮起胆子问了他一句，文森特舰长答道：

“他瞧着又瘦又憔悴，不过我看他并不像他自己以为的那样病得厉害。我敢说你们大伙儿都愿意知道，勋爵对我们昨天那趟活儿是满意的——那些文件是有些分量的，你们知道——而且总的说来，对阿米莉亚号也是满意的。那是一场古怪的追击，不是么？”他接着说，“那条塔尔塔纳，明明白白、毫无疑问是在躲着我们逃跑。可它对上阿米莉亚号，从来就没有过半点机会。”

===Original===
During the latter part of that speech the first lieutenant glanced astern as if asking himself how long Captain Vincent proposed to drag that tartane behind the *Amelia*. The two keepers in her wondered also as to when they would be permitted to get back on board their ship. Symons, who was one of them, declared that he was sick and tired of steering the blamed thing. Moreover, the company on board made him uncomfortable; for Symons was aware that in pursuance of Captain Vincent’s orders, Mr. Bolt had had the three dead Frenchmen carried into the cuddy, which he afterwards secured with an enormous padlock that, apparently, belonged to it, and had taken the key on board the *Amelia*. As to one of them, Symons’ unforgiving verdict was that it would have served him right to be thrown ashore for crows to peck his eyes out. And anyhow, he could not understand why he should have been turned into the coxswain of a floating hearse, and be damned to it.﻿ ﻿… He grumbled interminably.

===Chinese===
在他这番话的后半段，大副朝艉后瞥了一眼，像是在纳闷文森特舰长打算把那条塔尔塔纳在阿米莉亚号屁股后头拖到什么时候。船上那两个看守也心里嘀咕，不知何时才准他们回自己船上去。其中之一的西蒙斯直嚷嚷，说他厌烦透顶了，再不愿去掌这条该死的船的舵了。况且，船上的那些个“同伴”也叫他浑身不自在；因为西蒙斯知道，依照文森特舰长的命令，博尔特先生已把那三个法国死人搬进了后舱，随后又用一把显然本来就是那舱配的大得出奇的挂锁把舱锁了个严实，并把钥匙带回了阿米莉亚号。至于其中某一个，西蒙斯那不肯饶人的判词是：早该把他扔到岸上去，让乌鸦啄掉他的眼珠子，那才叫罪有应得。再说，不管怎样，他也想不通，自己怎么竟被征来做了一条水上灵车的舵手，真他娘的见鬼。……他嘟嘟囔囔地抱怨个没完。

===Original===
Just about sunset, which is the time of burials at sea, the *Amelia* was hove to and, the rope being manned, the tartane was brought alongside and her two keepers ordered on board their ship. Captain Vincent, leaning over with his elbows on the rail, seemed lost in thought. At last the first lieutenant spoke.

“What are we going to do with that tartane, sir? Our men are on board.”

“We are going to sink her by gunfire,” declared Captain Vincent suddenly. “His ship makes a very good coffin for a seaman, and those men deserve better than to be thrown overboard to roll on the waves. Let them rest quietly at the bottom of the sea in the craft to which they had stuck so well.”

The lieutenant, making no reply, waited for some more positive order. Every eye on the ship was turned on the captain. But Captain Vincent said nothing and seemed unable or unwilling to give it yet. He was feeling vaguely that in all his good intentions there was something wanting.

“Ah! Mr. Bolt,” he said, catching sight of the master’s mate in the waist. “Did they have a flag on board that craft?”

“I think she had a tiny bit of ensign when the chase began, sir, but it must have blown away. It is not at the end of her mainyard now.” He looked over the side. “The halliards are rove, though,” he added.

“We must have a French ensign somewhere on board,” said Captain Vincent.

“Certainly, sir,” struck in the master, who was listening.

“Well, Mr. Bolt,” said Captain Vincent, “you have had most to do with all this. Take a few men with you, bend the French ensign on the halliards and sway his mainyard to the masthead.” He smiled at all the faces turned towards him. “After all, they never surrendered and, by heavens, gentlemen, we will let them go down with their colours flying.”

===Chinese===
大约日落时分——这正是海葬的时辰——阿米莉亚号顶风停住了船，缆绳上一齐站满了人，那条塔尔塔纳被拽到了船舷旁边，船上那两名看守奉命回自己船上去。文森特舰长双肘撑在栏杆上俯身望着，似乎陷入了沉思。末了，大副开了口。

“我们打算拿那条塔尔塔纳怎么办，长官？我们的人已经回来了。”

“我们要用炮火把她击沉。”文森特舰长蓦地宣告道，“一个人自己的船，对航海人来说是再好不过的棺木；那几个人，不该只落得个被抛进海里、在浪头上翻滚的下场。就让他们安安静静地歇在这片海底，歇在他们如此忠贞不渝的那条船里罢。”

大副没有作答，等着更明确的号令。船上每一双眼睛都转向了舰长。可文森特舰长什么也没说，似乎既不能、也不肯这就下令。他隐约觉得，自己这满腔好意里，似乎还缺了点什么。

“啊！博尔特先生。”他一眼瞥见舯部那位准尉，便道，“那条船上原本有旗么？”

“追击刚起头的时候，我想是有一小块军旗的，长官，可八成是被风吹跑了。这会儿她主桁的桁端上并没有。”他往舷外一望，“不过升降索还穿着呢。”他补了一句。

“我们船上总该有一面法国军旗罢。”文森特舰长说。

“当然有，长官。”正听着的那位航海长插话道。

“那么，博尔特先生，”文森特舰长说，“这一摊事你插手最多。带几个人去，把那面法国军旗系到升降索上，把那条船的主桁升到桅顶上去。”他对着所有转向他的脸微微一笑。“说到底，他们不曾投降过；皇天在上，诸位，我们要让他们降着自家的旗子，沉下海去。”

===Original===
A profound but not disapproving silence reigned over the decks of the ship while Mr. Bolt with three or four hands was busy executing the order. Then suddenly above the topgallant rail of the *Amelia* appeared the upper curve of a lateen yard with the tricolour drooping from the point. A subdued murmur from all hands greeted this apparition. At the same time Captain Vincent ordered the line holding the tartane alongside to be cast off and the mainyard of the *Amelia* to be swung round. The sloop, shooting ahead of her prize, left her stationary on the sea, then putting the helm up, ran back abreast of her on the other side. The port bow-gun was ordered to fire a round, aiming well forward. That shot, however, went just over, taking the foremast out of the tartane. The next was more successful, striking the little hull between wind and water, and going out well under water on the other side. A third was fired, as the men said, just for luck, and that too took effect, a splintered hole appearing at the bow. After that the guns were secured and the *Amelia*, with no brace being touched, was brought to her course towards Cape Cicié. All hands on board of her with their backs to the sunset sky, clear like a pale topaz above the hard blue gem of the sea, watched the tartane give a sudden dip, followed by a slow, unchecked dive. At last the tricolour flag alone remained visible for a tense and interminable moment, pathetic and lonely, in the centre of a brimful horizon. All at once it vanished, like a flame blown upon, bringing to the beholders the sense of having been left face to face with an immense, suddenly created solitude. On the decks of the *Amelia* a low murmur died out.

---

===Chinese===
博尔特先生带着三四个人忙着执行命令的当口，全船的甲板上一片深沉却不带异议的寂静。随即，阿米莉亚号的上桅栏杆之上，忽然冒出了一根三角帆桁的上弧，桁尖垂着一面三色旗（tricolour）。一声压低了的众人的呢喃，迎接着这一景象的出现。与此同时，文森特舰长下令解开把塔尔塔纳拢在船旁的那根缆绳，并把阿米莉亚号的主桁转开。这条单桅快速帆船从她的战利品前头一冲而过，把她孤零零地撇在海面上，随即把舵一推，从另一侧与她并排着又跑了回来。左舷的艏炮奉命放上一炮，瞄的是船头偏前之处。然而那一炮稍稍打高了，倒把塔尔塔纳的前桅给打断了。第二炮便成功得多，正中那小小的船体在风与水之间的部位，从另一侧远远地钻到了水下。第三炮，据水手们说是“图个吉利”才放的，也中了，船头上现出一个炸裂的窟窿。此后炮位便被收妥，阿米莉亚号一缆不动地被引上了驶向西西耶角的航向。船上所有人背对着落日的天空——那片天宇在海面这块硬蓝宝石之上，清澄得像一抹淡黄色的托帕石——望着那条塔尔塔纳猛地一沉，紧接着便是一头缓慢的、无法阻止的下潜。到末了，唯有那面三色旗还孤零零、凄然地，在那盈满了的圆周地平线的正中央，显现了一个紧张而又漫长的瞬间。猛然间它消失了，像一团被吹了一口的火焰，叫看着它的人陡然生出一种被单独留下来、与一片骤然之间造就出来的巨大荒寂面面相对的感觉。阿米莉亚号的甲板上，一声低低的呢喃也渐渐沉寂下去。

---

===Original===
When Lieutenant Réal sailed away with the Toulon fleet on the great strategical cruise which was to end in the Battle of Trafalgar, Madame Réal returned with her aunt to her hereditary house at Escampobar. She had only spent a few weeks in town, where she was not much seen in public. The lieutenant and his wife lived in a little house near the western gate, and the lieutenant’s official position, though he was employed on the staff to the last, was not sufficiently prominent to make her absence from official ceremonies at all remarkable. But this marriage was an object of mild interest in naval circles. Those﻿—mostly men﻿—who had seen Madame Réal at home, told stories of her dazzling complexion, of her magnificent black eyes, of her personal and attractive strangeness, and of the Arlesian costume she insisted on wearing, even after her marriage to an officer of the navy, being herself sprung from farmer stock. It was also said that her father and mother had fallen victims in the massacres of Toulon after the evacuation of the town; but all those stories varied in detail and were on the whole very vague. Whenever she went abroad Mrs. Réal was attended by her aunt, who aroused almost as much curiosity as herself: a magnificent old woman with upright carriage and an austere, brown, wrinkled face showing signs of past beauty. Catherine was also seen alone in the streets, where, as a matter of fact, people turned round to look after the thin and dignified figure, remarkable amongst the passersby, whom she herself did not seem to see. About her escape from the massacres most wonderful tales were told, and she acquired the reputation of a heroine. Arlette’s aunt was known to frequent the churches, which were all open to the faithful now, carrying even into the house of God her sibylline aspect of a prophetess and her austere manner. It was not at the services that she was seen most. People would see her oftener in an empty nave, standing slim and as straight as an arrow in the shade of a mighty pillar as if making a call on the Creator of all things, with whom she had made her peace generously, and now would petition only for pardon and reconciliation with her niece Arlette. For Catherine for a long time remained uncertain of the future. She did not get rid of her involuntary awe of her niece as a selected object of God’s wrath until towards the end of her life. There was also another soul for which she was concerned. The pursuit of the tartane by the *Amelia* had been observed from various points of the islands that close the roadstead of Hyères, and the English ship had been seen from the Fort de la Vigie opening fire on her chase. The result, though the two vessels soon ran out of sight, could not be a matter of doubt. There was also the story told by a coaster, that got into Fréjus, of a tartane being fired on by a square-rigged man-of-war; but that apparently was the next day. All these rumours pointed one way and were the foundation of the report made by Lieutenant Réal to the Toulon Admiralty. That Peyrol went out to sea in his tartane and was never seen again was, of course, an incontrovertible fact.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉（Lieutenant Réal）随土伦舰队扬帆出海，踏上了那一场最终将终结于特拉法加海战（Battle of Trafalgar）的伟大战略巡航之时，雷亚尔夫人便携同姑母，回到了她在埃斯坎波巴（Escampobar）那座祖传的宅子里。她在城里不过住了几个星期，其间极少公开露面。中尉夫妇住在西门附近的一所小宅子里，中尉的公务差使虽然到末了仍是在参谋任上，却并不显赫，足以让她在官方仪典上的缺席显得如何惹眼。但这一桩婚事，在海军圈子里仍是个引人淡淡好奇的话题。那些——大多是男人——曾在家里见过雷亚尔夫人的，讲起她那亮得炫目的肤色，讲起她那一双华美的黑眼睛，讲起她身上那种既动人又古怪的异样气质，还讲起她执意要穿的那一身阿尔式装束（Arlesian costume），哪怕嫁给了海军军官也不肯改——她自己本是农家出身。也有人传说，她的父母是在弃城之后那场土伦大屠杀中罹难的；不过这些说法细节各异，总的说来都十分含糊。雷亚尔夫人每次出门，总有她那位姑母相陪；这位姑母所引起的猎奇之心，几乎不亚于她本人：一个气度不凡的老妇人，腰背笔挺，一张严峻、棕色、满是皱纹的脸上，还看得出当年美貌的痕迹。凯瑟琳（Catherine）也被人瞧见独自在街上走；事实上，行人见了她那瘦削而端庄的身影，无不回过头去追着看，在来来往往的人群中她是那样显眼，而她自己却似乎对谁都视而不见。关于她怎样逃出那场屠杀，流传着种种最离奇的传说，她也因此得了个女英雄的名声。阿尔莱特（Arlette）的这位姑母，是出了名的常进教堂——如今教堂已一律向信众敞开——她把那一副女先知般谜也似的神情和那严峻的做派，甚至带进了上帝的殿堂。人们最常看见她的，倒不是在做礼拜的时候。人们往往是在空无一人的正厅里瞧见她，纤细、笔直得像一支箭似的，立在一根巨柱的阴影里，仿佛是在登门造访那万物的造物主——她已慷慨地与他重修旧好，如今所求的，唯有宽恕，以及与她的外甥女阿尔莱特和解。因为凯瑟琳在很长一段时日里，对未来始终悬着心。直到她生命的暮年，她才终于摆脱了那一种身不由己的敬畏——把外甥女看作是被上帝选作震怒对象的畏惧。此外，也还有一个灵魂是她所挂念的。阿米莉亚号对塔尔塔纳的那场追逐，从合拢耶尔锚地的群岛上多处地点都被看见了；从维吉瞭望台（Fort de la Vigie）上也看见那条英国船朝着她所追的猎物开了火。结局虽然两船很快便驶出了视野，却是不容置疑的。此外，还有一条沿海小船带进弗雷瑞斯的一个说法，说一条横帆的军舰朝一条塔尔塔纳开了火；不过那显然是第二天的事了。所有这些风声都指向同一个方向，也成为雷亚尔中尉向土伦海军部所呈报告的依据。至于佩罗尔驾着他那条塔尔塔纳出海去、从此再没被人看见过，当然，乃是一个不容置辩的事实。

===Original===
The day before the two women were to go back to Escampobar Catherine approached a priest in the church of Ste Marie Majeure, a little unshaven fat man with a watery eye, in order to arrange for some masses to be said for the dead.

“But for whose soul are we to pray?” mumbled the priest in a wheezy low tone.

“Pray for the soul of Jean,” said Catherine. “Yes, Jean. There is no other name.”

===Chinese===
两位妇人启程回埃斯坎波巴的前一天，凯瑟琳在圣玛丽马若尔教堂（Ste Marie Majeure）里走向一位神父——一个胖胖的、没怎么刮脸、眼下水汪汪的小个子男人——去安排几场为亡者做的弥撒。

“可我们要为谁的灵魂祈祷呢？”那神父用一种喘着气的低声咕哝道。

“为让（Jean）的灵魂祈祷。”凯瑟琳说，“是的，让。再没有别的名字了。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Réal, wounded at Trafalgar, but escaping capture, retired with the rank of Capitaine de Frégate and vanished from the eyes of the naval world in Toulon, and indeed from the world altogether. Whatever sign brought him back to Escampobar on that momentous night was not meant to call him to his death but to a quiet and retired life, obscure in a sense but not devoid of dignity. In the course of years he became the Mayor of the Commune in that very same little village which had looked on Escampobar as the abode of iniquity, the sojourn of blood-drinkers and of wicked women.

===Chinese===
雷亚尔中尉在特拉法加海战中负了伤，却未被俘，以 *Capitaine de Frégate*（护卫舰上校）的军衔退役，自此既从土伦海军界的眼前消失，也简直是从这整个世界上消失了。无论那一夜是什么征兆把他召回了埃斯坎波巴，那征兆都不是要召他去赴死，而是要召他去过一种清静退隐的生活——某种意义上默默无闻，却并非毫无尊严。年深日久，他竟成了那同一座小村庄里的镇长；而这座村子，当年曾把埃斯坎波巴视作罪愆之窟、吸血之徒与恶毒妇人的栖身之地。

===Original===
One of the earliest excitements breaking the monotony of the Escampobar life was the discovery at the bottom of the well, one dry year when the water got very low, of some considerable obstruction. After a lot of trouble in getting it up, this obstruction turned out to be a garment made of sailcloth, which had armholes and three horn buttons in front, and looked like a waistcoat; but it was lined, positively quilted, with a surprising quantity of gold pieces of various ages, coinages and nationalities. Nobody but Peyrol could have put it there. Catherine was able to give the exact date; because she remembered seeing him doing something at the well on the very morning before he went out to sea with Michel, carrying off Scevola. Captain Réal could guess easily the origin of that treasure, and he decided with his wife’s approval to give it up to the Government as the hoard of a man who had died intestate with no discoverable relations, and whose very name had been a matter of uncertainty, even to himself. After that event the uncertain name of Peyrol found itself oftener and oftener on Monsieur and Madame Réal’s lips, on which before it was but seldom heard; though the recollection of his white-headed, quiet, irresistible personality haunted every corner of the Escampobar fields. From that time they talked of him openly, as though he had come back to live again amongst them.

===Chinese===
打破埃斯坎波巴日子之单调的，最早的一桩风波，是在某一个大旱之年、井水落得很低之时，于井底发现了一桩相当大的梗阻之物。费了好大劲把它捞上来一看，这梗阻之物竟是一件帆布做的衣裳，有两条袖洞，前面钉着三颗牛角扣，看着像件马甲；可它的夹层里——确切地说是絮着——满满地缝进了数量惊人的一堆金币，年号、铸地、国别各不相同。这事除了佩罗尔，再没第二个人干得出来。凯瑟琳能说出确切的日子；因为她记得清清楚楚，就在他带着米歇尔、押着塞沃拉出海之前的那个早上，亲眼瞧见他正在井边做着什么。雷亚尔上尉不难猜出这批财宝的来路，经妻子赞同，他决定把它上交政府，当作一个未立遗嘱、无可查考亲族、连姓名都未能确定——于他自己亦然——之人的遗赃。自那一桩事之后，佩罗尔这个悬而未决的名字，便越来越频繁地挂在雷亚尔先生和雷亚尔夫人的嘴边了，而从前它在这里是极少听人提起的；尽管他那白头发、沉静、不可抗拒的形象，仍像幽灵似的徘徊在埃斯坎波巴田野的每一个角落。从那时起，他们便不再避讳地谈论起他来，仿佛他已经回来，重新活在他们中间了。

===Original===
Many years afterwards, one fine evening Monsieur and Madame Réal, sitting on the bench outside the *salle* (the house had not been altered at all outside except that it was now kept whitewashed), began to talk of that episode and of the man who, coming from the seas, had crossed their lives to disappear at sea again.

“How did he get all that lot of gold?” wondered Madame Réal innocently. “He could not possibly want it; and, Eugène, why should he have put it down there?”

“That, *ma chère amie*,” said Réal, “is not an easy question to answer. Men and women are not so simple as they seem. Even you, *fermière* (he used to give his wife that name jocularly sometimes), are not so simple as some people would take you to be. I think that if Peyrol were here he could not perhaps answer your question himself.”

===Chinese===
多年之后，一个晴朗的黄昏，雷亚尔先生和雷亚尔夫人坐在 *salle*（正厅）门外那张长凳上（这房子从外面看丝毫没改过样，只不过如今一直刷得雪白），开始谈起了那一桩往事，谈起那个自海上而来、闯入他们一生、又在海上消失无踪的人。

“他怎么弄来那一大堆金币的呢？”雷亚尔夫人天真地纳闷道，“他压根儿就用不着这些呀；再说，欧仁，他当初为什么要把它搁到那下面去呢？”

“这个嘛，*ma chère amie*（我亲爱的）。”雷亚尔说，“可不是个容易回答的问题。男人和女人，并不像他们看上去那么简单。就连你，*fermière*（农妇）——他有时会打趣地这样叫妻子——也不像有些人以为的那么简单。我想，倘若佩罗尔就在这儿，恐怕他自己也答不上你这个问题。”

===Original===
And they went on, reminding each other in short phrases separated by long silences, of his peculiarities of person and behaviour, when, above the slope leading down to Madrague, there appeared, first the pointed ears and then the whole body of a very diminutive donkey of a light grey colour with dark points. Two pieces of wood, strangely shaped, projected on each side of his body as far as his head, like very long shafts of a cart. But the donkey dragged no cart after him. He was carrying on his back on a small packsaddle the torso of a man who did not seem to have any legs. The little animal, beautifully groomed and with an intelligent and even impudent physiognomy, stopped in front of Monsieur and Madame Réal. The man, balancing himself cleverly on the packsaddle with his withered legs crossed in front of him, slipped off, disengaged his crutches from each side of the donkey smartly, propped himself on them, and with his open palm gave the animal a resounding thwack which sent it trotting into the yard. The cripple of the Madrague in his quality of Peyrol’s friend (for the rover had often talked of him both to the women and to Lieutenant Réal with great appreciation﻿—“*C’est un homme ça*”) had become a member of the Escampobar community. His employment was to run about the country on errands, most unfit, one would think, for a man without legs. But the donkey did all the walking while the cripple supplied the sharp wits and an unfailing memory. The poor fellow, snatching off his hat and holding it with one hand alongside his right crutch, approached to render his account of the day in the simple words: “Everything has been done as you ordered, Madame”; then lingered, a privileged servant, familiar but respectful, attractive with his soft eyes, long face and his pained smile.

“We were just talking of Peyrol,” remarked Captain Réal.

“Ah, one could talk a long time of him,” said the cripple. “He told me once that if I had been complete﻿—with legs like everybody else, I suppose he meant﻿—I would have made a good comrade away there in the distant seas. He had a great heart.”

“Yes,” murmured Madame Réal thoughtfully. Then, turning to her husband, she asked: “What sort of man was he really, Eugène?” Captain Réal remained silent. “Did you ever ask yourself that question?” she insisted.

“Yes,” said Réal. “But the only certain thing we can say of him is that he was not a bad Frenchman.”

“Everything’s in that,” murmured the cripple, with fervent conviction, in the silence that fell upon Réal’s words and Arlette’s faint sigh of memory.

===Chinese===
他们就这样你一句我一句地继续着，在长长的沉默之间，用短短的话语彼此提醒着他的人和举止上的种种古怪之处；就在这时，在通向马德拉格（Madrague）的那道下坡的坡顶上方，先是冒出了两只尖尖的耳朵，紧接着便是一头淡灰色、四蹄和口鼻深色的、个头极小的毛驴的全身。两根形状古怪的木棍，从它身体两侧一直伸到与它的头齐平之处，像极了两根极长的车辕。可这毛驴身后并没有拖着什么车。它背上一副小小的驮鞍上，驮着一个男人的上半截身子——这男人似乎根本没有腿。这头被梳理得漂漂亮亮的小牲口，一副聪明相里还带着几分无赖气，在雷亚尔先生和夫人面前停了下来。那人凭着干瘪的双腿在身前盘着，机灵地在驮鞍上稳住了身子，一滑便下了地，麻利地从毛驴两侧抽出他的拐杖，往腋下一撑，又张开巴掌，照着那牲口就是一记响亮的巴掌，打得它小跑着进了院子。这位马德拉格的残疾汉（the cripple of the Madrague），凭着他是佩罗尔之友这一重身份（因为漫游者曾屡次对那几位妇人、也对雷亚尔中尉带着极大的称许谈起过他——“*C’est un homme ça*（那才算条汉子）”），已然成了埃斯坎波巴这个共同体的一员。他的差使是到乡间各处跑腿办事，照理说，对一个没有腿的人再不合适不过。可这一应行走之劳都是毛驴代劳，残疾汉所贡献的，是那副尖利的脑子和过目不忘的好记性。这可怜人摘下帽子，一手把它贴在右边的拐杖旁，走上前来，用简简单单的几句话回报他这一天的经过：“一切都照您吩咐的办妥了，夫人。”随后便迟迟不走，作为一个享有特权的仆役，熟稔而有礼，凭着他那双柔和的眼睛、那张长长的脸和他那带着痛楚的微笑，叫人看着只觉得可亲。

“我们正谈着佩罗尔呢。”雷亚尔上尉说道。

“唉，谈起他，可有的谈呢，能谈上好久。”残疾汉说，“他有一回跟我说，要是我是个全乎人——我猜他指的是像我之外人人都有两条腿——那我准能做个好伙计，陪他到那些遥远的海外去。他有一颗了不起的心。”

“是啊。”雷亚尔夫人沉思着喃喃道。接着，她转向丈夫，问道：“他到底是个什么样的人呢，欧仁？”雷亚尔上尉没有作声。“你自己可曾问过自己这个问题？”她追问道。

“问过。”雷亚尔说，“可我们唯一能拿得准地说出口的，只有这一句：他不是个坏法国人。”

“一切都在这一句话里了。”残疾汉带着热烈的笃信，在雷亚尔的话与阿尔莱特那一声追忆的轻叹之后落下的沉寂里，喃喃地说道。

===Original===
The blue level of the Mediterranean, the charmer and the deceiver of audacious men, kept the secret of its fascination﻿—hugged to its calm breast the victims of all the wars, calamities and tempests of its history, under the marvellous purity of the sunset sky. A few rosy clouds floated high up over the Esterel range. The breath of the evening breeze came to cool the heated rocks of Escampobar; and the mulberry-tree, the only big tree on the head of the peninsula, standing like a sentinel at the gate of the yard, sighed faintly in a shudder of all its leaves as if regretting the Brother of the Coast, the man of dark deeds, but of large heart, who often at noonday would lie down to sleep under its shade.

===Chinese===
那一派蔚蓝、平展的地中海——这大胆之人的魅惑者与欺骗者——守着它那蛊惑人心的秘密，在落日长空那一片奇绝的澄净之下，把古往今来一切战火、灾祸与风暴的牺牲者，都紧紧抱在它平静的胸膛上。埃斯泰雷尔山（Esterel range）上空高处，飘着几朵玫瑰色的云。晚风的一缕气息拂来，凉了埃斯坎波巴被晒热的岩石；而那棵桑树——半岛顶端唯一的一棵大树，像一个哨兵似的矗立在院门口——在它全部叶子的一个颤栗里轻轻地叹息了一声，仿佛是在怀念那位海岸兄弟会（Brothers of the Coast）的兄弟，那个行过黑暗之事、却胸怀阔大的人，那个常常在正午时分躺到它树荫之下去睡觉的人。
